胡雪岩｜中英对照版
原著：高阳

萧瑟洋场｜The Desolate Foreign Settlement

阅读说明：按完整语义段落排列，每段中文后接同一段英文；两版英文正文一致。下载站水印与少量疑字的处理见各批说明。

本书是中文小说的英文译读材料。阅读、完形、段落衔接、翻译与表达积累的学习提示另列在文末，不混入小说正文。

第一章｜CHAPTER ONE

因为如此，螺蛳太太的心境虽然跟胡雪岩一样，不同往年，还是强打精神，扮出笑脸，热热闹闹地过了一个年。接着便又要为胡老太太的生日，大忙特忙了。生日在三月初八，『浩治桃觞，恭请光临』的请帖，却在年前就发出去了。到得二月中旬，京中及各省送礼的专差，络绎来到杭州，胡府上派有专人接待；送的礼都是物轻意重，因为胡雪岩既有『财神』之号，送任何贵重之物，都等于『白搭』，惟有具官衔的联幛寿序，才是可使寿堂生色的。

For this reason, although Madam Luosi, like Hu Xueyan, felt quite unlike her usual self at the turn of the year, she forced herself to be cheerful, put on a smiling face, and saw the household through a lively New Year. Then came another rush of preparations, this time for Old Madam Hu’s birthday. The birthday fell on the eighth day of the third month, but invitations announcing that a birthday feast would be laid and respectfully requesting the guests’ presence had gone out before the New Year. By the middle of the second month, special messengers bringing gifts from the capital and the provinces were arriving in Hangzhou in a steady stream. Members of the Hu household had been assigned specifically to receive them. The gifts were modest in material value but rich in sentiment. Since Hu Xueyan already bore the title of God of Wealth, anything merely expensive would be so much wasted effort. What could truly lend distinction to the birthday halls were congratulatory couplets, embroidered banners, and birthday tributes bearing their donors’ official titles.

寿堂共设七处，最主要的一处，不在元宝街，而是在灵隐的云林寺。铺设这处寿堂时，胡雪岩带着请客，亲自主持，正中上方高悬一方红地金书的匾额，『淑德彰闻』，上铭一方御玺∶『慈禧皇太后之宝』，款书∶『赐正一品封典布政使衔东西候补道胡光墉之母朱氏』。匾额之下，应该挂谁送的联幛，却费斟酌了。原来京中除了王公亲贵，定制向不与品官士庶应酬往来以外，自大学士、军机大臣以下，六部九卿，都送了寿礼，李鸿章与左宗棠一样，也是一联一幛，论官位，武英殿大学士李鸿章，久居首辅，百僚之长，应该居中。但胡雪岩却执意要推尊左宗棠，便有爱人以德的一个名叫张爱晖的清客，提出规劝。

Seven birthday halls had been prepared. The most important was not in Yuanbao Street but at Yunlin Temple at Lingyin. Hu Xueyan brought his resident literary companions there and personally supervised its decoration. High in the center hung a plaque with gold lettering on a red ground: “Her Virtuous Conduct Is Widely Known.” Above the inscription appeared the imperial seal, “Treasure of the Empress Dowager Cixi”; below it was the dedication, “Bestowed upon Lady Zhu, mother of Hu Guangyong, expectant circuit intendant [the province is corrupt in the source], bearing the honorary rank of provincial treasurer and the honors of the senior first rank.” Whose couplets and banners should hang beneath it required some thought. In the capital, princes and members of the imperial family were ordinarily prohibited by established custom from exchanging such courtesies with officials or private citizens. Apart from them, however, everyone from the grand secretaries and grand councilors down through the heads of the Six Ministries and the Nine Courts had sent birthday gifts. Li Hongzhang, like Zuo Zongtang, had sent a pair of couplets and an embroidered banner. In terms of official standing, Li, Grand Secretary of the Wuying Hall, had long been the senior grand secretary and the foremost of all civil officials; his gifts belonged in the center. Hu Xueyan, however, insisted on giving Zuo pride of place. One of his literary companions, Zhang Aihui, believing that true concern for a friend required guiding him toward proper conduct, ventured to remonstrate.

『大先生，朝廷名器至重，李合服是首辅；左湘阴是东阁大学士，入阁的资格很浅，不能不委屈。这样的大场面，次序弄错了。要受批评；如果再有好事的言官吹毛疵，说大先生以私情乱纲纪，搞出啥不痛快的事来，也太无谓了。』『你的话不错。不过「花花轿儿人抬人」，湘阴这样看得起我，遇到这种场面，我不捧他一捧，拿他贬成第二，我自己都觉得良心上说不过去。』『话不是这么说。大先生，你按规矩办事，湘阴一定也原谅的。』

“Mr Hu, the ranks and honors conferred by the throne are matters of the utmost importance. Lord Li of Hefei is the senior grand secretary. Lord Zuo of Xiangyin is Grand Secretary of the Eastern Pavilion and entered the Grand Secretariat only recently. He must accept the lesser position. If the order of precedence is wrong at an occasion as grand as this, people will criticize it. And should some meddlesome censor choose to pick faults and accuse you of letting private attachments overturn the established order, whatever unpleasantness followed would be quite needless.” “You are right. But a splendid sedan chair needs people to lift it: we all depend on others to help us up. Xiangyin thinks so highly of me. If I fail to uphold him on an occasion like this and put him down in second place, my own conscience will reproach me.” “That is not the way to look at it, Mr Hu. If you follow the rules, Xiangyin will certainly understand.”

『就算他原谅，我自己没法子原谅，张先生，你倒想个理由出来，怎么能拿湘阴居中。』『没有理由。』张爱晖又说∶『大先生，你也犯不着无缘无故得罪李合肥。』胡雪岩不作声，局面看着要僵了；那常来走动的乌先生忽然说道∶『有办法，只要把下款改一改好了。』『怎么改法？』胡雪岩很高兴问。『加上爵位就可以了。』

“Even if he understands, I cannot forgive myself. Mr Zhang, do try to think of a reason that would let us put Xiangyin in the center.” “There is no such reason,” Zhang Aihui replied. “Besides, Mr Hu, you have no need to offend Lord Li of Hefei for nothing.” Hu Xueyan said nothing. The discussion was on the point of reaching an impasse when Mr Wu, a frequent visitor, suddenly spoke. “There is a way. We need only alter the signature at the bottom.” “How?” Hu asked eagerly. “Add his hereditary title.”

原来左宗棠送的寿幛，上款是『胡老伯母六秩晋九荣庆』，下款是『秃头』的『左宗棠拜祝』，平辈论交，本来是极有面子的事；乌先生主张加上左宗棠的爵位，变成『恪靖候左宗棠拜祝』；这一来就可居李之上了，因为李鸿章的下款上加全衔『武英殿大学士北洋大臣直隶总督部堂肃毅伯』，伯爵次侯爵一等，只好屈居左宗棠之次。那乌先生是个庙祝，只为他是螺蛳太太的『娘家人』；胡雪岩爱屋及乌，将他侧于清客之列。一直不大被看得起，此时出此高明的一着，大家不由得刮目相看了。

The banner Zuo Zongtang had sent was addressed, “To my honored Aunt Hu, in celebration of her sixty-ninth birthday.” At the bottom stood the bare signature, “Zuo Zongtang respectfully offers his congratulations.” For Zuo to treat Hu as a friend of his own generation was in itself a considerable distinction. Mr Wu proposed adding Zuo’s hereditary title, making the signature read, “Zuo Zongtang, Marquis Kejing, respectfully offers his congratulations.” This would place him above Li. Even if Li’s signature were furnished with all his titles—Grand Secretary of the Wuying Hall, Minister of Beiyang, Governor-General of Zhili, and Earl Suyi—an earl still ranked one grade below a marquis and would have to yield precedence. Mr Wu was only a temple keeper. Hu Xueyan had included him among his literary companions because he belonged to Madam Luosi’s own circle: affection for her extended to those connected with her. He had never been held in much regard. This masterly suggestion made everyone see him in a new light.

『不过大先生，我倒还要放肆，胡出一个主意。如果左湘阴居中，李合肥的联幛只好挂在东面板壁，未免贬之过甚；是不是中国挂一幅瑶池祝寿图，拿左、李的联幛分悬上下首，比较合适？』胡雪岩看乌先生善持大体，便请他专管灵隐这个最主要的寿堂，而且关照他的一个外甥张安明，遇事常找乌先生来商量；张安明是胡府做寿揽全局的大总管。

“Still, Mr Hu, let me presume to offer one more suggestion. If Lord Zuo of Xiangyin hangs in the center, Lord Li of Hefei’s couplets and banner will have to go on the paneled wall to the east. That seems rather too great a slight. Would it not be better to hang a picture of the immortals offering birthday greetings at the Jade Pool in the center, with Zuo’s and Li’s gifts on the more and less honored sides?” Seeing how carefully Mr Wu considered the whole situation, Hu put him in sole charge of the principal birthday hall at Lingyin. He also instructed his nephew Zhang Anming to consult Mr Wu whenever anything arose. Zhang was the chief steward responsible for the entire birthday celebration.

张安明自然奉命唯谨，当天就请乌先生小酌，诚意请教，『有件事，不晓得乌先生有啥好主意？他说，』寿堂虽有七处，贺客太多，身分不同，挤在一起，乱得一塌糊涂，一定要改良。『』寿堂是七处，做寿是不是也做七天？『『不错。大先生说，宫里的规矩「前三后四」，要七天。』张安明轻声答说∶『不过，这话对外面不便明说；只说老太太生日要「打七」，所以开贺也是七天。』『打七』便是设一坛水陆道场，是佛门中最隆重的法事，称为『水陆斋仪』，亦名『水陆道场』，俗称『打水陆』。斋仪又有繁简之分，讽经礼忏七七四十九日称为『打水陆』；为了祝厘延寿，通常只须七日，叫做『打七』。

Zhang Anming naturally obeyed to the letter. That very day he invited Mr Wu to drink with him and sincerely sought his advice. “There is something I should like your opinion on,” he said. “We have seven birthday halls, but there will be so many guests, of such different standing, that if they all crowd in together it will be chaos. We must find a better arrangement.” “There are seven halls. Will the celebrations last seven days as well?” “Yes. Mr Hu says the palace custom is ‘three days before and four days after,’ making seven days.” Zhang lowered his voice. “But we cannot say that openly. We shall simply say that the old lady’s birthday calls for a seven-day Buddhist observance, so guests will be received for seven days.” This observance was a shortened form of the Water and Land rite, the most solemn of Buddhist services, formally called the Water and Land Fasting Ritual and popularly known as “performing the Water and Land service.” The full ritual, with scripture recitation and rites of repentance, lasted seven times seven days, or forty-nine days. For prayers to secure blessings and prolong life, seven days were generally sufficient; hence the name “the seven-day observance.”

『有七处寿堂，又分七天受贺，大可分门别类，拿贺客错开来，接待容易，而且酒席也不致于糟蹋。』『这个主意好。我们来分它一分。』于是细细商量，决定第一天请官场，三品以上文武大员；五品以下文武职官，占了四个寿堂，此外是现奉差委的佐杂官，与文武候补人员各一；留下一处专供临时由外地赶到的官员祝寿之用。第二天请商场，丝、茶、盐、典、钱、药、绸各行各业的伙友，分开七处。第三天是各衙门的司事，以及吏户礼兵刑工六科的书办；第四天是出家人的日子；第五天、第六天请亲戚朋友，一天『官客』，一天『堂客』，一天『堂客』。第七天是寿辰正日，自然是自己人热闹热闹。

“With seven halls and seven days for receiving congratulations, you can easily divide the guests into categories and keep them apart. They will be easier to entertain, and the banquets will not go to waste.” “An excellent idea. Let us work out the divisions.” After careful discussion, they decided to invite the official world on the first day. Civil and military dignitaries of the third rank and above, together with civil and military officials of the fifth rank and below, would occupy four halls. Minor officials currently holding special assignments would take another, and expectant civil and military officials another. The seventh would be kept for officials arriving unexpectedly from out of town. The second day would be for the business world, with associates from the silk, tea, salt, pawnbroking, banking, medicine, and silk-fabric trades divided among the seven halls. The third would be for functionaries from the various government offices and the clerks of the six departments—Personnel, Revenue, Rites, War, Punishments, and Works. The fourth would be for members of religious orders. Relatives and friends would be invited on the fifth and sixth days, the men on one day and the women on the other. The seventh, the birthday itself, would naturally be a cheerful gathering of the family and their closest associates.

这样安排好了，去请示胡雪岩；他不甚满意，『自己人热闹热闹，用不着七处寿堂，而且光是自己人，也热闹不起来。』他说，『我看还要斟酌。而且我的洋朋友很多，他们来了，到哪里去拜寿？』『这样好了，专留一天给洋人。』乌先生说∶『一到三、四月里，来逛逛西湖的很多，大先生索性请个客，这一天的洋人，不论识与不识，只要来拜寿的，一律请吃寿酒。』『洋人捏不来筷子。』胡雪岩说∶『要请就要请吃大菜。』『这要请古先生来商量了。』请了古应春来筹划。由于洋人语言不尽相同；饮食习惯，亦有差异，好在有七处寿堂，决定英、法、德、美、日、俄、比七国，各占一处。

They took the completed arrangements to Hu Xueyan, who was not entirely satisfied. “We do not need seven halls merely for a family gathering. Besides, with no one but our own people it will hardly be lively enough. We must think again. And I have many foreign friends. Where are they to offer their congratulations when they come?” “Then let us set aside a day especially for foreigners,” Mr Wu suggested. “Many come to see West Lake in the third and fourth months. You might as well make it an open invitation: any foreigner who comes that day to offer birthday greetings, whether you know him or not, shall be invited to the feast.” “Foreigners cannot handle chopsticks,” Hu said. “If we invite them, we must serve Western food.” “We shall have to consult Mr Gu about that.” Gu Yingchun was summoned to plan it. The foreigners did not all speak the same language, and their eating habits differed too. Fortunately, there were seven halls. One each was assigned to the British, French, Germans, Americans, Japanese, Russians, and Belgians.

『应春，』胡雪岩说∶『这七处接待，归你总其成。大菜司务，归你到上海去请。』『好。』古应春说∶『要把日子定下来，我到上海，请字林西报的朋友登条新闻，到时候洋人自然会来。』『妙极！』张安明笑道∶『外婆生日，洋人拜寿∶只怕从古以来的老太太，只有外婆有这份福气。』果然，胡老太太听了也很高兴。胡家的至亲好友，更拿这件事当作新闻去传说，而且都兴致勃勃地要等看见洋人拜寿。

“Yingchun,” Hu said, “you will oversee the reception arrangements at all seven places. Go to Shanghai and engage the cooks for the Western meals yourself.” “Very well,” Gu replied. “Once we have fixed the day, I shall ask my friends at the North-China Daily News to print a notice when I go to Shanghai. The foreigners will then come of their own accord.” “Splendid!” Zhang Anming said, laughing. “Foreigners paying their respects on Grandmother’s birthday! I doubt any old lady in all history has enjoyed such good fortune.” Old Madam Hu was indeed delighted when she heard. The family’s close relatives and friends spread the story as a remarkable piece of news, all eagerly awaiting the sight of foreigners offering birthday congratulations.

这年杭州的春天，格外热闹，天气暖和，香客船自然就到得多，这还在其次；主要的是胡老太太做生日，传说如何如何豪华阔气，招引了好些人来看热闹。何况光算外地来拜寿的人，起码也增加了好几千人。到得开贺的第一天，城里四处，城外三处，张灯结彩，『清音堂名』细吹细打的寿堂周围，车马喧阗，加上看热闹的闲人、卖熟食的小贩，挤得寸步难行。只有灵隐是例外，因为三大宪要来拜寿，仁钱两县的差役以外，『抚标』亦派出穿了簇新号褂子的兵丁，自九里松开始，沿路布哨弹压，留下了极宽的一条路，直通灵隐山门。

Spring in Hangzhou was unusually lively that year. The warm weather naturally brought more boats carrying pilgrims, but that was a lesser cause. The chief attraction was Old Madam Hu’s birthday: accounts of the celebration’s magnificent extravagance had drawn crowds of curious onlookers. Those coming from other places specifically to offer their congratulations must alone have added several thousand people. On the first day for receiving guests, four halls inside the city and three outside it were festooned with lanterns and colored decorations. Around the halls, where ceremonial ensembles softly played their wind instruments and percussion, carriages and horses made a great clamor. Add idle spectators and vendors of cooked food, and the crowds were so dense that one could scarcely move. Lingyin alone was an exception. Since the three highest provincial authorities were coming, soldiers from the governor’s own command, wearing brand-new uniform jackets, joined the runners of Renhe and Qiantang counties. From Jiulisong onward they posted guards along the road to maintain order, leaving a broad passage clear all the way to the temple gate.

从山门到寿堂，寿联寿幛，沿路挂满；寿堂上除了胡雪岩领着子侄，等在那里，预备答谢以外，另外请了四位绅士『知宾』。一位是告假回籍养亲的内阁学士陈怡恭，专陪浙江巡抚刘秉璋∶一位是做过山西臬司，告老回乡的汤仲思；另外两位都是候补道，三品服饰，华丽非凡，是张安明受命派了裁缝，量身现做奉赠的。近午时分，刘秉璋鸣锣喝道，到了灵隐，藩臬两司，早就到了，在寿堂前面迎接；轿子一停，陈怡恭抢上前去，抱拳说道∶『承宪台光临，主人家心感万分。请，请！』肃客上堂，行完了礼，刘秉璋抬头先看他送的一堂寿序，挂在西壁最前端，与大学士宝均金送的一副寿联，遥遥相对；这是很尊重表示，他微微点头，表示满意。

From the gate to the birthday hall, congratulatory couplets and embroidered banners hung everywhere. In the hall, Hu Xueyan and the younger men of his family waited to return the visitors’ courtesies. Four gentlemen had also been invited to act as hosts. One was Chen Yigong, a subchancellor of the Grand Secretariat home on leave to care for his parents, who would attend specifically to Liu Bingzhang, Governor of Zhejiang. Another was Tang Zhongsi, a former provincial judicial commissioner of Shanxi who had retired to his native place. The other two were expectant circuit intendants, magnificently dressed in third-rank robes that Zhang Anming, acting on instructions, had sent a tailor to measure, make, and present to them. Near noon, Liu Bingzhang arrived at Lingyin to the beating of gongs and the cries of his advance attendants clearing the way. The provincial treasurer and judicial commissioner, who had arrived earlier, waited before the birthday hall to receive him. As soon as his sedan stopped, Chen Yigong stepped forward with his hands clasped. “Your Excellency’s presence fills our host with gratitude. Please come in.” He ceremoniously conducted the governor into the hall. After performing the proper greetings, Liu looked up first for the birthday tribute he himself had sent. It hung at the foremost end of the western wall, facing a pair of couplets sent by Grand Secretary Bao Yun. This was a marked sign of respect. He gave a slight, satisfied nod.

这时率领子侄在一旁答礼的胡雪岩，从红毡条上站起身来，含笑称谢∶『多谢老公祖步，真不敢当。』这『老公祖』的称呼，也是乌先生想出来的。因为胡雪岩是布政使衔的道员，老母又有正一品的封典，自觉地位并不下于巡抚，要叫一声『大人』，于心不甘；如用平辈的称谓，刘秉璋字仲良，叫他『仲翁』，又嫌太亢。这个小小的难题跟乌先生谈起，他建议索性用『老父母』的称呼；地方官是所谓父母官，士绅对县官称『老父母』，藩臬两司及巡抚则称『老公祖』，这样以部民自居，一方面是尊重巡抚，一方面不亢不卑反而留了身分。

Hu Xueyan, who had been leading the younger men of his family in returning the greetings from one side, rose from the strip of red felt on which he had knelt. Smiling, he offered his thanks. “We are deeply honored that our venerable grandparent has troubled to come.” The form of address, “venerable grandparent,” was another of Mr Wu’s inventions. Hu was a circuit intendant with the honorary rank of provincial treasurer, and his mother had been granted the honors of the senior first rank. He did not feel that his own standing was lower than the governor’s, and he was unwilling to address him as “Your Excellency.” Yet to address him as an equal, calling him “Mr Zhong” from his courtesy name Zhongliang, would be too presumptuous. When Hu mentioned this small difficulty to Mr Wu, the latter suggested simply using the language of parental authority. Local officials were called the people’s father and mother; members of the gentry addressed a magistrate as their “venerable parent,” and the provincial treasurer, judicial commissioner, or governor as their “venerable grandparent.” By speaking as one of the people under the governor’s jurisdiction, Hu could show due respect while preserving his own standing, neither arrogant nor servile.

刘秉璋自然称他『雪翁』，说了些恭维胡老太太好福气的话，由陈怡恭请到寿堂东面的客座中待茶，十六个簇新的高脚金果盘，映得刘秉璋的脸都黄了。稍坐一坐，请去入席。寿筵设在方丈之西的青猊轩；这座敞轩高三太六尺，一共六间，南面监时搭出极讲究的戏台，台前约两丈许，并排设下三席，巡抚居中，东西藩臬；大方桌前面系着平金绣花桌围，贵客面对戏台上坐，陈怡恭与胡雪岩左右相陪；后面另有四席，为有差使的候补道而设。偌大厅堂，只得七桌，连陪客都不超过三十个人，但捧着衣包的随从跟班，在后面却都站满了。

Liu Bingzhang naturally addressed him as “Mr Xue” and offered some complimentary remarks about Old Madam Hu’s good fortune. Chen Yigong then escorted him to the guest seats east of the hall for tea. Sixteen gleaming new gold fruit stands, raised on high stems, cast such a glow that even Liu’s face seemed yellow. After a short rest, the party was invited to the banquet. It was held in Qingni Pavilion, west of the abbot’s quarters, an open hall six bays wide and thirty-six chi high. A finely constructed temporary stage stood to the south. About two zhang in front of it, three tables had been placed in a row, with the governor in the center and the treasurer and judicial commissioner to east and west. The large square tables had embroidered coverings worked in flat gold thread hanging down their fronts. The honored guests sat in the upper seats facing the stage, with Chen Yigong and Hu Xueyan attending on either side. Four more tables behind were for expectant circuit intendants currently holding appointments. There were only seven tables in that enormous hall and fewer than thirty diners, even counting those present as companions. Behind them, however, stood a whole crowd of attendants carrying their masters’ bundles of spare clothing.

等安席既罢，戏台上正在唱着的『鸿鸾禧』暂时停了下来，小锣打上一个红袍乌纱、玉带围腰，口衔面具的『吏部天官』，一步三摆地，步到台前『跳加官』。这是颂祝贵客『指日高升』、『一品当朝』，照例须由在座官位最高的人放赏；不过只要刘秉璋交代一声就行了，主人家早备着大量刚出炉的制钱，盛在竹筐中，听得一个『赏』字，便有四名健仆，抬着竹筐，疾步上前，合力举起来向台上一泼，只听『哗喇喇』满台钱响，声势惊人。

Once everyone was seated, the performance of Hongluan Xi, then in progress, stopped briefly. To the tap of a small gong, the Celestial Official of the Ministry of Personnel appeared in a red robe, black official hat, and jade belt, holding his mask in place between his teeth. With ceremonious swaying steps, he advanced to the front of the stage to perform the Dance of Promotion. This conveyed wishes that the honored guests might “rise in office any day now” and “hold first rank at court.” By custom, the highest-ranking official present had to reward the performers. Liu Bingzhang needed only to give the order: the host had already prepared a great quantity of newly minted copper cash in a bamboo hamper. At the word “Reward!” four sturdy servants lifted the hamper, hurried forward, and together heaved it up and poured its contents onto the stage. Coins clattered across the boards in an astonishing display.

接下来便作戏班子的掌班，戴一顶红缨帽，走到筵前，一膝屈地，高举着戏折子说道∶『请大人点戏。』『点戏』颇有学问。因为戏名吉祥，戏实不祥，这种名实不副的戏文很多，不会点会闹笑话；或者戏中情节，恰恰犯了主人家或者哪一贵宾的忌讳，点到这样的戏，无异公然揭人隐私，因而成不解之仇者，亦时有所闻。刘秉璋对此道外行，决定藏拙；好在另有内行在，当下吩咐∶『请德大人点。』他指的是坐在东面的藩司德馨，他是旗人，出身纨绔，最好戏曲；当下略略客气了两句，便当仁不让地点了四出不犯忌讳而又热闹的好戏，第一出是『战宛城』，饰邹氏的朱韵秋，外号『羊毛笔』，是德馨紧赏识的花旦，演到『思春』那一段，真如用『羊毛笔』写赵孟钭郑崦耐鹱钊艘庀正当德馨全神贯注在台上时，有个身穿行装的『戈什哈』悄悄走到他身旁，递上一封信说∶『陈师爷派专人送来的。』陈师爷是德馨的亲信，此时派专人送来函件，当然是极紧要的事；因而当筵拆阅，只见他面现诧异之色，挥一挥手遣走『戈什哈』，双眼便不是专注在『羊毛笔』身上，而是不时朝刘秉璋那边望去。

Next the troupe’s manager, wearing a hat with a red tassel, came before the tables. Dropping to one knee, he raised the repertoire list above his head. “May Your Excellency choose the plays.” Choosing plays required considerable knowledge. Many had auspicious titles but unhappy plots; an ignorant choice could make one a laughingstock. Worse, something in a play might touch a sensitive point in the history of the host or one of the distinguished guests. Choosing such a work could amount to publicly exposing someone’s private affairs, and cases of lasting enmity arising from it were not unknown. Liu Bingzhang knew little about the theater and decided not to reveal his ignorance. Fortunately, an expert was at hand. “Ask Lord De to choose,” he said, indicating Dexin, the provincial treasurer, seated to the east. A bannerman brought up in privileged circumstances, Dexin loved the theater above all else. After a few modest words, he readily selected four lively and excellent plays that offended no taboos. The first was The Battle of Wancheng. Zhu Yunqiu, who played Lady Zou, was a lively female-role performer known as “the Goat-Hair Brush,” a particular favorite of Dexin’s. In the scene of awakening romantic longing, the performance was likened to calligraphy traced with such a brush [the comparison is partly illegible in the source]. While Dexin was wholly absorbed in the stage, a personal guard in traveling clothes quietly approached and handed him a letter. “Secretary Chen sent a special messenger with this.” Secretary Chen was Dexin’s trusted aide. A letter delivered by special messenger at such a time must concern something urgent. Dexin opened it at the table. Surprise appeared on his face. He waved the guard away, and his eyes no longer remained fixed on “the Goat-Hair Brush”: instead, he kept glancing toward Liu Bingzhang.

他是在注意胡雪岩的动静，一看他暂时离席，随即走了过去，将那封信递了过去，轻声说道∶『刚从上海来的消息。』刘秉璋看完信，只是眨眼在思索；好一会才将原信递给陈怡恭∶『年兄，你看，消息不巧；今天这个日子，似乎不宜张扬。』『是！』陈怡恭看完信说∶『这一来，政局恐不免有一番小小的变动。』『是的。』刘秉璋转脸问德馨说∶『请老兄在这里绷住场面，我得赶紧进城了。』

He was watching for a movement from Hu Xueyan. As soon as Hu left the table for a moment, Dexin went over and handed the governor the letter, saying quietly, “News just in from Shanghai.” Liu read it and sat blinking in thought. Only after some time did he pass the letter to Chen Yigong. “My friend, look at this. The timing is unfortunate. On a day like this, it hardly seems proper to make it known.” “Quite,” Chen said after reading it. “This may bring about a small change in the political situation.” “Yes.” Liu turned to Dexin. “Please keep things going here. I must hurry back to the city.”

德馨也想回衙门，听刘秉璋如此交代，只能答应一声∶『是。』于是刘秉璋回身招一招手，唤来他的跟班吩咐∶『提轿。』接着向陈怡恭拱一拱手，正待托他代向主人告辞时，胡雪岩回来了。『怎么？』他问∶『老公祖是要更衣？』『不是！』刘秉璋歉意地说∶『雪翁，这么好的戏、好的席，我竟无福消受；实在是有急事，马上得回城料理。』『呃、呃。』胡雪岩不便多问；只跟在刘秉璋后面，送上轿后方始问德馨∶『刘中丞何以如此匆匆？到底是什么急事？』『此处不便谈。』德馨与胡雪岩的交情极厚，以兄弟相称∶『胡大哥，有个消息，不便在今天宣扬，不过，消息不坏。』

Dexin, too, wanted to return to his office, but after the governor’s request he could only answer, “Certainly.” Liu turned and beckoned to his attendant. “Have the sedan brought up.” He then clasped his hands to Chen Yigong and was just about to ask him to make his excuses to the host when Hu Xueyan returned. “What is it?” Hu asked. “Does our venerable grandparent wish to retire for a moment?” “No,” Liu replied apologetically. “Mr Xue, such fine plays and such an excellent banquet, and I am denied the pleasure of enjoying them! An urgent matter requires me to return to the city at once.” “Ah, of course.” Hu could hardly press him. He followed Liu out and saw him into his sedan. Only then did he ask Dexin, “Why is Governor Liu in such a hurry? What can be so urgent?” “We cannot discuss it here.” Dexin and Hu were close enough to address each other as brothers. “Brother Hu, there is some news it would be improper to spread today. But it is not bad news.”

胡雪岩点点头不作声，回到筵前，直待曲终人散∶才邀德馨他借住的一间禅房中，细问究竟。『为什么今天不便宣扬呢？』德馨说道∶『李太夫人在武昌去世了。』去世的是李瀚章、李鸿章兄弟的老母。胡老太太做生日，自然不便宣布这样一个不吉利的消息。但这一来，李氏兄弟丁忧守制，左宗棠暂时去了一个政敌，对胡雪岩来说，当然是有利的，亦可说是喜事，不过只能喜在心里而已。『一下子两个总督出缺，封疆大吏要扳扳位了。不晓得哪个接直隶，哪个接湖广？』

Hu nodded without speaking and returned to the banquet. He waited until the music had ended and the company dispersed before inviting Dexin to the monks’ cell where he was staying and asking for the details. “Why would it be improper to spread it today?” Dexin said. “Old Madam Li has died in Wuchang.” The dead woman was the mother of the brothers Li Hanzhang and Li Hongzhang. Naturally, news of a death could not be announced during Old Madam Hu’s birthday celebration. But the Li brothers would now have to retire from office to observe the prescribed mourning. Zuo Zongtang would temporarily be rid of a political opponent. For Hu Xueyan this was certainly favorable, even a cause for rejoicing, though he could rejoice only inwardly. “Two governorships-general vacant at once! The leading provincial officials will all have to shift their places. I wonder who will succeed in Zhili and who in Huguang?”

这一问，恰恰说中德馨的心事。总督出缺，大致总是由巡抚调升；巡抚有缺，藩司便可竞争，刘秉璋与德馨，各有所图，所以都急着要赶进城去打听消息。不过德馨既有巡抚嘱咐，又有胡家交情在，不便就此告辞，心想何不就跟胡雪岩谈谈心事。『湖广，我看十之八九是涂朗轩接，直隶就不知道了。』涂朗轩就是湖南巡抚涂宗瀛，他替曾国藩办过粮，与李瀚章昔为同事，今为僚属，由他来接湖广总督，倒是顺理成章的事。『那末湖南巡抚呢？』胡雪岩笑着掉了句文∶『阁下甚有意乎？』『只怕人家捷足先登了。』

The question touched precisely on what occupied Dexin’s mind. A vacant governorship-general would normally be filled by promoting a governor; a vacant governorship would in turn be open to competition among the provincial treasurers. Liu Bingzhang and Dexin each had his own hopes. Both had therefore been eager to hurry back to the city and inquire into the news. But Dexin had the governor’s instructions to fulfill, as well as his friendship with the Hu family to consider, and could not simply take his leave. Why not discuss his thoughts with Hu? “For Huguang, I should say Tu Langxuan is almost certain to get it. Zhili I cannot guess.” Tu Langxuan was Tu Zongying, Governor of Hunan. He had once managed grain supplies for Zeng Guofan and had formerly been Li Hanzhang’s colleague; he was now Li’s subordinate. His succession to the governorship-general of Huguang would therefore be perfectly natural. “Then what about the governorship of Hunan?” Hu asked, smiling and adopting a literary turn of phrase. “Does Your Honor entertain an interest?” “I fear someone else may get there first.”

『那也说不定。』胡雪岩想了一下说∶『你先要把主意拿定了，才好想办法，倘或老大哥根本没有这个意思，也就不必去瞎费心思。』『水往低处流，人往高处爬，岂能无意。不过鞭长莫及，徒唤奈何。』『谋事在人，成事在天。』胡雪岩说∶『等我来打个电报给汪惟贤，要他去寻森二爷探探「盘口」。』此事不便假手于人，胡雪岩又拿不起笔，因而由他口述，让德馨执笔，电报中关照汪惟贤立即去觅宝森，托他向宝均金探探口气，藩司想升巡抚，该送多重的礼。

“That is by no means certain.” Hu thought for a moment. “First you must make up your mind. Then we can see what can be done. If you have no intention of seeking it, Brother, there is no point in wasting effort.” “Water flows downward, and men climb upward. How could I not want it? But the lash will not reach that far. I can only lament my helplessness.” “It is for men to make the attempt and Heaven to decide the outcome,” Hu said. “Let me send Wang Weixian a telegram asking him to find Second Master Sen and sound out the situation.” The matter could not safely be entrusted to an outsider, and Hu was hardly able to wield a writing brush himself. He therefore dictated while Dexin wrote. In the telegram, Wang was instructed to seek out Baosen immediately and ask him to sound out Bao Yun: how substantial a gift would be required for a provincial treasurer who wished to become governor?

德馨字斟句酌，用隐语写完，看了一遍说∶『宝中堂他们兄弟不和，森二爷或许说不上话。是不是请汪掌柜再探探皮硝李的口气。』『好！我赞成。』于是德馨改好了电报稿子；胡雪岩叫进贴身小跟班阿喜来，他专替主人保管一个一离家就要带着的西洋皮包，内中有个蜜码电报本，胡雪岩与德馨亲自动手，将蜜码译好，夕阳已经衔山了。『我本来不打算进城，现在非回支一趟不可了。』胡雪岩说∶『电报要送到上海去发，我派一个妥当的人去，叫他在上海等回电；如果是两三万银子，我先替你垫。多了就犯不上了。』『是，是。一切拜托，承情不尽。』

Dexin chose each word carefully and couched the message in guarded language. After rereading it, he said, “Grand Secretary Bao and his brother are not on good terms. Second Master Sen may have no influence with him. Should we ask Manager Wang to sound out Saltpeter Li as well?” “Yes. I agree.” Dexin revised the draft. Hu called in A’xi, his young personal attendant, whose particular duty was to look after the Western leather bag that accompanied his master whenever he left home. Inside it was a telegraphic codebook. Hu and Dexin encoded the message themselves. By the time they had finished, the setting sun was touching the hills. “I had not intended to go back to the city,” Hu said, “but now I must. The telegram has to be taken to Shanghai to be sent. I shall dispatch someone reliable and have him wait there for the reply. If it is a matter of twenty or thirty thousand taels, I shall advance it for you. More than that would hardly be worthwhile.” “Indeed, indeed. I leave everything in your hands. I cannot thank you enough.”

于是胡雪岩与德馨一起进城，两人品秩相同，但胡雪岩曾赏穿黄马褂，所以仪从较现任藩司的德馨更为煊赫；只是他的『高脚牌』只作陈列之用，出行只是前面一匹顶马、后面四匹跟马、八抬大轿的轿班，一共三班，轮流换肩一胡雪岩的班，在家亦是『老爷』；一回家就会听见丫头在喊∶『老爷回来了，赶快打水洗脚。』不过替胡雪岩抬力求虽是好差使，却很难当，因为既要快、又要稳，快到能跟着顶马亦步亦趋；稳到轿中靠手板上的茶水不致泼出来。因此，两人虽是同时动身，胡雪岩的轿于起步就领先，很快地将德馨在身后抛得老远了。

Hu Xueyan and Dexin then set off for the city together. Their official ranks were the same, but Hu had been granted the privilege of wearing the yellow riding jacket, so his ceremonial retinue was more imposing than that of Dexin, the serving provincial treasurer. Hu’s tall official placards were used only for display, however. When he traveled, there was simply one mounted outrider in front and four mounted attendants behind. His eight-man sedan had three teams of bearers, who took turns at the poles. Hu’s bearers were themselves addressed as “master” at home. When one returned, a maid would call, “The master is back! Hurry and bring water to wash his feet.” Yet however desirable the position, carrying Hu’s sedan was no easy task. He demanded both speed and steadiness: the men must keep pace with the leading horse, yet never spill the tea resting on the armboard inside. Thus, although Hu and Dexin started at the same time, Hu’s sedan took the lead from the first step and soon left Dexin far behind.

回到元宝街，老远就看到张灯结彩，灯烛辉煌；但寿堂中却颇安静，因为既已排定贺寿的日期，除了极少数的至亲以外，不会有人贸然登堂。胡雪岩下了轿，在寿堂中略作寒暄，随即着手处理德馨谋官之事。正唤来得力的家人在交代时，只风见螺蛳太太扶着一个小丫头的肩，悄然而至；看到胡雪岩有来，她远远地在一张丝绒椅上坐了下来。『你明天一大早就动身，在上海等消息，等北京的回电一到，马上赶回来，愈快愈好。』等家人答应着走了，螺蛳太太一面起身走近来，一面问道∶『你不在灵隐陪老太太、怎么回城来了？』『出了两个总督的缺，连带就会出两个巡抚的缺，德晓峰想弄一个，我只好进城来替他料理。』说到这里，胡雪岩发觉螺蛳太太神色有异，定睛看了一下问道∶『怎的，你哭过了？』『不要乱说！老太太的好日子，我哭什么？』螺蛳太太紧接着问∶『客人来得多不多？』

Approaching Yuanbao Street, Hu could see the lanterns, decorations, and brilliant lights from a distance. The birthday hall itself was fairly quiet. Since particular days had been assigned to the guests, only a few of the closest relatives would venture to call unannounced. Hu alighted, exchanged a few words in the hall, and immediately turned to the business of securing an office for Dexin. He had just summoned a capable household servant and begun giving his instructions when Madam Luosi quietly appeared, leaning on a little maid’s shoulder. Seeing that he was occupied, she seated herself some distance away in a velvet chair. “Leave first thing tomorrow morning and wait in Shanghai for news. As soon as the reply from Beijing arrives, come straight back. The sooner the better.” The servant assented and withdrew. Madam Luosi rose and approached. “Why have you come back to the city instead of staying at Lingyin with the old lady?” “Two governors-general’s posts have fallen vacant, which should mean two vacant governorships as well. De Xiaofeng wants one of them. I had to come in to make arrangements for him.” As he spoke, Hu noticed something unusual in her expression. Looking at her closely, he asked, “What is this? Have you been crying?” “Do not talk nonsense! Why should I cry on the old lady’s happy day?” She immediately changed the subject. “Were there many guests?”

『该来的都来了。』胡雪岩说∶『三品以上的官，本来没有多少，从明天起就要一天比一天忙了。我最耽心后后天，大家都说要去看热闹，不晓得会不会有啥笑话闹出来？』原来贺寿的日期，已经重新安排，第三天轮到外宾。『洋人拜寿』这四个字听起来，就会逗人好奇，都说不知道洋人拜寿是怎么个样子，是磕头还是作揖？吃寿面会不会用筷子；不会用用啥？叉子叉不住，只怕要用手抓。诸如此类等着看笑话的议论，不免使胡雪不安，怕闹出笑话来失面子。『喔，』螺蛳太太倒被提醒了，『有份礼在这里，你倒看看。』说着，例向窗外喊一声∶『来人！』进来的是螺蛳太太的亲信大丫头瑞香∶她已经听到了螺蛳太太的话，所以进门便说∶『洋人送的那份礼，送到老爷书房里去了。』胡雪岩心想，这个把月来，所收的寿礼，不知凡几？独独这份礼送到他书房，可知必有来历，便即问说∶『是哪个送的？』

“Everyone who ought to have come was there,” Hu said. “There are not many officials of the third rank or above to begin with. From tomorrow onward, each day will be busier than the last. What worries me most is the day after tomorrow. Everyone says they are going to watch the fun. I wonder whether something ridiculous will happen.” The schedule had been rearranged, and the third day was now reserved for the foreign guests. The very phrase “foreigners offering birthday greetings” excited curiosity. People wondered what the foreigners would actually do: kowtow or bow with clasped hands? Would they use chopsticks to eat the birthday noodles? If they could not, what would they use? A fork would never hold them; perhaps they would have to scoop them up with their fingers. All this talk of waiting to enjoy a joke made Hu uneasy. He feared an embarrassing scene that would cost him face. “Oh,” Madam Luosi said, reminded of something, “there is a present here you should look at.” She called through the window for a servant. Ruixiang, her trusted senior maid, entered. Having already heard her mistress, she said at once, “The gifts from the foreign gentleman have been taken to the master’s study.” Hu reflected that he had received countless birthday presents over the past month or so. If these alone had been sent to his study, there must be something special about them. “Who sent them?” he asked.

『我也清楚。』螺蛳太太说∶『是拱宸桥海关送来的，我想你来看，有份全帖在那里，你一看就晓得了。』『好！我到书房里去看。』『对！外面要开席了，我也要去照个面，敷衍敷衍。你呢？在哪里吃？』『太累，吃不下什么，吃点粥吧。』『老太太的寿面不能不吃。』螺蛳太太转脸吩咐∶『瑞香，你关照小厨房下碗鸡汤银丝面，鸡汤太浓，要把浮油撇干净。』于是主仆三人各散，胡雪岩一个人穿过平时就沿路置灯、明亮好走的长长的甬道，来到他的书房镜槛阁。

“I am not sure either,” Madam Luosi replied. “They came through the customs office at Gongchen Bridge. I wanted you to have a look. There is a formal visiting card with them; that will tell you.” “Very well. I shall go to the study.” “Yes, do. They are about to begin dinner outside, and I must put in an appearance and see to the guests. What about you? Where will you eat?” “I am too tired to eat much. Some congee will do.” “You must at least have some of the old lady’s birthday noodles.” Madam Luosi turned to give an order. “Ruixiang, tell the small kitchen to make a bowl of fine noodles in chicken broth. If the broth is too rich, have them skim off all the fat.” The three went their separate ways. Alone, Hu followed the long passage, which even on ordinary nights was lined with lamps and easy to walk along, to his study in Jingjian Pavilion.

这镜槛阁是园中一胜，前临平池，后倚假山，拾级而上时，那扶手是以铁杆为芯，外套在景德镇定烧的，朱翠相间，形如竹节的瓷筒；阁中有一面极大的镜砖，将阁外平池、池中鸳鸯、池上红桥、池畔重杨，一齐吸入镜中，这是仿北京玄武门外，什刹海畔恭亲王的别墅鉴园的规模所造，而精巧过之。胡雪岩进得阁来，在镜砖面前站了一回，看远处楼阁、近处回廊，都挂着寿庆的灯彩，到影入池，复又重生于有镜、镜中有镜、影中有影，疑真疑幻，全不分明了。正看得出神时，听得有个娇嫩的声音∶『老爷，房门开了。』

Jingjian Pavilion was one of the garden’s finest sights. A still pond lay before it and an artificial hill behind. The stair rail had an iron core sheathed in porcelain cylinders shaped like bamboo joints, made to order at Jingdezhen in alternating crimson and green. Inside stood an immense mirrored panel that drew into its surface the pond outside, the mandarin ducks on the water, the red bridge across it, and the willows along the banks. The design followed Jian Garden, Prince Gong’s retreat beside Shichahai outside Xuanwu Gate in Beijing, but surpassed it in delicacy. On entering, Hu stood before the mirror for a while. Distant towers and nearby galleries were hung with birthday lanterns and decorations. Their reflections fell into the pond and then appeared anew in the mirror: mirrors within mirrors, images within images, until reality and illusion could no longer be distinguished. As he gazed, absorbed, he heard a young voice. “Master, the room is open.”

胡雪岩抬头看时，这个小丫头仿佛见过，便问∶『你叫什么名字？』『我叫小梅。』『喔，你是新派过来的吗？』『不！我老早就在这里了。』『老早在这里？为啥不常看到你？』胡雪岩一面说，一面踏进书房，触目一大堆礼物；便顾不得跟小梅说话，先找全帖来看。

Hu looked up. He seemed to have seen the little maid before. “What is your name?” “Xiaomei.” “Oh. Have you just been assigned here?” “No. I have been here a long time.” “A long time? Why do I hardly ever see you?” As he spoke, he stepped into the study and caught sight of a great heap of presents. Forgetting Xiaomei for the moment, he looked first for the visiting card.

全帖的具名是『教遇弟赫鹭宾』。原来是总税务司英国人赫德。此人在华二十多年，说得一口极好的京腔，也识汉文；仰慕中华文化，兼且是朝迁的有顶戴的客卿，所以用他的英文名字的发音，自己起了一个中国名字叫做『赫鹭宾』。全帖以外还有礼单。寿礼一共四样，全喜精瓷茶具、一个装糖果的大银碗、整匹的呢料，另外一个老年人用的紫貂袖筒。

The formal card was signed, “He Lubin, your humble friend, honored by your instruction.” The sender was the Englishman Robert Hart, Inspector-General of Customs. He had lived in China for more than twenty years, spoke excellent Beijing Chinese, and could read Chinese writing. An admirer of Chinese culture and a foreign servant of the throne entitled to wear an official hat button, he had given himself the Chinese name He Lubin, fashioned from the sound of his English name. A gift list accompanied the card. There were four birthday presents: a complete set of fine porcelain tea things, a large silver bowl filled with sweets, a whole bolt of woolen cloth, and a purple sable muff suitable for an elderly lady.

『来啊！』他心目中使唤的是专管镜槛阁的两个大丫头一巧珠、巧珍两姊妹；但来的却是小梅。『两巧一巧都不巧。』小梅答说，∶都跟老太太到灵隐去了。『胡雪岩看她语言伶俐，料想也能办事，便即说道∶』你也一样。你去寻两个人来，把这四样东西搬到外面，叫人马上送到灵隐给老太太看，说是┅┅『这要说赫鹭宾就是赫德，这位』洋大人『戴的也是红顶子，那就太罗嗦了，怕传话的人说不清楚，所以停了下来。』老爷要啥！『

“Someone come here!” Hu had in mind the two senior maids who looked after Jingjian Pavilion, the sisters Qiaozhu and Qiaozhen. But it was Xiaomei who came. “Neither of the two Qiaos happens to be handy,” she said with a play on their names. “They have both gone to Lingyin with the old lady.” Her quick speech made Hu think she was quite capable of doing an errand. “You will do just as well. Find two people to carry these four presents outside, and have them sent straight to Lingyin for the old lady to see. Say that they are from—” He stopped. To explain that He Lubin was Hart, and that this foreign dignitary also wore a red button on his official hat, would be too complicated; the messenger might not convey it clearly. “What would you like, Master?”

『我要写字。』小梅听说，立刻走到书桌前面，掀开砚盖，注了一小杓清水，细细研墨。胡雪岩便坐了下来，提笔蘸墨，很吃力地在全帖上批了六个字∶『即总税司赫德』。小梅因为墨渖未干，便拿起全粘，嘟起小嘴朝字上吹气，正吹得起劲时，瑞香来了。

“I want to write something.” Xiaomei immediately went to the desk, lifted the inkstone’s cover, poured in a small spoonful of clean water, and began carefully rubbing the inkstick. Hu sat down, dipped his brush, and with considerable effort wrote six characters on the visiting card: “That is, Hart, Inspector-General of Customs.” As the ink was still wet, Xiaomei picked up the card and pursed her lips to blow on it. She was blowing busily when Ruixiang arrived.

见此光景，她先是一楞，接着便呵斥小梅∶『出去！这地方也是你来得的？』原来胡家也学了一套豪门世家的规矩，下人亦分几等，象小梅这种『做粗生活』的小丫头，是走不到主子面前的，否则便是僭越。这瑞香平日自恃是螺蛳太太的心腹，目中无余丫，人缘不好，小梅不大服她；此时无辜受责，大感委屈，她人小嘴利，当即反唇相讥，『巧珠不在，老爷来了，莫非我就不伺候？这又不是我瞎巴结差使，何用你来吼我？』她说∶『大家都是低三下四的人，摆你千金小姐的威风，摆给哪个看？』『啊！』瑞香脸都气白了，『你在嚼什么嘴？』说着，奔上去就要打。『小梅毫不示弱，又快又急地说∶』今天老太太的好日子，你敢打人？『

At the sight, Ruixiang first stared, then snapped at Xiaomei. “Get out! What right have you to be in here?” The Hu household had adopted the conventions of great aristocratic families, with several ranks even among the servants. A young maid like Xiaomei, employed on rough household work, was not supposed to appear before the master. To do so was to overstep her station. Ruixiang ordinarily presumed on her position as Madam Luosi’s confidante and treated the other maids as beneath her. She was unpopular, and Xiaomei was not inclined to submit to her. Blamed without cause, the girl felt deeply wronged. Young as she was, she had a sharp tongue and retorted at once. “Qiaozhu is away, and the master came in. Was I not to attend to him? I did not go looking for a chance to curry favor. Why are you shouting at me? We are all lowly servants. Who do you think you are impressing, putting on the airs of a wealthy young lady?” “What!” Ruixiang’s face went white with anger. “How dare you answer back!” She rushed forward to strike her. Xiaomei stood her ground and spoke quickly. “This is the old lady’s happy day. Would you dare hit someone?”

瑞香被吓阻住了，一只手好不容易放了下来，咬牙切齿地骂道∶『不看老太太的好日子，看我不撕烂你的小Ｘ！你等在那里，看我不收拾你。』这下小梅害怕了，瑞香的威风，她自然识得，情急之下，向胡雪岩双膝跑倒，『老爷，你看。』她说∶『请老爷做主。』『好了，好了！』胡雪岩解劝着∶『原是我叫她磨墨的。不看僧面看佛面，不必告诉你太太。『主人出面说情，瑞香总算扳回面子，出了口气，当下喝道Ｌ∶∶你还跪在这里想讨赏是不是，赏你一顿「毛笋炒腊肉」！滚！看见小梅盈盈欲泪，瑞香便又警告∶』今天是老太太的好日子，你敢哭出来！『小梅果然不敢哭，噙着两泡眼泪，退了出去。胡雪岩好生不忍，却不便当着瑞香支抚慰小梅。不过，眼前恰有一条现成的调虎离山之计，便是安排那份寿礼，送到灵隐。等瑞香下阁子去唤人时，胡雪岩便走到廊上，轻声说道∶』小梅，你不要怕，不要难过，明天我跟太太说∶提拔你。『胡雪岩对下人说太太，多半是指螺蛳太太，』我不要。『小梅答说∶』在瑞香手下，哪有好日子过？『

Ruixiang was checked. Only with an effort did she lower her raised hand. Grinding her teeth, she cursed. “If it were not for the old lady’s celebration, I would tear you apart, you filthy little—! Just wait. I shall deal with you.” Now Xiaomei was frightened. She knew only too well how much power Ruixiang wielded. In desperation she dropped to her knees before Hu Xueyan. “Master, you see how it is. Please be fair to me.” “There, there,” Hu said soothingly. “It was I who told her to grind the ink. If you will not excuse her for her own sake, do it for mine. There is no need to tell your mistress.” With the master himself pleading for her, Ruixiang had recovered some face and vented her anger. “Still kneeling there in the hope of a reward?” she shouted. “I shall reward you with a good beating! Get out!” Seeing tears well in Xiaomei’s eyes, she added, “This is the old lady’s happy day. Do not dare start crying!” Xiaomei did not dare. She withdrew with tears brimming in both eyes. Hu felt deeply sorry for her but could hardly comfort her in Ruixiang’s presence. Fortunately, a ready means of drawing Ruixiang away lay before him: the birthday gifts had to be sent to Lingyin. When Ruixiang went downstairs to summon help, Hu stepped into the gallery and said softly, “Xiaomei, do not be afraid. Do not be upset. Tomorrow I shall speak to your mistress and have you promoted.” When Hu spoke of “your mistress” to the servants, he usually meant Madam Luosi. “I do not want that,” Xiaomei replied. “How could I ever have a peaceful day under Ruixiang?”

胡雪岩正待再问时，不想瑞香来得好快，原来她一下阁子，就看到胡家四大管家婆之一，专管稽察花园出入的杨二太，亲自打一盏宫灯，领着古应春来见主人。于是瑞香便跟她换了差使，各自回头，一个去找人来料理赫德的礼，一个便领着古应春入阁。『你怎么回来了？』胡雪岩问。古应春原是预定留在灵隐，预备第二天接待来拜寿的英国人；只为得到赫德忽然到了杭州的消息，特为赶了来探问究竟。

Hu was about to ask her more when Ruixiang unexpectedly returned. On going downstairs, she had met Second Madam Yang, one of the household’s four chief women stewards, whose special charge was to supervise everyone entering or leaving the garden. Carrying a palace lantern herself, she was escorting Gu Yingchun to see the master. Ruixiang exchanged tasks with her, and each turned back: one went to find people to deal with Hart’s presents, while the other brought Gu upstairs. “Why have you come back?” Hu asked. Gu had intended to stay at Lingyin in preparation for receiving the British visitors who would offer birthday greetings the next day. But news that Hart had unexpectedly arrived in Hangzhou had brought him back to find out more.

『我也是刚刚看了拜帖才晓得是赫德，喏，』胡雪岩指着那四样礼物说∶『正预备送到灵隐，请老太太去过目呢。』于是古应春赏玩了礼物，点点头说∶『照洋人来说，这份礼送得很重了。』这自然是人家看重的缘故，胡雪岩不免得意，想了一下说∶『他不晓得住在哪里？今天晚了，来不及了，明天一大早，我同你先去拜访。这也是我们做主人该尽的道理。』『他住在梅藤更那里。』梅藤更是个英国教士，也是医生，到杭州传教，在中城大方伯开了一家医院；大方伯这个地方有一座桥，在宋朝叫广济桥，因此这家医院题名就用了双关的『广济』二字。梅藤更开设广济医院时，胡雪岩捐过一大笔钱，所以他跟梅藤更亦算是老朋友，当即说道∶『既然是住在梅藤更那里，我派人去通知一声，请他转告赫德，说我们明天一早去看他，请他问一问赫德什么时候方便。』『不必叫人去。好在晚上去看医生，不算冒昧，我自己去一趟，比较稳当。』

“I only discovered it was Hart when I read his card just now,” Hu said, pointing to the four presents. “Look, I was about to send these to Lingyin for the old lady to inspect.” Gu examined the gifts appreciatively and nodded. “By foreign standards, this is a very substantial present.” It was plainly a sign of the sender’s high regard, and Hu could not help feeling pleased. After a moment’s thought, he said, “I wonder where he is staying. It is too late today. First thing tomorrow morning, you and I should go and call on him. That is only proper, since we are the hosts.” “He is staying with Dr Meitenggeng.” Meitenggeng was an English missionary and physician who had come to Hangzhou to preach and established a hospital at Dafangbo in the center of the city. There was a bridge there that had been called Guangji Bridge in the Song dynasty, and the hospital took the same name, Guangji, which also conveyed the idea of bringing help to all. Hu had made a large donation when the hospital was founded and could count the doctor as an old friend. “Since he is staying there,” he said, “I shall send someone to ask the doctor to tell Hart that we will call tomorrow morning, and to find out what time would suit him.” “There is no need to send anyone. Calling on a doctor in the evening cannot be considered improper. I shall go myself; it will be more satisfactory.”

『也好！辛苦，辛苦。』胡雪岩问道∶『你吃了饭没有？』『忙得肚子饿都忘记了。实在也不饿。』『我也不饿，我等你一来一起吃。』『好！』『瑞香，你送古才爷下去。』胡雪岩忽又问道∶『这礼是啥辰光送来的？』『未末申初。』瑞香答说∶『梅院长派人送来的。』

“Very well. Thank you for the trouble. Have you eaten?” “I have been so busy I forgot to be hungry. In fact, I am not hungry now.” “Neither am I. I shall wait for you, and we can eat together when you return.” “Good.” “Ruixiang, show Mr Gu down.” Then Hu suddenly asked, “When did these presents arrive?” “About three in the afternoon,” Ruixiang replied. “Dr Mei sent someone with them.”

『那个时候！』胡雪岩蹙着眉说∶『照道理要送席。』『席是没有送。』瑞香接口，『送了个一品锅、四样点心，还有一篓水蜜蟠桃。太太叫我包了一个八封的赏封，打发来人，请他告诉梅院长，我们老爷在灵隐，所以不晓得这位洋大人的身分，不过总归是我们老爷的好朋友。梅院长是象自己人一样的，请他费心代为款侍，明天我们老爷回来了，再当面同他道谢。』瑞香咭咭呱呱一口气说下来，事情交代得清清楚楚；胡雪岩觉得螺蛳太太处置得颇为得体，很满意地说∶『亏得我不叫她到灵隐去，不然，没有人料理得来。』『也亏得强将手下无弱兵。』瑞香听出来是在夸赞她，古应春嫣然一笑，随即把头别了开去。古应春也笑，笑得眼角露出两条鱼尾纹。等瑞香送了古应春回来，向胡雪岩说道∶『面想来不要了。我已经关照小厨房，弄几样精致爽口的菜；请老爷的示，在哪里开饭？』

“At that hour!” Hu frowned. “We ought, properly, to have sent over a banquet.” “We did not send a banquet,” Ruixiang put in. “But we sent a first-class hotpot, four kinds of pastries, and a basket of juicy flat peaches. Madam had me prepare a gratuity envelope for the messenger [the amount is corrupt in the source]. She told him to explain to Dr Mei that our master was at Lingyin and therefore did not know who this foreign dignitary was, but that he must in any case be a good friend of our master’s. Dr Mei was like one of the family, and we asked him to take the trouble to entertain the guest on our behalf. When our master returned tomorrow, he would thank him in person.” Ruixiang rattled it all off in one breath, setting out everything clearly. Hu thought Madam Luosi had handled the matter most suitably. “A good thing I did not have her come to Lingyin,” he said with satisfaction. “Otherwise there would have been no one capable of attending to it.” “A good thing, too, that a strong commander has no weak soldiers.” Ruixiang recognized Gu’s compliment, smiled prettily at him, then turned her head away. Gu smiled as well, two crow’s-feet showing at the corners of his eyes. When Ruixiang returned from seeing him out, she said to Hu, “I suppose you will not want the noodles now. I have told the small kitchen to prepare a few delicate, refreshing dishes. Where would you like dinner served?”

『就在这里好了。』胡雪岩又说∶『我倒不晓得你这么凶！女人厉害，可以；凶，不可以，自己吃亏。』『太太当家，总要有个人来替她做恶人。莫非倒是太太自己来做恶人，我们在旁边替人家说好话？』胡雪岩觉得她的话竟无可驳；想了一下说∶『就做恶人也犯不着撒蠢；什么小Ｘ不小ＸＸ，难听不难听？』瑞香涨红了脸，欲待分辩，却又实在没有理由，以致于僵在那里有些手足无措的模样。

“Here will do,” Hu replied. Then he added, “I had no idea you were so vicious! A woman may be formidable; that is all very well. But viciousness will only hurt her in the end.” “Madam runs the household. Someone has to play the villain for her. Would you have her be the villain herself while we stand by putting in kind words for everyone?” Hu could find no answer to that. After a moment’s thought he said, “Even if you must play the villain, there is no need to behave like a fool. Those filthy insults you used—do you not hear how ugly they sound?” Ruixiang flushed. She wanted to defend herself but could think of no excuse, and stood rooted to the spot, at a loss what to do.

胡雪岩便又掉了一句文∶『「人必自悔而后人侮之。」』他说∶『如果人家回你一句∶我「小」你「大」！你一个大青娘，脸上挂得住、挂不住？』杭州人叫妙龄女郎为『大青娘』，是最多悉善感的时候；瑞香又羞又悔，眼圈红红的，要哭出来了。『咦，咦，咦！』胡雪岩大为诧异，『你叫人家不准哭，自己倒要哭了，为啥？莫非我的话说得重了。』一听这话，瑞香顿时收泪，抽出腋下的一方白纺绸绣一枝瑞香花的手绢，擤一擤鼻子答说∶『哪个哭了。』『不哭最好，你把牙牌拿来，再到前面看看，坐席坐到啥光景了？』

Hu offered a literary quotation. “‘A person must first demean herself before others will demean her.’ Suppose she had turned your own obscenity back on you,” he went on. “How would a young woman like you have held up her head?” In Hangzhou, the expression he used referred to a girl in the bloom of youth, the age at which feelings were most tender and easily stirred. Ruixiang was ashamed and remorseful. Her eyes reddened, and she was on the verge of tears. “Why, why, why!” Hu exclaimed in astonishment. “You forbade her to cry, and now you are about to cry yourself. What is the matter? Have I spoken too harshly?” At once Ruixiang checked her tears. She drew from beneath her arm a white silk handkerchief embroidered with a spray of daphne, the flower whose Chinese name was her own, and blew her nose. “Who is crying?” “Best not to cry. Bring me the dominoes, then go out front and see how far the dinner has got.”

瑞香答应着，取出一盒牙牌，倒在红木方桌上，然后下了阁子。胡雪岩一个人拿牙牌『通五关』打发辰光连着几副不通，便换了起数问前程。于是照牙牌神数的歌诀∶『全副牙牌一字开，中间看有几多开，连排三次分明记，上下中平内取裁。』头一次得了十六开，第二次更多，竟有廿一开，第三次却只得一副对子，一副分相，共计六开。

Ruixiang fetched a box of ivory dominoes, tipped them onto the square rosewood table, and went downstairs. Left alone, Hu played “Passing the Five Gates” to while away the time. After several unsuccessful rounds, he turned from the game to divination, seeking a sign of his future. Following the rhyme for consulting the domino oracle—“Lay the full set out in line; count the openings that you find; three successive layouts mark, then judge the high, the low, the middle”—he counted sixteen openings in the first layout, twenty-one in the second, but in the third only one matched pair and one unmatched combination, six openings in all.

胡雪岩是弄熟了的，一算是『上上、上上、中下』。诗句也还约略记得，但『解』与『断』，却须找书来看。找到『兰闺清玩』的『牙牌神数』，翻开来一看，那着诗是『一帆风顺及时扬，稳度鲸川万里航，若到帆随湘转处，下坡骏马早收缰。』一面念，一面心想∶『有点意思。』再往下看，『解曰∶谋为勿忧煎，成全在眼前，施为无不利，到处要周旋。』看到最后一句，不由得蓦然一拍桌子，大声自语∶『今天这个数起得神了！』语声刚终，有人接口∶『你在作啥？』抬眼看时，前面螺蛳太太手扶小丫头的肩，正踏进门来，后面跟着瑞香。『客散了？』

Hu knew the method well. He worked out the result as “highest auspice, highest auspice, lower middle.” He could more or less remember the verse, but he needed the book for its “Explanation” and “Judgment.” Finding the section on the domino oracle in Elegant Pastimes of the Ladies’ Chamber, he opened it and read: “With a fair wind, hoist your sail in time; / Cross safely the whale’s vast waters, ten thousand miles. / Should you reach the turn where the sail follows the Xiang, / Rein in the gallant horse before it takes the downward slope.” As he recited it, he thought, “There is something in that.” He read on: “Explanation: Do not torment yourself over your undertakings. Their fulfillment lies before your eyes. Whatever you attempt will prosper, but everywhere you must handle others tactfully.” At the last phrase, he suddenly slapped the table and exclaimed, “Today’s reading is uncannily accurate!” He had scarcely finished when someone asked, “What are you doing?” Looking up, he saw Madam Luosi entering with a hand on a little maid’s shoulder; Ruixiang followed behind. “Have the guests gone?”

『还没有，不过每桌都有人陪。』螺蛳太太说∶『我是听说七姐夫来了又出去了，不知道是不是有啥要紧的事，所以我特别来看看—』『他到梅藤更那里去了，说一句话就回来的。』胡雪岩接着又往下看『解』了以后的『断』。

“Not yet, but every table has someone to attend to it,” Madam Luosi replied. “I heard that Brother-in-law Gu had come back and gone out again. I wondered whether something important had happened, so I came specially to see.” “He has gone to Dr Meitenggeng’s. He will be back as soon as he has had a word with him.” Hu then went on to the “Judgment” that followed the “Explanation.”

『断曰∶黄节晚香，清节可贵，逝水回波，急流勇退。』最后这四个字，胡雪岩是懂得；而且这也正是内则老母、外则良友在一再劝他的。此刻不自觉地便仔细想了下去。螺蛳太太也常看他起数，但都不似此刻这么认真，而且是上了心事的模样，当然深感关切。『瑞香，去调一杯玫瑰薄荷露来，我解解酒。』说着，在胡雪岩对面坐了下来问道∶『你起的数，倒讲给我听听。』『今天起的这个数，我愈想愈有道理。』胡雪岩说∶『先说我一帆风顺，不过到时候要收篷。啥时候呢？「帆随湘转处」，灵就灵在这个「湘」字上，是指左大人；到左大人不当两江总督了，我就要「下坡骏马早收缰」了。』『还有呢？』

“Judgment: The yellow bloom gives its fragrance late; unsullied integrity is precious. Flowing water swirls back upon itself: have the courage to withdraw from the swift current.” Hu understood that final injunction perfectly. It was precisely what his mother at home and his good friends outside had repeatedly urged him to do. Without quite realizing it, he began to ponder it closely. Madam Luosi had often watched him cast a divination, but never with such seriousness or with anything weighing on his mind as it seemed to do now. Naturally, she was concerned. “Ruixiang, mix me a glass of rose-and-mint cordial to clear my head after the wine.” Seating herself opposite Hu, she asked, “Tell me about the reading you have had.” “The more I think about today’s reading, the more sense it makes,” Hu replied. “First it says I am sailing before a fair wind, but that when the time comes I must furl my sail. When? ‘Where the sail turns with the Xiang.’ The uncanny thing is that word Xiang. It means Lord Zuo. When Lord Zuo ceases to be Governor-General of Liangjiang, I must ‘rein in the gallant horse before it takes the downward slope.’” “And what else?”

『还有这两句，也说得极准∶』施为无不利，到处要周旋。「拿银子铺路，自然无往不利路路通了。『』还有呢？『『那就是「急流勇退。」』螺蛳太太点点头，喝了一大口玫瑰薄荷露说∶『我看只有「急流勇退」四个字说得最好。又是「下坡」又是「骏马」，你想收缰都收不住。』胡雪岩正要回答，只听外面人在报∶『古老爷回来了。』『瑞香，』螺蛳太太一面站起来，一面说∶『带人来开饭。』

“These two lines are remarkably accurate too: ‘Whatever you attempt will prosper, but everywhere you must handle others tactfully.’ Pave the way with silver and naturally every road opens before you.” “And what else?” “‘Have the courage to withdraw from the swift current.’” Madam Luosi nodded and took a long drink of her rose-and-mint cordial. “I think that last advice is the best of all. A gallant horse, and a downward slope as well—by then, even if you wanted to pull on the reins, you might not be able to hold it back.” Hu was about to answer when someone announced from outside, “Mr Gu is back.” Madam Luosi rose. “Ruixiang, bring the servants in to lay dinner.”

『讲妥当了？』胡雪岩也站了起来，迎上去问。『讲好了。明天上午八点钟去看赫德。然后他料理公事完毕中午到灵隐去拜寿。『』吃饭呢？『螺蛳太太急忙问说。『这就要好好商量了。』『对，对，好好商量。』胡雪岩扬一扬手，『我们这面来谈。』古应春跟到书桌旁边坐定了说∶『我不但见了梅藤更，还见了赫德，他说他这一次一则来拜寿；二则还有事要跟小爷叔约谈。』『什么事？汇丰的款子，应付的本息还早啊！』『是茧子的事。』

“Is everything arranged?” Hu stood up too and went to meet him. “Yes. We shall call on Hart at eight tomorrow morning. Then, once he has finished his official business, he will come to Lingyin at noon to offer birthday greetings.” “And what about his meal?” Madam Luosi asked at once. “We must discuss that carefully.” “Yes, yes, let us do so.” Hu waved a hand. “Come over here.” Gu followed him to the desk and sat down. “I saw not only Dr Meitenggeng but Hart himself. He says he has come partly to offer birthday greetings and partly because there is something he wishes to discuss with you, Uncle.” “What is it? The principal and interest due to HSBC will not fall due for some time yet.” “It concerns the cocoons.”

『这个，』胡雪岩问∶『怡和的大板怎么不来呢？』『已经来了，也住在梅藤更那里。』『这样说，是有备而来的。我们倒要好好儿想个应付的办法。』『当然。』古应春又说∶『小爷叔，你哪天有空？』『要说空，哪一天都不空。』胡雪岩答说∶『他老远从北京到这里，当然主随客便，我们只有看他的意思。』『既然小爷叔这么说，明天中午等他到灵隐拜了生日，请他到府上来吃饭，顺便带他逛逛园子。』『我也是这么想。』胡雪岩问∶『吃西餐，还是中国菜。』『还是西餐吧。』古应春说∶『我这回带来的六个厨子，其中有一个是法皇的御厨，做出来的东西，不会坍台的。』『来，来！』螺蛳太太喊道∶『来坐吧！』

“In that case,” Hu asked, “why has Jardine Matheson’s chief manager not come?” “He has. He is staying with Dr Meitenggeng too.” “Then they have come prepared. We had better think carefully about how to deal with them.” “Certainly,” Gu said. “What day would suit you, Uncle?” “If you mean a day when I have nothing to do, none would. But he has come all the way from Beijing. The host must accommodate the guest; we can only see what suits him.” “Since you feel that way, why not invite him to dine at your house tomorrow after he has offered birthday greetings at Lingyin at noon? You could show him the garden as well.” “That is what I had in mind. Shall we serve Western food or Chinese?” “Western food, I think. Of the six cooks I have brought this time, one formerly cooked for the Emperor of the French. His food will not disgrace us.” “Come, come!” Madam Luosi called. “Sit down!”

『来了！』胡雪岩走过来说道∶『明天中午总税务司赫德要来吃饭，吃西餐；厨子应春带来，席摆在哪里方便，要预备点啥，顶好趁早交代下去。』『有多少人？』『主客一共四位。』古应春答说。『应春，』胡雪岩问∶『你是说，怡和的大班也请？』一听这语气，古应春便即反问∶『小爷叔的意思呢？』『我看「阳春面加重，免免」了！』『我看预备还是要预备在那里，』螺蛳太太插进来说∶『说不定赫德倒带了他来呢？』洋人没有挟带不速之客的习惯。螺蛳太太对这方面的应酬规矩不算内行；不过多预备总不错，或许临时想起还有什么人该请，即不致于捉襟见肘。因此，胡雪岩点点头说∶『对，多预备几份好了。』说着，相将落座，喝的是红葡萄酒；古应春看着斟在水晶杯中、紫光泛彩的酒说∶『这酒要冰了，味道才出得来。』『那就拿冰来冰。』原来胡家也跟大内一样，自己有冰窖。数九寒天，将热水倒在物制的方形木盒中，等表里晶莹，冻结实了，置于掘得极深、下铺草荐的地窖，到来年六月，方始开窖取用。此时胡雪岩交代，当然提前开窖。

“Coming!” Hu walked over. “Inspector-General Hart will be dining here tomorrow at noon. Western food. Yingchun will provide the cook. Where it would be convenient to set the table and what else will be needed had best be settled early.” “How many people?” “Four altogether, including hosts and guests,” Gu replied. “Yingchun, do you mean to invite Jardine Matheson’s chief manager too?” At the sound of Hu’s voice, Gu immediately asked in return, “What would you prefer, Uncle?” “I think we shall have ‘a double helping of plain noodles’—mian, mian: let us leave him out!” “I think we should prepare for him anyway,” Madam Luosi put in. “Hart might bring him along.” Foreigners were not in the habit of arriving with uninvited companions. Madam Luosi did not fully understand their rules of social intercourse. Still, it could do no harm to prepare extra. They might suddenly think of someone else who ought to be invited, and then they would not be caught short. Hu nodded. “Quite right. Have a few extra places ready.” They sat down together. Red wine was served. Looking at the purple glow of the wine in the crystal glasses, Gu said, “This wine needs chilling to bring out its flavor.” “Then bring some ice and chill it.” Like the imperial palace, the Hu household had its own ice cellar. In the depths of winter, hot water was poured into specially made square wooden molds. Once it had frozen solid, clear through and through, the blocks were laid in a very deep cellar with straw matting underneath. Ordinarily, it would not be opened until the sixth month of the following year. Hu’s order meant opening it ahead of time.

这一来不免大费手脚，耽误工夫，古应春颇为不安，但已知胡雪岩的脾气愈来愈任性，劝阴无用，只好听其自然。趁这工夫，胡雪岩与古应春将次日与赫德会谈可能涉及的各方面，细细研究了一番。其时螺蛳太太已回到前面，等席散送客；镜槛阁中，凿冰冻酒，检点肴馔，都是瑞香主持，只见她来往俏影，翩翩如蝶，不时吸引着古应春的视线移转。

This required considerable trouble and caused a delay, which made Gu uneasy. But he knew Hu was growing more willful by the day. Remonstrance would be useless, so he could only let matters take their course. They used the interval to examine in detail all the questions that might arise in the next day’s discussion with Hart. Madam Luosi had meanwhile gone back to the front of the house to wait for the banquet to end and see the guests off. At Jingjian Pavilion, Ruixiang supervised the breaking of the ice, the chilling of the wine, and the checking of the dishes. Her graceful figure flitted to and fro like a butterfly, repeatedly drawing Gu’s eyes after her.

胡雪岩看在眼里，愈发觉得刚才胸中所动的一念，应该从速实现。等入了座，他先看一看桌上的菜，问道∶『还有啥？』『还有锦乡长寿面、八仙上寿汤。』瑞香答说∶『古老爷跟老爷还想吃点啥？我去交代。』『够了，够了。』古应春说∶『两个人吃八样菜，已经多了；再多，反而看饱了吃不下。』『什么叫八仙上寿汤？』『就是八珍汤。』瑞香笑道∶『今天是老太太的好日子，所以我拿它改个名字。』『好，晓得了。』胡雪岩答说∶『我想吃点甜的，你到小厨房去看看，等弄好了带回来。』这是胡雪岩故意遣开瑞香，因为他要跟古应春说的话，是一时不便让瑞香知道睥。

Hu noticed. All the more reason, he thought, to put into effect without delay the idea that had occurred to him earlier. Once seated, he surveyed the dishes on the table. “What else is there?” “Splendid Longevity Noodles and Eight Immortals Offering Birthday Blessings Soup,” Ruixiang replied. “Would you or Mr Gu like anything else? I shall tell the kitchen.” “Enough, enough,” Gu said. “Eight dishes for two people are more than sufficient. Any more and we should be full just from looking.” “What is Eight Immortals Offering Birthday Blessings Soup?” Hu asked. “Eight Delicacies Soup,” Ruixiang answered with a smile. “I gave it a different name because this is the old lady’s happy day.” “Good. I see. I should like something sweet. Go to the small kitchen and see what they have. Bring it back when it is ready.” Hu was deliberately sending Ruixiang away. What he wanted to discuss with Gu could not yet be allowed to reach her ears.

『老太太说，这回生日样样都好，美中不足的，就是七姐没有来。』『要美中不足才好。』古应春答说∶『曾文正分别号叫「求阙斋」，特为去求美中不足，那才是持盈保仄之道。醇亲王从儿子做了皇帝以后，置了一样古董，叫做「欹器」，盛水不能满，一满就翻倒了。』胡雪岩并未听出他话中的深意，管自己问道∶『七姐现在身子怎么样？』『无非带病延年。西医说∶中风调养比吃药重要；调养第一要心兀，她就是心静不下来，我怎么劝也没用。』『为啥呢？』胡雪岩问∶『为啥心静不下来？』『小爷叔，你晓得她的，凡事好强。自从她病倒以后，家里当然不比从前那样子有条理了，她看不惯，自己要指挥，话又说不清楚，丫头老妈子弄来总不如她的意。你想，一个病人一天到晚操心，还要生气，糟糕不糟糕？』说到这里，古应春叹口气，将酒杯放了下来。

“The old lady says everything about her birthday is wonderful this year. The one thing lacking is that Sister Seven has not come.” “It is better to have something lacking,” Gu replied. “Zeng Guofan called his study ‘Seeking Imperfection.’ He deliberately sought a flaw in perfection; that is the way to preserve prosperity and keep oneself safe. After his son became emperor, Prince Chun acquired an antique vessel called a tilting bowl. You cannot fill it right up: when it is full, it overturns.” Hu missed the deeper meaning of his words and pursued his own question. “How is Sister Seven’s health now?” “She can only nurse her illness and hope to live on. The Western doctor says that after a stroke, proper care matters more than medicine. The first requirement is peace of mind, but she simply cannot be at peace. Nothing I say will persuade her.” “Why?” Hu asked. “Why can she not settle her mind?” “You know her, Uncle. She insists on doing everything well. Since she fell ill, the house has naturally not been as orderly as before. She cannot bear to see it and wants to direct things herself, but she cannot speak clearly, and whatever the maids and older servants do never satisfies her. Think of it: a sick woman worrying all day and getting angry into the bargain. Is that not dreadful?” Gu sighed and set down his glass.

提起不愉快的事，害得他败了酒兴，胡雪岩不免歉然，但正因为如此，更要往深处去谈。『还有呢？』『还有，就是她总不放心我；常说她对不起，因为她病在床上，没法子照料我的饮食起居。我说，你千万不要这样想，这是没法子的事；再说，有丫头老妈子，我自己会指挥。她说∶没有体己的人，到底不一样。又说∶』中年丧妻大不幸，弄个半死不活的老婆在那里，你反而要为我操心，是加倍的大不幸。「常时谈得她也哭，我也哭。『说着，古应春又泫然欲涕了。『应春，你说得我也想哭了。你们真正是所谓伉俪情深，来世也一定是恩爱夫妻。不过，既然七姐是这样子的情形，我的想法倒又改过了。』『小爷叔，你有啥想法？』

Hu felt sorry to have raised an unhappy subject and spoiled his friend’s pleasure in the wine. Yet this was precisely why he had to pursue it. “And what else?” “She is always worrying about me. She often says she has failed me because she is confined to bed and cannot look after my meals and daily needs. I tell her she must not think like that: it cannot be helped, and besides, there are maids and older servants whom I can direct myself. She says that without someone close to me it is simply not the same. Then she says, ‘To lose your wife in middle age is a great misfortune. To have a wife who is neither quite alive nor dead, and to have to worry about her as well, is twice the misfortune.’ Often we talk until she is crying and I am crying too.” As he spoke, Gu was again close to tears. “Yingchun, you make me want to cry myself,” Hu said. “Yours truly is a marriage of deep affection. I am sure you will be a loving couple in the next life as well. Still, if that is how things stand with Sister Seven, it changes my thinking.” “What have you been thinking, Uncle?”

『我在想，要替你弄个人。这个人当然要你中意，要七姐也中意。人，我已经有了，虽说有把握，你们都会中意，不过，女人家的事情，有时候是很难说的，尤其是讨小纳妾，更加要慎重，所以我想过些日子，叫罗四姐到上海去一趟，当面跟七姐商量，照现在看，我想这件事，可以定局了。』一番话说得古应春心乱如麻，不知是喜是惧？定定神，理出一个头绪，先要知道，胡雪岩心目中，『已经有了』的那个人是谁？等他一问出来，胡雪岩答道∶『还有哪个，自然是瑞香。』古应春又惊又喜，眼前浮起瑞香的影子，耳边响起瑞香的声音，顿时生出无限的遐想。『应春，』胡雪岩问说∶『你看怎么样，七姐会不会中意她？』『我想，应该会。』

“I have been thinking of finding someone for you. Naturally, she must be someone you like and Sister Seven likes too. I already have someone in mind. Though I am confident you would both be pleased with her, matters involving women are sometimes difficult to judge, and taking a concubine calls for even greater care. I had therefore thought of sending Sister Luo to Shanghai in a few days to discuss it with Sister Seven in person. From what you have told me, however, I think we may be able to settle it.” Gu’s thoughts were in turmoil. He hardly knew whether he was pleased or afraid. Composing himself, he picked out the first question to be answered: who was the woman Hu already had in mind? When he asked, Hu replied, “Who else? Ruixiang, of course.” Gu was startled and delighted. Her figure rose before his eyes and her voice sounded in his ears, setting his imagination wandering. “Yingchun,” Hu asked, “what do you think? Would Sister Seven be pleased with her?” “I think she ought to be.”

『你呢？』古应春笑笑不答，只顾自己从冰桶中取酒瓶来斟酒。『我说得不错吧！这个人你们夫妻俩都会中意。』『话也不能这么说。』古应春将七姑奶奶得病以来说过的话，细细搜索了一遍，有些悲伤地说∶『小爷叔，有件事，我不能不提出来。阿七从来没有提过要替我弄一个人的话。』这使得胡雪岩一楞，心中寻思，七姑奶奶既然因为无法亲自照料丈夫的饮食起居而深感抱歉；同时也觉得没有一个得力的帮手替她治家，那末以她一向看得广、想得深的性情，一定会转过替古应春提过，这中间就大可玩味了。

“And you?” Gu smiled without answering and busied himself taking the wine bottle from the ice bucket to refill his glass. “Was I not right? Both husband and wife would be pleased with her.” “One cannot quite put it that way.” Gu searched his memory for everything the Seventh Lady had said since falling ill. With some sadness he continued, “Uncle, there is something I cannot leave unmentioned. A’qi has never once suggested finding someone for me.” That gave Hu pause. The Seventh Lady felt deeply sorry that she could no longer personally care for her husband’s meals and daily needs; at the same time, she plainly lacked a capable assistant to manage the household. Given her habitual breadth of view and depth of thought, she must surely have considered finding someone for Gu. Yet she had never raised the subject. There was much to ponder in that.

『应春，』他问∶『你自己有没有讨小的打算？』古应春仔仔细细地回忆着，而且在重新体认自己曾经有过的感想以后，很慎重地答说∶『如果说没有，我是说假话。不过，这种念头只要一起，我马我会丢掉，自己告诉自己∶不要自讨苦吃。』『这种心境，你同七姐谈过没有？』『没有。』『从来没有谈过？』『从没有。』『有没有露过这样的口风呢？』见他这样『打破沙锅问到底』，古应春倒不敢信口回答了，复又想了一下，方始开口∶『没有。』

“Yingchun,” he asked, “have you yourself thought of taking a concubine?” Gu searched his memory carefully and reconsidered feelings he had once had. At last he answered with great seriousness. “I should be lying if I said no. But whenever the thought arises, I immediately dismiss it. I tell myself not to go looking for trouble.” “Have you ever discussed these feelings with Sister Seven?” “No.” “Never?” “Never.” “Have you ever even hinted at them?” Hu’s insistence on getting to the bottom of the matter made Gu unwilling to answer casually. He thought again before saying, “No.”

『好！我懂了。』胡雪岩说∶『讨小讨得不好，是自讨若吃；讨得好，另当别论。我料七姐的心事，不是不想替你弄个人，是这个人不容易去觅。又要能干，又要体贴，又要肯听她的话；还要相貌看得过去，所以心里虽有这样的念头，没有觅着中意的人之前，先不开口。七姐做事向来是这样的，我晓得。』古应春觉得他的话也不无道理，倒不防探探妻子的口气。旋即转念，此事决不能轻发！倘若妻子根本不愿，一说这话，岂非伤了感情？『能干、体贴、听话、相貌过得去，这四个条件，顶要紧的是听话。七姐人情、世故熟透，世界上总是听话的老实无用；能干调皮捣蛋，她一个端正人，躺在床上，如果叫人到东，偏要到西，拿她有啥法子？那一来，不是把她活活气死？七姐顾虑来，顾虑去，就是顾虑这个。应春，你说对不对！』『是的。』古应春不能不承认∶『小爷叔把职七的为人，看得很透。』『闲话少说，我们来谈瑞香。四个条件，她占了三个；体贴或许差一点，不过那也是将来你们感情上的事，感情深了，自然会体贴。』『哪里就谈得到将来了？』古应春笑着喝了口酒说∶『这件事要慢慢商量。』

“Good. I understand,” Hu said. “A badly chosen concubine does indeed mean looking for trouble. A well-chosen one is another matter. I suspect Sister Seven is not opposed to finding you someone; the difficulty is finding the right person. She must be capable and considerate, willing to obey her, and reasonably good-looking as well. So although the idea is in her mind, she will say nothing until she has found someone suitable. That is how Sister Seven has always done things. I know her.” Gu thought there was some reason in his words. Perhaps he might sound his wife out. But immediately he checked himself. This was not a subject to raise lightly. If she was fundamentally unwilling, would mentioning it not wound her feelings? Hu went on, “Capable, considerate, obedient, and reasonably good-looking: of those four conditions, obedience matters most. Sister Seven knows human nature and the ways of the world thoroughly. Obedient people tend to be honest but useless; capable ones tend to be unruly and mischievous. She is an upright woman, lying there in bed. If she tells someone to go east and that person insists on going west, what can she do? It would anger her to death. That is the worry she turns over and over in her mind. Is that not so, Yingchun?” “Yes,” Gu had to admit. “You understand A’qi very well, Uncle.” “Enough talk. Let us consider Ruixiang. She meets three of the four conditions. Perhaps she falls a little short in consideration, but that will depend on your feelings for each other later. Once the affection is deep, consideration will come naturally.” “How can we already be talking about later?” Gu laughed and took a sip of wine. “This matter needs careful discussion.”

『你说谈不到将来，我说喜事就在眼前。』胡雪岩略略放低了声音∶『贤慧，瑞香当然还谈不到；不过，我同罗四姐两个人一起替你写包票，一定听七姐的话。你信不信。』古应春何能不信，亦何能不喜，但总顾虑着妻子如果真的有妨意，这件事就弄巧成拙了。看他脸上忽喜忽忧的神情，胡雪岩当然也能约略猜到他的心事。但夫妻之间的这种情形，到底只有同床共枕的人才能判断。所以他不再固劝，让它冷一冷，看古应春多想一想以后的态度，再作道理。

“You say we cannot talk about later; I say the happy occasion is just before us.” Hu lowered his voice a little. “I cannot yet call Ruixiang a model of womanly virtue. But Sister Luo and I will jointly guarantee that she obeys Sister Seven. Do you believe us?” How could Gu fail to believe him, or fail to be delighted? Yet he still feared that if his wife truly resented the idea, their clever plan might produce the opposite of what they intended. Hu could readily guess his thoughts from the way delight and anxiety alternated on his face. Still, only those who shared a bed could really judge such matters between husband and wife. He pressed no further, allowing the subject to cool. He would see what attitude Gu took after more thought and decide what to do then.

于是把话题扯了开去，海阔天空地聊了一阵，瑞香亲自提来一个细篾金漆圆笼，打开来看，青花瓷盘中，盛着现做的枣泥核花奶酥；是醇亲王厨子传授的。接着，小厨房另外送来寿面跟『八仙上寿汤』；寿面一大盘，炒得十分出色，但胡雪岩与古应春都是应应景，浅尝即止。『多吃点嘛！』瑞香劝道∶『这么好的寿面，不吃真可惜。』『说得不错。』古应春答说∶『我再来一点。』

He changed the subject, and they chatted freely for a while. Ruixiang herself brought in a round basket of finely woven bamboo coated with gold lacquer. Inside, on a blue-and-white porcelain plate, lay freshly made cream pastries filled with date paste and walnuts, made from a recipe passed on by Prince Chun’s cook. The small kitchen next sent in the birthday noodles and the Eight Immortals Offering Birthday Blessings Soup. There was a great platter of beautifully fried noodles, but both Hu and Gu merely tasted a little for the occasion. “Do have some more,” Ruixiang urged. “Such fine birthday noodles—it would be a pity not to eat them.” “You are right,” Gu replied. “I shall have a little more.”

于是好替他们各自盛了一小碗，古应春努力加餐，算是吃完了。胡雪尝了一口说道∶『我再来一点。』『糟蹋了实在可惜。』瑞香向外喊道，『小梅，你们把这盘寿面拿去，分了吃掉∶沾沾老太太的福气。』说着，亲自将一盘炒面捧了出去。胡雪岩看在眼里，暗自点头。等饭罢茶时，螺蛳太太亦已客散稍闲，来到镜槛阁休息；当然还有许多杂条要料理，走马换将，都交给瑞香了。

She filled a small bowl for each of them. Gu made an effort and finished his. Hu tasted a mouthful and said, “I shall have a little more.” “It would be a shame to let them go to waste.” Ruixiang called outside. “Xiaomei, take this platter of birthday noodles and share it among you. Have a little of the old lady’s good fortune!” She carried the platter out herself. Hu watched and nodded inwardly. When they had finished eating and were drinking tea, Madam Luosi came to Jingjian Pavilion to rest, her guests now departed. There were, of course, still many miscellaneous matters to attend to. The two women exchanged duties, leaving everything to Ruixiang.

『我刚刚跟应春谈了一件大事，现在要同你商量了。』商量的便是嫁瑞香之事；不等胡雪岩话毕，螺蛳太太便即说道∶『我早就有这个意思了。七姐夫，史要七姐一句话，我马上来办。』『就是这句话为难。』古应春答说∶『我自己当然不便提；就是旁人去提，也不大妥当。』『何以见得？』『人家去说，她表面上说不出不愿意的话来，心里有了疙瘩，对她的病，大不相宜。』

“I have just been discussing an important matter with Yingchun. Now I must consult you.” It was the proposed marriage of Ruixiang. Before Hu had finished, Madam Luosi said, “I have long had the same idea. Brother-in-law Gu, let Sister Seven say the word, and I shall arrange it at once.” “That one word is precisely the difficulty,” Gu replied. “I can hardly raise the subject myself, and it would not be quite proper even for someone else to do so.” “Why not?” “If someone speaks to her, she will be unable to say openly that she objects. But she may harbor a grievance in her heart, which would be very bad for her health.”

『我看七姐不会的。』胡雪岩对螺蛳太太说∶『下个月我到上海，你同我一起去，当面跟七姐谈这件事。』『那一来，她怎么样不愿意，也得很高兴。』古应春大为摇头∶『不妥，不妥！她决不肯说真心话的。』『我倒有个办法，我要由七姐自己开口。』此言一出，古应春、胡雪岩一齐倾身注目，倒要听听她是何好办法，能使得七姑奶奶自愿为丈夫纳妾。『办法很容易。』螺蛳太太说∶『我把瑞香带了去。只说我不放心她的病，特为叫瑞香去服侍她，帮她理家的。只要瑞香服侍得好，事事听她的话，她自然会想到，要留住瑞香只有一条路，让她也姓古。』『此计大妙！』岩胡雪拍着手说∶『准定这么办。』

“I do not think Sister Seven would,” Hu said. He turned to Madam Luosi. “Come with me when I go to Shanghai next month and discuss it with her face to face.” Gu shook his head vigorously. “Then, however unwilling she was, she would have to pretend to be delighted. No, no. She would never tell you her true feelings.” “I have a way,” Madam Luosi said. “I shall have Sister Seven herself bring it up.” At this, both men leaned forward and fixed their eyes on her, eager to hear how she could induce the Seventh Lady voluntarily to arrange a concubine for her husband. “It is quite simple. I shall take Ruixiang along. I shall merely say that I am worried about her illness and have brought Ruixiang specially to wait on her and help her manage the house. Provided Ruixiang serves her well and obeys her in everything, she will naturally see that there is only one way to keep her: let her become a member of the Gu family.” “A marvelous plan!” Hu clapped his hands. “That is settled, then.”

古应春也觉得这是个很妥当的办法，但螺蛳太太却提出了警告∶『七姐夫，不过我劝你不要心急，你最好先疏远瑞香一点。』『人逢喜事精神爽』古应春这一夜只睡了两个辰，一觉醒来，天还没有亮透，看自鸣钟上一直线，恰好六点钟响。他住的是胡家花园中的一处客房，名叫锁春院，茶木甚盛，揭开重帘，推出窗去，花香鸟语，令人精神一振，心里寻思，这天洋人拜寿，是他的『重头戏』，宁可赶早去巡查，看有什么不妥的地方，须先改正，庶几不负至交所托。于是漱洗早餐，随即带了跟班，坐着胡家替他预备的轿子，先巡视了设在城里的六处寿堂，一一检点妥当，然后出钱塘门到灵隐，不过九点刚过。这灵隐的寿堂，原规定了是英国人来拜寿的地方，只是洋人闹不清这些细节，有的逛了天竺、灵隐，顺便就来拜寿，人数不多，倒是看的人多，指指点点，嘻嘻哈哈，乱得很热闹。

Gu, too, thought it a very sound plan. Madam Luosi added a warning, however. “Brother-in-law Gu, do not be impatient. You had better keep your distance from Ruixiang at first.” Happiness renews a man’s spirits. Gu slept only four hours that night, and when he woke, it was not yet fully light. Looking at the clock, he saw the hands in a straight line just as it struck six. He was staying in a guest suite in Hu’s garden called Suochun Court, where flowers and trees grew thickly. He lifted the heavy curtains and pushed open the window. Fragrant blossoms and birdsong refreshed his spirits. Today’s reception for the foreign birthday visitors would be his great undertaking. He had better begin his inspections early, discover and correct anything amiss, and justify the trust his close friend had placed in him. He washed, ate breakfast, and set out with an attendant in the sedan the Hu household had provided. First he inspected the six birthday halls inside the city and checked that all was in order. Then he went out through Qiantang Gate to Lingyin. It was only just after nine when he arrived. The Lingyin hall had originally been assigned to the British visitors, but foreigners did not understand these fine distinctions. Some simply called to offer their congratulations after sightseeing at Tianzhu and Lingyin. There were not many guests, but a great many spectators, pointing, chattering, and laughing in lively disorder.

不久，胡雪岩到了，拉着古应春到一边说道∶『我看原来请到我那里吃西餐的办法得不通了。』『怎么呢？』『赫德到杭州来的消息，不知道怎么传出去了。德晓峰派人通知我，说要来作陪，他是好意，我怎么好挡驾？』胡雪岩又说∶『这一来，邀赫德到家，似乎不太方便。』古应春想了一下说∶『不要紧，中午在这里开席，晚上请他到府上好了。』『只好这样。』

Hu Xueyan arrived shortly afterward and drew Gu Yingchun aside. “I am afraid our plan to invite him to my house for a Western lunch will not work.” “Why not?” “Somehow the news that Hart is in Hangzhou has got out. De Xiaofeng has sent to say that he wishes to join us. He means well; how can I turn him away? But with him there, inviting Hart to the house would be rather awkward.” Gu thought for a moment. “Never mind. We can entertain him here at noon and invite him to your house in the evening.” “That will have to do.”

刚说完，已隐隐传来呜锣喝道之声，料想是德馨到了。胡雪岩迎出去一看，方知来的是赫德，原来此人极其醉心中国官场的气派，特为借了巡抚的绿呢大轿，全副『导子』，前呼后拥，趁机会大过了一番官瘾。他穿的自然是二品补服。红顶花翎的大帽子后面还装了根乌油油的大辫子；胡雪岩是见过的，不足为奇，其他游客闲人，何曾见过洋人有这样的打扮？顿时都围了上来，好在胡家的下人多，两面推排，留出一条路来，由胡雪岩陪着，直趋寿堂。于是『清音堂名』，咪哩吗啦地吹打了起来；赫德甩一甩马蹄袖，有模有样地在红毡条上跪了下去，磕完头起身，与陪礼的胡雪岩相互一揖，方始交谈。

They had scarcely finished speaking when the faint sound of gongs and cries clearing the way reached them. They supposed Dexin had arrived. Hu went out to receive him and discovered that the visitor was Hart. Enamored of the grandeur of Chinese official life, Hart had specially borrowed the governor’s large green-cloth sedan and a complete ceremonial escort, with attendants calling before and behind, to indulge his taste for official splendor. He naturally wore the insignia robe of the second rank. His hat had a red button and a peacock feather, and a long, glossy black queue had even been attached at the back. Hu had seen him like this before and found nothing unusual in it. But what sightseer or idle spectator had ever seen a foreigner dressed in such a manner? The crowd immediately closed around him. Fortunately, the Hu household had plenty of servants. They pushed people back on either side to open a path, and Hu escorted Hart straight into the birthday hall. The ceremonial musicians struck up a lively clamor. Hart flicked back his horsehoof cuffs and knelt on the strip of red felt with a convincing air of knowing what he was about. After kowtowing, he rose and exchanged a bow with Hu, who had knelt beside him to return the courtesy. Only then did they begin to talk.

『恭喜，恭喜。』赫德说得极好的一口京片子，『老太太在哪里，应该当面拜寿。』胡雪岩略有些踌躇，有这第一个戴红顶子的洋大人去见老母，实在是件很有起的事；但一进去他，女着就得回避，不免会有屏风后面，窃窃私议，失礼闹笑话就不妙了，因而答说∶『不敢当，我说到就是了。』赫德点点头，回身看见古应春说∶『昨天拜托转达雪翁的话，想必已经说过。』『是的。』古应春门见山地答说∶『雪翁的意思，今天晚上想请阁下到他府上便饭，饭后细谈。』『那就叨扰了。』赫德向胡雪岩说∶『谢谢。』

“Congratulations, congratulations!” Hart spoke excellent Beijing Chinese. “Where is the old lady? I ought to offer my birthday wishes in person.” Hu hesitated. It would be a matter of considerable interest for this first foreign dignitary to wear a red official button to meet his mother. Yet if he went inside, the women would have to withdraw. There might then be whispering behind screens, and an embarrassing breach of manners would be most unfortunate. “You are too kind,” he replied. “Allow me to convey your wishes.” Hart nodded, then turned and saw Gu. “I presume you have passed on the message I asked you to give Mr Xue yesterday.” “Yes.” Gu came straight to the point. “Mr Xue would like to invite you to a simple meal at his house this evening, with a fuller discussion afterward.” “Then I shall impose on your hospitality.” Hart turned to Hu. “Thank you.”

于是让到一边待茶。正在谈着，德馨到了；他是有意结纳赫德，陪着很敷衍了一阵。中午一起坐了面席，方始回城。这天原是比较清闲的一天，因为来拜寿洋人，毕竟有限。到得下午三点钟，古应春便已进城；略息一息亲自去接赫德，顺便邀梅藤更作陪，这是胡雪岩决定的。到时天还未黑，但萃锦堂上的煤油打汽灯，已点得一片烨烨白光。那萃锦堂是五开间的西式洋楼，楼前一个大天进，东面有座喷水池；西面用朱漆杉木，围成一个圆形栅栏，里面养着雌雄一对孔雀，一见赫德进来，冉冉开屏，不由得把他吸引住了。『这只孔雀戴的是「三眼花翎」。』赫德指着雀屏笑道∶『李中堂都没有它阔。』

They moved aside for tea. While they were talking, Dexin arrived. He was eager to cultivate Hart’s acquaintance and entertained him attentively for some time. They shared a birthday noodle banquet at noon before returning to the city. It was, in fact, a relatively quiet day: the number of foreigners coming to offer birthday greetings was limited. By three in the afternoon, Gu had already returned to the city. After a short rest, he went personally to fetch Hart, also inviting Dr Meitenggeng to join them, as Hu had decided. It was not yet dark when they arrived, but the pressurized kerosene lamps in Cuijin Hall were already blazing with brilliant white light. The hall was a Western-style building five bays wide, with a large courtyard in front. A fountain stood to the east. To the west, a circular enclosure of red-lacquered firwood housed a pair of peacocks. As soon as Hart appeared, the male slowly spread his tail and held the visitor spellbound. “This peacock wears a ‘three-eyed feather,’” Hart said, pointing at the fan and laughing. “Even Grand Secretary Li is not as grand as he is.”
