于是入座以后，便谈李鸿章了。赫德带来最新的消息，直隶总署是调两广总督张树声署理，湖广总督果然是由湖南巡抚涂宗瀛升任。『那末，两广呢？』『现在还不知道。』赫德答说∶『听说曾九帅很有意思谋这个缺。』『湖南，』胡雪岩又问∶『湖南巡抚不晓得放的哪个？』『这倒没有听说。』

Once they were seated, the conversation naturally turned to Li Hongzhang. Hart had brought the latest news: Zhang Shusheng, Governor-General of Liangguang, was being transferred to act as Governor-General of Zhili, while the governorship-general of Huguang would indeed go to Tu Zongying, Governor of Hunan. “And Liangguang?” “I do not know yet,” Hart replied. “I hear Ninth Marshal Zeng is very interested in obtaining that post.” “Hunan?” Hu asked. “Do you know who is to be appointed Governor of Hunan?” “I have heard nothing about that.”

就这时候，瑞香翩然出现，进门先福一福，拢总请了一个安，然后向胡雪岩说道∶『太太要我来说，小小姐有点发烧，怕是出痧子，想请梅先生去看一看。』『喔，』胡雪岩皱着眉说∶『梅先生是来作客的，皮包听筒也不晓得带了没有？』『带了，带了。』梅藤更是一口杭州话，『听筒是我的吃饭家伙，随身法宝，哪里会不带。』说着，从口袋中掏出一副听筒，向瑞香扬一扬说∶『我们走。』『小小姐』是螺蛳太太的小女儿，今年七岁，胡雪岩爱如掌珠；听说病了，不免有神思不属的模样，幸而有古应春陪着赫德闲谈，未曾慢客。『怎么样？』一见梅藤更回来，胡雪岩迎上去问∶『不要紧吧？』『不要紧，不要紧。』

At that moment Ruixiang entered gracefully, curtsied to the company as a whole, and addressed Hu. “Madam has sent me to say that the little miss has a slight fever. She is afraid it may be measles and would like Dr Mei to look at her.” “Oh.” Hu frowned. “Dr Mei is here as a guest. I do not even know whether he has brought his medical bag and stethoscope.” “I have it, I have it,” the doctor replied in his Hangzhou accent. “My stethoscope is how I earn my living, my indispensable companion. How could I leave it behind?” He took it from his pocket, held it up to Ruixiang, and said, “Let us go.” The little miss was Madam Luosi’s younger daughter, seven years old that year, whom Hu cherished like the pearl of his palm. Hearing that she was ill, he became somewhat distracted. Fortunately, Gu kept Hart engaged in conversation, so their guest was not neglected. As soon as the doctor returned, Hu went to meet him. “How is she? Nothing serious, I hope?” “Nothing serious at all.”

当梅藤便在开药方，交代胡家的管家到广济医院去取药时，赫德已开始与古应春谈到正事，刚开了一个头，因人入席而将话题打断了。进餐当然是照西洋规矩。桃花心木的长餐桌，通称『大餐桌』，胡雪岩与古应春分坐两端主位，胡雪岩的右手方是赫德，左手方是梅藤更。菜当然很讲究，而酒更讲究；古应春春有意为主人炫耀，命待者一瓶一瓶地将香槟酒与红葡萄酒取了来，为客人介绍哪一瓶为法国哪一位君王所御用；哪一瓶已有多少年陈，当然还有英国人所喜爱的威士忌，亦都是英国也很珍贵的名牌。

While Dr Meitenggeng wrote a prescription and instructed the Hu steward to fetch the medicine from Guangji Hospital, Hart began discussing business with Gu. They had barely started when the call to dinner interrupted them. The meal was naturally served in the Western manner. Hu and Gu took the host’s places at opposite ends of the long mahogany table, the kind commonly called a “Western dining table.” Hart sat on Hu’s right and Dr Meitenggeng on his left. The food was elaborate, but the wines were more remarkable still. Deliberately displaying his host’s wealth, Gu had the servants bring bottles of champagne and red wine one after another. He explained which had been served to which French monarch and how many years others had aged. There was also, of course, the whisky the English liked, all distinguished brands highly prized even in Britain.

这顿饭吃了有一个钟头，先是海阔天空地随意闲谈，以后便分成两对，梅藤更跟胡雪岩谈他的医院，说诊务愈来愈盛，医院想要扩充，苦于地基不足，胡雪岩答应替他想想办法；又说门前的路太狭，而且高低不平，轿马纷纷，加以摊贩众多，交通不便，向胡雪岩诉了许多苦，胡雪岩许了替他修路，但梅藤更请他向杭州府及钱塘县请一张告示驱逐摊贩，胡雪岩却婉言谢绝了。另一对是赫德与古应春，断续入席以前的话题，而是用英语交谈，谈的是广东丝业的巨头陈启沅。这陈启沅是广州南海县人，一直在南洋一带经商，同治末年回到家乡开了一家缫丝厂，招牌叫继昌隆，用了六、七百女工，规模很大，丝的品质亦很好，行销欧美，很受欢迎。

The meal lasted an hour. At first they talked freely of everything under the sun; later they split into two pairs. Dr Meitenggeng spoke to Hu about his hospital. The number of patients kept increasing, and he wished to expand, but there was not enough land. Hu promised to consider what could be done. The doctor went on to complain that the road in front was too narrow and uneven. With sedans and horses constantly coming and going, and so many street vendors besides, access was difficult. After hearing his many complaints, Hu promised to improve the road. But when the doctor asked him to obtain an official notice from the Hangzhou prefecture and Qiantang county authorities ordering the vendors away, Hu politely refused. The other pair, Hart and Gu, resumed their earlier conversation in English. They were discussing Chen Qiyuan, the leading figure in Guangdong’s silk industry. Chen came from Nanhai county in Guangzhou and had long traded in Southeast Asia. Near the end of the Tongzhi reign, he returned home and opened the Jichanglong Silk-Reeling Mill. It was a large establishment employing six or seven hundred women. Its silk was of excellent quality and sold well in Europe and America.

『他的丝好，是因为用机器，比用手工好。』赫德说∶『机器代替人工，是世界潮流。我在中国二十年，对中国的感情，跟对英国一样，甚至更为关切，因为中国更需要帮助；所以，我这一回来，想跟胡先生谈怡和丝厂开工一事，实在也为中国富强着眼。』『是的。我们都知道你对中国的爱护，不过，英国讲民主，中国亦讲顺应民情，就象细昌隆的情形，不能不引以为鉴。』原来陈启沅前两年改用机器，曾经引起很大的风潮；陈启沅不能不设法改良，制造一种一型的缫丝机，推广到农村，将机器之利，与人共享。赫德在宣扬机器的好处；古应春承认这一点，但隐然指出，想用机器替代人手，独占厚利是行不通的。

“His silk is good because he uses machinery, which is better than working by hand,” Hart said. “The replacement of manual labor by machinery is the way the world is going. I have been in China for twenty years. I feel as much for China as I do for Britain—indeed, I am even more concerned for her, because she is in greater need of help. That is why, in coming here to speak to Mr Hu about opening Jardine Matheson’s silk mill, I truly have China’s wealth and strength at heart.” “Yes. We all know your concern for China. But just as Britain speaks of democracy, China too speaks of respecting the feelings of the people. We cannot ignore the lesson of what happened at Jichanglong.” Chen’s adoption of machinery two years earlier had provoked considerable unrest. He had been obliged to find a better arrangement and developed a small silk-reeling machine for use in the villages, thus sharing the benefits of machinery with others. Hart was extolling its advantages. Gu acknowledged them but quietly made the point that replacing people with machines in order to monopolize the enormous profits would not work.

及至席散，梅藤更告辞先行，赫德留下来；与胡雪岩正式商谈时，赫德的话又同了。『雪翁！』他用中国官场的称呼，『你能不能跟怡和合伙？』胡雪岩颇为诧异，怡和洋行是英国资本，亦等于是英国官方的事业，何以会邀中国人来合伙？事情没有弄清楚以前，他不愿表示态度，只是含蓄地微笑着。

After dinner, Dr Meitenggeng took his leave, while Hart stayed. When he began formal discussions with Hu, his approach was different again. “Mr Xue,” he said, adopting the Chinese official style of address, “could you go into partnership with Jardine Matheson?” Hu was rather surprised. Jardine Matheson was financed by British capital and, as he understood it, virtually an official British enterprise. Why should it invite a Chinese merchant into partnership? Until he understood the matter, he would give no indication of his position. He merely smiled reservedly.

『我是说怡和洋行所人的丝厂。』赫德接下来说∶『他们愿意跟你订一张合同，丝都由你供应；市价以外，另送佣金。』还是为了原料！原来怡和丝厂，早在光绪元年便已开设，自以为财大势雄，派人到乡下收购茧子，价钱虽出得不坏，但挑剔得也很厉害，派人到乡下收购茧子，价线虽出得不坏，但执，甚至大起纠纷，恼了自浙江嘉与苏州一带，丝产旺地的几闲置的机器，又因保养不善，损坏，生的生锈，只好闭歇。

“I mean the silk mill owned by Jardine Matheson,” Hart continued. “They would be willing to sign a contract with you for all their silk supplies. On top of the market price, they would pay a commission.” So it was still a question of raw materials! Jardine Matheson’s silk mill had opened as early as the first year of the Guangxu reign. Confident in its wealth and influence, the firm had sent buyers into the countryside to purchase cocoons. Though the prices it offered were not bad, its buyers were extremely exacting. Serious disputes had even arisen [the ensuing passage about opposition in the silk-producing districts around Jia— in Zhejiang and Suzhou is damaged in the source]. Machinery left idle had then suffered from poor maintenance: some was damaged, some rusted. The mill had no choice but to close.

但就这两三年，日本的机器缫丝业，大为发达，怡和丝厂在去年重整鼓，新修厂房，买了意利造新机器，准备复业。此外，有个澄州人叫黄佐卿，开一家公和永丝厂，向法国买的机器，亦已运到；另有公平洋行，亦打算在这方面投资。这三家丝厂一开工，需要大量原料，丝价必定上涨，胡雪岩早就看准了。可是，他是站在反对丝厂这方面的，因为有陈启沅的例子在，机器马达一向，不知道有多少养蚕做丝人心惊肉跳。

During the past two or three years, however, machine silk-reeling had made great advances in Japan. Last year Jardine Matheson had regrouped, renovated its mill buildings, and bought new Italian machinery in preparation for reopening. In addition, Huang Zuoqing, a man from Chengzhou, had opened the Gongheyong Silk Mill, and the machinery he had bought from France had arrived. Gongping Trading House also intended to invest in this field. Once the three mills began work, they would require huge quantities of raw material, and silk prices would inevitably rise. Hu had foreseen all this long ago. Yet he stood with the opponents of the mills. Chen Qiyuan’s example was there for all to see: the moment the motors began to run, who could say how many people raising silkworms and making silk would tremble for their livelihoods?

第二章｜CHAPTER TWO

江浙的养蚕人家，大部分是产销合一的。茧子固然亦可卖给领有『部帖』的茧行，但茧行估价不高，而且同行公议，价格划一，不卖茧则已，卖茧子一定受剥削；再则收茧有一定的日子，或者人等不及，急于要钱用；或者茧子等不及，时间一长蚕蛾会咬破茧子，所以除非万不得已，或者别有盘算，总是自家养蚕、自家做丝，这就要养活许多人了，因为做丝从煮茧开始，手续繁多，缫丝以后『捻丝』、『拍丝』，进炼染炼染，纬丝捻成经丝，还有『掉经』、『牵经』等等名目，最后是『接头』，到此方可上机织绸。一旦出现了机器缫丝厂，茧子由机器这头进去，丝由那头出来，什么『拍丝』、『牵经』都用不着了，这一行的工人，亦都敲破饭碗了。更为严重的是，江浙农村，几乎家家户户都有缫丝的纺车，妇女无分老幼，大都恃此为副业；孤寒寡妇的『棺材本』，小家碧玉的『嫁时装』，出在一部纺车上的，比比皆是，如果这部纺车一旦成为废物，那就真要出现『一路哭』的场面了。

Most silkworm-raising households in Jiangsu and Zhejiang both produced and marketed their own silk. They could, of course, sell their cocoons to cocoon firms holding licenses from the ministry. But those firms offered low prices, and the members of the trade had agreed to a uniform rate. Once a family decided to sell cocoons, exploitation was unavoidable. Moreover, cocoons were bought only on specified days. Sometimes the growers could not wait because they needed money urgently; sometimes the cocoons could not wait, since in time the moths would bite their way out and ruin them. Unless driven by necessity or guided by some special calculation, families therefore raised their own silkworms and made their own silk. This provided a livelihood for many people. From boiling the cocoons onward, silk-making involved a multitude of operations. After reeling came twisting and beating, scouring and dyeing, twisting weft silk into warp silk, and the various stages of preparing and drawing out the warp. Only after the final joining of the threads could the silk go onto a loom to be woven into fabric. Once a mechanized silk-reeling mill appeared, however, cocoons went in at one end and silk emerged at the other. The beating and drawing of threads were no longer needed, and the workers who had performed them lost their livelihoods. More serious still, almost every rural household in Jiangsu and Zhejiang had a reeling wheel. Women of all ages relied on it for supplementary income. A poor widow’s savings for her coffin, a girl from a modest household’s dowry—countless such sums came from a single wheel. If that wheel became useless, there would indeed be weeping all along the road.

因此，早就不断有人向胡雪岩陈情，要求他出面控制机器缫丝厂；就因为他的力量太大，手头经常握有价值三百万两银子的一万包丝在手里，可以垄断市场，所以恰和洋行竟搬动了『二品大员』的赫德来谈条件。条件是很好。所谓『市价以外，另送佣金』，便是两笔收入，因为『市价』中照例每包有二两五钱的佣金，由介绍洋行买丝的中间人与红纵栈对分；如果『另送佣金』，每包至少亦有一两，坐享厚利，在他人求之不得，而胡雪岩却只好放弃。麻烦的是，赫德的情面不能不顾；至少要想个虽拒绝而不伤赫德面子，让他能向怡和洋行交代的说法。转了转念头，决定采取拖延的手段。

For this reason, people had long been coming to Hu Xueyan with petitions, asking him to intervene and restrain the mechanized mills. His power was immense. He regularly held ten thousand bales of silk worth three million taels of silver, enough to dominate the market. That was why Jardine Matheson had even persuaded Hart, a dignitary of the second rank, to negotiate terms. The terms were very good. “A commission on top of the market price” meant two sources of income. The market price already included the customary commission of two taels and five mace per bale, divided equally between the intermediary who introduced the foreign buyer and the silk warehouse. An additional commission would be at least another tael per bale. Here was a handsome profit for no effort, an opportunity others would have coveted; Hu could only let it go. The difficulty was that he could not disregard Hart’s feelings. At the very least, he must find a way to refuse without costing Hart face, so that the latter could still give Jardine Matheson an acceptable account of the meeting. After turning the matter over, he decided to play for time.

『鹭翁，』他从从容容地答道∶『中国人有句话，叫做「在商言商」，怡和这样好的条件，在我求之不得。不过，鹭翁总也晓得广东的情形，缫丝的机器都打坏了；如果我同怡和订了合同，起了风潮，不是我一个人的损失，地方上亦要受害。鹭翁，请你想一想，外到我们浙江巡抚，内到军机处、总理衙站，岂不都要怪我？「都老爷」的厉害，鹭翁在京多年，总也晓得，他们会饶得了我？』看看是水都泼不进去了，不道胡雪岩突然一转，『不过，』他的语声很重，『鹭翁，你不是替怡和做说客，你是为了我们中国富强，这件事情，一定要弄它成功，等我同各方面筹划出一个妥当办法出来，只要不起风潮，不弄坏市面原来靠养蚕缫丝的人家，有条生路，我一定遵鹭翁的吩咐，只跟怡和一家订约。至于额外的佣金，是鹭翁的面子，决不敢领。』这番话说得很漂亮，但赫德有名的老奸巨猾，对中国的人情世故，摸得透熟；心想不起风潮，不坏市面，还要养蚕人家有生路，要避免这三点的『妥当办法』，花十年的工夫也未见得能筹划得出来。然则什么『只跟怡和一家订约』，额外佣金『不敢领』，无非是有名无实的『口惠』而已。话虽如此，但仍能体谅胡雪岩的苦心，明明是办不到；或者说他不肯抹煞良心，不顾利害去做的事，有他则才前半段的话，也就够了，而还有后斗段『不过』以下的补充，是一种很尊重客人的表现，其意还是可感的。

“Mr Lubin,” he replied calmly, “we Chinese have a saying: ‘In business, speak as a businessman.’ Terms as good as Jardine Matheson’s are more than I could hope for. But you must know what happened in Guangdong, where the silk-reeling machines were smashed. If I signed a contract with Jardine Matheson and disturbances followed, I should not be the only one to suffer. The whole locality would suffer too. Consider it: from our governor here in Zhejiang to the Grand Council and Zongli Yamen in the capital, would they not all blame me? You have spent many years in Beijing and know how formidable the censors are. Would they let me off?” His case seemed watertight. Then he abruptly changed course. “But”—he emphasized the word—“you are not merely speaking for Jardine Matheson. Your concern is the wealth and strength of China. We must find a way to make this succeed. Let me work out a suitable arrangement with everyone concerned. Provided there is no unrest, the market is not disrupted, and the families who have lived by raising silkworms and reeling silk retain a livelihood, I shall certainly do as you ask and contract with Jardine Matheson alone. As for the extra commission, out of regard for you I could never accept it.” It was a handsome speech. But Hart, famously shrewd and thoroughly versed in Chinese social dealings, reflected that a satisfactory plan meeting all three conditions—no unrest, no market disruption, and a living for the silkworm-raising families—might not be devised in ten years. The promise to contract exclusively with Jardine Matheson and the refusal of extra commission were thus generous words with nothing behind them. Nevertheless, he could appreciate Hu’s care. The thing was plainly impossible, or at least something Hu would not do at the cost of silencing his conscience and disregarding the consequences. The first half of his reply would have been sufficient. Everything after “but” was an additional mark of respect for his guest, and the intention deserved gratitude.

因此，他深深点头，『雪翁真是明理的人，比京中那几位大老，高明得太多了。』他说∶『我总算也是不虚此行。』『哪里，哪里！』胡雪岩答说∶『都象鹭翁这么样体谅，什么都好谈。』侍者上菜，暂时隔断了谈话。这道菜是古应春发明的，名为『炸虾饼』，外表看来象炸板鱼，上口才知味道大不相同，是用虾仁捣烂，和上鸡胸肉切碎的鸡绒，用豆腐衣包成长方块，沾了面包粉油炸，做法仿佛杭州菜中的『炸响铃』，只是材料讲究得太多了。赫德的牙齿不太好，所以特别赞赏这道菜。这就有了个闲谈的话题，赫德很坦率地说，他舍不得离开中国，口腹之欲是很大的一个原因。『董大人常常请我吃饭。』他不胜神往地说∶『他家的厨子，在我看全世界第一！』

Hart nodded deeply. “You are a man who understands reason, Mr Xue—far more enlightened than those venerable gentlemen in Beijing. My journey has not been in vain.” “You are too kind,” Hu replied. “If everyone were as understanding as you, anything could be discussed.” A servant brought in another dish, briefly interrupting the conversation. It was Gu’s invention, called Fried Shrimp Cakes. At first sight it resembled fried flatfish; only on tasting it did one discover how different it was. Pounded shrimp was mixed with finely minced chicken breast, wrapped in tofu skin in rectangular parcels, coated in breadcrumbs, and fried. The method resembled Hangzhou’s fried “ringing bells,” but the ingredients were far more luxurious. Hart’s teeth were poor, and he particularly appreciated the dish. This supplied a lighter topic. He freely admitted that the pleasures of the table were a major reason for his reluctance to leave China. “Lord Dong often invites me to dinner,” he said longingly. “To my mind, his cooks are the finest in the world!”

『董大人？是指户部尚书董恂，在总理衙门』当家『；他是扬州人，善于应酬，用了两个出身于扬州』八大盐商『家的厨子，都有能做』全羊席『、』全鳝席『的本事。董恂应酬洋人，还有一套扬州盐商附庸风雅的花样，经常来个』投壶『、』射虎『的雅集。有时拿荷马、拜伦的诗，译成』古内『或』近体『。醉心中国文化的赫德，跟他特别投缘。』白乐天在贵外杭州做的诗∶『未能抛得杭州去，一半勾留为此湖。「我倒想改一改，」未能抛得中华去，一半勾留是此┅┅「』赫德有点抓瞎，搔着花白头发『此』了好一会，突然双眉一掀，『肴！一半勾留是此肴。』

Lord Dong was Dong Xun, Minister of Revenue, who managed affairs at the Zongli Yamen. A native of Yangzhou and an accomplished host, he employed two cooks who had worked in the households of that city’s Eight Great Salt Merchants. Both could prepare entire banquets made from mutton or from eel. In entertaining foreigners, Dong also adopted the salt merchants’ displays of cultivated taste: gatherings for pitch-pot or riddle guessing, sometimes even translating Homer and Byron into ancient-style or regulated Chinese verse. Hart, enamored of Chinese culture, felt a particular affinity with him. “Bai Letian wrote a poem while in your city of Hangzhou: ‘I cannot bring myself to leave Hangzhou; / Half of what holds me here is this lake.’ I should like to alter it: ‘I cannot bring myself to leave China; / Half of what holds me here is this—’” Hart was stuck. Scratching his graying hair, he repeated “this” for some time. Then his eyebrows suddenly rose. “Fare! ‘Half of what holds me here is this fare.’”

胡雪岩暗中惭愧，不知道他说的什么。古应春倒听懂了一半，便即问道∶『听说赫大人常跟董大人一起做诗唱和，真是了不起！』『唱和还谈不到，不过常在一起谈诗、谈词。』赫德又说∶『小犬是从小读汉文，老师也是董大人荐来的；现在已经开手做八股了，将来想在科场里面讨个出身，董大人答应替我代奏，不知道能准不能准？』这番话，胡雪岩是听明白了。『洋娃娃』读汉文、做八股『已经是奇事；居然还想赴考，真是闻所未闻了。』一定会准。『古应春在回答。』难得贤乔梓这样子仰慕中华，皇上一定恩出格外。『』但原能准。『赫德忽然说道∶』我想起一件，趁现在谈，免得回头忘记。雪翁，有件事，想请你帮忙，怡和洋行派人到湖州去买丝，定洋已经付出去了；现在有个消息，说到新丝上市，不打算交货了。将来真的这样子，恐怕彼此要破脸了。『胡雪岩隐约听说过这回事，其中还牵涉到一个姓赵的』教民『，但不知其详，更不知谁是谁？不过赫德话中的分量，却是心里已经掂到了。

Hu felt secretly ashamed: he had no idea what Hart was talking about. Gu understood at least part of it and asked, “I hear that you and Lord Dong often exchange poems. A remarkable accomplishment!” “I could hardly claim to exchange poems with him. But we often discuss poetry and lyrics.” Hart continued, “My son has studied Chinese writing from childhood. Lord Dong recommended his teacher. He has now begun composing eight-legged essays, and he hopes eventually to gain a degree through the examinations. Lord Dong has promised to submit a memorial on his behalf. I do not know whether permission will be granted.” This Hu understood. A foreign child reading Chinese and writing eight-legged essays was strange enough; that he should actually hope to sit the examinations was unheard of. “It will certainly be granted,” Gu answered. “Such admiration for China on the part of both father and son is rare. His Majesty is sure to show exceptional favor.” “I only hope so.” Hart suddenly said, “That reminds me of something. Let me mention it now before I forget. Mr Xue, there is a matter in which I should like your help. Jardine Matheson sent someone to Huzhou to buy silk, and the deposits have been paid. Now we hear that when the new silk comes onto the market, the sellers do not intend to deliver. If that really happens, I fear there will be an open rupture.” Hu had heard something vaguely about the affair. It involved a Christian convert named Zhao, but he knew neither the details nor exactly who was concerned. He had, however, already weighed the significance of Hart’s words.

『鹭翁，』他问∶『你要我怎么帮怡和的忙，请你先说明了，我来想想办法。』『雪翁一言九鼎。既然怡和付了定洋，想请雪翁交代一声，能够如期交货。』胡雪岩心想赫德奸滑无比，他说这话，可能是个陷井，如果一口应承，他回到京里说一句，养蚕做丝的人家，都只凭胡某人一句许，他们的丝，说能卖就卖；说不能卖，谁也不敢卖。那一来总理衙门就可能责成他为了敦睦邦交，一定要让怡和在乡下能直接买丝，这不是很大的难题。于是胡雪岩答说∶『一言九鼎这句话，万万不敢当。丝卖不卖，是人家的事，我姓胡的，不能干预；干预了他们亦未必肯听。不过交易总要讲公道，收了定洋不交货，说不过去；再有困难，至少要还定洋。鹭翁特为交代的事，我不能不尽心力去办。这样，』他沉吟了一下说∶『听说其中牵涉到一个姓赵的，在教堂做事；我请应春兄下去，专门为鹭翁料理这件事。』『承情之至。』赫德拱拱手道谢。

“Mr Lubin,” he asked, “tell me first exactly how you would like me to help Jardine Matheson, and I shall see what can be done.” “Your word carries enormous weight. Since Jardine Matheson has paid the deposits, I should like you to give instructions that delivery be made on time.” Hu reflected that Hart was exceedingly cunning. This might be a trap. If he agreed too readily, Hart could go back to Beijing and say that all the silkworm growers and silk producers acted solely on Hu’s word: when he said their silk could be sold, they sold it; when he said it could not, nobody dared sell. The Zongli Yamen might then order him, in the interests of friendly foreign relations, to ensure that Jardine Matheson could buy silk directly in the countryside. What a difficulty that would create! “I cannot possibly accept your description of my influence,” he replied. “Whether people sell their silk is their own affair. I have no right to interfere, and even if I did, they might not listen. But business must be conducted fairly. To accept a deposit and then refuse delivery is indefensible. Whatever the difficulty, at the very least the deposit must be returned. Since you have specially asked me, I must do all I can. Let us put it this way”—he paused to consider—“I hear the affair involves a man named Zhao who works at a church. I shall send Brother Yingchun down expressly to deal with it for you.” “I am most grateful.” Hart clasped his hands in thanks.

『请问赫大人，』古应春开口问道∶『能不能让怡和派个人跟我来接头。』『怡和的东主艾力克就在杭州。』赫德用英语问道∶『你们不是很熟吗？』『是的，很熟。而且听说他也到杭州来了，不知道什么地方可以找得到他。』『你到我这里来好了。』梅藤更插进来说。

“May I ask,” Gu said, “whether Jardine Matheson could send someone to make contact with me?” “Ailike, the proprietor of Jardine Matheson, is here in Hangzhou,” Hart replied in English. “Are you not well acquainted?” “Yes, very well. I had heard he was here, but I did not know where to find him.” “Come to my place,” Dr Meitenggeng put in.

『好。』古应春答说∶『我明天上午到广济医院去。』送走了客人，胡雪岩跟古应春还有话要谈。酒阑人散，加以胡家的内眷，都在灵隐陪侍老太太，少了二、三十个丫头，那份清静简直就有点寂寞了。『难得，难得！今天倒真是我们弟兄挖挖心里的话的辰光。应春！今天很暖和，我们在外面坐。』『外面』指的镜槛阁的前廊，因为要反映阁外的景致，造得格外宽大，不过凭栏设座，却在西面一角，三月十一的月亮也很大了，清光斜照，两人脸上都是幽幽地一种肃散的神色。『应春，』胡雪岩说∶『我这几天有个很怪的念头，俗语说「人在福中不知福」，这句话不晓得对不对？』古应春无从回答，因为根本不知道他为什么有这样一个『很怪的念头』。

“Very well,” Gu replied. “I shall call at Guangji Hospital tomorrow morning.” When the guests had gone, Hu still had things to discuss with Gu. The drinking was over and the company dispersed. Most of the women of the Hu household were at Lingyin attending on the old lady, taking twenty or thirty maids with them. The resulting quiet was almost lonely. “A rare opportunity, a rare opportunity! Tonight we brothers can really speak from the heart. Yingchun, it is warm. Let us sit outside.” Outside meant the gallery in front of Jingjian Pavilion, built unusually broad to allow the scenery beyond to be reflected inside. The seats by the railing were in a corner to the west. The moon of the eleventh day of the third month was already large. Its clear light fell obliquely across the two men, giving their faces a faintly somber and withdrawn look. “Yingchun,” Hu said, “I have had a very strange thought these past few days. There is a saying: ‘A man surrounded by good fortune does not know how fortunate he is.’ I wonder whether it is true.” Gu could not answer, having no idea what had prompted this strange thought.

『我们老太太常说要惜福，福是怎么个惜法？』『这——』古应春一面想，一面说∶『无非不要太过分的意思福不要享尽。』『对，不过那一来就根本谈不到享福了。你只要有这样子一个念头在心里，喝口茶、吃口饭都要想一想，是不是太过分？做人做到这个地步，还有啥味道？』古应春觉得他多少是诡辩，但驳不倒他，只好发问∶『那末，小爷叔，你说应该怎么样呢？』『照我想，反倒是「人在福中不知福」，才真是在享福。』『小爷叔，你的意思是一个人不必惜福？』『不是，我不是这个意思。我是说∶享福归享福，发财归发财，两桩事情不要混在一起，想发财要动脑，要享福就不必去管怎么样发财。』『小爷叔』，古应春笑道∶『你老人家的话，我愈听愈不懂。』

“My mother is always telling me to cherish my blessings. But how does one cherish them?” “Well…” Gu thought as he spoke. “I suppose it means not carrying things too far—not using up all one’s good fortune.” “Quite. But then one can scarcely speak of enjoying good fortune at all. Once that thought is in your mind, every sip of tea and every mouthful of rice makes you wonder whether you are going too far. What pleasure is there in a life like that?” Gu felt this was something of a sophistry, but he could not refute it. He could only ask, “What do you suggest, then, Uncle?” “I think it is precisely the man who does not know how fortunate he is who truly enjoys his good fortune.” “Do you mean one need not cherish one’s blessings?” “No, that is not what I mean. I mean that enjoying oneself is one thing and making money another. Do not confuse the two. To make money, one must use one’s brains; to enjoy oneself, one must stop thinking about how to make money.” Gu laughed. “Uncle, the more I hear, the less I understand.”

胡雪岩付之一笑，『不但你愈听愈不懂，我也愈想愈不懂。』他急转直下地说∶『我们来想个发财的法子——不对，想个又能发财，又要享福的法子。』古应春想了一会，笑了，『小爷叔，』他说，『法子倒有一个，只怕做不到；不过，就算能够做到了，恐怕小爷叔，你我也决不肯去做。』『说来听听，啥法子？』『「嫖能倒贴，天下营生无双」。那就是又发财又享福的法子。』『这也不见得！』胡雪岩欲语不语，『好了，我们还是实实惠惠谈生意。今天我冒冒失失答应赫德了，你总要把我这个面子绷起来。』『那还要说！小爷叔说出去了，我当然要做到，好在过了今天就没有我的事；明天上半天去看艾力克，下半天来开销我带来的那班人，后天就可以动身。』『要带什么人？』

Hu smiled back. “The more I think about it, the less I understand it myself.” Then he abruptly changed direction. “Let us think of a way to make money—no, a way both to make money and enjoy ourselves.” Gu considered for a while, then laughed. “There is one way, but I doubt it can be done. Even if it could, I am afraid neither you nor I would agree to it.” “Tell me. What is it?” “‘To visit courtesans and have them pay you—there is no business like it under Heaven.’ That is a way to make money and enjoy yourself at once.” “Not necessarily!” Hu seemed about to say more, then stopped. “Enough. Let us talk about practical business. I rashly gave Hart my word today. You must make sure I do not lose face over it.” “That goes without saying. Once you have spoken, I must see it through. Fortunately, after today my duties here will be over. Tomorrow morning I shall see Ailike; in the afternoon I shall settle up with the people I brought. The day after tomorrow I can leave.” “Whom will you take with you?”

古应春沉吟一会说∶『带一个丝行里的伙计就够了。要人，好在湖州钱庄典当、丝行里都可以调动，倒是有一样东西不可不带。』『是啥？』『藩司衙门的公事——』『为啥？』胡雪岩迫不及待地追问。『这道公事给湖州府，要这样说∶风闻湖州教民赵某某仗势欺人，所作所为都是王法所不容，特派古某某下去密查，湖州府应该格外予以方便。』『古某某』是古应春自称。他捐了个候补通判的职衔，又在吏部花了钱，分发到浙江。实际上他不想做官，又不想当差，只是有了这样一个头衔，有许多方便；甚至于还可以检便宜，这时候就是用得到的时候了。

After a little thought, Gu said, “One clerk from the silk firm will be enough. If I need anyone else, the banks, pawnshops, and silk firms in Huzhou can all provide men. But there is one thing I must take.” “What is it?” “An official dispatch from the provincial treasurer’s office.” “Why?” Hu asked eagerly. “It must be addressed to the Huzhou prefecture and say something like this: reports have been received that a Christian convert in Huzhou, one Zhao, abuses his influence to bully others and acts in ways intolerable under the law. Gu is therefore being sent specially to investigate in secret, and the prefecture is to give him every assistance.” By “Gu,” he meant himself. He had purchased the title of expectant assistant prefect and paid further money at the Ministry of Personnel to be assigned to Zhejiang. He did not actually wish to hold office or take an official assignment. Such a title merely afforded many conveniences and could even bring opportunities for personal advantage. This was one occasion when it would prove useful.

『我有了这个奉宪命查案的身分，就可以跟赵某人讲斤头了，斤头谈不拢，我再到湖州府去报文，也还不迟。』『这个法子不坏！』胡雪岩说∶『明天上午我们一起去见德晓峰。』『上午我约好要去看艾力克，是不是下午看德藩台？』『只怕公事当天赶不及。』胡雪岩紧接着，『晚一天动身也不要紧。』『好，那就准定后天动身。』

“With the status of an investigator acting under the provincial authority’s orders, I can bargain with Zhao. If we cannot come to terms, it will still be time enough to present my credentials to the Huzhou prefecture.” “Not a bad plan,” Hu said. “Let us call on De Xiaofeng together tomorrow morning.” “I have already arranged to see Ailike in the morning. Could we call on Treasurer De in the afternoon?” “I am only afraid they may not get the dispatch ready that same day.” Hu added at once, “There is no harm in leaving a day later.” “Very well. Then we shall definitely leave the day after tomorrow.”

『应春，』胡雪岩换了个话题，『你明天见了艾力克，要问他要帐，他到底放出去多少定洋，放给什么人，数目多少，一定要他开个花名册。』『这——』古应春迟疑着，『只怕他开不出来，帐都在他洋行里。』『不要紧，等他回上海再开。你告诉他，只要花名册开来，查过没有花帐，一定如数照付，叫他放心好了。』『小爷叔，』古应春郑重警告∶『这样做法很危险。』『你是说风险？』胡雪岩问∶『我们不背风险，叫哪个来背？』古应春想了一说∶『既然如此，何不索性先把款子付了给他，也买个漂亮。』『我正是这个意思，也不光是买个漂亮，我是要叫他知难而退；而且这一来，他的那班客户都转到我手里来了。』『还是小爷叔厉害。』古应春笑道∶『我是一点都没有想到。』谈到这里，只见瑞香翩然而至，问宵夜的心开在何处？胡雪岩交代∶『就开到这里来！』古应春根本就吃不下宵夜，而且也有些疲累，很想早点归寝，但仿佛这一下会辜负瑞香的一番殷勤之意，怕她会觉得扫兴，所以仍旧留了下来。

Hu changed the subject. “Yingchun, when you see Ailike tomorrow, ask for his accounts. Exactly how much has he advanced in deposits? To whom, and in what amounts? He must provide an itemized list of names.” “Well…” Gu hesitated. “He may not be able to do that. His accounts are at the firm.” “Never mind. He can prepare it when he returns to Shanghai. Tell him that once the list arrives, and we have checked that there are no fictitious entries, we shall pay the whole amount. He need not worry.” “Uncle,” Gu warned gravely, “that is dangerous.” “You mean there is a risk? If we do not bear it, who will?” Gu thought for a moment. “In that case, why not pay him the money first and make a handsome gesture of it?” “That is precisely my intention. And it is not merely a handsome gesture. I want him to see the difficulties and retreat. Besides, all his suppliers will then pass into my hands.” Gu laughed. “You are the shrewder man, Uncle. That never occurred to me.” At this point Ruixiang appeared, asking where the late supper should be served. “Bring it here,” Hu instructed. Gu had no appetite at all and was rather tired. He wanted to go to bed early, but felt that leaving now would slight Ruixiang’s attentiveness and disappoint her. So he stayed.

不过一开了来，他倒又有食欲了，因为宵夜的只是极薄的香粳米粥，六样粥菜，除了醉蟹以外，其他都是凉拌笋尖之类的素肴。连日饱沃肥甘，正思清淡食物，所以停滞的胃口又开了。盛粥之先，瑞香问道∶『古老爷要不要来杯酒？』『好啊！』古应春欣然答说∶『我要杯白兰地。』『有我们太太用人参泡的白兰地，我去拿。』说着，先盛了两碗粥，然后去取来浸泡在水晶瓶里的药酒，取来的水晶杯也不错，是巨腹矮脚，用来喝白兰地的酒杯。这就使得古应春想到上个月在家请客，请的法国的一个家有酒窑的巨商，饭前酒、饭后酒，什么菜配红酒，什么菜配白酒，都有讲究。古应春原有全套的酒杯，但女仆不懂这套规矩，预备得不周全；七姑奶奶不知道怎么知道了，在床空着急。如果有瑞香在，她便可以不必操心了。这样想着，不自觉抬头去看瑞香，脸上自然是含着笑意∶瑞香正在斟酒，不曾发觉，胡雪岩冷眼旁观，却看得很清楚。『湘阴四月里要出巡，上海的制造局是一定要去看的，那时候我当然要去等他。应春，我想等老太太的生日一过，让罗四姐先去看七姐；到时候我再跟他换班，那就两头都顾到了。你看好不好？』

Once supper appeared, his appetite returned. There was only a very thin gruel of fragrant rice and six accompaniments. Apart from drunken crab, all were vegetable dishes such as cold dressed bamboo-shoot tips. After days of rich eating, he had been longing for something plain, and his sluggish appetite revived. Before serving the gruel, Ruixiang asked, “Would you like a glass of wine, Mr Gu?” “Gladly. I should like some brandy.” “We have some brandy in which Madam has steeped ginseng. I shall fetch it.” She filled two bowls of gruel first, then brought the medicinal liquor in a crystal decanter. The crystal glass she chose was right too: a large-bowled, short-stemmed glass designed for brandy. It reminded Gu of a dinner at home the month before for a wealthy French merchant whose family owned wine cellars. There were rules for everything: drinks before and after the meal, red wine with one dish and white with another. Gu possessed a full set of glasses, but the maidservants did not understand the rules and had not prepared them properly. Somehow the Seventh Lady learned of it and could only fret helplessly in bed. With Ruixiang there, she would not have to worry. Thinking this, Gu unconsciously looked up at Ruixiang with a smile. She was pouring the brandy and did not notice, but Hu, watching quietly, saw it all. “Xiangyin will be making an inspection tour in the fourth month,” he said. “He is certain to visit the arsenal in Shanghai, and naturally I must be there to wait for him. Yingchun, once Mother’s birthday is over, I think I shall have Sister Luo go ahead to see Sister Seven. Then I can take her place when I arrive. That way we shall attend to both ends. What do you think?”

『怎么不好？』古应春答说∶『这回罗四姐去，就住在我那里好了。』

“What could be better?” Gu replied. “Sister Luo must stay at my house on this visit.”

『当然，当然，非住你那里不可的，不然就不方便了。』古应春觉得他话中有话，却无从猜测；不过由左宗棠出巡到上海，却想到了好些事。『湘阴到上海，我们该怎么预备？』『喔，这件事我早想到了，因为老太太生日，没有工夫谈。』胡雪岩答说∶『湘阴两样毛病，你晓得的，一样是好虚面子，一样是总想打倒李二先生。所以我在想，先打听打听李二先生当年以两江总督的身分到上海，是啥场面？这一回湘阴去了，场面盖过李二先生，他就高兴了。』『我记得李二先生是同治四年放江督的，十几年的工夫，情形不大同了。当年的「常胜军」，算是他的部下，当然要请他去看操；现在各国有兵舰派在上海，是人家自己的事，不见得会请他上船去看。』『提起这一层，我倒想到了。兵舰上可以放礼炮；等他坐船到高昌庙的时候，黄浦江里十几条外国兵舰一齐放礼炮，远到昆山、松江都听得到，湘阴这个面子就足了。』『这倒可以办得到，外国人这种空头人情是肯做的。不过，俄国兵舰，恐怕不肯。』这是顾虑到伊犁事件中，左宗棠对俄国采取敌对态度之故。但胡雪岩以为事过境迁，俄国兵舰的指挥官，不见得还会记着这段旧怨。

“Of course, of course. She must stay with you. It would be inconvenient otherwise.” Gu felt that there was more behind Hu’s words, but could not guess what it was. The thought of Zuo Zongtang’s inspection visit to Shanghai, however, brought several other matters to mind. “What preparations should we make for Xiangyin’s arrival?” “Ah, I thought of that some time ago, but Mother’s birthday left no opportunity to discuss it. You know his two weaknesses: he loves outward marks of prestige, and he is always intent on bringing down Second Master Li. I thought we should first find out what sort of reception Li had when he came to Shanghai as Governor-General of Liangjiang. If we give Xiangyin a grander reception, he will be pleased.” “I remember that Li was appointed in the fourth year of Tongzhi. That was more than ten years ago, and things have changed. The Ever-Victorious Army was under his command, so naturally he was invited to review it. The foreign warships now stationed at Shanghai are there for their own countries’ purposes. They may not invite him aboard for an inspection.” “That reminds me of something. Warships can fire salutes. When his vessel reaches Gaochangmiao, let a dozen or more foreign warships on the Huangpu fire together. The sound will carry as far as Kunshan and Songjiang. That should give Xiangyin all the prestige he wants.” “It can be arranged. Foreigners are quite willing to confer that sort of empty honor. The Russian ships, however, may refuse.” Gu was thinking of Zuo’s hostile attitude toward Russia during the Ili affair. Hu thought circumstances had changed and the Russian commanders might no longer bear that old grudge.

『应春，这件事你要早点去办，都要讲好，俄国人那里，可以转托人去疏通；俄国同德国不是蛮接近的吗？』『好。我会去找路子。』『我想，来得及的话，罗四姐跟你一起去，倒也蛮好。胡雪岩说了这一句，眼尖瞥见瑞香留心在听，便招招手将她唤了过来，有话问她。『瑞香，』他说∶『太太要到上海去看七姑奶奶，你要跟了去。』

“Yingchun, arrange it early and settle everything beforehand. Someone else might help smooth the way with the Russians. Are Russia and Germany not rather close?” “Very well. I shall look for a way.” “If there is time, it would be quite good for Sister Luo to travel with you.” As he said this, Hu noticed Ruixiang listening closely. He beckoned her over. “Ruixiang, Madam is going to Shanghai to see the Seventh Lady. You are to go with her.”

『是。』『我再问你一句话，太太有这个意思，想叫你留在上海，帮七姑奶奶管家，你愿意不愿意。』『要说管家，我不敢当。七姑奶奶原有管家的。』『那末，照应七姑奶奶的病呢？』『这，当然是应该的。』瑞香答说∶『只要老爷、太太交代，我当然伺候。』『伺候不敢当。』古应春插进来说∶『不过她病在床上，没有个人跟她谈得来的，心里难免闷气，病也不容易好了。我先谢谢你。』说着，站了起来。『不敢当，不敢当。』瑞香想按他的肩，不让他起立，手伸了出去，才想到要避嫌疑，顿时脸一红往后退了两步，把头低着。『好！这就算说定规了。』胡雪岩一语双关地说∶『应春，你放心到湖州去吧！』

“Yes, Master.” “There is something else I want to ask. Madam is thinking of leaving you in Shanghai to help the Seventh Lady manage the house. Would you be willing?” “I would not presume to manage the house. The Seventh Lady already has someone to do that.” “Then what about caring for her during her illness?” “That would certainly be my duty. If you and Madam instruct me, of course I shall attend to her.” “We could hardly ask you to serve us,” Gu interposed. “But she lies ill in bed without anyone she can really talk to. She is bound to feel depressed, and that makes recovery more difficult. Let me thank you in advance.” He rose. “Please do not, please do not!” Ruixiang reached out to press his shoulder and keep him seated. Only after extending her hand did she remember that she must avoid any appearance of impropriety. Blushing, she stepped back twice and lowered her head. “Good! Then that is settled,” Hu said with a double meaning. “Yingchun, you can go to Huzhou with an easy mind.”

胡家自己有十二条船，最好的两条官船，一大一小；古应春一行只得四个人，坐了小的那一条，由小火轮拖带，当天便到了湖州以北的南浔。这个位于太湖南岸的市镇，为东南财赋之区的精华所聚，名气不大，而富庶过于有名的江西景德镇、广东佛山镇，就因为这里出全中国最好的『七里丝』。古应春对南浔并不陌生，随同胡雪岩来过一回，自己来过两回，这一次是一年之中，再度重临，不过去年是红叶乌桕的深秋，今年是草长莺飞的暮春。船是停在西市梢，踏上石埠头，一条青石板铺的『纤路』，却有一条很宽的死巷子，去到尽头才看到左首有两扇黑油铜环，很气派的大门，门楣上嵌着一方水磨砖嵌字的匾额，篆书四字∶『莲池精舍』。

The Hu household owned twelve boats. The best were two official-style vessels, one large and one small. Gu’s party numbered only four, so they took the smaller, towed by a little steam launch, and reached Nanxun, north of Huzhou, that same day. This town on the southern shore of Lake Tai concentrated the wealth of the prosperous southeast. Less famous than Jingdezhen in Jiangxi or Foshan in Guangdong, it was richer than either, for it produced the finest silk in China, known as Qili silk. Gu knew Nanxun well enough. He had come once with Hu and twice on his own. This was his second visit within a year, but last time it had been late autumn, with red leaves on the tallow trees; now it was late spring, with grass growing high and orioles on the wing. They moored at the far end of the western market. From the stone landing, a towpath paved with blue stone led to a broad blind alley. Only at its end did they see, on the left, an imposing pair of black-lacquered doors with bronze rings. A plaque of polished brick inlaid with lettering above the lintel bore four seal-script characters: Lotus Pond Retreat.

『这里就是了。』古应春向跟着身后的同伴雷桂卿说∶『如果我一个人来，每回都住在这里。』说着，找到门上有个扣环，拉了两下，只听门内琅琅铃响，不久门开；应门的是二十来岁的女子，穿着淡青竹布僧袍，却留着一头披到肩下的长发。雷桂卿在船上就听古应春谈过『莲池精舍』这座家庭，与众不同；他处家庵大多是官宦人家老主人的姬妾，年纪有比『少爷』、『少奶奶』还轻的，老主人下世，既不能下堂求去，又嫌在家拘束，往往由小主人斥资造一座家庵，置百十亩良田，供她长斋礼佛，带发修行。惟独这座莲池精舍的『住持』，原是苏州自立门户的一个名妓，只为先后结过两个已论嫁娶的恩客，一个病故，一个横死，勘透情关，造了这座莲池精舍，奉莲池大师的『净土宗』，忏悔宿业。这法名悟心的住持，在家时，便以豪爽善应酬驰名于十里山塘；出了家，本性难改，有谈得来的男客，一样接待在庵里住，但不能动绮念。倘不知趣，她有王熙凤收拾贾瑞的手段，叫人吃了哑巴亏而无可奈何。

“This is the place,” Gu told Lei Guiqing, who followed him. “Whenever I come alone, I stay here.” Finding a pull-ring on the door, he tugged it twice. A bell rang inside, and presently the door opened. The woman who answered was in her twenties. She wore a pale blue-green cotton monastic robe, but her hair hung long below her shoulders. On the boat, Gu had already told Lei how unusual this private Buddhist retreat was. Such foundations were generally established for the concubines of deceased officials or wealthy householders. Some of these women were younger than the heir and his wife. After the old master’s death, they could neither leave the family to make new lives nor endure its restrictions. The new master would often pay to build a private retreat, endow it with roughly a hundred mu of good farmland, and support the woman in a permanent vegetarian life of Buddhist devotion, practiced without shaving her hair. The woman presiding over Lotus Pond Retreat was different. She had once been a famous Suzhou courtesan with an establishment of her own. On two occasions she had become attached to patrons with whom marriage had already been discussed. One died of illness, the other met a violent death. Disillusioned with love, she built Lotus Pond Retreat and followed the Pure Land teaching of Master Lianchi to repent the karmic debts of her former life. Taking the religious name Wuxin, she became its mistress. Even in her worldly days, her generosity and skill in entertaining had been famous throughout Shantang. Religious retirement had not changed her nature. She still received male guests whose company she enjoyed and allowed them to stay at the retreat, but they must entertain no amorous designs. If a man failed to understand, she possessed all Wang Xifeng’s skill in dealing with Jia Rui: she could make him suffer a humiliation he dared neither complain of nor remedy.

古应春是当她在风尘中时，便曾有一面之缘，第一回到南浔来，听人谈起，特地来访。古应春文雅而风趣，肚子里的『杂货』很多，谈什么都能谈出个名堂来，加以善于体贴，在花丛中是到处受欢迎的客人；到了『方外』，亦复如是，悟心跟他很投缘，第一次作客莲池以后，坚约以后到南浔来，一定要以她这里为居停，不过这一回却有负悟心的好意了。『小玉，』古应春向应门的女子说∶『这位是雷三爷。』『雷三爷请。』小玉一面关门，一面问道∶『古老爷，怎么不先写封信来？』『临时有事才决定到湖州来一趟。』古应春问道∶『你师父呢？那只哈叭狗怎么不见？』悟心有条善解人意的哈叭狗，每回听到古应春的声音——哪怕是脚步声，都会摇着项下的金铃，蹒蹒跚跚地跑来向他摇尾巴大吠；此时声息全无，所以他诧异地问。『师父让黄太太请了去了。』小玉答说∶『大概也快回来了，请到师父的禅房里坐。』悟心的禅房是一座五开间的敞轩，正中铺着佛堂，东首是两间打通的客座，收拾得纤尘不染。小玉肃客落座，随即便有一个十二三岁与小玉般打扮的小姑娘，走来奉茶。

Gu had met her once when she was still a courtesan. On his first visit to Nanxun, hearing people speak of her, he had made a special call. Cultivated and witty, with a rich store of miscellaneous knowledge, he could make something interesting of almost any subject. His consideration for others made him welcome wherever women entertained, and he was equally welcome in this retreat from the world. He and Wuxin got on extremely well. After that first visit, she had insisted that whenever he came to Nanxun, he must make her house his lodging. This time, however, he would have to disappoint her. “Xiaoyu,” he said to the woman at the door, “this is Third Master Lei.” “Please come in, Third Master Lei.” As she closed the door, Xiaoyu asked, “Mr Gu, why did you not write ahead?” “The business that brings me to Huzhou arose quite suddenly. Where is your teacher? And where is the little lapdog?” Wuxin had an exceptionally responsive little dog. Whenever it heard Gu’s voice—or even his footsteps—it would come waddling up, the gold bell at its neck jingling, to wag its tail and bark at him. Now there was neither sound nor movement from it, which puzzled him. “Madam Huang has invited my teacher over,” Xiaoyu replied. “She should be back soon. Please sit in her rooms.” Wuxin’s quarters occupied an open pavilion five bays wide, with a Buddhist shrine in the center. The two eastern bays had been joined into a reception room kept spotless. Xiaoyu ceremoniously showed them to their seats. Soon a girl of twelve or thirteen, dressed like Xiaoyu, came to serve tea.

『是你的师弟？』古应春说，『去年没有见过。』『今年正月里来的。』接着便叫∶『阿文，这位古老爷，这位雷三爷。』阿文腼腼腆腆地叫了人，向小玉说道∶『三师兄，老佛婆说师父今天在黄家，总要吃了斋才回来，她也要回家看孙子去了。』古应春知道这里的情形，所以懂她的意思，老佛婆烧得一手好素菜；这天不在庵里，回头款客的素斋，便无着落，特意提醒小玉。因此，古应春不等小玉开口，先抢着说道∶『我们不在这里吃饭。船菜还多得很，天气热了，不吃坏掉也可惜。喔，还有，这一回我不能住在你们这里，我同雷三爷回船去睡。』『古老爷，』小玉微笑道∶『都等我师父回来了再说。』

“Is she a junior fellow disciple of yours?” Gu asked. “I did not see her last year.” “She came in the first month of this year.” Xiaoyu then called to her. “A’wen, this is Mr Gu, and this is Third Master Lei.” A’wen shyly greeted them, then addressed Xiaoyu. “Third Senior, Old Buddha Granny says Teacher will stay at the Huangs’ for the vegetarian meal before coming back. Granny is going home to see her grandson.” Gu knew the arrangements here and understood what she meant. Old Buddha Granny was an excellent vegetarian cook. If she was away, there would be no one to prepare the visitors’ meal. A’wen was tactfully alerting Xiaoyu. Before Xiaoyu could speak, Gu said, “We shall not eat here. There is plenty of food on the boat, and in this warm weather it would be a pity to let it spoil. Oh, and I cannot stay here this time. Third Master Lei and I shall sleep aboard.” Xiaoyu smiled. “Mr Gu, let us wait until my teacher returns before deciding all that.”

古应春点头，问些庵中近况。不一会阿文来上点心；家庵中的小吃，一向讲究质地，不重形式，端出来的枣泥方糕，不甚起眼，但上口才知道香甜无比，本以初次作客，打算浅尝即止的雷桂卿忍不住一连吃了三块。吃得一饱，正待告辞，悟心翩然而归，一见便有惊喜之色；等古应春引见了雷桂卿，少不得有一番客套。雷桂卿看她三十五、六年纪，丰神淡雅，但偶尔秋波一转，光如闪电，别有一股摄人的魔力，雷桂卿不由得心旌摇摇。及至悟心与古应春说话时，开出口来，让雷桂卿大感惊异，悟心竟是直呼其名∶『应春！』她问，『你不说二月里会来吗？何以迟到现在？』

Gu nodded and asked about recent happenings at the retreat. Soon A’wen brought refreshments. Snacks at such private retreats were valued for their ingredients rather than their appearance. The square date-paste cakes looked unremarkable, but one bite revealed an incomparable fragrance and sweetness. On his first visit, Lei had intended to do no more than taste one politely. Instead he found himself eating three in succession. Satisfied, they were preparing to take their leave when Wuxin returned with graceful steps, surprise and pleasure lighting her face at the sight of them. Gu introduced Lei, and the usual courtesies followed. She looked thirty-five or thirty-six, with a quiet elegance. But an occasional turn of her eyes flashed like lightning with a peculiar, compelling power, and Lei felt his heart stir. When she spoke to Gu, he was astonished: she addressed him directly by his given name. “Yingchun, did you not say you would come in the second month? Why have you left it until now?”

『原来是想给胡老太太拜寿以前，先来看看你，哪知道一到杭州就脱不了身。』『这话离奇。』悟心说道∶『胡老太太做生日，前后七天，我早就听说了。今天还在七天当中，你怎么倒脱身了呢？』『那是因为有点要紧事要办。』古应春问道∶『有个人，不知道听说过没有？赵宝禄。』『你跟我来打听他，不是问道于盲吗？』『听你这么说，我大概是打听对了。』古应春笑道∶『你们虽然道不同，不过都是名人，不应该不知道。』『我算什么「名人」？应春，你不要瞎说！让雷先生误会我这莲池精舍六根不净。』『不，不！』雷桂卿急忙分辩∶『哪里会误会。』『我是说笑话的，误会我也不怕。雷先生，你不必介意。』悟心转脸问道∶『应春，你打听赵宝禄为点啥？』『我也受人之托。为生意上的事。』

“I meant to see you before going to Old Madam Hu’s birthday celebration. But once I reached Hangzhou, I could not get away.” “That is a strange story,” Wuxin replied. “I heard long ago that her birthday festivities would last seven days. Those seven days are not over yet. How have you managed to get away now?” “There is some urgent business to attend to. I wonder whether you have heard of someone: Zhao Baolu.” “Asking me about him is like asking a blind person for directions.” “From the way you say that, I think I have come to the right person.” Gu smiled. “You may follow different paths, but you are both well-known figures. You can hardly be unaware of him.” “What sort of ‘well-known figure’ am I? Do not talk nonsense, Yingchun. You will make Mr Lei think this Lotus Pond Retreat is still full of worldly passions.” “No, no!” Lei hastily protested. “I could never misunderstand it so.” “I am joking. And I should not be afraid even if you did misunderstand. Do not trouble yourself.” Wuxin turned back to Gu. “Why are you inquiring about Zhao Baolu?” “Someone has entrusted me with a matter of business.”

古应春说∶『这话说来很长，你如果对此人熟悉，跟我谈谈他的为人。』『谈到他的为人，最好不要问我。』接着便向外喊道∶『小玉，小玉！』等把小玉唤了来，她说∶『你倒讲讲，你家婶娘信教的故事。』小玉一时楞住了，不知如何回答；古应春便提了一个头∶『我是想打听打听赵宝禄。』『喔，这个吃教的！「小玉鄙夷不屑地说∶』开口耶稣，闭口耶稣，骗杀人，不偿命。『』骗过你婶娘？『『是啊。说起来丢丑——』看小玉有不愿细谈的模样，古应春很知趣地说∶『丑事不必说了。小玉，我想问你，他是不是放定洋，买了好些丝？』『定洋是有，没有放下来。』『这话是怎么个说法？』『他说，上海洋行里托他买丝，价钱也不错，先付三成定洋，叫人家先打收条，第二天去收款子。』小玉愤愤地说∶『到第二天去了，他说要修教堂，劝人家奉献；软的硬的磨了半天，老实的认了；厉害的说∶没有定洋没有丝，到时候打官司好了。话是这么说，笔据在他手里，还不知道怎么样呢？』

“It is a long story,” Gu said. “If you know the man, tell me what he is like.” “On his character, you had better not ask me.” She called outside. “Xiaoyu! Xiaoyu!” When Xiaoyu came, she said, “Tell them about your aunt’s conversion.” Xiaoyu was taken aback and did not know how to begin. Gu supplied an opening. “I am trying to find out about Zhao Baolu.” “Oh, that man who makes a living out of religion!” Xiaoyu said contemptuously. “Jesus this, Jesus that—and he could swindle a person to death without answering for it.” “Did he cheat your aunt?” “Yes. It is shameful to speak of—” Seeing her reluctance to go into details, Gu tactfully said, “There is no need to tell an embarrassing story. What I want to ask is this: has he paid deposits on a large quantity of silk?” “There are deposits, but they were never paid out.” “What do you mean?” “He said a Shanghai foreign firm had commissioned him to buy silk. The price was good, with a thirty-percent deposit. He had people sign receipts first and told them to come for the money next day.” Xiaoyu spoke indignantly. “When they went, he said the church needed repairing and urged them to contribute. After endless coaxing and pressure, the meek ones gave in. The bolder ones said, ‘No deposit, no silk. Let there be a lawsuit when the time comes.’ But he holds the written receipts. Who knows what will happen?”

『那应该早跟他办交涉啊！夜长梦多，将来都是他的理了。』『古老爷，要伺候「蚕宝宝」啊。』其实，不必她说，古应春便已发觉，话问错了，环绕太湖的农家，三、四月间称为『蚕月』，家家红纸粘门，不相往来，而且有许多禁忌。因为养蚕是件极辛苦的事，一个照料不到，生了『蚕瘟』或者其他疾病，一年衣食就要落空了。所以明知该早办交涉，也只好暂且抛开。

“Then they should take it up with him at once. Delay only breeds trouble. Later he will have all the evidence on his side.” “Mr Gu, they have the silkworm babies to look after.” In fact, before she spoke, Gu had already realized that his question was ill judged. Around Lake Tai, the third and fourth months were known as the silkworm months. Every household pasted red paper on its doors, stopped visiting its neighbors, and observed numerous taboos. Raising silkworms was exhausting work. One lapse of attention could bring silkworm plague or another disease and destroy a family’s food and clothing for the whole year. Though they knew they ought to deal with the matter promptly, they could only put it aside for the time being.

『应春，』悟心问道∶『你问这件事，总有缘故吧？』『当然，我就是为此而来的，他受上海怡和洋行之托，在这里收丝；放出风声去，说到时候怕不能交丝，说不定有场官司好打，闹成「教案」。人家规规矩矩做生意的外国人，不喜欢闹教案，想把定洋收回，利息也不必算了。我就是代怡和来办这件事的。』『难！人家预备闹教案了，存心耍赖，恐怕你弄他不过。』『他不能不讲道理吧？』悟心沉吟了一回说道∶『你先去试试看，谈不拢再说。』看这情形，悟心似乎可以帮得上忙，古应春心便宽了∶向雷桂卿说∶『我们明天一早进城；谈得好最好，如果他不上路，我们回来再商量。』『好！』悟心接口∶『今天老佛婆不在庵里，明天我叫她好好弄几样素菜，请雷先生。』话虽如此，由小玉下厨整治的一顿素斋，亦颇精致入味；加以有自酿的百果酒，色香俱佳，雷桂卿陶然引杯，兴致极好。古应春怕他酒后失态，不让他多喝；匆匆吃完，告辞回船。

“Yingchun,” Wuxin asked, “you must have some reason for inquiring into this?” “Of course. It is why I have come. He has been commissioned by Jardine Matheson in Shanghai to buy silk here, but he is spreading word that delivery may not be possible and that there may be a lawsuit—perhaps even a dispute involving the church. Foreign merchants who do honest business do not want such a dispute. They would rather recover their deposits and waive the interest. I have come to deal with it on Jardine Matheson’s behalf.” “It will be difficult. If he is preparing to make it a religious dispute and means to evade his obligations, you may find him too much for you.” “Surely he must listen to reason?” Wuxin considered for a while. “Go and try first. If you cannot reach an agreement, we shall see.” She seemed to have some means of helping, and Gu felt easier. “We shall go into the city first thing tomorrow,” he told Lei. “If the discussion goes well, excellent. If he will not behave reasonably, we shall come back and talk it over.” “Good,” Wuxin said. “Old Buddha Granny is away today. Tomorrow I shall have her prepare some good vegetarian dishes for Mr Lei.” Even so, the vegetarian dinner Xiaoyu prepared was delicate and flavorful. There was also home-brewed mixed-fruit wine, excellent in color and bouquet. Lei drank with contented enjoyment and grew increasingly cheerful. Afraid he might lose his composure in drink, Gu prevented him from having too much, finished the meal quickly, and took his leave to return to the boat.

到了第二天清晨，正待解缆进城时，只见两乘小轿，在跳板前面停住，轿中出来两个白面书生，仔细看时，才知是悟心跟小玉。由于她们是易装来的，自以不公然招呼为宜，古应春只担心她们穿了内里塞满棉花的靴子，步履维艰，通过晃荡起伏的跳板会出事，所以亲自帮着船案，把住伸到岸上作为扶手之用的竹篙，同时不断警告∶『慢慢走，慢慢走，把稳了！』等她们师徒战战兢兢地上了船，迎入舱中，古应春方始问道∶『你们也要进城？』『对！』悟心流波四转，『这只船真漂亮，坐一回也是福气。小玉，你把纱窗帘拉起来。』船窗有两层窗帘，一层是白色带花纹的外国纱，一层是紫红丝绒，拉起纱帘，舱中仍很明亮，但岸上及别的船却看不清舱中的情形了。

Early next morning, just as they were about to cast off for the city, two small sedan chairs stopped before the gangplank. Two pale-faced young scholars stepped out. Only on looking closely did Gu recognize Wuxin and Xiaoyu. Since they had come in disguise, it was best not to greet them openly. Gu’s chief concern was that their boots, stuffed inside with cotton, made walking difficult. Crossing the swaying gangplank might be dangerous. He therefore helped the boatman steady the bamboo pole extended to the bank as a handrail, repeatedly cautioning them, “Slowly, slowly. Keep your footing!” Once teacher and disciple had come aboard with nervous steps and been shown into the cabin, Gu asked, “Are you going into the city too?” “Yes.” Wuxin’s eyes traveled around the cabin. “What a beautiful boat. It is a privilege to have a ride in it. Xiaoyu, draw the gauze curtains.” The windows had two layers of curtains, one of white patterned foreign gauze, the other of purple-red velvet. With the gauze drawn, the cabin remained bright, but people on the bank or in other boats could not see clearly inside.

于是悟心将那顶帽后缀着一条假辫子的青缎瓜皮帽摘了下来，头晃了两下，原来藏在帽中的长发便都披散下来；然后坐了下来，脱去靴子，轻轻捏着脚趾。这样的行径，不免予人以风流放诞的感觉。古应春不以为奇，而雷桂卿却是初见，心中不免兴起若干绮想。『你知道我进城去做什么？』悟心问说。『我也正要问你这话。』古应春答说∶『看你要到哪里，我叫船老大先送你。』『我哪里也不去，等下，我在船上等你们。』悟心答说∶『你们跟赵宝禄谈妥当了最好，不然，我替你们找个朋友。』原来是特为来帮忙的，雷桂卿愈发觉得悟心不同凡俗，不由得说道∶『悟心师太，你一个出家人，这样子热心，真是难得。』

Wuxin removed her blue satin skullcap, with its false queue attached at the back, and shook her head twice. The long hair concealed beneath it fell loose. Then she sat down, took off her boots, and gently massaged her toes. Her behavior had an air of free and unconventional charm. Gu found it unremarkable, but Lei, seeing it for the first time, could not help entertaining romantic thoughts. “Do you know why I am going into the city?” Wuxin asked. “I was just about to ask. Tell me where you want to go, and I shall have the boatman take you there first.” “I am not going anywhere. I shall wait for you aboard. If you settle things with Zhao Baolu, so much the better. If not, I shall find a friend to help you.” So she had come expressly to assist them. Lei admired her all the more as someone out of the ordinary. “Mistress Wuxin, such readiness to help from someone who has left the world is truly rare.”

『我也不算出家人，就算出了家，人情世故总还是一样的。』『是、是。』雷桂卿合十说道∶『我佛慈悲！』那样子有点滑稽，大家都笑了。说笑过了，古应春问道∶『你要替我找个怎么样的朋友？』『还不一定，看哪个朋友对你们有用，我就去找哪个。』此言一出，不但雷桂卿，连古应春亦不免惊奇，看来悟心交游广阔，而且神通广大，但这份关系是如何来的呢？雷桂卿心里也存着同样的疑问，只是不便出口；悟心却很大方，从他们脸上，看到他们心里，笑笑说道∶『你们一定在奇怪，我又不是湖州人，何以会认识各式各样的人？说穿了，不足为奇，我认识好些太太，都跟我很谈得来，连带也就认识她们的老爷了。』『喔，我倒想起来了。古应春问∶』昨天你就是到黄太太那里去了？『『是啊。』悟心答说∶『这黄老爷或许就能帮你的忙。这黄老爷是——』这黄老爷单然一个毅字，是个候补知县，派了在湖州收竹木税的差使。同治初年曾国藩派遣幼童赴美时，他是随行照料的庶务，在美国住过半年，亦算深通洋务，所以湖州府遇到有跟洋人打交道的事，不管知府还是知县都要找他；在湖州城里亦算是响当当的一个人物。

“I can hardly count myself as having left it. And even for those who have, human feelings and the ways of the world remain the same.” “Yes, yes.” Lei pressed his palms together. “The Buddha is merciful!” There was something comical in his manner, and everyone laughed. When the laughter subsided, Gu asked, “What sort of friend will you find for us?” “That depends. I shall find whichever of my friends can be useful to you.” This surprised even Gu, to say nothing of Lei. Wuxin seemed to know people everywhere and possess extraordinary influence. Where had all these connections come from? Lei wondered the same thing but could hardly ask. Wuxin was frank enough. Reading their thoughts in their faces, she smiled. “You must be wondering how someone who does not come from Huzhou knows so many different people here. There is nothing remarkable about it. I know a good many wives, and we get on very well. Through them, I have come to know their husbands.” “Oh, that reminds me,” Gu said. “Yesterday you went to Madam Huang’s, did you not?” “Yes. Her husband may be able to help you. Mr Huang is—” His given name was Yi. An expectant magistrate, he had been assigned to collect taxes on bamboo and timber in Huzhou. When Zeng Guofan sent boys to study in America early in the Tongzhi reign, Huang had accompanied them as the officer responsible for their practical arrangements. He had spent six months in America and was considered well versed in foreign affairs. Whenever dealings with foreigners arose in Huzhou, both the prefect and the county magistrate would turn to him. He was a figure of considerable consequence in the city.

『那太好了。』古应春很高兴地说∶『既然替湖州府帮忙办洋务，教会里的情形一定熟悉，赵宝禄不能不买他的帐。悟心，你这个忙帮得大了。』到了湖州城里，问清楚赵宝禄的教堂在何处，就在附近挑个清静之处泊舟。古应春与雷桂卿带着一个跟班上岸；悟心在船上等，她带来一个食盒，现成的素菜，在船上热一下便可食用，正整治好了尚未动箸，不道古应春一行已经回船了。『怎么这么快？』『事情很顺利。不过太顺利了。』『这是怎么说？』悟心又说∶『我总当你们办完事下馆子，我管我自己吃饭了，现在看样子，你们也还没有吃，要不要先将就将就？』『我们也还有点船菜，不必再上岸了。我要把经过情形告诉你，看有什么法子，不让赵宝禄耍花样。』原来古应春到得教堂，见到赵宝禄，道明来意，原以为他必有一番支吾，哪知他绝口否认有任何耍赖的企图。『做人要讲信用，对洋人尤其重要，我吃了多年的教，当然很明白这层道理。两位请放心，我收了怡和洋行的定洋，丝也定好了，到时候大家照约行家，决无差错。』『可是，』古应春探询似地说∶『听说赵先生跟教友之间，有些瓜葛？』

“Excellent!” Gu Yingchun said with pleasure. “If he helps the prefecture with foreign affairs, he must know the church’s circumstances well. Zhao Baolu will have to take him seriously. Wuxin, you have done us a great service.” On reaching Huzhou, they asked where Zhao’s church was and chose a quiet mooring nearby. Gu and Lei Guiqing went ashore with an attendant, while Wuxin waited aboard. She had brought a food box containing vegetarian dishes that needed only heating. Everything was ready, but she had not yet lifted her chopsticks when the men returned. “Back so soon?” “Things went smoothly. Too smoothly.” “What do you mean?” Wuxin added, “I assumed you would eat at a restaurant after finishing your business, so I was going to eat by myself. It seems you have not eaten either. Will you make do with this?” “We have some food aboard too. There is no need to go ashore again. I must tell you what happened and see whether you can think of a way to prevent Zhao Baolu from playing tricks.” At the church, Gu had found Zhao and explained his purpose. He had expected evasions, but Zhao flatly denied any intention of defaulting. “A man must keep his word, especially when dealing with foreigners. I have lived within the church for many years and naturally understand that. Rest assured, gentlemen. I received Jardine Matheson’s deposits, and the silk has been contracted for. When the time comes, everyone will fulfill his agreement. Nothing will go wrong.” “But,” Gu asked tentatively, “I hear there is some difficulty between you and your fellow Christians?”

『什么瓜葛？』赵宝禄不待古应春回答，自己又说∶『无非说我逼教友捐献。那要自愿，他不肯我不好抢他的；总而言之，到时候如果出了差错，两位再来问我，现在时候还早。』明知道他是敷衍，也明知他将来会耍赖，但却什么劲都用不上，真叫无可奈何。古应春从未遇到过这样的对手，所以神色之间，颇为沮丧。『你不要烦恼！』悟心劝慰着说∶『一定有办法，你先吃完了饭再说。』古应春胃口不开，但经不住悟心殷殷相劝，便拿茶泡了饭，就着悟心带来的麻辣油焖笋，匆匆吞了一碗；雷桂卿吃得也不多，两个都搁下筷子，看悟心捏着三镶乌木筷，慢慢在饭中拣稗子，拣好半天才吃一口。

“What difficulty?” Without waiting for an answer, Zhao went on, “Merely the claim that I force believers to make donations. Donations must be voluntary. If someone refuses, I cannot rob him. In short, if anything goes wrong when the time comes, come and question me then. It is too early now.” Gu knew he was being put off and was equally certain that Zhao would evade his obligations later. Yet there was nothing he could bring to bear on him. It was maddeningly helpless. Never having encountered such an opponent, Gu looked thoroughly discouraged. “Do not worry,” Wuxin comforted him. “There is bound to be a way. Finish your meal first.” Gu had no appetite, but could not resist her repeated urging. He poured tea over his rice and hurriedly swallowed a bowl with the spicy oil-braised bamboo shoots she had brought. Lei ate little too. Both laid down their chopsticks and watched Wuxin slowly pick barnyard-grass grains from her rice with ebony chopsticks bearing three inlays. Only after a long search would she take a mouthful.

『这米不好，是船老大在这里买的。』古应春歉意地说∶『早知道，自己带米来了』。悟心也省悟了，『对不起，对不起。』她说∶『我吃得慢，两位不必陪我，请宽坐用茶。』雷桂卿却舍不得走，尤其是悟心垂着眼皮注视碗中时，是个恣意贪看的好机会，所以接口说道∶『不要紧，不要紧，你尽管慢用。』悟心嫣然一笑，对她的饭不再多挑剔，吃得就快了。

“The rice is poor,” Gu said apologetically. “The boatman bought it here. Had I known, I should have brought some.” Wuxin realized that she was keeping them waiting. “Forgive me. I eat slowly. Please do not feel obliged to sit with me. Make yourselves comfortable and have some tea.” Lei was reluctant to leave. With her eyes lowered over her bowl, he could gaze at her to his heart’s content. “Not at all, not at all,” he said. “Take your time.” Wuxin gave a lovely smile and ceased to be so particular about the rice. Her meal went more quickly.

等小玉来收拾了桌子，水也开了。沏上一壶茶来，扑鼻一股杏子香，雷桂卿少不得又要动问了。『那没有什么诀窍。』悟心答说∶『挑没有熟的杏子，摘下来拿皮纸包好，放在茶叶罐里，隔两天便有香味了。不但杏子，别的果子，也可以如法炮制。』『悟心师太，』雷桂卿笑道∶『你真会享清福。』悟心笑笑不作声，转脸问古应春∶『你的心事想得怎么样了？』古应春确是在想心事，他带着藩司衙门的公文，可以去看湖州知府，请求协助；但如传了赵宝禄到案，他仍旧是这套说法，那就不但于事无补，而且还落一个仗势欺人的名声，太划不来了。

By the time Xiaoyu had cleared the table, the water was boiling. The tea she brewed gave off a fragrance of apricots, prompting another question from Lei. “There is no great secret,” Wuxin answered. “Pick unripe apricots, wrap them in bark paper, and put them in the tea canister. After two days the tea will be fragrant. Other fruit can be used in the same way.” “Mistress Wuxin,” Lei said, laughing, “you certainly know how to enjoy a peaceful life.” She smiled without answering, then turned to Gu. “Have you made any progress with what is troubling you?” He had indeed been thinking. With the provincial treasurer’s dispatch, he could call on the Huzhou prefect and ask for assistance. But if Zhao were summoned and repeated the same arguments, nothing would be gained. Worse, Gu would acquire a reputation for using official influence to bully others. It would scarcely be worth it.

等他说了心事，悟心把脸又转了过去∶『雷先生，要托你办件事。』『是、是。』雷桂卿一叠连声地答应，『你说，你说。』『我写个地址，请你去找一位杨师爷；见了面，说我请他来一趟，有事求他。』悟心又加一句∶『他是乌程县的刑名师爷。』做州县官，至少要请两个幕友，一个管刑名、一个管钱谷，权柄极大。请乌程县的刑名师爷来料理此案，不怕赵宝禄不就范。雷桂卿很高兴地说∶『悟心师太，你真有办法！把这位杨师爷请了来对付赵宝禄，比什么都管用。』『也不见得，等请来了再商量。』于是悟心口述地址，请古应春写了下来，船老大上岸雇来一顶轿子，将欣欣然的雷桂卿抬走了。

When he had explained, Wuxin turned back to Lei. “Mr Lei, I must ask you to do something.” “Yes, yes,” he answered eagerly. “Tell me.” “I shall give you an address. Go and find a certain Secretary Yang. Tell him I should like him to come here; there is something I must ask of him.” She added, “He advises the Wucheng magistrate on criminal and legal affairs.” A prefect or magistrate needed at least two private secretaries: one for criminal and legal matters, the other for revenue and grain. Their influence was considerable. With Wucheng’s legal secretary brought in to manage the affair, Zhao could surely be made to comply. Lei was delighted. “Mistress Wuxin, you truly know how to get things done! Bringing Secretary Yang to deal with Zhao will be more effective than anything.” “Not necessarily. Let us discuss it once he is here.” She dictated the address and had Gu write it down. The boatman hired a sedan ashore, and Lei was carried off in high spirits.

『你要不要去睡个午觉？』悟心说道∶『雷先生要好半天才会回来。』『怎么？那杨师爷住得很远，是不是？』『不但住得远，而且要去两个地方。』『为什么？』悟心诡秘地一笑说道∶『这位雷先生，心思有点歪，我要他吃点小苦头。』『什么苦头？』古应春有点不安，『是我的朋友，弄得他惨兮兮，他会骂我。』『他根本不会晓得，是我故意罚他。』原来这杨师爷住在县衙门，但另外租了一处房子，作为私下接头讼事之用，为了避人耳目，房子租在很荒僻的地方，又因为荒僻之故，养了一条很凶的狗。雷桂卿找上门去，一定会扑空，而且会受惊。

“Would you like an afternoon nap?” Wuxin asked. “Mr Lei will not be back for some time.” “Why? Does Secretary Yang live far away?” “Not only far away. He will have to go to two places.” “Why?” Wuxin gave a mysterious smile. “This Mr Lei’s thoughts stray a little. I mean to make him suffer a small penalty.” “What penalty?” Gu was uneasy. “He is my friend. If you make him miserable, he will blame me.” “He will never know I am punishing him deliberately.” Secretary Yang lived at the county yamen, but had rented another house for private consultations about lawsuits. To avoid attention, he had chosen a very secluded place. Its isolation in turn made it necessary to keep a fierce dog. Lei would certainly fail to find him there and would get a fright into the bargain.

『怎么会扑空呢？悟心解释∶』除非杨师爷自己关照，约在哪里见面，不然他就是在那里，下人也会说不在，有事到衙门去接头。『』怎么会倒在其次，让狗咬了怎么办？『『不会！那条狗是教好了的，来势汹汹把人吓走了就好了，从不咬人。』听这一说，古应春才放下心来；他知道悟心有午睡的习惯，便即说道∶『我倒不困，你去打个中觉。』『好！』悟心问说∶『哪张是你的铺？』『跟我来。』

“Why will he fail to find him?” “Unless Secretary Yang himself has arranged where to meet,” Wuxin explained, “even if he is there, the servants will say he is out and direct callers to the yamen.” “Missing him is a lesser matter. What if the dog bites Lei?” “It will not. The dog is trained. It rushes at people fiercely and frightens them away, but never bites.” Only then was Gu reassured. Knowing that Wuxin habitually slept after lunch, he said, “I am not sleepy. You should take your nap.” “Very well. Which berth is yours?” “Come with me.”

后舱一张大铺，中间用红木隔成两个铺位，上铺洋式床垫，软硬适度，悟心用手揿一揿床垫，又看一看周围的陈设，不由得赞叹∶『财神家的东西，到底不同。』『这面是我的铺。』古应春指着左面说∶『你睡吧，我在外面。有事拉这根绳子。』悟羽将一根红弦绳一拉，前舱的银铃琅琅作响；小玉恰好进前舱，闻声寻来，一看亦有惊异之色。『真讲究！』小玉抚摸着红木～＊子说∶『是可以移动的。』『索性把它推了过去。』古应春说∶『一人个睡也宽敞些。』小玉便依言将红木～＊子推到一边。古应春也退了出去，在中舱喝茶闲眺，心里在盘算，杨师爷来了，如果谈得顺利，还来得及回庵；倘或需要从长计议，是回庵去谈呢？还是一直谈下去，夜深了上岸觅客栈投宿，让悟心师徒住在船上。转念未定，听得帘挂钩响动，是小玉出来了，『古老爷，』她说，『你请进去吧，我师父有事情商量。』到得后舱，只见悟心在他的铺位上和衣侧卧，身上半盖着一条绣花丝被，长发纷披，遮盖了大半个枕头；一手支颐，袖子褪落到肘弯，奇南香手串的香味，俞发馥郁了。『你有事？』古应春在这一面铺前的一张红木骨牌凳上坐了下来。

A broad sleeping platform in the aft cabin was divided into two berths by a rosewood partition. Western mattresses, neither too soft nor too hard, covered it. Wuxin pressed one with her hand and surveyed the furnishings. “Things belonging to the God of Wealth’s household really are different!” she exclaimed. “Mine is this side.” Gu pointed to the left. “Lie down. I shall be outside. Pull this cord if you need anything.” Wuxin tugged a red cord, and a silver bell rang in the forward cabin. Xiaoyu, who had just entered it, followed the sound and looked equally impressed. “How beautifully arranged!” She ran a hand over the rosewood partition. “It can be moved.” “Push it aside altogether,” Gu said. “There will be more room for one person to sleep.” Xiaoyu did so, and Gu withdrew to the middle cabin to drink tea and look out at the scenery. He considered their plans. If the discussion with Secretary Yang went well, there would still be time to return to the retreat. If more thought was needed, should they go back there to continue it, or talk on aboard, however late it grew? They could then find an inn ashore and leave the boat to Wuxin and Xiaoyu. Before he decided, he heard the curtain rings move. Xiaoyu emerged. “Mr Gu, please come in. Teacher wishes to discuss something.” In the aft cabin, Wuxin lay on her side in his berth, fully clothed, with an embroidered silk quilt half covering her. Her long hair spread over most of the pillow. One hand supported her chin; the sleeve had slipped down to her elbow, and the fragrance of her agarwood prayer beads seemed richer than ever. “What is it?” Gu sat on a rosewood stool beside the other berth.

『杨师爷很晚才回来。』悟心说道∶『恐怕要留他吃饭，似乎要预备预备。』『菜倒是有。』古应春说，『船家一早就上岸去买了菜，只以为中午是在城里吃了，你又带了素菜来，所以没有弄出来。你闻！』悟心闻到了，是火腿炖鸡的香味，『你引我动凡心了。』她笑着又说∶『酒呢？』『那更是现成，一坛花雕是上船以后才开的。我还有白葡萄酒，你也可以喝。』古应春又说∶『倒是有件事得早早预备，今晚上你跟小玉睡在船上，我跟雷桂卿住客栈，得早一点去定妥当了它。』『不！』悟心说道∶『睡在船上不妥当了，我还是回庵；不过船家多吃一趟辛苦。』『那没有什么。好了，说妥当了，你睡吧！』

“Secretary Yang will not be back until quite late,” Wuxin said. “We may have to ask him to dinner. Perhaps something should be prepared.” “There is food. The boatman went ashore to buy it early this morning. We thought we should lunch in the city, and you brought vegetarian dishes as well, so it was not served. Smell that!” She caught the aroma of chicken stewing with ham. “You are tempting me back to worldly desires,” she said with a laugh. “And wine?” “Ready too. We opened a jar of huadiao after coming aboard. I have white wine as well, which you can drink. There is one thing we ought to arrange early: you and Xiaoyu can sleep aboard tonight while Lei and I stay at an inn. I had better reserve the rooms.” “No. It would not be proper for me to sleep here. I shall return to the retreat, even if it means another journey for the boatmen.” “That is no trouble. Then it is settled. Sleep now.”

『我还不困，陪我谈谈。』说着，悟心拍拍空铺位，示意他睡下来。古应春有些踌躇，但终于决定考验自己的定力，在雷桂卿的铺位横倒，脸对脸不到一尺的距离。『古太太的病怎么样？好点了没有？』『还是那样子。总归是带病延年了。』『那末，你呢？』悟心幽幽地说∶『没有一个人在身边，也不方便。』古应春想把瑞香的事告诉她，转念一想，这一来悟心一定寻根究底，追问不休，不如不提为妙。

“I am not sleepy yet. Stay and talk.” Wuxin patted the empty berth, inviting him to lie down. Gu hesitated, then decided to test his self-command. He stretched out in Lei’s berth, their faces less than a chi apart. “How is Mrs Gu? Is she any better?” “Much the same. She can only nurse her illness and hope to live on.” “And you?” Wuxin asked softly. “It must be inconvenient, with no one beside you.” Gu thought of telling her about Ruixiang. But she would be sure to pursue every detail and question him endlessly. Better not mention it.

『也没有什么不便。多一事不如少一事。』『什么事都好省，这件事省不得，除非——』悟心忽然笑了起来。这一笑实在诡秘，古应春忍不住问∶『话说半句，无缘无故发笑，是什么花样？除非什么？』『除非你也看破红尘，出家当了和尚，那件事才可以省，不然是省不了的。』『这话也没有啥好笑啊！』『我笑是笑我自己。』

“It is no great inconvenience. One less complication is better than one more.” “You may dispense with anything else, but not this—unless—” Wuxin suddenly laughed. There was something so mysterious in the laugh that Gu could not help asking, “You leave your sentence unfinished and laugh for no reason. What game is this? Unless what?” “Unless you too renounce the world and become a monk. Only then can you dispense with that need. Otherwise you cannot.” “There is nothing so funny about that.” “I am laughing at myself.”

『在谈我，何以忽然笑你自己。』古应春口滑，想不说的话。还是说了∶『总与我有关吧？』『不错，与你有关。我在想，你如果出家做了和尚，不晓得是怎么个样子？想想就好笑了。』『我要出家，也做头陀，同你一样。』『啥叫头陀？』『亏你还算出家，连头陀都不懂。』古应春答说∶『出家而没有剃发，带发修行的叫做头陀；岂不是跟你一样。』『喔，我懂了，就是满头乱七八糟的头发，弄个铜环，把它箍住，象武松的那种打扮？』『就是。』『那叫「行者」！不叫头陀，我那里有本《释氏要贤》说得清清楚楚。』原来她是懂的，有意相谑，这正是悟心的本性；古应春苦笑着叹了口气，无话可说。

“We are discussing me. Why suddenly laugh at yourself?” Gu’s tongue slipped into the very question he had meant not to ask. “It must have something to do with me.” “It does. I was imagining what you would look like as a monk. The thought made me laugh.” “If I renounced the world, I would become a toutuo—like you.” “What is a toutuo?” “And you call yourself a religious person! You do not even know what a toutuo is. Someone who renounces the world without shaving his head and practices with his hair intact is called a toutuo. Is that not just like you?” “Oh, I see. A head of unruly hair held in place with a copper band, dressed like Wu Song?” “Exactly.” “That is called a xingzhe, not a toutuo! My copy of Shishi Yaoxian makes it perfectly clear.” So she had known all along and was deliberately teasing him. It was typical of Wuxin. Gu gave a rueful smile and sighed, with nothing to say.

『应春，我们真希望你是出家的行者。』『为什么？』『那一来，你不是一个人了吗？』古应春心一跳，故意问说∶『一个人又怎么样了呢？』『你不懂？』『我真不懂。』『不跟你说了。』悟心突然一翻身，背对古应春。古应春心想，这就是考验自己定力的时候了，心猿意马地几次想伸手去扳她的身子，却始终迟疑不定。终于忍不住要伸手了，而且手已快碰到悟心的身子了，突然听得扑通一声，是重物落水的声音，古应春一惊缩手，随即听见有人大喊∶『有人掉到河里去了！』

“Yingchun, I really wish you were a xingzhe who had renounced the world.” “Why?” “Then you would be alone, would you not?” Gu’s heart gave a leap, but he deliberately asked, “And what if I were?” “Do you not understand?” “I truly do not.” “Then I shall say no more.” Wuxin suddenly turned her back on him. This, Gu thought, was the moment to test his resolve. His thoughts raced. Several times he wanted to reach out and turn her toward him, but hesitation held him back. At last he could resist no longer. His hand was almost touching her when a sudden splash—the sound of something heavy falling into water—made him start and draw back. Someone shouted, “A person has fallen into the river!”

悟心也吓得坐了起来，推着古应春说∶『你去看看。』等他出去一看，失足落水的一个半大孩子，已经被救了起来。是一场虚惊。回到后舱，略说经过，只见悟心眼神湛然，脸色恬静，从容说道∶『刚才「扑通」那一声，好比当头棒喝。』绮念全消的古应春，亦有这样的感觉，不过当悟心『面壁』而卧时，居然亦跟他一样意马心猿，却使他感到意外。『我在想一个人能不做坏事，也要看看运气。』悟心一翻身拉开丝绒窗帘，指着透过纱窗，影绰绰看得到的一座贞节牌坊说∶『我不相信守寡守了几十年的人，真正是自始至终，冰清玉洁，没有动过不正经的念头，不过没有机会，或者临时有什么意外，打断了「好事」而已。如果因为这样子，自己就以为怎样了不起，依我说，是问心有愧的。』这番话说得古应春自惭不如，笑笑说道∶『你睡吧！我不陪你「参禅」了。』雷桂卿直到黄错日落，方始回船，样子显得有些狼狈，一双靴子溅了许多烂泥。古应春心知其故，也有些好笑，但不敢现于形色，只是慰劳地说∶『辛苦，辛苦。』『还好，还好！』雷桂卿举起脚说∶『路好难走，下了轿，过一顶独木桥，又是一段烂泥路，好不容易找到那里，说杨师爷在县衙门。』『那么，你又到县衙门？』

Wuxin sat up in alarm and pushed him. “Go and see.” By the time he went out, the half-grown boy who had lost his footing was already rescued. It had been no more than a fright. Gu returned to the aft cabin and briefly explained. Wuxin’s eyes were clear, her expression tranquil. “That splash just now was like a blow and a shout awakening a deluded mind,” she said calmly. Gu, whose amorous thoughts had vanished, felt the same. What surprised him was that even while lying with her back to him, she too had been as agitated by desire as he. “I was thinking that whether a person avoids doing wrong depends partly on luck.” Wuxin turned and pulled back the velvet curtain, pointing to a chastity arch dimly visible through the gauze. “I do not believe a woman who has remained a widow for decades has truly been pure as ice and jade from beginning to end, never once entertaining an improper thought. She may simply have had no opportunity, or some accident at the last moment may have interrupted her ‘happy encounter.’ If she then thinks herself wonderfully virtuous on that account, I say she has something to answer for in her own conscience.” Gu felt that he fell short of her candor. Smiling, he said, “Sleep now. I shall not stay to meditate with you.” Lei returned only at sunset, looking somewhat disheveled, his boots splashed with mud. Gu knew why and was amused, but dared not show it. “You have had a tiring errand.” “Not so bad.” Lei lifted a foot. “What a dreadful road! After leaving the sedan I crossed a single-plank bridge, then trudged through mud. When I finally found the place, they said Secretary Yang was at the county yamen.” “So you went there as well?”

『当然。』雷桂卿说，『还好，这一回没有扑空。人倒很客气，问我悟心是不是有什么事找他？我说∶请你来了就知道了。他说还有件公事，料理完了就来。大概也快到了。』正在谈着，悟心翩然出现，脸上刚睡醒的红晕犹在，星眼微饧，别具一种媚态。雷桂卿一看，神情又不同了。『交差，交差。他很起劲地，但却有些埋怨地∶』悟心师太，你应该早告诉我，杨家有条大狗——『』怎么？『悟心装得吃惊是，』你让狗咬了？『』咬倒没有咬，不过性命吓掉半条。『雷桂卿面有余悸，指手划脚地说∶『我正在叫门，忽然发现后面好象有两只手搭在我肩膀上，回头一看，乖乖，好大一条狗，拖长了舌头，朝我喘气。这一吓，真正魂灵要出窍了。』『唷，唷，对不起，对不起！』悟心满脸歉意，『我是晓得他家有条狗，不晓得这么厉害。后来呢？』『后来赶出来一个人，不住口跟我道歉，问我吓到了没有？我只好装「大好佬」，我说∶没有什么，我从前养过一条狗，比你们的狗还大。』『好！』古应春大笑，『这牛吹得好。』悟心也笑得伏在桌上，抬不起头来；雷桂卿颇为得意，觉得受一场虚惊，能替他们带来一场欢乐，也还值得。『你看！』他指着远远而来的一顶轿子，『大概杨师爷来了。』

“Of course. Fortunately, I did not miss him this time. He was very courteous and asked whether Mistress Wuxin wanted him for something. I told him he would find out when he came. He said he had an official matter to finish first. He should be here soon.” As they talked, Wuxin appeared, her face still flushed from sleep, her eyes slightly drowsy, with a charm all their own. At the sight, Lei’s expression changed again. “I have carried out my commission!” he said eagerly, though with a trace of complaint. “Mistress Wuxin, you ought to have told me there was a great dog at the Yang house.” “What?” She feigned alarm. “Did it bite you?” “No, but it frightened half the life out of me.” Still shaken by the memory, Lei gestured as he told them. “I was calling at the door when I suddenly felt what seemed like two hands on my shoulders from behind. I turned—and heavens, what a dog! Its tongue was hanging out, and it was panting in my face. My soul nearly left my body.” “Oh, I am so sorry!” Wuxin looked thoroughly apologetic. “I knew they had a dog but not that it was so fierce. What happened then?” “A man came running out, apologizing over and over and asking whether I had been frightened. I had to put on a brave face. I said, ‘Not at all. I once kept a dog even bigger than yours.’” Gu burst out laughing. “Good! That was a fine boast!” Wuxin laughed too, until she bent over the table and could not lift her head. Lei was rather pleased. A fright was worth enduring if it brought them such merriment. “Look,” he said, pointing to a sedan approaching in the distance. “That must be Secretary Yang.”

果然，轿子停了下来，一个跟班正在打听时，雷桂卿出舱走到船头上去答话。『是不是杨师爷？』于是杨师爷下轿，古应春亦到船头上去迎接，进入舱内，由悟心正式引见。那师爷是绍兴人，年纪不大，只有三十四、五岁，不过绍兴师爷一向古貌古心，显得很老成的样子，所以骤看竟似半百老翁了。彼此请教名字，那杨师爷号叫莲坡，古应春便以『莲翁』相称，寒暄了一会，悟心说道∶『你们喝酒吧！一面喝，一面谈。』于是摆设杯盘，请杨莲坡上坐；悟心不上桌，坐在一旁相陪。

Sure enough, the sedan stopped. While an attendant made inquiries, Lei came out of the cabin and went forward to answer. “Is that Secretary Yang?” Yang alighted, and Gu also went to the bow to welcome him. Once they were inside, Wuxin made the formal introductions. The secretary came from Shaoxing and was only thirty-four or thirty-five. But Shaoxing legal advisers cultivated an old-fashioned appearance and manner that made them seem exceptionally mature; at first glance he looked almost fifty. They exchanged names. Yang’s literary name was Lianpo, so Gu addressed him as “Mr Lian.” After a little polite conversation, Wuxin said, “Have some wine. You can talk as you drink.” Cups and dishes were set out, and Yang Lianpo was invited to take the place of honor. Wuxin did not join the table but sat to one side to keep them company.

话题当然也要她开头，『老杨！』她说，『雷老爷我是初识；应春是多年的熟人，他有事请你帮忙。他的事就是我的事。』『我晓得。』杨莲坡答说∶『四海之内皆兄弟，你就不说，我也要尽心尽力，交个朋友。』『多谢、多谢！』古应春敬了一杯酒，细谈此行的来意，以及跟赵宝禄见面的经过。杨莲坡喝着酒，静静听完全，开口问道∶『应翁现在打算怎么办？』『这要问你啊！』悟心在一旁插嘴，『人家无非要有个着落。』『所谓着落有两种，一是将来要他依约行事，一是现在就有个了断。不知道应翁要哪一样？』

Naturally, she had to open the subject. “Yang, I have only just met Mr Lei, but I have known Yingchun for years. He needs your help. His business is my business.” “I understand,” Yang replied. “All within the four seas are brothers. Even without your asking, I would do my best and make a friend.” “Thank you, thank you!” Gu offered him a cup of wine, then described the purpose of his journey and his meeting with Zhao Baolu. Yang drank and listened quietly to the whole account before asking, “Mr Yingchun, what do you propose to do now?” “That is what we are asking you,” Wuxin put in. “He simply wants a dependable outcome.” “There are two kinds of outcome: having Zhao fulfill the agreement later, or settling the matter now. Which does Mr Yingchun want?”

『这个人很难弄，将来一定会有麻烦，不如现在就来个了断。』古应春说，『此刻要他退钱，不知道办得到，办不到？』『不怕讨债的凶，只怕欠债的穷。如果他钱已经用掉了，想退也没法子。』这是实话，不过古应春亦并不是要赵宝禄即时退钱不可，怡和洋行那方面，只要将与赵宝禄所订的契约转过来，胡雪岩已承诺先如数退款，但将来要有保障，赵宝禄有丝交丝，无丝退还定洋。只是要如何才有保障，他就不知道了。『最麻烦的是，他手里有好些做丝人家写给他的收据，一个说付过钱了，一个说没有收到，打起官司来，似乎对赵宝禄有利。』『不然。』杨师爷说∶『打官司一个对一个，当然重在证据，就是上了当，也只好怪自己不好。如果赵宝禄成了众矢之的，众口一词说他骗人，那时候情形就不同了。不过上当的人，官司要早打，现在就要递状子进来。』『你也是。』悟心插嘴说道∶『这是啥辰光，家家户户都在服侍蚕宝宝！哪里来的工夫打官司？』

“He is a difficult man, and trouble is certain to arise later. Better to settle things now,” Gu said. “Could he be made to return the money at once?” “A fierce creditor is less to be feared than an impoverished debtor. If he has spent the money, he cannot return it even if he wishes.” This was true. Gu, however, did not insist that Zhao repay immediately. As far as Jardine Matheson was concerned, Hu had already promised to refund the full sum in advance once its contract with Zhao was transferred to him. But there had to be security for the future: Zhao must deliver silk if he had it, or return the deposit if he did not. How to secure that guarantee, Gu did not know. “The greatest difficulty is that he holds receipts from a number of silk-producing families. He says he has paid; they say they received nothing. If it comes to a lawsuit, the position seems to favor him.” “Not necessarily,” Yang replied. “In a case between two individuals, the evidence naturally carries great weight. Even if one party has been cheated, he may have only himself to blame. But if Zhao becomes the target of everyone’s complaints, and they all say with one voice that he deceived them, the situation changes. The victims must bring their case early, however. Their petitions should be filed now.” “You too!” Wuxin broke in. “What season do you think this is? Every household is tending its silkworm babies. Where are they to find time for a lawsuit?”

杨师爷沉吟了一回说道∶『办法是有，不过要按部就班，一步一步都要走到。赵宝禄有没有「牙帖」？』交易的介绍人，古称『驵侩』，后汉与四夷通商，在边境设立『互市』；到唐朝，『互市』扩大，且由边境延伸到长安，特设『互市监』，掌理其事，『互市』中有些『互郎』，即是『驵侩』，互市之物，孰贵孰贱，孰重孰轻，只凭他一句话，因而得以操纵其间，是个很容易发财的行业，不过第一、须通番语；第二、要跟互市监拉得上关系。所以胡人当互郎的很多，如安禄山就是。不过胡人写汉字，笔划不真切，互字不知如何写成『牙』字，以论传论，称为『牙郎』；后世简称为『牙』，一个字叫起来不便，就加一个字，名之为『牙行』。『牙行』是没本钱生意，黑道中人手里握一杆秤，在他的地盘上强买强卖，两面抽佣，甚至于右手买进、左手卖出，大『戴帽子』。所以有句南北通行的谚语∶『车船店脚牙，无罪也该杀』，车案、船老大、店小二、脚案，无非欺侮过往的陌生旅客；只有牙行欺侮的不是旅客而是本地人。

Secretary Yang considered for a while. “There is a way, but we must proceed in order and omit none of the steps. Does Zhao Baolu have a broker’s license?” Intermediaries in trade had anciently been called zangkui. When the Later Han traded with the peoples beyond its frontiers, mutual markets were established along the borders. Under the Tang, these expanded and extended as far as Chang’an, with special market supervisors to administer them. Some intermediaries were called hulang. Their word determined which goods were dear or cheap, weighty or insignificant, allowing them to manipulate transactions. It was an easy occupation in which to grow rich, but it required both a command of foreign languages and connections with the market supervisors. Many foreigners therefore became hulang; An Lushan was one. Their Chinese handwriting, however, was imprecise, and somehow the character hu came to be written as the similar-looking ya. The error spread, and the intermediaries became known as yalang, later shortened to ya. Since a single syllable was awkward to use, another character was added, giving yahang, or brokerage. It was a business that required no capital. A man from the underworld could seize a pair of scales and force people within his territory to buy and sell through him, taking commission from both sides. He might even buy with one hand and sell with the other, piling on an enormous markup. Hence a proverb common to north and south: “Carters, boatmen, inn servants, porters, and brokers deserve death even without a crime.” The first four merely preyed on strangers passing through; brokers preyed on the local people themselves.
