当然也有适应需要，为买卖双方促成交易、收取定额佣金的正式牙行，那要官府立案，取得户部或者本省藩司衙门所发的执照，称为『牙帖』，方能从事这个行当。赵宝禄不过凭借教会势力，私下在做牙行，古应春推测他是不可能领有牙帖的。『我想他大概也不会有。』杨师爷说∶『怡和洋行想要有保障，要写个禀帖来。县衙门把赵宝禄传来，问他有没有这回事？他说「有」；好，叫他象牙帖出来看看。没有牙帖，先就罚他。』『罚过以后呢？』『要他具结，将来照约行事。』杨师爷说∶『这是怡和跟他的事，将来要打官司，怡和一定赢。』『赢是赢了，就是留下刚才所说的，不怕讨债的的凶，只怕欠债的穷，他如果既交不出丝，又还不出定洋，莫非封他的教堂？』

There were, of course, legitimate brokers who met a real need, bringing buyers and sellers together for a fixed commission. They had to register with the authorities and obtain a license from the Ministry of Revenue or the provincial treasurer before entering the trade. Zhao, however, merely relied on the church’s influence to act as a broker privately. Gu supposed it unlikely that he held a license. “I suspect he has none either,” Yang said. “If Jardine Matheson wants security, it must submit a petition. The county yamen can summon Zhao and ask whether the matter is as stated. If he says yes, very well: have him produce his broker’s license. If he has none, begin by fining him.” “And after the fine?” “Make him give a written undertaking to fulfill the contract. That concerns him and Jardine Matheson. If a lawsuit follows, the firm is certain to win.” “It may win, but we still face what you said just now: the difficulty is not a fierce creditor but a poor debtor. If he can neither deliver silk nor repay the deposit, can we seal up his church?”

『虽不能封他的教堂，可以要他交保。那时如果受骗上当的人，进状子告他，就可以办他个「诈伪取财」的罪名。』杨师爷又说∶『总而言之，办法有的是。不过「凡事豫则立」；刑名上有所谓「抢原告」，就是要抢先一步，防患未然。你老兄照我的话去做，先叫怡和洋行写禀帖来，这是最要紧的一着。』『是，是！多承指点，以后还要请多帮忙。』正事谈得告一段落，酒也差不多了。杨师爷知道悟心还要赶回庵去，所以不耽误她的工夫，吃完饭立即告辞；古应春包了个大红包犒赏他的仆从，看着杨师爷上了轿，吩咐解缆回南浔。

“We cannot seal up the church, but we can require him to provide surety. Then, if the people he has deceived file complaints, he can be prosecuted for obtaining money by fraud.” Yang continued, “In short, there are plenty of ways. But preparation is the foundation of success. In legal practice there is what we call ‘seizing the plaintiff’s position’: act first and forestall trouble. Follow my advice and have Jardine Matheson write its petition. That is the essential first move.” “Yes, yes. I am grateful for your guidance and shall need your help again.” The business discussion had reached a stopping point, and they had drunk enough. Knowing that Wuxin still had to return to the retreat, Yang did not detain her. He took his leave immediately after dinner. Gu prepared a generous gratuity envelope for his attendants, watched him into his sedan, and ordered the boat to cast off for Nanxun.

归寝已是三更时分，雷桂卿头一着枕，突然猛吸鼻子，发出『嗤，嗤』的响声，古应春不由得诧异。『怎么？』他问∶『有什么不对？』我枕头上有气味。『『气味？』古应春更觉不解，『什么气味？』『是香气。』雷桂卿说，『好象悟心头发上的香气。你没有闻见？』『我的鼻子没有你灵。』古应春心想，这件事实在奇怪，悟心并没有用他的枕头，何以会沾染香味？这样想着，不免侧脸去看，一看看出蹊跷来了。雷桂卿的枕头上，有一根长长的青丝，可以断定是悟心的头发，然则她真的用过雷桂卿的枕头？『不对！』雷桂卿突然又喊∶『这不是我的枕头，是你的。』他仰起身子说∶『我记得很清楚，这对鸳鸯枕，你绣的花样的鸳，我的是鸯，现在换过了。』古应春恍然大悟，点点头说∶『不错，换过了。你知道不知道，是哪个换的？』

It was the third watch when they went to bed. The moment his head touched the pillow, Lei began sniffing noisily. Gu was puzzled. “What is it? Is something wrong?” “There is a smell on my pillow.” “A smell? What kind?” “A fragrance. It seems like the scent of Wuxin’s hair. Did you not notice it?” “My nose is not as keen as yours.” Gu thought this very strange. Wuxin had not used Lei’s pillow. How could her fragrance have reached it? Turning to look, he discovered something curious: a long black hair lay on the pillow, unquestionably hers. Had she really used it after all? “No!” Lei suddenly exclaimed. “This is not my pillow. It is yours.” He raised himself. “I remember perfectly. Of these two mandarin-duck pillows, yours had the male embroidered on it and mine the female. They have been exchanged.” Gu understood at once and nodded. “Yes, they have. Do you know who exchanged them?”

『莫非是悟心？』『不错，一定是她。她有打中觉的习惯；原来睡的是我的枕头，现在换到你那里了。』『这——』雷桂卿惊喜交集地，『这，这是啥意思？』说着将脸伏下去，细嗅枕上的香气。古应春本来不想『杀风景』，见此光景不能不扫他的兴了，『「贾氏窥帘韩掾少，宓妃留枕魏王才」，桂卿，』他说∶『你要想一想，两样资格，你有一样没有？』

“Could it have been Wuxin?” “Certainly. She always naps after lunch. She originally used my pillow, and now it has been put on your side.” “Then—” Lei was astonished and delighted. “What does it mean?” He buried his face in the pillow to inhale its fragrance. Gu had not intended to spoil his pleasure, but at this sight felt obliged to do so. “‘Lady Jia peered through the curtain at young Han the clerk; / The Goddess of the Luo left her pillow for the gifted Prince of Wei.’ Guiqing, consider: do you possess either of those two qualifications?”

『我不懂你的意思。』古应春的意思是说，除非雷桂卿觉得在年轻英俊，或者博学多才这两个条件占有一个，就难望获得悟心的青睐。而悟心一向好恶作剧，他去请杨师爷所吃的苦头，就是悟心对他的轻佻所予的惩罚。如今将留有香泽的枕头换给他，是一个陷阱，也是一种考验；雷桂卿倘或再动绮念，后面就还有苦头吃。雷桂卿倒抽一口冷气，对悟心的感觉当然受过了；不过那只是片刻之间的事，古应春所说的话，到底不及他脑中『美目盼兮，巧笑倩兮』的印象来得深刻，所以仍为枕上那种非兰非麝、似有似无的香味，搅得大半夜六神不安。

“I do not understand.” Gu meant that unless Lei could claim either handsome youth or exceptional learning and talent, he could hardly expect to win Wuxin’s favor. She had always enjoyed practical jokes. The ordeal he had endured in fetching Yang was her punishment for his frivolous behavior. Giving him the pillow scented by her hair was another trap, another test. If he again indulged amorous thoughts, more suffering lay ahead. Lei drew a sharp breath. His feelings toward Wuxin changed, but only briefly. Gu’s words could not efface the image of her lovely eyes and enchanting smile. The faint, elusive scent on the pillow, neither orchid nor musk, kept him restless for much of the night.

第二天醒来，已是阳光耀眼，看表上是九点钟，比平时起身，起码晚了两个钟头；出舱一看，古应春静静地在看书喝茶。『昨晚上失眠了？』他问。雷桂卿不好意思地笑一笑，顾而言他地问∶『我们怎么办？』『你先洗脸。』古应春说∶『悟心一早派人来请我们去吃点心，我在等你。』雷桂卿有点迟疑，很想不去，但似乎显得心存芥蒂，气量太小；如果去了，又怕自己沉不住气，脸上现出悻悻之色，因而不置可否，慢慢地漱洗完了，只见小玉又来催请了。那就容不得他再多作考虑，相将上岸，到了莲池精舍，仍旧在悟心禅房中的东间坐落，那只小哈叭狗只往雷桂卿身上扑，他把它抱了起来，居然不吠不动，乖乖地躺在他怀里。『它倒跟你投缘。』雷桂卿抬头一看，悟心含笑站在门口；哈叭狗看见主人，从雷桂卿身上跳了下来。转入悟心怀中，用舌头去舐主人的脸。

When he awoke, sunlight dazzled his eyes. His watch showed nine, at least two hours later than he ordinarily rose. Coming out of the sleeping cabin, he found Gu quietly reading and drinking tea. “Could you not sleep last night?” Gu asked. Lei smiled awkwardly and changed the subject. “What are we to do?” “Wash first. Wuxin sent an invitation early this morning for us to take refreshments with her. I have been waiting for you.” Lei hesitated. He wanted to refuse, yet that might make him seem resentful and small-minded. If he went, however, he feared he might lose his composure and reveal his annoyance. Giving no definite answer, he washed slowly. Then Xiaoyu arrived to urge them to come, leaving no more room for hesitation. They went ashore together and returned to the eastern reception room in Wuxin’s quarters. The little lapdog kept jumping at Lei. He picked it up, and it lay quietly in his arms without barking or struggling. “It has taken to you.” Lei looked up to see Wuxin smiling in the doorway. At the sight of its mistress, the dog sprang from him into her arms and licked her face.

『不要闹！』悟心将狗放了下来，『到外面去玩。』狗通人性，响着颈下的小金铃，摇摇摆摆地往外走去，雷桂卿笑道∶『这只狗真好玩。』『你欢喜，送了给你好不好？』雷桂卿大感意外，不知道她这话是真是假，更不知道她说这话的用意；由于存着戒心之故，就算她是真话，他亦不敢领受这份好意。『谢谢，谢谢！君子不夺人之所好。』『我是真的要送你。』『真的我也不敢领。』雷桂卿说，『而且狗也对你有感情了。』

“Do not make a fuss!” She put it down. “Go and play outside.” The intelligent creature waddled out, its little gold bell ringing. Lei laughed. “What an amusing dog.” “If you like it, shall I give it to you?” Lei was taken completely by surprise. He did not know whether she meant it, still less why she had offered. On his guard now, he would not dare accept even if she was sincere. “Thank you, thank you. A gentleman does not deprive another of something cherished.” “I really mean to give it to you.” “Even so, I could not accept. Besides, it is attached to you.”

这时点心已经端出来，有甜有咸，颇为丰盛；一直未曾开口的古应春便说∶『悟心，我想赶回去办事，中午的素斋，下次来叨扰。好在吃这顿点心，中饭也可以不必吃了。』『喔，』悟心问道∶『你总还要回来，哪一天？』这就问到古应春为难之处了。原来他在来到湖州之前就筹划好了的，在湖州的交涉办得有了眉目，未了事宜由雷桂卿接下来办，以便他能脱身赶到上海，安排迎接左宗棠出巡。如今照原定计划，应该由雷桂卿在怡和洋行与杨师爷之间任联络之责；可是这一来少不得还是要托悟心居间，他怕雷桂卿绮念未断，与悟心之间发生纠纷，因而不知如何回答。『咦！』悟心问道∶『你怎么不开口？』『我在想。』『怎么到这时候你才来想？』这样咄咄逼人的姿态，使得古应春有些发窘，只好再想话来搪塞。『这件事很麻烦，我要跟桂卿回去以后，跟怡和商量以后再说。』

The refreshments had arrived, a plentiful assortment of sweet and savory dishes. Gu, who had so far said nothing, spoke. “Wuxin, I must hurry back to attend to business. Let us leave the vegetarian lunch until next time. After all these refreshments, we shall hardly need lunch.” “Oh? You will be coming back, then. When?” This touched on Gu’s difficulty. Before coming to Huzhou, he had planned that once negotiations were under way, Lei should take over the unfinished business, freeing him to go to Shanghai and arrange Zuo Zongtang’s reception. Under that plan, Lei should now serve as liaison between Jardine Matheson and Secretary Yang. But Wuxin would still have to act as intermediary. Gu feared that Lei’s continuing infatuation might lead to trouble between them and did not know how to answer. “Well?” she asked. “Why are you silent?” “I am thinking.” “Why have you waited until now to think?” Her pressing manner embarrassed him. He sought an excuse. “The matter is complicated. Guiqing and I must go back and consult Jardine Matheson before deciding.”

『以我说也不必这么费事。』『你有什么好办法？』『依我说，你回去办怡和洋行的禀帖，雷老爷不妨留下来，「蚕禁」马上要过了，做丝虽忙，说几句话的工夫总有，哪个收了赵宝禄多少定洋，大家算算清楚，说说明白，如果要进状子告赵宝禄，里面有杨师爷，外面有雷老爷，事情就好办了。』悟心又说∶『这是昨天晚上我跟小玉商量出来的办法。她有好几家亲戚，我也有几个熟人都跟赵宝禄有纠葛；难得你们替怡和来出面，大家是一条线上的。』这个意外的变化，不但古应春想不到，雷桂卿更感意外，心里有好些话要说，但照理应该由古应春先表示意见，所以默然等待。古应春是完全赞成悟心的办法，但先要说好一个条件，『不错，内有杨师爷，外有雷老爷。』他说∶『不过，你也不要忘记，中有悟心师太，都要靠你联络。』『那当然。』

“I do not think so much trouble is necessary.” “Have you a better plan?” “You should go back to obtain Jardine Matheson’s petition. Mr Lei can stay here. The silkworm restrictions will soon be over. Silk-making is busy work, but people can spare time for a few words. Let everyone work out and make clear how much, if anything, he received from Zhao in deposits. If they decide to sue him, with Secretary Yang working inside the yamen and Mr Lei outside, matters will be much easier.” Wuxin added, “Xiaoyu and I worked this out last night. Several of her relatives and a few people I know have disputes with Zhao. It is fortunate that you are acting for Jardine Matheson. We are all pursuing the same end.” This unexpected development surprised Gu, and Lei even more. Lei had much to say but properly left Gu to express his view first. Gu thoroughly approved, but wanted one condition settled. “Yes, Secretary Yang inside and Mr Lei outside. But you must not forget Mistress Wuxin in the middle. We shall rely on you to keep everyone in touch.” “Of course.”

『你怎么联络法？』古应春说∶『雷老爷在这里人生地不熟，再遇到那么一条吓坏人的狗，不是生意经。』『不会了。』悟心答说，『我保险不会再遇到。』说罢嫣然一笑。这一笑又让雷桂卿神魂飘荡了；不过这一回古应春却不再担心，他担心的是悟心会出花样，既然她如此保证，而且要靠雷桂卿办事，也不敢再恶作剧。至于雷桂卿这面，已经对他下过警告，倘或执迷不悟，那是他自己的事。转念到此，便向雷桂卿笑道∶『这一来我也放心了。你虽不是曹植、韩寿，不过做了鲁仲连，反而更吃香了。』悟心不知道他为雷桂卿讲过『贾氏窥帘韩掾少，宓妃留枕魏王才』这两句诗的典故，便叩问说∶『你在打什么哑谜。』『不错，是个哑谜；你要想知道，等我不在的时候，你问他好了。』悟心这下大致可以猜到了，这个哑谜与她有关。此时当然不必再问，一笑置之。『我们谈谈正事。』古应春说，『悟心，我准定你的办法，今天吃过中饭，我就回杭州，桂卿一半帮你们的忙，照应他的责任，都在你身上。』『那当然。我庵里不便住，我另外替雷老爷找个好地方借住，一定称心如意。』

“And how will you do that?” Gu asked. “Mr Lei knows neither the people nor the place. Another encounter with a terrifying dog would hardly help the business.” “There will not be another,” Wuxin replied. “I guarantee it.” She smiled beautifully. Lei was captivated once more, but Gu no longer worried. His concern had been Wuxin’s tricks. Having given her guarantee and needing Lei’s help, she would hardly play another prank. As for Lei, he had been warned. If he persisted in his delusions, that was his own affair. Gu smiled at him. “Then I can rest easy. You may not be Cao Zhi or Han Shou, but play the mediator Lu Zhonglian and you will be more welcome still.” Unaware that he had explained the verse about Lady Jia, Han Shou, the goddess’s pillow, and the Prince of Wei to Lei, Wuxin asked, “What riddle are you speaking in?” “A riddle, indeed. If you want the answer, ask him when I am gone.” Wuxin could now guess that it concerned her. There was no need to pursue it. She let it pass with a smile. “Let us return to business,” Gu said. “I accept your plan. I shall go back to Hangzhou after lunch today. Guiqing will be working partly on your behalf, and I leave his welfare entirely to you.” “Naturally. He cannot conveniently lodge in my retreat, but I shall find him a good place elsewhere. I am sure he will be pleased.”

刚谈到这里，小玉来报，说船老大带了个陌生人来觅古应春。此刻人在大殿上，请去相见。出去一看，才知道是胡雪岩特遣的急足来投信。信上说∶左宗棠已自江宁起程，一路视察防务、水利，在镇江、常州、苏州都将逗留，大概十天以后，可到上海，在杭州所谈之事，希望古应春即速办理，可由湖州径赴上海，省事得多。这一来，计划就要重新安排了，古应春吩咐来人回船待命∶随即拿着信报找悟心与雷桂卿去商量。『左大人出巡到上海，胡大先生要替他摆摆威风，这件事我要赶紧到上海托洋人去办。桂卿，我看，你要先回一趟杭州，把情形跟胡先生说清楚了再回来。『』怡和的禀帖呢？『雷桂卿问∶』你在上海办妥了，不如直接寄湖州，似乎比寄到杭州多一个周折来得妥当。『』好！湖州寄到哪里，是——『古应春的话犹未完，悟心抢着说道∶』寄给杨师爷，请他代呈好了。『

Just then Xiaoyu reported that the boatman had brought a stranger looking for Gu. He was waiting in the main hall. Gu went out and found that Hu Xueyan had sent a special express messenger with a letter. Zuo had already left Jiangning, it said, inspecting defenses and waterworks along the way. He would stop in Zhenjiang, Changzhou, and Suzhou and reach Shanghai in about ten days. Hu urged Gu to attend promptly to what they had discussed in Hangzhou. It would save trouble to go straight from Huzhou to Shanghai. The plan had to be revised again. Gu told the messenger to wait aboard, then took the letter back to consult Wuxin and Lei. “Lord Zuo is coming to Shanghai on inspection, and Mr Hu wishes to give him an imposing reception. I must hurry there and arrange matters with the foreigners. Guiqing, you had better go to Hangzhou first, explain the situation to Mr Hu, and then return here.” “What about Jardine Matheson’s petition?” Lei asked. “Once you have it ready in Shanghai, it would be better to send it directly to Huzhou, rather than through Hangzhou.” “Good. Where in Huzhou should it be sent? To—” Before he finished, Wuxin said, “Send it to Secretary Yang and ask him to submit it.”

『可是信里说些什么，桂卿不知道啊！』『杨师爷知道，莫非不能问他？你如果再不放心，抄个底子寄到我这里转，也可以。不过，光寄封信，你自己也不好意思吧？』『你说，你说，你要啥，我给你寄了来。』『敲你一个小竹杠，到洋房里买一包洋糖给我寄来。』『还有呢？』『就这一样。』『好了，我知道了。』古应春对雷桂卿说∶『你坐一会，我回船去写了信再来。』

“But Guiqing will not know what it says.” “Secretary Yang will. Can he not ask him? If you are still uneasy, send a copy to me and I can pass it on. But surely you will be ashamed to send nothing except a letter?” “Tell me what you want, and I shall send it.” “I shall levy a small tribute. Buy me a packet of foreign sweets at a foreign shop.” “Anything else?” “That is all.” “Very well. I understand.” Gu turned to Lei. “Stay a moment. I shall go back to the boat, write a letter, and return.”

『何必回船上去写？我这里莫非连纸墨砚笔都没有？』说着，悟心抬一抬手，将古应春带到后轩，是她抄经做功课的所在。『到上海往东走，回杭州往南走，船你坐了回去。』古应春向悟心说道；「能不能请你派人打听一下，往上海的船是啥辰光有？『『每天都有。几点钟开，我就不晓得了。我去问。』等悟心一走，古应春向雷桂卿笑道∶『这是意外的机缘。悟心似乎有还俗的意思，你断弦也有两年了，好自为之。』雷桂卿笑笑不作声；不过看得出来，心里非常高兴。『我只劝你一句，要顺其自然，千万不可心急，更不可强求。』『我明白，你放心好了。』

“Why go back to the boat? Have I no paper, ink, inkstone, or brushes here?” With a gesture, Wuxin led him to the rear pavilion, where she copied scriptures and performed her devotions. Gu addressed Lei about the journey. “Shanghai is east, Hangzhou south. You take the boat back.” Then he asked Wuxin, “Could you send someone to find out when there is a boat for Shanghai?” “There is one every day, but I do not know the time. I shall inquire.” Once she had gone, Gu smiled at Lei. “This is an unexpected opportunity. Wuxin seems inclined to return to secular life. You have been a widower for two years. Conduct yourself wisely.” Lei smiled without answering, but his delight was plain. “I have only one piece of advice: let things take their course. Do not be impatient, and above all do not force anything.” “I understand. You need not worry.”

胡雪岩替老母做过了生日，第二天就赶往上海，那是在古应春回家的第六天。一到当然先去看七姑奶奶，絮絮不断地谈了好久，直到吃晚饭时，才能谈正事，『左大人已经到苏州了，预定后天到上海，小爷叔来得正是时候。』『他来了当然住天后宫。转运局是一定要来的，你看应该怎么接待？』『左大人算是自己人，来看转运局是视察属下，我看不必弄得太客气，倒好象疏远了。』『太客气虽不必，让他高兴高兴是一定要的。』胡雪岩说∶『我想挑个日子，请他吃饭陪客除了我们自己官面上的人以外，能不能把洋人的总领事、司令官都请来。』『这要先说好。照道理，请他们没有不来的道理。』古应春又说∶『放礼炮的事，已经谈妥当了，不过，日子不晓得哪一天？』

The day after his mother’s birthday celebrations ended, Hu hurried to Shanghai. It was the sixth day since Gu’s return home. Naturally, his first visit was to the Seventh Lady. They talked at length, and only at dinner could business be discussed. “Lord Zuo has reached Suzhou and expects to arrive here the day after tomorrow. You have come at just the right time, Uncle.” “He will stay at Tianhou Temple, of course, and he is certain to visit the Transport Bureau. How should we receive him?” “He is one of our own people. Visiting the bureau means inspecting his subordinates. There is no need for excessive ceremony; that would only make us seem distant.” “Perhaps not excessive ceremony, but we must certainly please him. I should like to choose a day to give him a dinner. Besides our own officials, can we invite the foreign consuls-general and naval commanders?” “It must be arranged beforehand, but there is no reason for them to refuse.” Gu continued, “The salutes are arranged. What I do not know is the day.”

『何不到道台衙门去问一问？』古应春不作声，胡雪岩看出其中别有蹊跷，便即追问是怎么回事？『「排单」是早已来了，哪天到，哪天看哪个地方，哪天什么人请客，都规定好了，就是我们转运局去要排单，推说没有。』胡雪岩不由得生气，『他们是什么意思呢？』他问∶『我们转动局一问也很敬重他们的。明天我倒要去看看邵小村，怎么跟我说的。』古应春始而默然，继而低声说道∶『小爷叔，你不要动意气。我听到一个说法，不晓得是真是假？据说李合肥已经派人通知邵小村，关照他跟盛杏荪联络，不许左湘阴的势力伸到上海。有人在邵小面前献计，说左湘阴容易对付，就是胡某人不大好惹，要防左，先要防胡。』胡雪岩听完，不大在意这话，『他们防我也不止今天一天了。』他说∶『见怪不怪，其怪自败，你不必把这件事看得太认真。』看他这种掉以轻心的态度，古应春不免兴起一种隐忧，但此时不便再多说什么，自己私下打了一个主意，要为胡雪岩作耳目，多方注意李鸿章与左宗棠在两江明争暗头，倘或有牵涉及于胡雪岩的可能时，更要预先防备，弭祸于无形。由于古应春的极力活动，同时也由于左宗棠本身的威望，上海英、法两租界的工部局，以及各国驻沪海军，都以很隆重的礼节致敬；经过租界，派出巡捕站岗、仪队前导，尤其是出吴淞口阅兵时，黄浦江上的各国兵舰，都升起大清朝的黄龙旗，呜放十三响礼炮，声彻云霄，震动了整个上海，都知道左宗棠到上海来了。

“Why not ask at the intendant’s yamen?” Gu said nothing. Seeing that something lay behind his silence, Hu pressed him. “The itinerary arrived long ago. His arrival, his visits, his hosts for each day—everything has been fixed. But when our bureau asks for a copy, they say there is none.” Hu was angry. “What do they mean by it? Our bureau has always treated them with respect. I shall see Shao Xiaocun tomorrow and hear what he has to say.” Gu was silent at first, then said quietly, “Do not let anger carry you away, Uncle. I have heard something, though I do not know whether it is true. They say Li of Hefei has sent word to Shao to work with Sheng Xingsun and prevent Zuo of Xiangyin from extending his influence into Shanghai. Someone advised Shao that Zuo would be easy to deal with, but Hu was not a man to provoke lightly: to guard against Zuo, first guard against Hu.” Hu took little notice. “They have been on their guard against me for more than a day. Ignore a strange trick, and it defeats itself. Do not take it too seriously.” His dismissive attitude gave Gu an uneasy sense of danger, but he could say no more then. Privately, he resolved to act as Hu’s eyes and ears, watching the open and covert rivalry between Li and Zuo in Liangjiang. If it threatened to involve Hu, he must take precautions before harm arose. Through Gu’s strenuous efforts, and because of Zuo’s own prestige, the municipal councils of Shanghai’s British and French settlements and the foreign navies gave Zuo an exceptionally ceremonious reception. When he passed through the settlements, police stood guard and an honor escort went before him. Most strikingly, when he went out through Wusong to review the forces, the foreign warships on the Huangpu hoisted the Qing yellow-dragon flag and fired thirteen-gun salutes. The thunder filled the sky and shook all Shanghai. Everyone knew Zuo Zongtang had arrived.

行馆设在天后宫，上海道邵友濂率领松江知府及所属各县『庭参』，接着是江海关税务司及工部局的董事拜会，在上海的文武官员谒见，然后是邵友濂联合在上海有差使的道员，包括胡雪岩、盛宣怀在内，『恭宴爵相』，散席时，已经起更了。胡雪岩与古应春当然留在最后，『大人今天很累了。』胡雪岩说∶『请早早安置，明天再来请安。』『不、不！』左宗棠摇着手说∶『我明天看了制造局，后天就回江宁了。有好些事情跟你谈谈，不忙走。』胡雪岩原是门面话，既然左宗堂精神很好，愿意留他相谈，自是求之不得，答应一声，坐了下来。

Zuo’s temporary headquarters were at Tianhou Temple. Shao Youlian, the Shanghai intendant, led the prefect of Songjiang and the subordinate county magistrates in paying formal respects. Next came the commissioner of the Jiang Customs and directors of the municipal councils, followed by the civil and military officials stationed in Shanghai. Shao then joined the intendants holding appointments there, including Hu Xueyan and Sheng Xuanhuai, in a banquet honoring the marquis and grand secretary. By the time it ended, the first night watch had begun. Hu and Gu naturally stayed until last. “Your Excellency has had a tiring day,” Hu said. “Please retire early. We shall return tomorrow to pay our respects.” “No, no!” Zuo waved a hand. “I inspect the arsenal tomorrow and return to Jiangning the day after. There are many things I must discuss with you. Do not hurry away.” Hu had offered only the customary polite words. Since Zuo was in good spirits and wished him to stay, he could ask for nothing better. He assented and sat down.

『陆防、海防争了半天，临到头来，还是由我来办，真是造化弄人。』说罢，左宗棠仰空大笑，声震屋瓦。这一笑只有胡雪岩明白，是笑李鸿章。原来同治十一年五月，俄国见新疆回乱，有机可乘，出兵伊犁；十三年三月，日本借口琉球难民事件，派军入侵台湾，一时陆防、海防相继告警，因而出现了陆防与海防孰重的争论；相争两方的主角，正就是左宗棠与李鸿章。左宗棠经营西北，李鸿章指挥北洋，各有所司，亦各有所持，朝迁认为兹事体大，命各省督抚，各抒所见。其时湖南巡抚王文韶，正好回杭州扫墓，胡雪岩便问他∶『赞成陆防，还是海防？』

“After all that argument over land defense and maritime defense, in the end I have to attend to it myself. What tricks fortune plays!” Zuo threw back his head and laughed so loudly that the roof seemed to shake. Only Hu understood that the laughter was directed at Li Hongzhang. In the fifth month of the eleventh year of Tongzhi, Russia had taken advantage of the Muslim rebellion in Xinjiang to send troops into Ili. In the third month of the thirteenth year, Japan used the incident involving shipwrecked Ryukyuans as a pretext to invade Taiwan. Threats on land and at sea followed one another, provoking debate over which kind of defense should take priority. Its principal opponents were Zuo and Li. Zuo managed affairs in the northwest, Li commanded Beiyang; each had his own responsibilities and convictions. Considering the issue of great importance, the court ordered governors-general and governors throughout the provinces to state their views. Wang Wenshao, then Governor of Hunan, happened to be back in Hangzhou to visit his ancestral graves. Hu asked him whether he favored land or maritime defense.

王文韶反问一句∶『你看呢？』『你当湖南巡抚，自然应该帮湖南人讲话。』『不错。为政不得罪巨室。』王文韶说∶『我为这件事，一直踌躇不决，现在听老兄一句话，算是定了主意。李大先生的交情，暂时要搁一搁了。』原来王文韶跟李鸿章的关系很深，为了在湖南做官顺利，王文韶决定赞成陆防，复奏说道∶『江海两防，亟宜筹备，然海疆之患，不能无因而至，其关键则在西陲军务，俄人据我伊犁，强有久假不归之势，我师迟一日，则俄人进一日，事机之急，莫此为甚。』就因为这个奏折，使得陆防论占了上风。不久同治驾崩，争端暂息。光绪元年，争议复起，慈禧太后命亲郡王、大学士、六部九卿，会议海防事宜。李鸿章上折请罢西征；左宗棠当然反对，最后是由于文祥的支持，派左宗棠以钦差大臣督办新疆军务，显然的，海防论又落了下风。

“What do you think?” Wang countered. “As Governor of Hunan, you should naturally speak for a Hunan man.” “Quite. A man governing a province must not offend its powerful families. I have been unable to decide this matter. Your words have settled it. My friendship with Lord Li must be put aside for the moment.” Wang’s ties with Li were deep, but for the sake of governing Hunan smoothly, he decided to support land defense. In his memorial he wrote: “Defenses on both river and sea urgently require preparation. Yet a threat to the coast cannot arise without a cause; the crucial matter lies in the military situation on our western frontier. The Russians occupy our Ili and show every sign of holding it indefinitely. Each day our troops delay gives them another day’s advance. Nothing could be more urgent.” This memorial gave the land-defense argument the advantage. Soon afterward the Tongzhi Emperor died, and the dispute subsided temporarily. It revived in the first year of Guangxu, when Empress Dowager Cixi ordered princes, grand secretaries, and the heads of the Six Ministries and Nine Courts to confer on maritime defense. Li memorialized for an end to the western expedition; Zuo naturally opposed him. Finally, with Wenxiang’s support, Zuo was appointed imperial commissioner to direct military affairs in Xinjiang. Plainly, the maritime-defense argument had again lost ground.

不过陆防之议，实际上是由伊犁事件而来，及至曾纪泽使俄，解决了中俄纠纷，陆防论就不再有人提起。到得左宗常西征收功，内召入军机；不久又外放两江，李鸿章旧事重提，这回大获全胜，海防的计划，朝廷完全同意，首先要办的是三件事∶一是在营口设营，编练新工海军；二是筹款续造『钢面铁甲』兵轮，招商局原应归还的官款暂缓归还，拨作购铁甲船之用；三是南北洋各紧要海口修船坞、修炮台，同时并举。哪知正在干得如火如茶之时，李太夫人病殁汉口，李鸿章丁忧回籍，调两广总督张树声署理直督，筹设海防一事，便暂时拦下来了。

In practice, however, the case for land defense had arisen from the Ili affair. After Zeng Jize’s mission to Russia settled the Sino-Russian dispute, it ceased to be raised. When Zuo completed his western campaign, he was summoned to the Grand Council, then soon appointed Governor-General of Liangjiang. Li revived the old question and this time won a complete victory. The court accepted his maritime-defense plan in full. Three measures were to come first: establishing a base at Yingkou to train a new naval force; raising funds to continue building steel-armored warships, with government loans due for repayment by China Merchants temporarily retained and diverted to the purchase of ironclads; and simultaneously building docks and forts at the important harbors of both Nanyang and Beiyang. Just as the work gathered momentum, Old Madam Li died in Hankou. Li Hongzhang returned home to observe mourning, and Zhang Shusheng, Governor-General of Liangguang, was transferred to act in Zhili. The coastal-defense preparations temporarily came to a halt.

『海防，北洋可管，南洋又何尝不可管；而且经费大部分出在两江，南洋来管，更觉名正言顺。我现在想先从船坞、炮台这两件事着手。已经派人去邀彭宫保了；我要赶回江宁，就因为他从长江上游巡阅下来，日内可到江宁，客临主不在，未免失礼。』左宗棠一口气说到这里，突然叫一声∶『雪岩！』『大人有什么吩咐？』『福克在不在上海？』『在。』胡雪岩答说∶『他本来要回国了，因为听说大人巡视上海，特为迟一班轮船走。明天一定会来见大人。』『喔，他回德国以后，还来不来？』『来，来。』『那好。正好趁他回国之便，我们再商量商量，看有什么新出的利器，托他采办。』

“If Beiyang can manage maritime defense, why should Nanyang not do so? Most of the money comes from Liangjiang anyway, making it still more proper for Nanyang to take charge. I mean to begin with docks and forts. I have already sent for Lord Peng. The reason I must hurry back to Jiangning is that he is coming down the Yangtze on an inspection and should arrive within days. It would hardly be courteous for the host to be absent when his guest arrives.” Having said all this in one breath, Zuo suddenly called, “Xueyan!” “What are your instructions, Your Excellency?” “Is Focke in Shanghai?” “Yes. He was about to return home, but on hearing of your inspection he specially postponed his departure by one steamer. He will certainly call on you tomorrow.” “Will he come back after returning to Germany?” “Yes, indeed.” “Good. Then we can discuss the matter further and take advantage of his journey home to have him purchase any new and effective weapons.”

胡雪岩正待回答，只见一名戈什哈掀帘而入，手里持着一个卷夹，走到左宗棠面前，一言不发，只将卷夹打了开来，里面张纸；左宗棠拿起来看完，随手便递了给胡雪岩。接过来一看，是一份密电的译文∶『申局探呈左爵相，（享密）沅帅督粤，即明发。』署名是一个『云』字，胡雪岩知道，是徐用仪发来的密电。这『沅帅』当然是指号沅甫的曾国荃，胡雪岩笑道∶『两广是好地方。曾九帅这回不会象去年那样，陕甘总督当不到半年，就因为太苦而一定要求去了。』左宗棠点点头，沉吟了一会，抬起头来，徐徐说道∶『叫曾老九到两广，可见张振仙是不会回任，要真除直督了。雪岩，我要乘此机会，大加整顿，南洋的归南洋，北洋的归北洋，把李少荃那只看不见的「三只手」消除出去。』『是。』胡雪岩心想李鸿章在南洋的势力，已有根深柢固之势，要清除不容易；但真的办到了，将来另有一番局面，这件事值得出一番大气力。

Hu was about to reply when a personal guard lifted the curtain and entered with a document folder. Without speaking, he approached Zuo and opened it. There was a sheet inside. Zuo read it and immediately passed it to Hu. It was the decoded text of a confidential telegram: “Shanghai office respectfully reports to Marquis Zuo: [confidential] Marshal Yuan to govern Liangguang; public appointment decree imminent.” The signature was the single character “Yun.” Hu knew the message came from Xu Yongyi. Marshal Yuan was, of course, Zeng Guoquan, whose courtesy name was Yuanfu. Hu smiled. “Liangguang is a good place. This time Ninth Marshal Zeng will not be like last year, when he insisted on leaving the governorship-general of Shaanxi and Gansu after less than six months because conditions were too harsh.” Zuo nodded and considered for a while. Raising his head, he said slowly, “Sending Zeng the Ninth to Liangguang shows that Zhang Zhenxian will not return there. He is to be confirmed in Zhili. Xueyan, I must use this opportunity for a thorough reorganization. Let Nanyang belong to Nanyang and Beiyang to Beiyang. We must remove Li Shaoquan’s invisible meddling hand.” “Yes, Your Excellency.” Hu reflected that Li’s influence in Nanyang was deeply rooted and would not be easy to eliminate. If it could be done, however, an entirely new situation would open before them. It was worth a great effort.

『明天我去看制造局，你最好跟我一起去，看看有什么可以改良的地方。』『是。我明天一早来伺候。』辞出行辕，不过九点多钟，十里洋场正是热闹的时候；上车时，古应春的车案悄悄说道∶『老爷，七小姐那里的约会是今天。』『你倒比我记得还清楚。』古应春说道∶『是不是七小姐特为关照，要你到时候提醒我。』那车案笑嘻嘻地不作声，只扬鞭驱车，往南而去。『七小姐是哪个？』胡雪岩问。

“I am going to inspect the arsenal tomorrow. You had better come with me and see what can be improved.” “Certainly. I shall attend you early tomorrow morning.” When they left the headquarters, it was only a little after nine, and Shanghai’s foreign settlement was at its liveliest. As they climbed into the carriage, Gu’s driver quietly said, “Master, your appointment with Seventh Miss is tonight.” “You remember it more clearly than I do. Did Seventh Miss specially ask you to remind me?” The driver merely grinned, cracked his whip, and drove south. “Who is Seventh Miss?” Hu asked.

『爱月楼老七。』古应春答说，『刚从苏州来的。』『人长得怎么样？』『不过大方而已。应酬工夫可是一等。』『看样子不止于应酬工夫。』胡雪岩笑道∶『扎客人的工夫也是一等。』『小爷叔看了就知道了。』转眼之间，马车在宝善街兆荣里停了下来，爱月楼老七家就在进弄堂右首第二家，相帮高喊一声∶『后厢房。』即时便有一名娘姨迎了出来。

“Seventh Miss Aiyuelou. She has just come from Suzhou.” “What does she look like?” “Presentable, that is all. But her skill in entertaining is first-rate.” “From the look of things, it is not only her entertaining that is first-rate.” Hu laughed. “She knows how to hold on to a client too.” “You will see for yourself, Uncle.” Soon the carriage stopped at Zhaorong Lane off Baoshan Street. Seventh Miss Aiyuelou’s establishment was the second house on the right. An attendant called, “Rear wing!” and a serving woman immediately came to receive them.

古胡二人便站在天进中等，只见那名娘姨插了满头红花，擦一脸白粉，丑而且怪，真是所谓鸠盘荼，但开出口来，那一口娇滴滴的吴侬软语，恰如十七八女郎，这就是苏州人所说的『隔壁西施』！『喔唷，古老爷，耐那哼故歇才来介？七小姐等是等得来。』及至发现胡雪岩，愈发大惊小怪，『喔唷唷唷，难末事体大格哉！啥叫财神老爷还清得来哉介？她这一喊不打紧，楼上纷纷开窗，探出好几张俊俏面庞，住天井中探望；其中有一个大声喊道∶』胡老爷，胡老爷，耐阿记得我介？奴是湘云老四，晏歇到倪搭来坐。『胡雪岩涉历花丛，阅人甚多，记不得有么一个湘云老四，只连声答应∶』好！好！『当下随着娘姨上楼，只见后厢房门口，有个花信年华的女子，打起门帘，含笑等待；等一进门，古应春说道∶『老七，你大概没有见过胡老爷？』

Hu and Gu waited in the courtyard. The woman’s hair was covered with red flowers, her face thick with white powder. Ugly and outlandish, she looked like a demoness. Yet when she spoke, her soft, delicate Wu dialect sounded like that of a girl of seventeen or eighteen: a true “beauty on the other side of the wall,” as Suzhou people called such women. “Oh, Mr Gu, why are you so late? Seventh Miss has been waiting and waiting!” On discovering Hu, she made a greater fuss still. “Oh, my goodness! What an occasion! How have we managed to invite the God of Wealth himself?” Her cries brought windows opening upstairs and several pretty faces peering down into the courtyard. One called loudly, “Mr Hu! Do you remember me? Fourth Miss Xiangyun! Come and sit with me later.” Hu had met so many women in his visits to the pleasure quarters that he could not remember this Fourth Miss Xiangyun. He merely answered, “Certainly! Certainly!” Following the serving woman upstairs, they found a woman of about twenty-four holding up the curtain at the rear room’s door, smiling as she waited. As they entered, Gu said, “Seventh Miss, I suppose you have never met Mr Hu?”

『啥叫见过歇？奴见过格。』说着敛衽见礼，口中说道∶『胡老爷，耐发福哉。』『喔，』胡雪岩问道∶『七小姐，我们在哪里见过？』『山塘畹？是大前年年脚边浪格事体哉。格日子是勒抚台格大少爷请客。胡老爷还转过奴一个局，耐末贵人多忘事，奴是一直记好勤心里浪向。』说着，便上前来替胡雪岩解钮扣，卸马褂。胡雪岩闻到她头发上的香味，记起有这么一回事，那年年底路过苏州，江苏巡抚勒方琦的长子，在上海便是稔友；特地在虎丘一家局书寓中请客，仿佛是在席间转过局，面貌依稀，但名字却记不起，但决不是三个字。『那时候你不叫爱月楼吧？』『伊个辰光叫惜芳。』

“Never met him? Of course I have.” She curtsied. “Mr Hu, you have grown more prosperous-looking.” “Oh? Where did we meet, Seventh Miss?” “At Shantang, near the end of the year before the year before last. Governor Le’s eldest son gave the dinner. You even called me over to entertain you. A great man has too much to remember, but I have always treasured it.” She came forward to undo Hu’s buttons and remove his riding jacket. The scent of her hair stirred his memory. Passing through Suzhou near the year’s end, he had been entertained by the eldest son of Le Fangqi, Governor of Jiangsu, whom he already knew well in Shanghai. The dinner had been given at a courtesan’s establishment at Tiger Hill. He vaguely remembered calling one of the women over during the banquet. Her face seemed familiar, but her name escaped him. It had certainly not consisted of three characters. “You were not called Aiyuelou then?” “I was called Xifang.”

『怪不得了。』胡雪碉笑笑寒喧∶『这几年还好吧？』『为仔好嘞，混到上海滩来格。』爱月楼老七向古应春瞟了一眼，『自从古老爷来捧仔场，慢慢叫好起来格哉。』『今朝日脚，勿壳张财神菩萨驾到，格末加二要好格哉畹！』插嘴的是那鸠盘荼，胡雪岩与古应春是听惯了这种奉承话，不以为意；倒是爱月楼老七听得刺耳，当即说道∶『耐闲话那哼介多介？』说着，又使个眼色，让她退了出去。这时果盘已经摆上来了，等胡雪岩与古应春坐了下来，爱月楼老七一面敬瓜子、敬茶，一面寒喧。『胡老爷是落里一日到格介？』『来是来了两三天了。』古应春代为回答∶『不过今天头一回出来吃花酒。』

“That explains it.” Hu smiled and asked politely, “Have the last few years treated you well?” “I came to Shanghai hoping for better things.” Aiyuelou glanced at Gu. “Since Mr Gu began supporting my establishment, things have gradually improved.” “And with the God of Wealth arriving so unexpectedly today, they will improve still more!” the serving woman put in. Hu and Gu were accustomed to such flattery and paid it little heed. Aiyuelou, however, found it grating. “Why do you talk so much?” she said, giving the woman a look that sent her out. Fruit trays had now been set out. Once the men were seated, Aiyuelou offered melon seeds and tea and continued the courtesies. “When did Mr Hu arrive?” “Two or three days ago,” Gu answered for him. “But this is his first evening out at a courtesan’s dinner.”

『啊唷！头一转就到奴搭，格是看得起奴畹！多谢、多谢。』『早知道你们是老相好，我昨天就请我们小爷叔来了。』『那哼叫小爷叔？古老爷，耐姓半个胡畹，啥叫是叔侄辈子？』『妙！』胡雪岩笑道∶『应春，我还是头一回听说，你姓半个胡。』古应春也笑了，回顾一班小大姐说∶『你们以后就叫我半胡老爷好了。』『格就呒趣哉！』爱月楼老七接口说道∶『吃酒末吃半壶，碰麻雀末一和还勿和。阿要作孽？』

“Oh! And you came to me on your very first outing! What an honor. Thank you!” “Had I known you were old friends, I should have brought our Little Uncle yesterday.” “Why Little Uncle? Your surname Gu is only half of Hu. How can you be uncle and nephew?” “Excellent!” Hu laughed. “Yingchun, this is the first time I have heard that your surname is half a Hu.” Gu laughed too. Turning to the young attendants, he said, “You may all call me Mr Half-Hu from now on.” “That would be no fun!” Aiyuelou replied. “Half a pot of wine to drink, and not even one complete winning hand at mahjong! Would that not be a miserable fate?”

胡雪岩看她心思灵活、口齿便给，颇有好感；古应春看出他的心思，便即说道∶『小爷叔，今天这个客，你来请了吧？』胡雪岩跟他走马章台，已历多年，间或也有这种『让贤』之举；正在考虑是否接受此番美意时，爱月楼老七却开口了。『勿作兴格！古老爷，耐今朝格台酒那哼好赖？停吃得有兴末，翻台到前厢房，胡老爷耐看阿好？』『前厢房？』胡雪岩问，『是湘云老四那里。』既然人家都已画好道了，逢场作戏惯了的胡雪岩毫无异议，只问古应春∶『请哪些人？』

Hu liked her quick wit and ready tongue. Gu saw it and said, “Uncle, why do you not take over as host tonight?” They had visited the pleasure quarters together for many years, and Gu occasionally yielded his place in this way. Hu was considering whether to accept when Aiyuelou spoke. “That will not do! Mr Gu, how can you escape paying for tonight’s dinner? If everyone is enjoying himself afterward, we can move to the front room for another round. Would that suit you, Mr Hu?” “The front room? Fourth Miss Xiangyun’s?” Since she had already arranged their course, Hu, well accustomed to these social games, had no objection. He merely asked Gu, “Whom shall we invite?”

『小爷叔想看哪些人。』于是胡雪岩随口报了四、五个名字，都是青楼中善会凑趣的人物；古应春下笔如飞，写好了请柬，点一点主客一共七人，便即说道∶『我们来个八仙过海。』说着，又写一张请柬∶『飞请三马路长发栈，沙大爷印一心，惠临一叙。』赘上名字以后，另外又用小字注了一行∶『有贵客介见，千请勿却。』巧得很，偏偏就是这个特邀的客人，因病未能赴约。不过今雨不来旧雨来，有个胡雪岩与古应春都认识的兵部司官林茂先，外放福建的知府，路过上海也住在那家客栈，得知古应春请吃花酒；这是照例可以闯席的，逆旅无聊，便作了不速之客。

“Whom would you like to see?” Hu named four or five men known for making good company in the pleasure quarters. Gu’s brush flew as he wrote the invitations. Counting seven hosts and guests in all, he said, “Let us make Eight Immortals Crossing the Sea.” He wrote another invitation: “Urgently inviting Mr Sha Yixin, staying at Changfa Inn on Third Road, to join us.” After signing it, he added in smaller characters, “A distinguished guest awaits an introduction. Please do not decline.” By an odd chance, this specially invited man was prevented by illness from coming. An old acquaintance arrived instead. Lin Maoxian, whom both Hu and Gu knew, was a Ministry of War official newly appointed prefect in Fujian. Passing through Shanghai, he was staying at the same inn. On hearing that Gu was giving a courtesan’s dinner—an occasion one could customarily join without invitation—he relieved the tedium of travel by appearing unannounced.

『好极，好极！』古应春颇为欢迎，因为这林茂先也是很有趣的人，谈锋极健，肚子里掌故很多，声色场中宴饮，必得要有这样一个人，席面上才不会冷落。台面铺设好了，名为『双台』，其实仍是一张圆桌；爱月楼老七拿一方簇新的白洋布，裹着一把镶银象牙筷，走到古应春面前问道∶『客人可曾齐？』『还差一位。不过开席吧！』这时胡雪岩便发话了，因为勾栏虽非官场，但席次也讲身分地位；胡雪岩名正言顺是首座，他不等人家来请，抢着前面逊谢。『今天这个首座，林茂翁推都推不掉的——』『雪翁，雪翁！』『足下听我说完，如果不在道理上，你再驳我。』胡雪岩挥手拦住他说∶『第一，你是远客；第二，你有喜事；第三，除我跟应春以外，其余跟足下都是初会，理当客气。』话一完，大家都说道理很通，林茂先便拱拱手说道∶『有僭、有僭。』等爱月楼老七安了席，首先落座。

“Excellent! Excellent!” Gu welcomed him warmly. Lin was entertaining, a brilliant talker with a store of anecdotes. A dinner in the pleasure quarters needed such a man to keep it from falling flat. The banquet was laid. Though called a “double table,” it was still only one round table. Aiyuelou approached Gu with a bundle of silver-mounted ivory chopsticks wrapped in a fresh white cloth. “Are all the guests here?” “One is still missing, but let us begin.” Hu now spoke. Though a courtesan’s house was no government office, seating still reflected rank and standing. He was unquestionably entitled to the place of honor, but before anyone could invite him to it, he declined. “Mr Maoxian cannot possibly refuse the first seat tonight—” “Mr Xue, Mr Xue!” “Hear me out. If my reasons are unsound, you may refute them.” Hu raised a hand to stop him. “First, you have come from afar. Second, you have something to celebrate. Third, apart from Yingchun and myself, everyone here meets you for the first time and ought to show you courtesy.” All agreed that his reasoning was sound. Lin clasped his hands. “Then forgive my presumption.” Once Aiyuelou had arranged the places, he sat first.

次席当然胡雪岩，其余都是稔友，不分上下，只留了主位给古应春，等他一坐下，小大姐立即捧上一个黑木盘，内中笔砚以外，便是一叠局票。『茂翁，你叫哪位？』『这里我是外行，而且昨天刚到，今天是第一回来观光，请你举贤吧！』『叫湘云老四好了。』胡雪岩说，『我记得她那张嘴很能说，跟茂翁的谈锋倒相配。』古应春略想一想，写了下来，便又问道∶『小爷叔你自己呢？』胡雪岩的相识可是太多了，笑笑说道∶『你替我作主好了。』

Hu naturally took the second seat. The others were old friends and did not distinguish among their places, leaving only the host’s seat for Gu. As soon as he sat down, a young attendant brought a black wooden tray holding brush, inkstone, and a stack of summons cards for the courtesans. “Mr Maoxian, whom shall you call?” “I know nothing of the place. I arrived only yesterday, and this is my first visit. Recommend someone.” “Call Fourth Miss Xiangyun,” Hu suggested. “I remember she has a ready tongue. She would suit Mr Maoxian’s conversation.” Gu considered briefly, wrote the name, and asked, “And for you, Uncle?” Hu knew so many women that he merely smiled. “Choose for me.”

古应春点点头说∶『我替小爷叔叫两个，一个是好媛老九。一个是——』『不、不！我想起来。』胡雪岩说∶『另外一个叫娇凤老五。』『何必叫她呢？』古应春皱着眉说。『你不要管，我找她有事。』于是一一写好局票，发了出去；首先来的是近在前厢房的湘云老四，小足伶仃，扶着十三四岁的一个小大姐的肩膀，进门问道∶『落里一位是林老爷？』『喏、喏！』胡雪指着说道∶『就是这位京里来的林老爷，现任的知府大人。老四，我特为给你做这个媒』湘云老四因为胡雪岩没有叫她，心里老大不悦；现在才知道是有意把她推给别人，愈发生气∶『谢谢耐！』她说得极快，同时将一双杏儿眼往旁边一瞟，都看得出来，她是生气了。

Gu nodded. “I shall call two for you. Ninth Miss Haoyuan, and—” “No, wait. I remember. Let the other be Fifth Miss Jiaofeng.” “Why call her?” Gu frowned. “Never mind. I have something to discuss with her.” The summons cards were completed and sent out. First to arrive was Fourth Miss Xiangyun, close at hand in the front room. Moving on tiny bound feet, she leaned on the shoulder of a maid of thirteen or fourteen. “Which gentleman is Mr Lin?” she asked on entering. “Here!” Hu pointed. “This is Mr Lin from the capital, now an appointed prefect. Fourth Miss, I have specially arranged this introduction for you.” Xiangyun had already been displeased that Hu had not called her for himself. Discovering that he meant to hand her over to someone else made her angrier still. “Thank you!” she said rapidly, casting her almond-shaped eyes aside. Everyone could see that she was angry.

原来这也是胡雪岩待客的一番苦心。这林茂先在京中亦是一个嫖客，但喜欢逛『茶室』。因为『八大胡同』的『清吟小班』，犹如上海的『长三』，而『茶室』则相当于『幺二』，前者号称『卖嘴不卖身』，非花钱花到相当程度，不能为入幕之宾；后者则比较干脆，哪怕第一次『开盘子』，只要条件谈拢了，便可灭烛留髡。林茂先走马章台，喜欢图个痛快，这就是他常逛茶室的缘故。因为如此，他举荐湘云老四，因为她在长三中以『裤带松』出名。胡雪岩心想难得与林茂先客途相逢，要为他谋一夕之欢，所以作此安排；但湘云老四未必明白其中的委曲，索性向她说明了吧。打定主意，自以趁好媛、娇凤未来以前，速办为宜。因此，等湘云老四照例一一敬酒、交代门面话，绕圈子下来最后到次席的胡雪岩时，他便含笑问道∶『我转你一个局好不好？』

Hu had actually made the arrangement out of consideration for his guest. Lin patronized courtesans in Beijing but preferred the establishments called tea rooms. The small refined-singing houses of the Eight Great Alleys were like Shanghai’s changsan establishments, while tea rooms corresponded to the yao’er class. The former professed to sell conversation rather than sexual favors; a man had to spend a substantial amount before becoming an intimate guest. The latter were more direct. Even on his first visit, provided terms were agreed, he could have the candles extinguished and stay the night. Lin liked prompt gratification, which explained his preference for tea rooms. That was why Hu had recommended Xiangyun, notorious among the changsan courtesans for being accommodating in bed. Meeting Lin by chance on his travels was a rare pleasure, and Hu wanted to arrange an enjoyable night for him. Xiangyun might not understand his intention, so he had better explain. It should be done before Haoyuan and Jiaofeng arrived. After Xiangyun had made the usual round of toasts and polite remarks and finally reached Hu in the second seat, he smiled. “May I call you over for a private conversation?”

『随便耐！奴是啥人介？高兴来，招招手就来；不高兴来，一脚踢到仔东洋大海。』胡雪岩笑一笑，向林茂先说道∶『茂翁，对不起，老四跟我为了别人的事，有点误会，我转个局跟她有说清楚了，完璧归赵。如何？』

“As you please. What am I, after all? When you want me, one wave and I come. When you do not, you kick me away into the eastern ocean.” Hu smiled and turned to Lin. “Forgive me, Mr Maoxian. Fourth Miss and I have a misunderstanding over someone else’s affairs. Let me borrow her to clear it up, then return the jade intact to its rightful owner. Will that do?”

『啊唷唷！』有个惯在花丛中混，除非大年三十不回家的『洋行小鬼』江罗勃，学着苏白说道∶『格是出新闻哉！啥叫我倪湘云老四是清倌人畹！』大家都知道这是故意曲解『完璧』取笑湘云老四；她不懂这个典故，但知道是在开她的玩笑，却是看得出来，索性老一老面皮，学四马路『野鸡』的口吻，回敬江罗勃∶『不错，阿拉是的的刮刮的清水货。「酱萝卜」，你来啥！』就在满座轰笑声中，胡雪岩将湘云老四拉到一边，促膝密语，『老四，』他说，『我替你做这个媒，你看怎么样？』『奴那哼好说弗好？耐胡老爷又看我弗起，吃仔格碗把势饭来，有啥办法？』胡雪岩原来欠了她一个情——有一回答应捧她的场，结果忘掉了；这天恰有机会补这个情，也应酬了林茂先，所以此时开门见山地问∶『林老爷要到福建去上任，只怕没有工夫到你那里「做花头」，你能不能陪陪他。』『那哼陪法？』

“Oh, my!” exclaimed Jiang Luobo, a foreign-firm clerk so constantly at home in the pleasure quarters that only New Year’s Eve brought him back to his own house. Imitating the Suzhou accent, he said, “This is news indeed! Since when has our Fourth Miss Xiangyun been a chaste courtesan?” Everyone understood that he was deliberately twisting “intact jade” into a joke about her virginity. Xiangyun did not know the allusion, but she could see that she was being teased. Brazening it out, she adopted the manner of a streetwalker from Fourth Road. “Certainly! I am pure, unadulterated fresh goods. Pickled Radish, why do you not come and try?” Her nickname for Jiang played on the sound of his name. Amid general laughter, Hu drew her aside and sat close to speak privately. “Fourth Miss, what do you think of the match I am making for you?” “How can I say it is no good? You do not think much of me yourself, Mr Hu. Earning my rice by this trade, what choice have I?” Hu owed her a favor: he had once promised to support her establishment and forgotten. Here was a chance both to make amends and to entertain Lin. He came straight to the point. “Mr Lin is leaving to take up his appointment in Fujian. I fear he has no time for an elaborate courtship at your house. Could you keep him company?” “In what way?”

『这还要说吗？』湘云老四脸一红，『呒拨格号规矩格！』她说，『传仔出去末，奴落里还有面孔见人介？』『当然也不是一个花头都不做，等下翻台过去，是我做主人；明天下午，他到你那里碰和，晚上摆个双台，下来「借干铺」。你看好不好？』『借干铺』是长三中对恩客的一种掩耳盗铃的手法，意思只是客人喝醉了，或者路太远，天时突变，临时借宿一宵，规矩是开销六两银子。当然，到底是干是湿，是没有人问的。湘云不作声，看意思是有点活动了；胡雪岩便趁机补情，『老四，』他说，『林老爷是我的朋友，你就算委屈一回，林老爷人很爽快的，出手不会太小气。另外，你到大马路方九霞去挑一副金镯头，算是我送你的。』声色场中，向来黄金能买美人心，湘云老四想一想说道∶『胡老爷。耐为朋友，格能操心法子，实头少见笃。不过格是耐胡老爷的想法，你兴俚到看奴不入眼呐？我啊弗能桠上去畹。』胡雪岩懂她的意思，是怕万一好呈不成，金镯落空，当即答说∶『总归我是心尽到了，只要林老爷今天上船到福建，明天你就到方九霞去挑镯头，好了，就这样说定了，』话宗，胡雪岩先站起来回席。

“Must I spell it out?” Xiangyun blushed. “That is not how things are done! If word got out, how could I face anyone?” “Of course there would be some of the usual formalities. We shall move to your rooms shortly, with me as host. Tomorrow afternoon he can play mahjong there, followed by a double-table dinner in the evening, and afterward ‘borrow a dry bed.’ Would that do?” She said nothing. Among changsan courtesans, “borrowing a dry bed” was a transparent pretense offered to favored clients: the man was supposedly drunk, or lived too far away, or had been caught by a change in the weather, and merely borrowed a bed for one night. The customary charge was six taels. Whether the bed was truly “dry” was nobody’s business. Seeing that Xiangyun was wavering, Hu took the chance to make amends. “Mr Lin is my friend. Make an exception this once. He is generous and will not be mean with his money. Besides, go to Fang Jiuxia’s on First Road and choose a pair of gold bracelets as my gift.” In the pleasure quarters, gold had always been able to win a beauty’s heart. Xiangyun considered. “Few men would take such trouble for a friend. But these are your wishes, Mr Hu. What if he does not care for me? I cannot force myself upon him.” Hu understood: she feared that if the affair came to nothing, so would the bracelets. “I shall have done my best in any case. If Mr Lin boards his boat for Fujian today, you may go to Fang Jiuxia’s tomorrow and choose the bracelets. There, it is settled.” With that, Hu rose and returned to the table.

其时莺莺燕燕，陆续来到，而且都带了『乌师先生』，笙歌嗷嘈，热闹非凡。就在这时候，听得楼下『相帮』高喊∶『后厢房客人。』『必是沙一心赶来了。』古应春连忙起身，迎出门外，果然就是沙一心。『应春兄，』沙一心在楼梯口拉住他说∶『我的行李已经下长江轮船了，天亮就要上船。因为你说要替我引见一位朋友，所以特为赶了来，不知道是什么朋友？倘或本来是住在上海的，等我半个月以后，从广州回来再见面，好不好。』略停一停，他接着又说∶『实不相瞒，我还要回去过瘾。』古应春考虑了一下说道∶『我要替你引见的这位朋友，就是胡雪岩胡大先生，这样，你进去先见个面，跟大家招呼一下，然后，我替你说明缘故，放你回长发栈，等你从广州回来，如果胡大先生还在上海，我们再畅叙如何？』『这倒行。』于是古应春将他引到筵席，一一介绍，其中一大半是初识。这沙一心三十多年纪，丰神俊朗，说一口带川音的京腔，音吐清亮，颇予人好感。胡雪岩很喜欢这个新朋友。他是候补同知的班子，所以彼此以官衔相称，『胡观察名满天下，今天才能识荆，可见孤陋。不过，到底也拜见了一尊大菩萨，幸何如之。』他举杯说道∶『借花献佛。』说完，一饮而尽照一照杯。

The other courtesans were arriving one after another, all with their professional musicians. Music and song filled the rooms with a cheerful clamor. Just then the attendant downstairs called, “A guest for the rear room!” “That must be Sha Yixin.” Gu rose and went out. It was indeed Sha. At the staircase, Sha took his arm. “Brother Yingchun, my luggage is already aboard the Yangtze steamer, and I must embark at daybreak. Your note said you wished to introduce a friend, so I have come specially. Who is it? If he lives in Shanghai, could I meet him when I return from Guangzhou in half a month? To be frank, I still have to go back and satisfy my opium craving.” Gu considered. “The friend is Mr Hu Xueyan himself. Come in, meet him, and greet the company. Then I shall explain and let you return to Changfa Inn. If Mr Hu is still in Shanghai when you come back from Guangzhou, we can have a proper gathering. How would that suit you?” “That would do.” Gu led him in and made the introductions. More than half the company were strangers to him. Sha was in his thirties, handsome and distinguished, speaking clear Beijing Chinese with a Sichuan inflection. His voice made a favorable impression, and Hu liked his new acquaintance. Sha held the rank of expectant deputy prefect, so they addressed one another by official titles. “Intendant Hu is known throughout the world, yet only today do I meet you. How provincial I must be! Still, I have at last paid my respects to a great benefactor. What good fortune!” Raising his cup, he added, “I borrow another’s flowers to offer them to the Buddha.” He emptied it and showed the bottom.

『不敢，不敢。』胡雪岩声明∶『第一回，我不能不干。』『胡观察吃花酒是有规矩，向不干杯。』江罗勃说道∶『今天是沙司马的面子。来，来，大家都干一杯。』沙一心人本谦和，看面子十足，赶紧站起来说∶『承各位抬爱，实在不敢当，理当我来奉敬。』说着，自己满斟一杯，干了酒不断地说∶『谢谢！』这时写局票的木盘又端上来了，古应春便看着沙一心问∶『仍旧是小金铃老三，如何？』『不，不！应春兄，我今天豁免了吧！你知道的，我今天的情形不一样。』沙一心又说∶『而且偷此片刻之暇，不向胡观察好好讨教一番，虚耗辰光，也太可惜。』『也好。』古应春点点头，『回头我另作安排。』『我已经有安排了。』胡雪岩接口说道∶『等一等我们翻到前厢房，替林太尊、沙司马饯行。』

“You are too kind,” Hu replied. “On our first meeting, I must drain my cup.” “Intendant Hu has a rule against draining his cup at courtesan dinners,” Jiang Luobo remarked. “Today he makes an exception for Deputy Prefect Sha. Come, let us all drink a full cup.” Naturally modest, Sha rose at once, grateful for the honor. “You are all too kind. It is I who ought to toast you.” He filled and emptied another cup, repeatedly thanking them. The tray for writing summons cards was brought round again. Gu looked at Sha. “Third Miss Little Golden Bell, as usual?” “No, no, Brother Yingchun. Excuse me today. You know my circumstances are different tonight. Besides, having snatched these few moments, it would be a pity not to seek Intendant Hu’s instruction instead of wasting the time.” “Very well.” Gu nodded. “I shall make another arrangement presently.” “I have already made one,” Hu put in. “Shortly we shall move to the front room for a farewell dinner in honor of Prefect Lin and Deputy Prefect Sha.”

『不敢当，不敢当。』林茂先、沙一心异口同声地说。古应春已经知道胡雪岩要为林茂先与湘云老四拉拢的本意；而他的另作安排是看胡雪岩与沙一心颇为投缘，要匀出工夫来让他们能作一次深谈，这一下正好合在一起来办，当即说道∶『各位听见了。我代胡大先生作主人。老四，你现在就回去预备吧。』湘云老四喜孜孜地站起身来，先含笑向胡雪岩说∶『格末奴先转去，拨台面先端整起来。』接着，提高了声音说∶『各位老爷，晏歇才要请过来，勿作兴溜格噢！江大少，格桩事体末，我拜托仔耐哉畹！』『包拉我身浪，一个缺。不过，老四，耐那哼谢谢我呐？』『耐讲！』『香个面孔阿好？』『瞎三话四，讲讲就呒淘成哉！』说着白了江罗勃一眼，翩然而去。

“We could not presume!” Lin and Sha exclaimed together. Gu already understood Hu’s intention of bringing Lin and Xiangyun together. His own plan was to make time for a substantial conversation between Hu and Sha, who seemed to have taken to one another. The two purposes could now be combined. “You have all heard. I shall act as host on Mr Hu’s behalf. Fourth Miss, go and prepare.” Xiangyun rose happily and smiled at Hu. “Then I shall go back and have the table set.” Raising her voice, she added, “Gentlemen, you must all come over shortly. No slipping away! Mr Jiang, I leave that to you.” “Leave it to me. Not one will be missing. But how will you thank me?” “You tell me.” “May I kiss your cheek?” “Nonsense! You cannot speak without becoming improper.” She gave him a reproachful glance and went gracefully out.

林茂先久居北方，见惯了亢爽有余、不解蕴藉的北地胭脂，这天领略了娇俏柔媚、妖娆多变的南朝金粉，大为着迷。大家都知道，这天的主客的是林沙二人，同时也从古应春『代作主人』的宣布中，意会到胡雪岩与沙一心或许有事要谈，便趁机起哄，都道不如此刻就翻台过去。『这样吧！』古应春正好重新安排，『一心兄，你就请在这里过瘾，胡大先生陪你谈谈。我先陪大家过去，回头过足了瘾再请过来。』说道，站起身来；客人因为就在前厢房，倒省了一番穿马褂、点灯笼、出门进门的麻烦。爱月楼老七却仍守着她送客的规矩，站在房门口一一招呼；等该走的客人都走了，回身向胡雪岩说道∶『胡老爷搭沙老爷请过来吧！』

Having lived long in the north, Lin was accustomed to women whose forthright manner lacked subtlety. The delicate charm and changeable allure of these southern beauties captivated him. Everyone knew that Lin and Sha were the evening’s principal guests. Gu’s announcement that he would act as host also suggested that Hu and Sha might have something to discuss, so the company eagerly proposed moving at once. “Then let us arrange it this way,” Gu said. “Brother Yixin, satisfy your craving here while Mr Hu keeps you company. I shall take the others over first. Join us when you have had enough.” He rose. Since the other room was just at the front, the guests were spared the trouble of putting on riding jackets, lighting lanterns, and going out into the street. Aiyuelou nevertheless observed her usual rule of seeing everyone off, standing in the doorway to address each guest. Once those leaving had gone, she turned to Hu. “Mr Hu, Mr Sha, this way, please.”

后面是爱月楼老七的卧室，靠里一张大铜床，已在床中间，横置了一个烟盘，两条绣花湖绉面的被子，叠成长条，上面摆了两只洋式枕头。胡雪岩虽不抽鸦片，却知道抽烟的人向左侧卧，为的是右手在上，动作方便，因而道声『请』；让沙一心躺了下来，自己在烟盘对面相陪。『沙老爷！』爱月楼老七手上持着一只明角烟盒，走来说道∶『呒拨啥好个烟膏请耐，只有「云土」，晓得阿好迁就？』说着，拖张小凳子在床前坐下来。『蛮好、蛮好。七小姐，我自己来，不敢劳动。』『呒拨格号规格畹！』『老七，』胡雪岩便说∶『你就不必客气了，我晓得你打烟也不怎么在行。既然沙老爷这么说，你就让沙老爷自己来。』『格末奴也只好恭敬勿如从命哉。』说着，将烟盒放下，检点了热茶、糖果，又去削了一盘水果来，然后说道∶『有啥事体末，招呼一声末哉，奴就来浪前头。』等她放下门帘离去时，沙一心已揭开盒盖，自己拿烟签子在水晶『太谷打』上开始打烟泡了，右手烟签、左手象牙小砧，一面打、一面卷，手法干净利落，不一会打成一个『黄、高、松』三字俱全的大烟光，装在斗门上，又转过来、转过去，一面烘、一面捏，装好了用热烟签在烟泡中间打个到底的眼子，然后抛过来将烟枪伸向胡雪岩。

Behind lay her bedroom, with a large brass bed against the inner wall. An opium tray had been placed across the middle, flanked by two embroidered Huzhou-crepe quilts folded into long bolsters and topped with Western pillows. Though Hu did not smoke opium, he knew smokers lay on their left side to leave the right hand uppermost and free to work. He invited Sha to recline and lay opposite him across the tray. Aiyuelou came with a translucent horn opium box. “I have no fine opium to offer you, Mr Sha, only Yunnan opium. I hope you can make do with it.” She drew a small stool to the bedside. “Excellent, excellent. Please let me prepare it myself, Seventh Miss. I could not trouble you.” “That would be against all custom!” “Do not stand on ceremony,” Hu said. “I know you are not particularly skilled at preparing it. Since Mr Sha asks, let him do it himself.” “Then I can only obey.” She set down the box, checked the hot tea and sweets, cut a plate of fruit, and said, “Call if you need anything. I shall be in the front room.” By the time she had lowered the curtain and gone, Sha had opened the box and begun working the opium on the crystal taiguda. With the opium pick in his right hand and a small ivory anvil in his left, he rolled and shaped it neatly and swiftly. Soon he had made a large pellet possessing all three desired qualities: golden, tall, and airy. He fitted it over the pipe bowl, turning, warming, and pressing it into place, then pierced it through the center with the heated pick. Turning toward Hu, he offered him the pipe.

『请，请。』胡雪岩急忙摇手，『我没有享「福寿膏」的福气。』听此一说，沙一心便不再客套，对准了火『沙、沙、沙』地一口气抽完，拿起烫手的山茶壶嘴对嘴喝一口热茶，眼睛闭了一下，才从鼻孔中喷出淡白色的烟雾来。这一筒烟下去，沙一心才有谈话的精神——实在是兴致。谈起胡雪岩很熟的一个人——为人骂作『汉奸』的龚孝拱。

“Please, go ahead.” Hu waved it away. “I am not fortunate enough to enjoy the ‘Paste of Happiness and Longevity.’” Sha made no further attempt at courtesy. He held the pipe to the flame and finished it in one continuous draw, then lifted the hot teapot and drank directly from its spout. His eyes closed briefly before pale smoke emerged from his nostrils. Only after this pipe did he have the energy—or rather the inclination—to converse. He began with someone Hu knew well: Gong Xiaogong, whom people denounced as a traitor to China.

此人是道光年间大名士龚定庵的儿子。龚家是杭州世家，龚定庵的父祖都是显宦，他本人才气纵横，做得极好的诗，而又不仅辞章；幼年受他外祖父金坛段玉裁之教，于『小学』——文字之学，亦有极深的造诣；但中举以后，会试不利，几番落第。原来宣宗的资质性情，很象明朝的末代皇帝思宗，他倒是有心做个英主，但才具甚短，而又缺乏知人之明，信任的宰相曹振镛，是个妨贤妨能、瞒上期下的庸才，专门劝宣宗吹毛求疵，察察为明，所以政风文风，两皆不振；试卷中的文章好坏在其次，最要紧的是格式不能错，错了就是违犯『功令』，文章再好，亦遭摒弃。龚定庵几次名落孙山，都是为此。

Gong was the son of Gong Ding’an, the celebrated man of letters of the Daoguang reign. The Gongs were an old Hangzhou family. Ding’an’s father and grandfather had held distinguished offices. He himself possessed prodigious talent and wrote excellent poetry, but his accomplishments were not confined to literary composition. Taught in childhood by his maternal grandfather Duan Yucai of Jintan, he had also acquired profound learning in philology, the study of written language. After passing the provincial examination, however, he repeatedly failed at the metropolitan level. Emperor Xuanzong’s temperament and abilities resembled those of Sizong, the last Ming emperor. He wished to be an enlightened ruler, but lacked both ability and judgment of men. His trusted chief minister, Cao Zhenyong, was a mediocrity who resented talent, deceived his superiors, and oppressed his inferiors. He constantly urged the emperor to mistake fault-finding and petty scrutiny for wisdom. Government and literary life both languished. In examination papers, the quality of the writing mattered less than observing the prescribed format. A mistake was a breach of official regulations, and even the finest essay would then be rejected. This was why Gong Ding’an repeatedly failed.

好不容易会试中了，大家都说他必点『翰林院庶吉士』，哪知殿试卷子因为书法不佳，不与翰林之选。龚定庵牢骚满腹，无可发泄，叫他的姨太太、丫头都用『大卷子』练书法，真有写得『黑、大、光、圆』四字俱全，极好的『馆阁体』的，每每向人夸耀，说『此举如能赴试，必点翰林』。其时有个满洲才女，叫『西林太清春』，做的词与纳兰性德齐名。她是贝勒奕绘的侧福晋，住宅在京城西南角的太平湖，就是后来的醇王府，也就是光绪皇帝出生的『潜邸』。龚定庵因为在宗人府当差，又因为深通文字音韵之学，会说满州话及蒙古话，所以不但为了『回公事』，经常出入亲贵府邸，而且亦颇得若干亲贵的赏识。奕绘人很开通，不禁西林太清与朝贵名士唱和，龚定庵就是与西林太清春诗笺往还最密的一个人。

When he finally passed the metropolitan examination, everyone expected him to be selected as a Hanlin bachelor. But his handwriting in the palace examination was deemed poor, and he was excluded. Full of resentment with no outlet, he had his concubines and maids practice calligraphy on large examination sheets. Some achieved the black, large, polished, rounded script admired in the academies and official halls. He would boast of them: “If this one could sit the examination, she would certainly enter the Hanlin Academy.” At that time, a talented Manchu woman named Xilin Taiqingchun wrote lyrics esteemed alongside those of Nalan Xingde. She was a secondary consort of Prince Yihui. Their residence beside Taiping Lake in the southwestern corner of Beijing later became Prince Chun’s mansion, the birthplace of the Guangxu Emperor before his accession. Gong worked in the Imperial Clan Court. His command of philology and phonology enabled him to speak Manchu and Mongolian, so he not only entered princely residences frequently to report on official business but also won the appreciation of several members of the imperial family. Yihui was broad-minded and did not forbid Taiqingchun from exchanging poems with eminent officials and scholars. Gong was among her most frequent correspondents.

龚定庵因为科名晚，到了四十多岁，还只是一个『司官』，前程有限，俸禄微薄，便动了解官之念，那里江淮的盐商还很阔，而盐商又多喜附庸风雅，象龚定庵这样名动公卿的人，『打秋风』亦可以过很舒服的日子。主意一定，毅然而行，不道京城里已起了谣言，说他解官是迫不得已，因为与西林太清春之间，有一段不可告人的秘密，倘不辞官出京，便有不测之祸；不幸的是，辞官不久，就了一个书院的山长，一夕暴毙，实在是中风，而传说他是被毒死的。龚孝拱是龚定庵的长子，名字别号甚多，晚年自号『半伦』，据说他自己以为君臣、父子、夫妇、兄弟、朋友这五伦之中，无一可取，不过有一个爱妾，勉强好说尚存『半伦』。由这个别号，可以想见是个狂士。

Having gained his degree late, Gong remained only a ministry official into his forties. His prospects were limited and his salary small, so he considered resigning. The salt merchants of the Yangtze and Huai regions were still wealthy and fond of displays of literary culture. A man of Gong’s fame among the great could live comfortably enough by soliciting their patronage. Once decided, he acted resolutely. Rumors had already arisen in Beijing, however, that he was being forced out: he and Taiqingchun shared a shameful secret, and unless he resigned and left the capital, disaster would befall him. Unfortunately, not long after resigning he became the head of an academy and died suddenly one night. In fact, he suffered a stroke, but rumor claimed that he had been poisoned. Gong Xiaogong was his eldest son. He used many names and literary sobriquets; in later life he called himself “Half a Relationship.” It was said he regarded none of the five relationships—ruler and minister, father and son, husband and wife, brothers, and friends—as worth preserving. Only a beloved concubine allowed him, with some indulgence, to claim that half a relationship survived. The name alone suggested an eccentric.

龚孝拱天资甚高，由于遗传及家学，亦精通满洲、蒙古文字，比他父亲更胜一筹的是，还会英文。咸丰年间，龚孝拱住在上海，由一个姓曾的广东人介绍，得识英国公使威妥玛；英法联军之役，威妥玛北上，带了龚考拱治文书、备顾问。及至英法联军破京城，火烧圆明园，传说是龚孝拱领的头，而且趁火打劫，盗取了一批珍宝，在上海租界上作富公，挥霍无度，穷困而死，这就是他为人骂作『汉奸』的由来。『这是冤枉他的。』胡雪岩答说∶『我同他很熟。狂是有的，不过还不致于做汉奸。』『说得是。此人很可惜！』沙一心说∶『现在讲究洋务，真正能够摸透洋人性情的并不多，龚孝拱是其中之一；他如果不是自暴自弃，在现在可以替那班有心学洋人长处，或者真想做一番事业的督抚，帮许多忙。』『那末照一翁看，当今督抚之中，哪几位是真想做一番事业的？』胡雪岩随口问说。『象张振轩就是。』

Xiaogong was exceptionally gifted. Through inherited talent and family learning, he too mastered Manchu and Mongolian, and surpassed his father in knowing English as well. During the Xianfeng reign he lived in Shanghai, where a Cantonese man named Zeng introduced him to the British envoy, Thomas Wade. When the Anglo-French forces campaigned in the north, Wade took Gong with him to handle documents and serve as an adviser. After the allies entered Beijing and burned the Yuanmingyuan, stories claimed that Gong had led them and taken advantage of the destruction to steal treasures. He was said to have lived as a wealthy man in Shanghai’s foreign settlement, squandered his money, and died in poverty. Hence the denunciations of him as a traitor. “That is an injustice,” Hu said. “I knew him well. He was eccentric, certainly, but not a traitor.” “Quite right. A great pity!” Sha replied. “Foreign affairs matter so much now, yet few men truly understand foreigners’ ways. Gong was one. Had he not given himself up to despair, he could have done much for governors and governors-general who sincerely wished to learn from foreign strengths or accomplish something worthwhile.” “Then which of the present governors and governors-general,” Hu asked casually, “do you consider genuinely intent on achieving something?” “Zhang Zhenxuan, for one.”

第三章｜CHAPTER THREE

张振岩便是现署直隶总督的张树声。提到此人，胡雪岩不能不关心，因为左宗棠既然有意要驱逐李鸿章在两江的势力，眼前就会跟张树声直接发生利害冲突，有机会倒要打听打听这个人。『听说张制军是秀才的底子，由军功起家。现在京里一班清流，架子大得不得了，行伍出身的老粗，能吃得消他们？』胡雪岩又说∶『以前在广东，还可说是天高皇帝远，现在驻扎天津，南来北往由海道经过那里的翰林不知多少，他这个总督恐怕很头痛吧？』『张振轩倒不算老粗。他是廪生出身——』

Zhang Zhenxuan was Zhang Shusheng, the acting governor-general of Zhili. Hu could scarcely remain indifferent to any mention of him. Since Zuo Zongtang intended to drive Li Hongzhang’s influence out of Liangjiang, his interests would soon clash directly with Zhang’s. Here was a good opportunity to learn something about the man. “I hear Governor-General Zhang began as a licentiate and rose through military service,” Hu said. “The moral critics in the capital put on tremendous airs these days. Can a rough soldier stand up to them? When he was in Guangdong, he could at least say Heaven was high and the emperor far away. Now that he is stationed in Tianjin, who knows how many Hanlin scholars pass through on their journeys north and south by sea? Being governor-general must give him a headache.” “Zhang Zhenxuan is hardly a rough soldier. He was a stipendiary licentiate—”

『原来是禀生。』胡雪岩觉得说张树声是行伍出身老粗，未免失言，因为他知道廪生在秀才之中，仅仅次于拔贡，一县之主，县衙门里可以领一份钱粮，童生进学，亦须廪生作保，照例亦须送一份谢礼，反以资深的秀才，不但要有真才实学，而且品行也要端正，否则学政是不肯将这个有限名额而有丰富收入的廪生，轻易畀予的。『张振轩这个廪生出身，后来占了很大的便宜。』沙一心继续谈张树声的经历，『他起先在李合肥的淮军中，名气不但比不上程学启、刘秉璋、郭松林、刘铭传，甚至还不及潘鼎新。可是由军功保到五品，改了同组，由武入文，这就占便宜了。同治四年夏天署理淮海道；刘六麻子是直隶总督，官拜一品，可是他情愿不要这个一品官员，回合肥老家去吃闲饭。雪翁，你知道不知道，这是什么道理？』

“A stipendiary licentiate!” Hu felt that he had spoken too hastily in dismissing Zhang as an uncultivated soldier. Among licentiates, he knew, stipendiary scholars ranked just below those selected for tribute scholarships. They received an allowance from the county treasury; candidates admitted to the county school also required their sponsorship and customarily gave them a fee in thanks. Such senior licentiates therefore needed both substantial learning and good conduct. Otherwise the provincial education commissioner would hardly confer one of these limited, well-remunerated places upon them. “His standing as a stipendiary licentiate later gave him a considerable advantage,” Sha Yixin continued. “At first, in Li Hongzhang’s Huai Army, his reputation was far below those of Cheng Xueqi, Liu Bingzhang, Guo Songlin, and Liu Mingchuan—even below Pan Dingxin’s. But when military merit brought him promotion to the fifth rank, he transferred to a deputy prefect’s post. Moving from military to civil office was a great advantage. In the summer of the fourth year of Tongzhi, he became acting intendant of the Huaihai Circuit. Liu the Sixth Pockmark was governor-general of Zhili, a first-rank official, yet he preferred to give up that first-rank post and retire to an idle life at home in Hefei. Do you know why, Mr Xueyan?”

这道理胡雪岩懂。『刘六麻子』是刘铭传的外号，他的故事，胡雪岩也听人谈过。原来一省绿营兵的最高弄官是提督，通称『军门』，在军队里很神气；一遇见督抚就矮了半截，因为总督挂兵部尚书衔；巡抚挂兵部侍郎衔，都算是兵部的『堂官』，也都是提督的上司，一品的提督要受二品的巡抚的节制；而且正式见礼时，要用『堂参』的大礼。刘铭传自命为儒将，刻过一部《大潜山房诗集》，认为武官即使一品亦不值钱，所以告病开缺，潜居在他的『山房』中。『是的，武官不值钱。张振轩那时虽只是一个道员，可是一升直隶臬司，一帆风顺，同治十年就以漕运总督署理两江总督。他之得意，李合肥自然很提携他，关系交情不同泛泛，反以这回李合肥丁忧开缺，特保张振轩署理，自然是有作用的。』『啊，啊，我懂了。』胡雪岩恍然大悟，『原来他是替李合肥暂且看家。』『正是。不过，李合肥不知道，昔日部属，已非吴下防蒙，张振轩跟清流结交上了，那是大前年——』大前年——光绪五年十一月，两江总督沈葆桢病殁在任上，朝命以两广总督刘坤一调任两江；留下来的缺，由张树声以广西巡抚升任。

Hu did know. “Liu the Sixth Pockmark” was Liu Mingchuan’s nickname, and Hu had heard his story. The highest commander of a province’s Green Standard troops was the provincial commander-in-chief, commonly addressed as “General.” He cut a splendid figure among soldiers, but became a lesser person in the presence of a governor or governor-general. A governor-general held the concurrent title of minister of war, and a governor that of vice-minister; both thus ranked as heads of the ministry, and both were the commander-in-chief’s superiors. A first-rank commander had to obey a second-rank governor and perform the full ceremonial obeisance when formally received. Liu Mingchuan considered himself a scholar-general and had published a collection of verse entitled Poems from the Daqian Mountain Lodge. He felt that even first-rank military office counted for little, so he pleaded illness, relinquished his appointment, and secluded himself in his “mountain lodge.” “Yes, military rank counts for little. Zhang Zhenxuan was only a circuit intendant then, but once he became provincial judge of Zhili, his advancement went smoothly. By the tenth year of Tongzhi, he was governor-general of Grain Transport and acting governor-general of Liangjiang. Li Hongzhang naturally did much to promote him. Their relationship was closer than most. So when Li left office to mourn his mother and specially recommended Zhang to act in his place, he had a purpose.” “Ah, I see!” Hu exclaimed. “He was simply leaving Zhang to mind the house for him.” “Exactly. What Li did not realize was that his former subordinate was no longer the unpolished junior he had once known. Zhang had made connections with the moral critics. That began three years ago—” In the eleventh month of the fifth year of Guangxu, Shen Baozhen, governor-general of Liangjiang, died in office. The court transferred Liu Kunyi, governor-general of Guangdong and Guangxi, to Liangjiang, and promoted Zhang Shusheng from governor of Guangxi to fill the vacancy he left.

广州是八旗驻防之地，广州将军叫长善，出身满洲八大贵族之一的他他拉氏。此人很风雅，乐予奖掖后进，尤其是没有满汉的畛域之见。将军署的后花园，颇有花木之胜，长善常常邀请广州的一班少年名士作文酒之会。前年庚辰科会试，闱中由工部尚书翁同齸主持，实学真才多能脱颖而出，其广东的梁鼎芬、广西的于式枚便常常作长善座上客，而且都点了翰林。在广州时，张树声的儿子张华奎，亦常受长善的招邀，所以跟于式枚、梁鼎芬，还有一个文名盛於于、梁但禀表会试不幸落第的江西人文廷式。都是极熟的朋友。这时张华奎随父到直隶总督任上，便经常进京，与于、梁、文等三人盘桓。

Guangzhou had an Eight Banner garrison. Its general, Changshan, belonged to the Tatara clan, one of the eight great Manchu noble houses. He was a cultivated man, eager to encourage younger talent and, particularly, free of prejudice between Manchus and Han Chinese. The rear garden of his headquarters was renowned for its flowers and trees, and he often invited the city’s promising young scholars to gather there for literary conversation and wine. At the metropolitan examination in the gengchen year, two years before, Minister of Works Weng Tonghe had presided, allowing men of genuine learning to distinguish themselves. Liang Dingfen of Guangdong and Yu Shimei of Guangxi, both frequent guests of Changshan, had been selected for the Hanlin Academy. Zhang Shusheng’s son, Zhang Huakui, had also often received Changshan’s invitations while in Guangzhou. He was therefore a close friend of Yu and Liang, and of Wen Tingshi from Jiangxi, whose literary reputation surpassed theirs but who had unfortunately failed the metropolitan examination. Having accompanied his father to the governor-generalship of Zhili, Zhang Huakui now frequently visited the capital and spent time with these three friends.

虽说他乡遇故，旧雨情深，但张华奎却是另有企图。原来这几年言路的势力极大，尤其是一班兼讲官的翰林，一言九鼎，连慈禧太后及恭王都不能不听，这班人就是『清流』，其中最有名的四个人，号为『翰林四谏』。于式枚、梁鼎芬虽是翰林后辈，但文名久著，所以亦常与清流有往还；而张华奎便是凭借了于、梁的关系，得以上文张佩纶、盛吴这一班响当当大清流。这张华奎是个举人，年纪虽轻，人很能干，而且赋性廉和可亲，加以『北洋分所』积存的『公款』很多，凡是应酬京官，无不可以报销，使得张华奎愈发长袖善舞，清流们集会，不论是在松筠庵，还是『畿辅先哲寺』，或者陶然亭、崇效寺这些名胜之处，乃至于八大胡同『相公』的下处，筵宴所需，都是他来备办，有事需要奔走联络，张华奎更是义不容辞，因而得了个『青牛腿』的外号。

Though old friendships were warm and meeting familiar faces away from home was a pleasure, Zhang Huakui had another object. In recent years, officials entitled to remonstrate had acquired immense influence. The Hanlin scholars who also lectured to the throne were especially powerful: their words carried such weight that even the Empress Dowager Cixi and Prince Gong could not disregard them. These were the “Pure Stream,” the court’s moral critics; the four most famous were known as the Four Hanlin Remonstrants. Yu Shimei and Liang Dingfen were junior Hanlin members, but their longstanding literary distinction brought them into frequent contact with these men. Through Yu and Liang, Zhang Huakui gained access to such leading critics as Zhang Peilun and Sheng Wu. Zhang Huakui was a provincial graduate, young but capable, with a modest and agreeable disposition. Moreover, the Beiyang branch office had accumulated substantial “public funds,” against which any expense incurred in entertaining metropolitan officials could be charged. This gave his talents still greater scope. Wherever the critics met—at Songyun Hermitage, the Shrine of Former Worthies of the Capital Region, such celebrated places as Taoran Pavilion and Chongxiao Temple, or even the lodgings of male entertainers in the Eight Great Lanes—he provided their banquets. Whenever errands or arrangements were required, he willingly undertook them. Thus he acquired the nickname “the Green Ox’s Legs.”

『青牛』是清流的谐音。民间家家有『春牛图』，春为东，东为木，木色青，所以『青牛』也就是春牛。画春牛图时，头、身、角、耳、腹、尾、胫、蹄、部位分明。因而好事者，用青牛的各部分，来形容清流中人，牛头是同治皇帝的师傅李鸿藻，他门下两张——张之洞、张佩纶是牛身、牛腹。也有人说，李鸿藻是驱牛的勾芒神，张佩纶才是牛头，因为他头上的一对角厉害不过，凡被触及，必受巨创。张华奎因为替清流效奔走之劳，所以名之为『腿』；但也有人说，他连『清流腿』都不够资格，只是『清流靴子』为『清流腿』服务而已。

“Green ox,” qingniu, sounded like qingliu, “Pure Stream.” Every household had its picture of the spring ox. Spring belonged to the east, the east to wood, and wood to the colour green; a green ox was therefore a spring ox. In these pictures the head, body, horns, ears, belly, tail, legs, and hooves were distinctly marked. Wits accordingly used the various parts to represent members of the Pure Stream. The head was Li Hongzao, tutor to the Tongzhi emperor; his two followers named Zhang—Zhang Zhidong and Zhang Peilun—were the body and belly. Others said Li Hongzao was Goumang, the deity driving the ox, and that Zhang Peilun was its head: no one could withstand those two horns, and anyone they struck suffered grievously. Since Zhang Huakui did the running about for the critics, they called him the legs. Some, however, said he was not qualified even for that: he was merely the Pure Stream’s boots, serving its legs.

不管是『清流腿』还是『清流靴子』，张华奎很受人瞩目是事实。不过因此而引起了李鸿章门下的敌视，认为他『图谋不轨』，第一是因为他常巴结翁同齸，而翁同齸一向是与李鸿章不睦，同时清流多为北派领袖李鸿藻门下，而翁同齸是南派巨擘，对政事的见解，一向是有差异的；第二，张华奎拼命拉拢清流，显然是在为他父亲培养声名，目的是想取李鸿章而代之。这些加油添酱的谗言，不断传到合肥，在『闭门读礼』的李鸿章不由得也动了疑心。他的一班徒党，因而开始谋划逐张迎李之计，不久便找到了可乘之机。

Whether legs or boots, Zhang Huakui certainly attracted attention. He also incurred the hostility of Li Hongzhang’s followers, who accused him of disloyal ambitions. First, he constantly cultivated Weng Tonghe, who had always been at odds with Li Hongzhang. Most of the Pure Stream, meanwhile, followed Li Hongzao, leader of the northern faction, whereas Weng was a leading figure of the southern faction; their political views had always differed. Second, Zhang Huakui’s energetic courtship of the critics plainly aimed to build up his father’s reputation so that he could replace Li Hongzhang. Embellished accusations of this kind reached Hefei continually. Secluded there in the ritual observances of mourning, Li himself began to grow suspicious. His followers accordingly started planning to oust Zhang and bring Li back. Before long they found their opportunity.

原来张佩纶满腹经纶，颇有用世之志，张华奎便向他父献计，仿照当年左宗棠奏调袁葆恒来提高本人声价的办法，不妨奏调张佩纶『帮办北洋军务』，专门督办水师。张树声同意以后，张华奎极力向张佩纶游尽；那时产洋的水师，已拥有好几艘铁甲兵轮，规模壮阔，前程无量，张佩纶怦然心动，终于同意了。于是天津、保定等处，很快地传出消息，还说张佩纶帮办北洋军务后，将大加整顿，『四道八镇』，一律要参。直隶总督属下，有四名道员，八名总兵，总兵驻防之地称为『镇』；四道八镇便是直隶文武官员的经制，当然全部都是李鸿章所派的。

Zhang Peilun was full of learning and eager to put it to public use. Zhang Huakui therefore advised his father to follow Zuo Zongtang’s earlier example: Zuo had enhanced his own standing by petitioning for Yuan Baoheng’s services. Why not request Zhang Peilun as assistant commissioner for Beiyang military affairs, with particular responsibility for the navy? Once his father agreed, Huakui worked hard to persuade Zhang Peilun. The Beiyang fleet already possessed several ironclad warships; its scale was impressive and its prospects boundless. Zhang was greatly tempted and eventually consented. News soon spread through Tianjin, Baoding, and elsewhere. It was also said that, once appointed, Zhang would institute sweeping reforms and impeach all “four intendants and eight brigade commanders.” The governor-general of Zhili had four circuit intendants and eight brigade commanders under him; each brigade’s station was called a zhen. The phrase thus denoted the province’s regular civil and military establishment. All these men had, of course, been appointed by Li Hongzhang.

不道在此要紧关头，张树声父子一则操之过急；二则不明京朝掌故，以至于走错了一步。原来封疆大吏，准许奏调京官到省任职，但不准奏调翰林。这个禁例在乾隆年间更为严格。因为翰林如兼日讲起居注官，随传在皇帝身边，一言一动，无不深知；而且有机会看到各种奏章，参预国家机密，如为疆吏所奏调，便有泄密之虞，因而有此厉禁。到得洪杨以后，禁例虽不如以前之严，但第一要看请奏调的人，够不够分量；第二要奏调的时机，是否确有需要。当年左宗棠是封拜相的勋臣；奏调袁葆恒总理粮台，又有正当大举西征，用兵深资倚赖的理由，自然容易照准。如今张树声的资格远不如左宗棠，且亦非军务所必需，因而请奏调张佩纶的折子一到军机处，竟奉旨驳斥。这一下不但张树声以封疆大吏碰这么个硬钉子，大伤威望，张佩纶的面子更加难看。

At this critical moment, however, Zhang Shusheng and his son made a false move. They were overhasty and unfamiliar with the precedents governing court appointments. Provincial governors-general and governors could petition to have metropolitan officials transferred to their provinces, but not members of the Hanlin Academy. The prohibition had been particularly strict under Qianlong. Hanlin scholars who also served as daily lecturers and recorders of the emperor’s activities could be summoned to his presence at any time and knew his every word and action. They also had opportunities to read memorials and share in state secrets. Transferring them at a provincial chief’s request created a risk of disclosure; hence the strict rule. Since the Taiping rebellion, enforcement had relaxed, but two questions still mattered: whether the petitioner had sufficient standing, and whether the appointment was truly necessary at that moment. When Zuo requested Yuan Baoheng to direct his supply administration, he was a distinguished commander honoured with a noble title and high ministerial rank, about to undertake a major western campaign in which Yuan’s assistance was essential. Approval had naturally been easy to obtain. Zhang Shusheng’s standing fell far short of Zuo’s, and there was no pressing military need. When his memorial requesting Zhang Peilun reached the Grand Council, it was rejected by imperial order. This sharp rebuff gravely damaged the governor-general’s prestige; for Zhang Peilun, it was still more humiliating.

照张佩纶的想法，他应该是『诸侯之上客』，张树声应该北面以师礼相事，如今答应帮办北洋军务，已嫌委屈；张树声果然有心延揽，应该设法疏通军机，用『特旨』派他到北洋，才够面子。加今上谕中责备张树声『冒昧』，确是太冒昧了。李鸿章一系的北洋官僚，看到张树声碰钉子，自然高兴；又听说张佩纶对张家父子有不满的表示，更是大喜过望，认为挑拨离间的良机，决不可失。恰好张树声上奏的那天有『考差』——两榜出身的京官，须经考试合格，才能放出去当乡试主考；一任考官，所得可以维持一两年的生活，所以绝少有人放弃考差；但张佩纶因为有丧服在身，不能派任考官，考差自然不必参加。这个缘故，外人不会知道，因而别有用心者，就可以造他一个谣言，说他故意避考，在家等待准为张树声所请的上谕，以便走马上任。这个中伤的谣言，传布得很快也很广；张佩纶的清誉大损，不免恼羞成怒，自然是迁怒到张家父子身上。

Zhang Peilun considered himself worthy of being “the honoured guest of princes.” Zhang Shusheng ought to have treated him with the reverence of a pupil towards a master. Merely agreeing to assist with Beiyang military affairs had already seemed a concession. If the governor-general truly wished to recruit him, he should have arranged matters with the Grand Council and secured a special imperial appointment to Beiyang, giving him the dignity he deserved. Now the edict rebuked Zhang Shusheng for presumption. Presumptuous he certainly had been! Li Hongzhang’s officials in Beiyang rejoiced at the rebuff. Hearing that Zhang Peilun had expressed resentment towards father and son, they were delighted beyond measure. Such an opportunity to divide them must not be lost. It happened that, on the day Zhang Shusheng submitted his memorial, an examination was held for prospective provincial examiners. Metropolitan officials with provincial or metropolitan degrees had to pass it before being sent out to preside over provincial examinations. One such appointment could provide enough income for a year or two, so few men willingly missed the qualifying examination. Zhang Peilun, however, was in mourning and ineligible for appointment; he therefore had no need to sit it. Outsiders did not know this. Malicious people could thus spread a rumour that he had deliberately stayed away, waiting at home for the edict approving Zhang Shusheng’s request so that he could set off for his new post. The slander spread swiftly and widely. His reputation for integrity suffered severely, and his humiliation turned to anger—naturally redirected at the Zhangs.

『丰润学士的气量小是大家都知道的，他一定会复仇，张振轩弄巧成拙，直督一定保不住。』沙一心说∶『现在只是在一个可以让李合肥夺情回任的理由，这个理由一找到，张振轩就要交卸。』这段内幕，对胡雪岩很有用；原以为李鸿章即会回任，也是父母之丧二十七个月以后的事，不过只要有理由，随时可以回。照此看来，左宗棠想驱逐李鸿章在两江的势力，应该加速进行才是。其时沙一心的瘾已过足，便由胡雪岩陪着到湘云老四妆阁中，飞觞醉月地闹了一回酒。沙一心起身告辞，余客亦知胡雪岩与古应春第二天一早要左宗棠巡视制造局，都说要走，只有林茂先在湘云老四那里『借干铺』。『沙一心这个人很有用，』在归途中，胡雪岩对古应春说∶『你以后不妨跟他多联络联络，他对淮军及北洋的情形很熟，有事可以请他打听。』『我的原意就是如此。小爷叔放心好了，我会安排。』

“Everyone knows how narrow-minded the Academician from Fengrun is. He will certainly take revenge. Zhang Zhenxuan has outsmarted himself and cannot possibly keep Zhili,” Sha said. “All that is needed now is a reason for Li Hongzhang to be recalled before completing his mourning. Once they find one, Zhang must hand over the office.” This inside information was useful to Hu. He had assumed that even if Li returned, it would be only after the twenty-seven months of parental mourning. Yet with a suitable reason, he could return at any time. Zuo ought therefore to accelerate his efforts to expel Li’s influence from Liangjiang. By now Sha’s craving for opium had been satisfied. Hu accompanied him to Fourth Miss Xiangyun’s rooms, where they drank merrily, passing the cups beneath the moon. When Sha rose to leave, the other guests remembered that Hu and Gu were to accompany Zuo on an inspection of the arsenal early the next morning and also took their leave. Only Lin Maoxian stayed on for a “dry bed” at Fourth Miss Xiangyun’s. “Sha Yixin is a useful man,” Hu told Gu on the way home. “You might keep in closer touch with him. He knows the Huai Army and Beiyang well. We can ask him to make inquiries when necessary.” “That was my intention from the beginning. Leave it to me, Little Uncle. I shall arrange it.”

江南制造局在上海县城外，濒临黄浦江的高昌庙，本来是一片荒地，自从曾国藩奏请设制造局以后，人烟日起，造一条石子马路，东通县城南门。不过左宗棠这天仍旧是在天前宫后辕前面下船，沿黄浦江直达制造局的专用码头，制造局的总办，候补道李勉林用他的绿呢大轿，将左宗棠接到大堂，然后引见属员，一一参谒。接下来请示∶先看哪一处？『先看船坞吧』左宗棠说∶『我去年陛辞出京，上头特别交代，洋防要紧，要我分外留意。制造局的船坞，规模虽不及福建，到底是中国第二个造船厂，能人尽其用、地尽其用、物尽其用，对洋防亦颇有裨益。』这一段开场白，便有些教训的意义，李勉林听入耳中，当然不很舒服，脸上不免有尴尬之色，见此光景，胡雪岩便在一旁替李勉林说好话，总算将场面圆过来了。

The Jiangnan Arsenal stood at Gaochangmiao, on the Huangpu River outside Shanghai’s county town. The site had once been wasteland, but since Zeng Guofan petitioned for the arsenal’s establishment, settlement had steadily increased. A gravel road led east to the town’s south gate. That day, however, Zuo boarded a boat in front of his temporary headquarters behind the Temple of the Heavenly Empress and travelled straight down the Huangpu to the arsenal’s own landing. Its director, the expectant circuit intendant Li Mianlin, received him in his large green-cloth sedan chair and conveyed him to the main hall. There he presented his subordinate officers, who paid their respects in turn, before asking which section His Excellency wished to see first. “The dockyard,” Zuo replied. “When I took leave of the throne last year, particular emphasis was laid upon maritime defence, and I was instructed to give it special attention. Your dockyard may be smaller than Fujian’s, but it is nevertheless China’s second shipyard. If its men, grounds, and materials are all put to their fullest use, it can contribute substantially to our coastal defences.” This opening address carried a hint of reprimand. Li Mianlin was naturally uncomfortable, and his embarrassment showed. Hu intervened with a few words in Li’s favour and managed to smooth the occasion over.
