船坞中乱糟糟一片，看不出一个名堂来，左宗棠只好问了∶『彭宫保整年巡阅长江海口、江防、洋防的形势，周览无遗，写信给我，以兵船不敷调度为虑，说至少要添造小火轮十号，照我看，十号亦还不够，最好再能仿造新式快船五艘，你看你这里能不能造？』『小火轮能造，新式快船，限于机器，力所不逮。』『那末，造小火轮每一号要多少钱呢？』『这要估起来看。』话又有些碰僵了，幸好左宗棠没有在意只问∶『要多少日子才能估得出来？』『估价欲求精确，还得找福建船政局，他们那里图说全备，材料的行情也比较准。大人如果决意要造，局里马上派人到福建，大概有一个月的工夫，细帐就可以出来了。』『好！请你马上就办。『船坞旁边就是枪炮厂，左宗棠对这里很感兴趣，因为西征，得力就在器械精良；尤其是对洋枪，他已经很内行了，但看得多，用得多，洋枪如何制成，却还是初次见识，所以从炼钢厂看起，每一部门都看得很仔细。

The dockyard was in such confusion that Zuo could make little of it. He had to ask directly. “Grand Guardian Peng spends the year inspecting the Yangtze’s estuary and its river and sea defences. He has surveyed every aspect and written to me expressing concern that there are too few warships to meet our needs. He says at least ten small steamers must be added. I think even ten are insufficient; it would be better to build five vessels on the latest fast-ship model as well. Can you construct them here?” “We can build small steamers. The new fast vessels are beyond the capacity of our machinery.” “Then how much would each small steamer cost?” “We should have to prepare an estimate.” Again the conversation threatened to become awkward. Fortunately Zuo took no offence, asking only how long the estimate would require. “For accuracy, we should still have to consult the Fujian Naval Dockyard. They have complete plans and fuller information on material prices. If Your Excellency is determined to proceed, we shall send someone to Fujian at once. The detailed figures should be ready in about a month.” “Very well. Please see to it immediately.” Beside the dockyard stood the arms factory, which greatly interested Zuo. His western campaigns had owed much to superior equipment, and he was particularly knowledgeable about foreign rifles. Yet for all his experience of seeing and using them, this was his first opportunity to observe their manufacture. Beginning with the steelworks, he inspected every department closely.

最后到了检验处，附设有个靶场，乒乓乒乓地声音很热闹。左宗棠一踏了进去，坐在高凳上的一个老头子跳了下来，躲到一边；李勉林便喊∶『姚司务，见见左大人！』这姚司务面红似火，发白如银，一双眼一大一小，大的那只右眼，炯炯有神；手臂亦是一粗一细，侔不相伦。左宗棠平生阅历甚富，看过不少异人；一看这姚司务形相古怪，不由得便加了几分注意。等姚司务磕过一个头起身，李勉林便看着左宗棠说∶『这姚司务是制造局一宝，不管什么枪，经他手里出去的，「准头」一定好。』『喔，』左宗棠对军械的兴趣最浓，当下抬起头来，看了一下问∶『这就是你验枪的所在？』『是。』李勉林代为回答。『怎么验法？』『说起来大人恐怕不信，他只是瞄一眼、开一枪就知道了。』『这倒是神乎其技了。』左宗棠欣然说道∶『我倒要见识见识。』『是。』李勉林转脸对姚司务说∶『你演练演练给大人看。』

At last they reached the inspection section, which had a firing range attached. Shots cracked noisily. As Zuo entered, an old man jumped down from a high stool and retreated to one side. “Master Yao,” Li Mianlin called, “come and pay your respects to His Excellency Zuo!” Yao’s face was red as fire and his hair white as silver. His eyes differed in size; the larger, the right, shone brightly. His arms were likewise mismatched, one thick and the other thin. Zuo had seen much of the world and encountered many unusual people, but Yao’s singular appearance caught his attention at once. When the old man had risen from his kowtow, Li said, “Master Yao is one of the arsenal’s treasures. Any rifle that passes through his hands is certain to shoot true.” Ordnance was Zuo’s keenest interest. Looking up, he asked, “Is this where you test the rifles?” “Yes,” Li answered for Yao. “How do you test them?” “Your Excellency may find it hard to believe. He takes one look down the sights and fires one shot; then he knows.” “That is extraordinary skill!” Zuo said with pleasure. “I should like to see it.” “Certainly.” Li turned to Yao. “Give His Excellency a demonstration.”

姚司务似乎很木讷，连一声『是』都不会答应，只点一点头去掇开那张高凳，意思是站着验枪。『不，不！』左宗棠急忙阻止，『你照平常一样。平常坐着，现在不是坐着。』姚司务不敢答应，仍旧须李勉林说一声∶『你照大人的吩咐。』姚司务这才又将高凳搬回原处，踩着凳上所附的踏级，坐了上去。他面前是用墙砌出来的，狭长的一条弄堂，尽头处是个六个同心圆的靶子，中心弹痕累累；姚司务便大声喊道∶『换个靶！』

Yao seemed so inarticulate that he could not even say yes. He merely nodded and moved the high stool away, intending to test the rifles standing up. “No, no!” Zuo stopped him hastily. “Do as you normally do. If you usually sit, sit now.” Yao dared make no response. Li had to tell him, “Do as His Excellency instructs.” Only then did Yao replace the stool, climb its attached steps, and sit down. Before him, walls enclosed a long, narrow passage. At its far end stood a target of six concentric circles, its centre riddled with bullet marks. Yao called loudly, “A new target!”

枪靶后面有人在照料，顿时换了新靶。左宗棠看他左面摆着两个长木箱，右面又有两个大箩筐，里面乱堆着枪枝，长木箱中是刚修好的枪，有个人在照管。『来！』听得姚司务这一声，那人便取一枝枪，抛了上去，姚司务左手接住，交到右手，眯起眼睛看了一下，便即听得『砰』的一声；接着又听得『彭』的一声，那枝枪已被他扔在前面那个箩筐里了。左宗棠根本没有看清楚，他是如何单手在扣扳机，不过新靶上正中红心有个小洞，却看得很清楚。

Someone behind the target attended to it, and a fresh one appeared immediately. Zuo noticed two long wooden chests on Yao’s left and two large baskets on his right, with rifles heaped carelessly inside. The long chests held newly repaired rifles, tended by another man. “Here!” Yao called. The attendant took a rifle and tossed it up to him. Yao caught it in his left hand, transferred it to his right, and narrowed his eyes for a glance. A shot rang out, followed almost immediately by a thump: he had thrown the rifle into the nearer basket. Zuo had not even seen clearly how he pulled the trigger with one hand. The little hole in the exact centre of the new target, however, was plain enough.

听这时又是『砰砰彭彭』好一阵，有的枪丢在外面箩筐，有的枪丢在里面箩筐，不过外面少，里面多。『是这样，』李勉林为左宗棠解释，『丢在外面的，没有修好，拿回去重修；丢在里面的，是修好了的。』左宗棠有些不大相信，『就这么看一眼、放一枪，就能听得出来？』他说∶『似乎有点不可思议。』『是！是有点不可思议，不过确实如此。』『我倒有点不明白。』左宗棠便趁空隙喊道∶『姚司务！姚司务！』那姚司务纹风不动，恍若未闻，李勉林赶紧又解释，『他重听，耳鼓让枪声震坏。平时说话，只看人的嘴。』接着他走上前去，拍一拍姚务的身后，让他下来。

Now came a rapid succession of cracks and thumps. Some rifles went into the outer basket and others into the inner one, though far fewer went into the outer. “Those in the outer basket have not been properly repaired and must go back,” Li Mianlin explained. “Those in the inner one are ready.” Zuo was not entirely convinced. “One glance and one shot, and he can tell by the sound? It seems scarcely credible.” “Indeed it does, but that is how he does it.” “There is something I do not understand.” During a pause, Zuo called, “Master Yao! Master Yao!” The old man did not stir, as though he had heard nothing. Li hastened to explain. “He is hard of hearing. Gunfire has damaged his eardrums. In ordinary conversation he watches people’s lips.” He stepped forward, tapped Yao on the back, and signalled him to come down.

『姚司务，』左宗棠问∶『你今年多大？』『六十六岁。』『你玩枪玩了少年了？』姚司务屈指算了一下∶『四十八年。』左宗棠也在心里略为算了一下说∶『这么说，你在道光那年就干这一行了？』『是。』『你跟谁学的？』『先是德国人，后来是英国人。』『喔！』左宗棠问∶『你说德国的枪好，还是英国的枪好？』『德国。』

“How old are you, Master Yao?” Zuo asked. “Sixty-six.” “How many years have you been handling guns?” Yao counted on his fingers. “Forty-eight.” Zuo made a quick calculation of his own. “Then you were already in this trade during the Daoguang reign?” “Yes.” “Who taught you?” “First a German, then an Englishman.” “And which guns do you think better, German or English?” “German.”

听这一说，左宗棠便回身去看，胡雪岩知道是找他，便从一大堆官员中挤上前去。『雪岩，』左宗棠问道∶『福克来了没有？』『没有。』胡雪岩问∶『大人有什么吩咐？我马上告诉他。』『我是要找一枝「温者斯得」的枪。』『呃，』胡雪岩答说∶『我已经分派给新兵，在用了。』『好、好！拿一枝来。』这枝枪是交到姚司务手里，问他见过没有？答说没有。不过他只略为看了一下，便转开一个螺丝，接着一样一样拆了下来，不过几分钟的工夫，一枝新枪成了一堆零件。这显出真工夫来了，左宗棠不能不服他，当下问道∶『这枪好不好？』那姚司务竟不回答，只看着李勉林。左宗棠不知是怎么回事；胡雪岩却看出来了，姚司务一说好，左宗棠说不定马上就会交代购买那一种。那一来，岂不断了采购委员的财路。因此，胡雪岩便说一句∶『只怕不见得好。』

Zuo turned to look behind him. Knowing whom he sought, Hu pushed forward through the crowd of officials. “Xueyan, has Focke come?” “No. Have you any instructions for him, Your Excellency? I shall pass them on at once.” “I want one of those Winchester rifles.” “I have already issued them to the recruits. They are using them.” “Good. Bring one here.” The rifle was placed in Yao’s hands, and he was asked whether he had seen one before. He had not. Yet after a brief examination, he turned a screw and began removing the parts one by one. In a few minutes, the new rifle lay in pieces. This was genuine skill, and Zuo could not withhold his admiration. “Is it a good rifle?” he asked. Yao gave no answer, merely looking at Li Mianlin. Zuo did not understand, but Hu did. If Yao pronounced it good, Zuo might immediately order rifles of that kind, cutting off the purchasing officers’ opportunities for profit. Hu therefore remarked, “Perhaps it is not so good.”

谁知李勉林恰好相反，连连说道∶『好，好，好得很。』表面彼此客气，实际上已等于短兵相接，也是彼此猜忌。本来江南制造局是李鸿章的禁脔，不管自造也好，外购也好，都轮不到胡雪岩来插手，所以他之说『怕不见得好』，便有不愿跟制造局『抢生意』的意味在内；反过来说，他如果要『抢生意』，唾手可得。这就使李勉林深深感到，劲敌当前，必须小心了。这笔买『温者斯得』来福枪的生意，自然还是归胡雪岩，但大发利市的却是福克。

Unexpectedly, Li took the opposite line. “Good, good—excellent!” he repeated. On the surface they were being polite; in reality, they were already crossing swords and regarding one another with suspicion. The Jiangnan Arsenal was Li Hongzhang’s exclusive preserve. Whether weapons were manufactured there or purchased abroad, Hu had no part in the business. His remark that the rifle might not be so good therefore signalled that he did not intend to compete for the arsenal’s trade. Conversely, if he chose to compete, that trade was within easy reach. Li Mianlin felt keenly that a formidable rival stood before him and that caution was necessary. The purchase of these Winchester rifles did, naturally, come Hu’s way. The man who profited handsomely, however, was Focke.

原来这种枪的在华代理权，属于福克的洋行，第一批进了五百枝，四处兜销，只卖去一百多，起初亦并未想到左宗棠，因为他知道西征军中来福枪极多，左宗棠甚至还送了一批给醇王，供神机营使用。及至听说胡雪岩要到上海，心想左宗棠的『小队』也许要用这种比较精良的新枪，送了二十枝当样品，估量着，即使能做到这笔生意，充其量也不过百把枝，库存还有一半，不知销场何在？哪知由胡雪岩转来的消息，说要买两千五百枝，预备分发江南各防营使用。福克喜出望外，却又发愁，因为能够供应的现贷，连个零头都不足。『胡先生，』福克透过古应春的翻译，向胡雪岩说∶『我拿库中存货先交，其余的，准备三个月内交齐；我回国去一趟，专门办这件事。

Focke’s firm held the Chinese agency for these rifles. Of the first five hundred imported, barely more than a hundred had been sold despite efforts in every direction. He had not initially thought of Zuo, knowing that the western armies already had many rifles; Zuo had even sent a consignment to Prince Chun for use by the Shenji Battalion. Hearing that Hu was coming to Shanghai, however, Focke thought Zuo’s personal detachment might need these superior new weapons and sent twenty as samples. Even if this produced an order, he expected no more than a hundred or so. Half his original stock would still remain, with no buyer in sight. Then word came through Hu: Zuo wanted two thousand five hundred, to be distributed among the various Jiangnan garrisons. Focke was delighted, but troubled as well. The goods available for immediate delivery did not amount even to the odd five hundred. “Mr Hu,” he said through Gu’s interpretation, “I shall deliver what I have in stock first and supply the rest within three months. I shall go home myself expressly to arrange it.”

胡雪碉便跟古应春商量，他亦看出李勉林对他深具戒心，认为不宜一开始就树敌，免得以后的障碍愈来愈多。这笔军火是左宗棠亲自交代，不能不办；正愁着李勉林会『吃味』，难得福克供应不足，恰好打消了这笔生意，避免得罪李勉林。他将他的意思告诉了给古应春，又说∶『我看就此推掉为妙。你跟他说，马上要用，要现货，没有现货就免谈了。』『这话他不会相认的。』古应春说∶『小爷叔在左大人面前讲话的分量，他不是不知道，哪一次买军火都是先送样品，看中意了再下定单，如今说全部都要现货，不是明明为难他？』『这话倒也是。』胡雪岩踌躇了一会说∶『这样，你叫他自己去看左大人。而且我们要避嫌疑，你叫他先到制造局去看李观察，请李观察带他去见左大人。生意成不成，看他自己的运气。』『这办法！行得通吗？』古应春不免怀疑，『我们犯不着把自己的路子，交给人家。』『不！现在他们怕我们防得厉害，犯不着为这点小事，做成个死对头。不如现在大方一点，以后办事反而顺手。』古应春心想，这是欲取姑予的手法，亦未尝不可用。两千五百枝枪的佣金，虽至少有五千佣金，别人看来是个大数目，但在胡雪岩眼中，却是小事；既然他要『大方』，就照他的意思办好了。

Hu discussed this with Gu. He too had seen how wary Li Mianlin was of him. It would be unwise to make an enemy at the outset and encounter increasing obstacles thereafter. Since Zuo had personally ordered these arms, he could hardly refuse to act; yet he feared Li’s resentment. Focke’s insufficient stock offered a convenient reason to abandon the transaction and avoid offending Li. He explained this to Gu. “We had better turn it down now. Tell him the rifles are needed immediately. We must have them in stock, or there is nothing to discuss.” “He will not accept that explanation. He knows how much influence you have with His Excellency. Arms purchases always begin with samples; if they are approved, an order follows. To insist now that the entire quantity be available at once would plainly be making difficulties for him.” “That is true.” Hu hesitated. “Then let him see His Excellency himself. To avoid any suspicion, have him first visit Intendant Li at the arsenal and ask Li to take him to His Excellency. Whether he secures the business will depend on his luck.” “Will that work?” Gu asked doubtfully. “We need not hand our own connections over to someone else.” “No. They are so afraid of us and so much on their guard that it would be foolish to make a sworn enemy over this small matter. A little generosity now will make future business easier.” Gu reflected that giving now in order to gain later was a legitimate tactic. The commission on two thousand five hundred rifles would be at least five thousand taels—a large sum to others, but a trifle to Hu. Since he wished to be generous, Gu would do as he asked.

但胡雪岩的顾虑与打算，福克是怎么样也无从知道的，因此一听古应春的话，大感困惑，多年合作得好好地，何以有这种见拒的态度？莫非胡雪岩在左宗棠面前，说话已经没有力量了，还是另有其他原因？当下率直向古应春发问。古应春当然不能跟他说实话，只说胡雪岩是尊重江南制造局。这话在福克半信半疑，他在华多年，官场中的情形，亦相当了解，向来是谁有办法，谁就可以争权夺利；权责并不分明，尊重更是假话。福克做事很老练，先去打听胡雪岩在左宗棠那里的『行情』，所得到的答复是绝未失宠。这一来，他就不能不怀疑，另有人在钻军火生意的路子，想取他而代之；胡雪岩是一种让他知难而退的态度。去问古应春，古应春绝口否认。这一下，福克释然了，中国官场不足跟外人道的花样很多，不必去多打听。反正自己仍旧抱定利益均沾的宗旨，将胡雪岩拉紧了，保持了多年合作的关系，总是不错的。

Focke could have no knowledge of Hu’s anxieties or calculations. He was bewildered by Gu’s message. After so many years of successful cooperation, why this rejection? Had Hu lost his influence with Zuo, or was there some other reason? He questioned Gu frankly. Unable to tell him the truth, Gu said only that Hu wished to respect the Jiangnan Arsenal’s position. Focke half believed him. After many years in China, he understood official life fairly well: whoever had the means could contend for power and profit. Authority and responsibility were ill-defined; talk of respect was still less credible. An experienced operator, Focke first inquired into Hu’s standing with Zuo and learned that he had certainly not fallen from favour. He therefore began to suspect that someone else was trying to supplant him in the arms trade, and that Hu meant to discourage him into withdrawing. Gu denied this absolutely. Focke then felt reassured. Chinese officialdom had many complications that could not be explained to foreigners; there was no need to investigate them all. In any event, he could not go wrong by holding to his principle of sharing profits, keeping Hu close, and preserving their longstanding cooperation.

于是福克便带了一名翻译到制造局求见李勉林。那时的官场，对洋人都是另眼看待，何况福克是上海洋商领袖之一，所以名刺一报进去，正在花厅中会客的李勉林，丢下他人，在签押房接见福克。动问来意，福克透过翻译说道∶『左大人要买两千五百枝温者斯得来福枪，可是我现货只有三百多枝，其余准三个月内交足。胡观察说不行，要我来见李观察，请你带我去见左大人当面谈。』听得这话，李勉林不免诧异，定购西洋军火，向来都是期货；目前内外无事，又不是打仗遇到劲敌，急需要精良武器才足以克制，何必一定非现货不可？仔细想一想，显然是胡雪岩不愿意经手这件事，但又为什么不愿意呢？唯一的缘故是左宗棠已非西征统帅，而是两江总督、南洋大臣，两个头衔中一『江』、『南』，就彰明较著地表明了，这一案应该由江南制造局主办。对于胡雪岩的能守分际，李勉林颇为佩服，胡雪岩的手腕很厉害，但还是『上路的』。

Focke therefore took an interpreter to the arsenal to see Li Mianlin. Officials gave foreigners special treatment in those days, and Focke was one of Shanghai’s leading foreign merchants. As soon as his card arrived, Li left the guests he was receiving in the garden reception room and met him in his private office. Asked his business, Focke explained through the interpreter: “His Excellency Zuo wishes to purchase two thousand five hundred Winchester rifles. I have only three hundred and some in stock, but can guarantee delivery of the rest within three months. Intendant Hu says that will not do and has sent me to ask you to take me to His Excellency so that I may discuss it with him in person.” Li was surprised. Foreign arms were normally ordered for future delivery. With no present disturbance at home or abroad, and no formidable enemy in battle requiring superior weapons urgently, why insist on immediate availability? Reflection made the answer plain: Hu did not wish to handle the purchase. Why not? There could be only one explanation. Zuo was no longer commander of the western expedition; he was governor-general of Liangjiang and commissioner for the Southern Ports. The “jiang” and “nan” in those titles unmistakably indicated that this business belonged to the Jiangnan Arsenal. Li admired Hu’s respect for the proper boundaries. Hu’s methods might be formidable, but he understood how things should be done.

当下欣然答说∶『可以、可以！左大人明天动身回江宁，我本来就要去见他；我们一起去好了。』于是约定当天下午三点钟，在天后宫行辕见面。到时候会齐李勉林先递书本谒见，然后找个谈话的空隙，说福克在外，等候接见，有事面禀。左宗棠已经接到胡雪岩的报告，认为胡雪岩所说，此案由江南制造局承办，一切签约、付款等等手续，都比较方便的看法不错，所以听得李勉林的话，立即接见福克。他跟福克很熟，也很欣赏福克的有条理，温言相接，颇假以词色；谈到买枪一事，也很爽快的答应了，先交若干现货，余数立定期限，陆续解交。价格方面，福克与李勉林细谈。

“Certainly!” Li answered readily. “His Excellency leaves for Jiangning tomorrow. I was going to see him in any case. We can go together.” They arranged to meet at three that afternoon at Zuo’s headquarters in the Temple of the Heavenly Empress. Once they had assembled there, Li sent in his card and was received first. At a suitable pause, he mentioned that Focke was waiting outside and had something to discuss in person. Hu had already reported to Zuo and argued that having the arsenal handle the transaction would make the contract, payments, and other formalities more convenient. Zuo approved this reasoning and immediately admitted Focke. He knew him well and appreciated his orderly way of doing things, so his greeting was warm and gracious. He also agreed readily to the proposed arrangement: the available rifles would be delivered first, and the remainder in instalments by a fixed deadline. Focke and Li were to settle the price between them.

『这两千五面枝枪是交绿营用的。』左宗棠交代李勉林∶『你收到枪，马上交给李朝斌好了。』李朝斌的官衔是江南提督，绿营的最高长官。『是。』『听说你要回国。』左宗棠转脸问福克∶『什么时候动身，什么时候回来？』『十天以后动身，两个月就回来。』『我现在要整顿水师；水师的利器，是鱼雷不是？』『是的。』『我想买一批鱼雷，你有没有？』『有、有。』福克答说∶『左大人知道的，东西洋各国凡有新出的利器，一定把样品跟说明书，送到我洋行里来的。尤其是这趟我回国，可以亲自打听到最新式的运了来。』『能不能连技师一起请了来。』『当然。凡是采购中国从前所没有的新式武器，一定由技师派来，教导如何演放。这是必有的规矩，不会错的。』『喔，你没有弄清我的意思，我是说能制造鱼雷的技师。』『那也有。』福克答说∶『不过要先看制造局，有没有能造鱼雷的机械。』『你跟李观察商量。』左宗棠又问∶『还有种「碰雷」，作何用处？』

“These two thousand five hundred rifles are for the Green Standard troops,” Zuo instructed Li. “As soon as you receive them, hand them over to Li Chaobin.” Li Chaobin was the Jiangnan commander-in-chief, the highest officer of the Green Standard forces. “Yes, Your Excellency.” Zuo turned to Focke. “I hear you are going home. When do you leave, and when will you return?” “In ten days. I shall be back in two months.” “I now intend to reorganize the navy. Torpedoes are an effective naval weapon, are they not?” “They are.” “I should like to purchase a consignment. Can you supply them?” “Certainly. As Your Excellency knows, whenever a new weapon appears in any country, East or West, samples and instructions are sent to my firm. On this journey home, I can personally inquire into the newest models and bring them out.” “Could you bring technicians as well?” “Of course. Whenever China buys a new weapon it has not used before, technicians are always sent to demonstrate its operation. That is an established practice; there will be no difficulty.” “You have misunderstood me. I mean technicians who can manufacture torpedoes.” “They can be found too, but we must first see whether the arsenal has suitable machinery.” “Discuss that with Intendant Li.” Zuo continued, “There is also something called a ‘contact mine.’ What is that used for?”

『是——』福克向翻译弄清楚了『碰雷』二字的意思，方始回答∶『那叫水雷，是专门为了防备对方兵舰用的。譬如一个港口，不愿意对方舰闯进来，就可以在港口海面上布下水雷，船一碰到就会爆炸。』『自己的船呢？』『自己的船，一样也会爆炸。』福克又说∶『水雷的威力很大，麻烦是不长眼睛，所以非遇到与外国交锋，打算断绝水路交通，不用水雷。』『事后呢？』『事后要清理。专门有种船叫扫雷艇。』『照此说来，这件事牵涉很广，暂作罢论，你只管替中国采购最新式的鱼雷好了。细节你跟李观察去商议。』『是！』看看没有话了，福七克在翻译示意之下，起身告辞。李勉林虽被留了下来，但从头到底没有能容他说一句话，内心万分不悦。

“It is—” Focke first clarified the term with his interpreter. “That is a naval mine, used specifically against enemy warships. For example, if you wish to keep their vessels out of a harbour, you can lay mines across its entrance. A ship touching one causes it to explode.” “What about our own ships?” “They would cause an explosion too. Mines are very powerful, but unfortunately they cannot distinguish friend from foe. They are therefore not used unless war with a foreign country makes it necessary to close the waterways.” “And afterwards?” “Afterwards they must be cleared. There is a special kind of vessel called a minesweeper.” “Then the matter has wide implications. We shall leave it aside for now. Simply obtain the latest torpedoes for China. Discuss the details with Intendant Li.” “Yes, Your Excellency.” There seemed nothing more to say. Prompted by the interpreter, Focke rose and took his leave. Li was asked to remain, but he had not been able to get a word into the entire conversation and was deeply displeased.

至于左宗棠将李勉林留了下来，是要谈半公半私的事。不过私事倒也不是他的个人之私，是为了曾国藩的小女婿聂规缉。原来曾国藩的欧阳夫人，共生三子六女。长子及五女，自幼夭折；在世的有两子五女，长子纪泽，文章政事俱是第一流，而且由自修而通英文，为国藩所看重，后来袭封侯爵，以钦差大臣出使西洋，与郭嵩焘都是真正懂洋务的大才。次子纪鸿中举以后，会试一直不利；曾国藩也知道『场中莫论文』，考试要碰运气，但功名这念，横亘胸中；期望亦未免过切，总说他的次子不用功。偏偏运气也真坏，直到曾国藩去世，始终是个举人，以后也一直没有能够中进士，与长兄相较，境遇大不相同，以至于在京郁郁以终，身后还是左宗棠替他料理的。

Zuo had kept Li behind to discuss something partly public and partly private. The private interest was not his own, however; it concerned Zeng Guofan’s youngest son-in-law, Nie Guiji. Zeng’s wife, Madam Ouyang, had borne three sons and six daughters. The first son and fifth daughter died in childhood, leaving two sons and five daughters. The elder surviving son, Jize, excelled both in letters and in public affairs. He had also taught himself English, which greatly pleased his father. Later he inherited the marquisate and went to the West as an imperial envoy. He and Guo Songtao were men of exceptional ability who truly understood foreign affairs. The younger son, Jihong, passed the provincial examination but repeatedly failed at the metropolitan level. Zeng knew that literary merit did not guarantee success in the examination hall and that luck played a part. Yet official distinction remained an obsession, and his expectations were too pressing; he constantly accused his younger son of not studying hard enough. Jihong’s luck was indeed poor. When Zeng died, he was still only a provincial graduate, and he never subsequently obtained the metropolitan degree. His circumstances differed so greatly from his elder brother’s that he died despondent in the capital. It was Zuo who attended to his affairs after his death.

比起曾纪鸿来，他的姐妹们的境遇，又更不如他了，有的婆婆太凶，有的丈夫没出息。曾国藩持家极严，说他见过许多名门之女，贪恋母家富，往往不肯在夫家尽子妇之道，到后来都无好结果，因此他的女儿虽都遇人不淑，但因曾国藩不许她们归宁，只好在夫家受罪，个个都是终日以泪洗面。其中四小姐嫁得不错，偏又青年守寡，所以曾国藩生前常说，他的『坦运不佳』。六小姐是取小的女儿，湖南人称为『满小姐』，名叫曾纪芬，她是曾国藩去世后才嫁的。本来由她叔叔『九帅』作媒，许婚于衡山聂家，定在同治十一年出阁。不意就在这年二月初，曾国藩中风殁于两江总督任上；到得服满已是光绪年间。

Compared with Jihong, his sisters fared worse still. Some had fierce mothers-in-law; others had worthless husbands. Zeng governed his household strictly. He said he had seen many daughters of prominent families who clung to the wealth of their parents’ homes and neglected their duties as wives and daughters-in-law, and that none had come to a good end. Although his own daughters had made unfortunate marriages, he would not permit them to return home. They had to endure their misery with their husbands’ families, spending their days in tears. The fourth daughter had married well, only to be widowed young. Zeng often said that his luck in sons-in-law was poor. The sixth daughter, his youngest—what Hunan people called the “last little miss”—was Zeng Jifen. She married only after his death. Her uncle, Ninth Marshal Zeng, had arranged the match with the Nie family of Hengshan, and the wedding was set for the eleventh year of Tongzhi. But early in the second month of that year, Zeng suffered a stroke and died in office as governor-general of Liangjiang. By the time mourning ended, the Guangxu reign had begun.

曾纪芬的夫婿聂规缉，字芸台，他家是衡山世家，先世以行善出名。但聂规缉却连个举人都没有考上，以致于只能混个小差使；他有个姐夫为先前的两江总督刘坤一委为『筹防局总办』，聂规缉单身跟到江宁，在筹防局当差，只得八两银子的车马费，但却要接眷；原来聂规缉到了江宁，才知道曾国藩真是门生故吏满天下，将他妻子以『曾文正的满小姐』这个『头衔』搬出来，在裙带上着实能拖出来一点好处，这就是他接眷的打算。果然，曾纪芬照她丈夫的嘱咐，由湖南坐船经武昌时，特为去拜见湖广总督李瀚章的夫人，稍为谈一谈丈夫的境况，聂规缉立即被委为湖南督运局驻江宁的委员，月支津贴五十两，日子过得很舒服了。

Jifen’s husband, Nie Guiji, whose courtesy name was Yuntai, belonged to an established Hengshan family long known for charitable works. He had failed even to gain the provincial degree and could obtain only minor employment. One of his brothers-in-law had been made director of the Defence Preparations Bureau by the former governor-general of Liangjiang, Liu Kunyi. Nie followed him to Jiangning alone and took a position in the bureau, receiving only eight taels for transport expenses. Yet he wanted to bring his wife there. Once in Jiangning, he had discovered how widely Zeng’s former pupils and subordinates were dispersed. By presenting his wife under the title “Lord Wenzheng Zeng’s youngest daughter,” he could extract considerable benefit from her connections. That was why he wished her to join him. Following his instructions, Jifen made a special call on the wife of Li Hanzhang, governor-general of Huguang, when her boat from Hunan passed through Wuchang. She said a little about her husband’s circumstances. Nie was promptly appointed the Hunan Transport Supervision Bureau’s representative in Jiangning, with a monthly allowance of fifty taels. Life became quite comfortable.

及至左宗棠接刘坤一的手，到了江宁不久，便将曾纪芬接到总督衙门叙旧，曾国藩生在嘉庆十六年辛未；左宗棠生在壬申小一岁，因而以叔父自居。左宗棠在曾国荃克江宁后，与曾国藩失和，有三四年不通音问，但当左示棠奉命西征，曾国藩命湘军刘松山相助，大为得力，这使得左宗棠大为感动，而况平生功名，关键所在是曾国藩知道他的才具，派他独当一面收复浙江，与曾氏兄弟同时封爵。拜相封侯，位极人臣，饮水思源，亦不能不感激曾国藩；所以表面上倔强如昔，仍旧处处要批评曾国藩，私底下的态度，却已大为改变，曾国藩殁后，他致送的挽联，道是∶『谋国之忠，知人之明，自愧不如元辅』，这等于认输，以左宗棠的性情来说，是很难得的事。

Soon after succeeding Liu Kunyi at Jiangning, Zuo invited Jifen to the governor-general’s residence to talk over old times. Zeng had been born in the xinwei year, the sixteenth of Jiaqing; Zuo, born in the following renshen year, was a year younger and assumed the role of her uncle. After Zeng Guoquan captured Jiangning, Zuo and Zeng Guofan had quarrelled and ceased corresponding for three or four years. But when Zuo was ordered to campaign in the west, Zeng sent Liu Songshan of the Xiang Army to assist him. His help proved invaluable and deeply moved Zuo. Moreover, the decisive opportunity in Zuo’s career had come when Zeng recognized his ability and entrusted him with independent command to recover Zhejiang, enabling him to receive a noble title alongside the Zeng brothers. Having risen to the highest ministerial honours and a marquisate, Zuo could hardly forget the source of his good fortune. Outwardly he remained as stubborn as ever, criticizing Zeng at every turn, but privately his attitude had changed greatly. When Zeng died, Zuo sent a funeral couplet whose first line read: “In loyalty to the state and discernment of men, I acknowledge myself inferior to the chief minister.” This amounted to conceding defeat—an uncommon act for a man of Zuo’s temperament.

至于照应曾国藩的后人，是为了要证实他的挽联中的下一句∶『同心若金，攻错若石，相期无负平生』，与曾国藩是为国事而争，私交丝毫无损。特别是老年人，往往有一种将朋友的女儿看作自己掌上珠的通性，爱屋及乌，对聂规缉亦就另眼相看，派了他营务处的差使，每天中午会食，一定找聂规缉；对他的肯说实话、留心西学，颇为赞许，有心要培植他。这回左宗棠出省阅兵，聂规缉作随员，李勉林跟他是熟人，左宗棠故意相问∶『勉林，你跟聂芸台熟不熟？』李勉林各州兴锐，早年曾替曾国藩办过粮台，当即答道∶『他是曾文正的满女婿，我当然很熟。』『那就再好没有。我看你也很忙，我想派他来当你的会办。』

His care for Zeng’s descendants was intended to bear out the couplet’s second line: “Our shared purpose was firm as metal; our corrections of each other, a whetstone; may neither betray the hopes of a lifetime.” Their disagreements concerned affairs of state and had never impaired their private friendship. Elderly men, moreover, often regarded a friend’s daughter as a precious child of their own. Zuo’s affection extended to Nie. He appointed him to the military administration office and invariably summoned him to share the midday meal. He approved of Nie’s willingness to speak honestly and his interest in Western learning, and intended to foster his career. Nie accompanied him on this tour of military inspection outside the province. Li Mianlin knew the young man, and Zuo deliberately asked, “Mianlin, do you know Nie Yuntai well?” Li, whose personal name was Xingrui, had once managed military supplies for Zeng. “He is Lord Wenzheng’s youngest son-in-law,” he replied. “Of course I know him well.” “Excellent. You seem very busy. I should like to appoint him your assistant director.”

『大人眷念故人，要调剂调剂聂仲芳，这番至意，我们当然要体仰；我想，每个月送地五十两银子薪水，仍旧在大人那里当差好了。』左宗棠一听愕然，『怎么，勉林，』他问∶『你不欢迎聂仲芳？』『不敢欺大人，聂仲芳在大人那里，亲自教导督责，他不敢越轨；到了我这里，也许会故态复萌。他是曾文正的满女婿，我不便说他，耽误了公事，大家不好。』这一说，原来有些生气的左宗棠，心平气和地问说∶『你说他「故态复萌」，请问，是什么故态？』『聂仲芳是纨绔，他比满小姐小三岁，光绪元年成婚；到光绪四年，才廿四岁，已经娶了姨太太。』『这件事我知道，他的那个早就遣走了。』左宗棠问∶『还有呢？』

“Your Excellency’s concern for an old friend and wish to improve Nie Zhongfang’s circumstances deserve our fullest sympathy,” Li replied. “I suggest that we send him fifty taels a month in salary while he continues to serve with you.” Zuo was astonished. “What, Mianlin? You do not want Nie Zhongfang?” “I dare not conceal the truth. Under Your Excellency’s personal instruction and supervision, he would not step out of line. Here he might revert to his old ways. As Lord Wenzheng’s youngest son-in-law, he would be difficult for me to reprimand. If public business suffered, it would be bad for everyone.” Zuo had begun to feel angry, but now asked calmly, “What old ways do you mean?” “Nie is an idle young gentleman. He is three years younger than the youngest Miss Zeng. They married in the first year of Guangxu, and by the fourth year, when he was only twenty-four, he had already taken a concubine.” “I know about that. He sent her away long ago. What else?”

『还有，曾劼刚那年奉派出使英、法两国，二小姐的故爷陈松生与聂仲芳都想跟去当随员，结果劼刚带了陈松生，没有带聂仲芳。劼刚路过上海的时候，我问他同为妹婿，何以厚此薄彼。劼刚说∶我带了他去是个累。又说∶』你看了我的日记就知道了。『李勉林又说∶』他们郎舅至亲，尚且如此，大人倒想，我怎么敢用他？『『喔，』左宗棠问∶『你看了劼刚的日记没有呢？』『看了。』『日记中怎么说？』『我录得有副本，回头送来给大人看。』『好！请你送来我看看。』

“When Zeng Jiegang was appointed envoy to Britain and France, both Chen Songsheng, the second daughter’s husband, and Nie wanted to accompany him as members of his staff. He took Chen but not Nie. Passing through Shanghai, he explained when I asked why he treated two brothers-in-law differently: ‘Taking him would be a burden.’ He added, ‘Read my diary and you will understand.’” Li continued, “If a close relation by marriage felt that way, Your Excellency, how could I dare employ him?” “Did you read Jiegang’s diary?” “I did.” “What did it say?” “I copied it. I shall send you the copy later.” “Good. Please let me see it.”

李勉林答应着，一回去马上将曾劼刚日记的副本，专程送到天后宫行辕。左宗棠灯下无事，细细看了一遍，其中有两条对聂规缉的批评不好，一条记于光绪四年二月十三日∶『接家报，知聂仲芳乖张已甚，季妹横被凌折，忧闷之至。』这是家务，清官难断，另外有一条记于当年九月十五日，说他不用聂仲芳的原因∶『午饭后，写一函答妹婿聂仲芳，阻其出洋之请，同为妹婿，挈松生而阻仲芳，将来必招怨恨，然而万里远行，又非余之私事，势不能徇新戚之情面，苟且迁就也。松生德器学识，朋友中实罕其匹，同行必于使事有益。仲芳年轻而纨绔习气太重，除应酬外，乃无一长，又性根无定，喜怒无常，何可携以自累，是以毅然辞之。』左宗棠心想，这不是什么不可救药的毛病。如果当时聂规缉如曾纪泽所言，现在看来却无此毛病，正好说明此人三四年以来，力矫前失，肯求上进。李勉林在制造局有许多毛病，怕落在聂规缉眼中，故而拿曾劼刚作挡箭牌，不必理他。

As soon as Li returned, he had the copy of Zeng Jiegang’s diary specially delivered to the Temple of the Heavenly Empress. With nothing to occupy him that evening, Zuo read it carefully by lamplight. Two entries criticized Nie Guiji. The first, dated the thirteenth day of the second month in the fourth year of Guangxu, read: “A letter from home reports that Nie Zhongfang has become exceedingly unreasonable. My youngest sister has suffered unwarranted humiliation and ill-treatment. I am deeply troubled and distressed.” This was a domestic matter, which even an upright magistrate found hard to judge. The other entry, dated the fifteenth day of the ninth month that year, explained why Zeng would not take Nie: “After luncheon, I wrote to my brother-in-law Nie Zhongfang declining his request to go abroad. To take Songsheng and refuse Zhongfang, when both are my brothers-in-law, is certain to arouse resentment. Yet this is a journey of ten thousand miles and no private undertaking of mine. I cannot give way to family feeling and make an irresponsible accommodation. Among my friends, few equal Songsheng in character, capacity, and learning. His presence will benefit the mission. Zhongfang is young and much too steeped in the habits of a pampered idler. Beyond social accomplishments, he has no particular ability. He is also unstable in temperament, with unpredictable changes of mood. How could I burden myself with him? I have therefore firmly refused.” Zuo reflected that none of these faults was incurable. If Nie had been as Zeng Jize described him then, the absence of those faults now merely showed that over the past three or four years he had worked to correct them and improve himself. Li had many failings in his management of the arsenal and feared that Nie would notice them. He was using Zeng Jiegang as a shield. There was no need to take him seriously.

主意虽定，但因第二天便须启程江宁，无法与李勉林面谈，因而亲自执笔写了一封信说∶『曾文正尝自笑坦运不佳，于诸婿中少所许可，即纪鸿亦不甚得其欢心，其所许可者，只劼刚一人，而又颇忧其聪明太露，此必有所见而云然。然吾辈待其后昆，不敢以此稍形轩轾。上年弟在京寓，目睹纪鸿苦窘情状，不觉慨然，为谋药饵之资，殡殓衣棺及还丧乡里之费，亦未尝有所歧视也。劼刚在伦敦致书言谢，却极拳拳，是于骨肉间不敢妄生爱憎厚薄之念，亦概可想。兹于仲芳，何独不然。日记云云，是劼刚一时失检，未可据为定评。』写到这里，自觉有些强词夺理；以他的地位，便是仗势欺人，所以凝神细想了一会，想出一番说得过去的道理。『传曰∶』思其人犹爱其树，君子用情，惟其厚焉「，以此言之，阁下之处仲芳不难，局员非官僚之比；局务非政事之比。仲芳能则进之，不能则撤之，其幸而无过也容之，不幸而有过则攻之讦之，俾有感奋激励之心，以生其鼓欣鼓舞、激励震惧之念，庶仲芳有所成就，不至弃为废材，而阁下有以处仲芳，即有以对曾文正矣。『左宗棠自觉这段话说得光明正大，情理周至，但意思还不足，因而又添了一段∶』弟与文正论交最早，彼此推诚相与，天下所共知；晚岁凶终隙末，亦天下所共见，然文正逝后，待文正之子若弟，及其亲友，无异文正之生存也。阁下以为然耶否耶？『

His mind was made up, but he had to leave for Jiangning the next day and could not discuss the matter with Li in person. He therefore wrote a letter himself: “Lord Wenzheng used to laugh at his own poor luck in sons-in-law and approved of few among them. Even Jihong did not greatly please him. Jiegang alone won his approval, yet even with him he feared that intelligence was too openly displayed. No doubt he had grounds for this judgment. We, however, must not make such distinctions in our treatment of his descendants. Last year, while staying in the capital, I saw Jihong’s distress and poverty and was deeply moved. I arranged funds for his medicines, burial clothes and coffin, and the return of his remains to his native place. I made no adverse distinction. Jiegang’s letter of thanks from London was most earnest; one may infer that he too would not lightly indulge in partiality or dislike among his own kin. Why should Zhongfang alone be an exception? What appears in the diary was a momentary lapse of discretion on Jiegang’s part and must not be taken as a final judgment.” At this point Zuo felt that he was forcing the argument. Given his position, it amounted to using authority to overbear Li. He paused in thought and devised a more defensible case. “Tradition says: ‘Remembering the man, we cherish even his tree. A gentleman’s affections should be generous.’ Viewed thus, your treatment of Zhongfang presents no difficulty. A bureau employee is not the same as a regular official, nor bureau work the same as the government of a jurisdiction. If he is capable, advance him; if incapable, remove him. If he commits no fault, allow him his place; if he does err, criticize and expose the fault. Let him feel both encouragement and warning, so that hope, resolve, and apprehension may stir him to achievement rather than leave him discarded as useless material. In treating Zhongfang thus, you will have fulfilled your obligation to Lord Wenzheng.” Zuo felt that this was forthright and reasonable, allowing fully for both principle and affection. Yet something remained to be said, and he added: “My friendship with Lord Wenzheng began very early. Our mutual sincerity was known to all; so too was our estrangement in later years. Yet since his death, I have treated his sons, his brother, and his relatives and friends exactly as I did while he lived. Do you consider that right, or not?”

送走了左宗棠，李勉林刚回制造局，便收到了左宗棠的信及送还的曾纪泽的日记。信上一篇大道理，不但坚持原意，而且隐隐责备他，不肯照顾聂规缉，反而离间人家郎舅至亲的感情，对不起曾国藩生前栽培之德。李勉林自然很不高兴。没有法子！他心里在想，不怕官，只怕管；左宗棠要派聂规缉来当会办，是他的职权，写信解释，还是客气的做法。接下来又想，左宗棠赏识聂规缉，是因为他肯说实话，而且肯留心『西学』，不用说，制造局造船造枪械，他不会是外行；不是外行又肯说实话，制造局的许多见不得人的内慕，就瞒不住了。左宗棠派此人来当会办，说不定就是专门来捉他的毛病的。

After seeing Zuo off, Li had scarcely returned to the arsenal when the letter arrived, together with the diary copy. Its lofty reasoning not only insisted on the original proposal but implicitly reproached him for refusing to care for Nie, sowing discord between close relations, and failing to repay Zeng’s earlier patronage. Li was naturally displeased. Yet there was nothing he could do. It was not high office that one feared, he reflected, but the authority directly over oneself. Zuo had the power to appoint Nie assistant director; writing to explain his decision was a courtesy. Then another thought occurred to him. Zuo valued Nie because he spoke honestly and attended to Western learning. He could hardly be ignorant of the arsenal’s shipbuilding and weapons manufacture. A knowledgeable man willing to speak the truth would discover its many discreditable secrets. Perhaps Zuo was sending him precisely to find fault with its director.

这样转着念头，不免心事重重，但还是得强打精神来应付，当即将亲信的文案、庶务都找了来，宣布聂规缉即将来当会办，关照文案备禀请派任的公事，措词要客气、要夸奖。然后交代庶务两件事∶第一、替会办找个宽敞的公馆，陈设布置，务求华美；第二，派专人携带三个月的薪水，到江宁去接『聂会办』夫妇来上任。这个厂务叫王伯炎，是李勉林的心腹，名为厂务，并不只管制造局的冗杂小事，他不但顾问可以干预工程及购料，甚至还是李勉林的智囊，随时可以提出建议；当然，他也是李勉林的耳目，外界对制造局的批评，一直很注意的。将李勉林交代的事，办妥了来复命时，王伯炎提到福克，『跟福克的那张合约，』他问∶『总办是打算自己跟他谈呢，还是等聂会办来谈？』『你看呢？』

The thought weighed upon Li, but he had to pull himself together and respond. He summoned his trusted drafting and administrative staff, announced Nie’s forthcoming appointment, and instructed the drafting officer to prepare a formal request for it, in courteous and flattering terms. He then gave the administrative officer two orders: find a spacious residence for the assistant director and furnish it splendidly; and send someone to Jiangning with three months’ salary to escort “Assistant Director Nie” and his wife to their new post. This officer, Wang Boyan, was Li’s confidant. Although nominally responsible for administrative matters, he did far more than attend to routine details. As an adviser he could intervene in engineering and purchasing, and as Li’s strategist he could offer suggestions whenever necessary. He was also Li’s eyes and ears, closely following outside criticism of the arsenal. Reporting that the arrangements had been made, he brought up Focke. “Do you intend to negotiate the contract with Focke yourself, Director, or wait for Assistant Director Nie?” “What do you think?”

『这要看总办的意思。』王伯炎说∶『各有各的好处。等聂会办来谈，好处是左大人的面子十足，聂会办也很高兴，而且，聂会办如果弄了好处，就有个把柄在总办手里，以后不怕他不就范。』『嗯、嗯！』李勉林问∶『坏处呢？』『坏处就是他不要好处。公事上是开了个例，以后这种合约都归他来谈，总办的大权旁落了。』『李勉林想了一下答说∶』他刚刚来，决不敢弄好处，不会有把柄落在我们手里；反而开了个恶例『』说得是。总办的做法也很高明，尽量跟他客气，敷衍得他舒舒服服；就是不给他实权，叫他少管公事。『』对！怎么把他敷衍得舒舒服服，就交给你办了；大不了多花几两银子，不要紧。『』是！『王伯炎答说∶』福克昨天来问道，什么时候谈合约，我说这两天左大人在这里，总办没有工夫，等左大人走了再说。现在我就通知他了，叫他马上来谈。『』好！你跟他谈。『

“That depends on your wishes. Each course has advantages. If we wait for Nie, His Excellency will be thoroughly gratified, and Nie will be pleased too. Moreover, if Nie takes a personal profit, you will have something to hold over him and need never fear his disobedience.” “And the disadvantage?” “That he may take no profit. Then a precedent will have been established: all such contracts will be negotiated by him, and your authority will pass into other hands.” Li considered. “Newly arrived, he certainly will not dare take anything. He will give us no hold over him; we shall merely establish an unfortunate precedent.” “Exactly. Your present course is very wise: be as courteous as possible, make him comfortable in every way, but give him no real authority and little public business to attend to.” “Yes. I leave it to you to keep him comfortable. A few extra taels do not matter.” “Very well. Focke asked yesterday when we could discuss the contract. I said that while His Excellency was here you had no time, and that we must wait until he left. I shall now tell him to come at once.” “Good. You negotiate with him.”

福克是早就预备好了的，品类、价格、交货期限，合约底稿；价格是照数量多寡决定，买得愈多愈便宜，但佣金却照比例实足计算。军火的佣金，高低不等，但最少也得一个二八扣，不过福克开的佣金，只得一个折扣；王伯炎便向翻译笑道∶『福克先生在中国多年，怎么说外行话？』『是，是佣金的折扣不对？』『不是佣金的折扣不对。』王伯炎又换了一个说法∶『是拿我们当外行看。』翻译跟福克叽哩咕噜谈了一阵，转脸向王伯炎说道∶『福克的意思是，这笔生意因为是面奉左大人交代，价钱格外克已，所以他是照成本开的，等于白当差；要请王老爷原谅。』『言重，言重！』王伯炎说∶『我们要请他原谅，这个数目，我怎么向上头交代？莫非他跟胡大先生做交易，也是这个折扣？』

Focke had long since prepared everything: specifications, prices, delivery dates, and a draft contract. Prices depended on quantity—the larger the purchase, the cheaper each item—but the commission was calculated at the full agreed percentage. Commissions on arms varied, though twenty per cent was the minimum. Focke, however, offered only ten. Wang Boyan smiled at the interpreter. “After so many years in China, why does Mr Focke speak like a novice?” “Is the commission rate wrong?” “No. He is treating us as novices.” The interpreter conferred with Focke for a while, then turned back. “Mr Focke says that, because His Excellency personally instructed him to undertake this transaction, he has kept the price exceptionally low. He has quoted at cost and is effectively doing the work for nothing. He asks you to understand.” “He is too kind! It is we who must ask his understanding. How am I to account to my superiors for such a figure? Does he offer Mr Hu the same rate?”

『是的，「福克居然透过翻译，这样回答；不过他也有解释，』以前如果跟胡先生自己谈。什么话都好说；倘或是跟左大人自己谈，胡先生是连一个回扣都不要的。『』唏！唷！『王伯炎大惊小怪地，』照这样说，他还算特为照应我们的？『『话也不是这么说。』翻译答说∶『据我们所知，回扣有多有少，看情形而定；好在以后还有生意，总有补报的时候。』『我是头一回，总要让我有个面子，你跟他说，我下一回补报他。』翻译跟福克又是谈了好半天，最后无可奈何地回复王伯炎，『王老爷，』他说∶『福克的意思，回扣多少都行，不过价钱要提高。』『提高到多少呢？』『这要看王老爷，要多少就是多少。』『喔，他的意思是「戴帽子」？』『是的。』

“Yes,” Focke actually replied through the interpreter, though he added an explanation. “When I negotiated directly with Mr Hu, any terms could be discussed. But when I dealt personally with His Excellency Zuo, Mr Hu took no rebate at all.” “Well, well!” Wang made a great show of surprise. “Then he is granting us a special favour?” “That is not quite what he means,” the interpreter answered. “As we understand it, rebates vary with circumstances. There will be more business later, and opportunities to make up for this.” “This is our first transaction. He ought to let me make a respectable showing. Tell him I shall make it up to him next time.” The interpreter spoke with Focke at length, then returned helplessly to Wang. “Mr Focke says any rebate can be arranged, but the price must rise accordingly.” “To what figure?” “That depends on you. Whatever amount you wish.” “Ah, he means to put a hat on the price?” “Yes.”

『那末戴了帽子他承认不承认呢？』『当然承认。不过┅┅』那翻译吞吞吐吐地没有再说下去。王伯炎当然要追问，『不过什么？』他说∶『大家头一回做交易，要以诚相待。』『那末，我说老实话，价目表早就开出去了。』『开给哪个？』『胡大先生。』翻译赶紧又补了一句∶『不是这两天的事。』王伯炎一听这话，大为光火；脸色青一阵、红一阵的，最后吐出一句话来∶『原来是个圈套！』

“And will he stand by the price with its hat on?” “Certainly. But…” The interpreter faltered. “But what?” Wang demanded. “This is our first transaction. We must be frank with each other.” “Then to be frank, the price list has already been sent out.” “To whom?” “Mr Hu.” The interpreter hastily added, “Not just within the last few days.” Wang was furious. His face went from ashen to crimson before he finally uttered the words: “So it is a trap!”

当下弄得不欢而散，王伯炎愤愤不平，再一打听，还有气人的事，原来福克决意跟胡雪岩保持良好的关系，所以在这笔军火的佣金中，为他保留了一个折扣；虽然胡雪岩表示，不愿不劳而获，但福克还是照原来的计划。买军火两成回扣，是最起码的行情，还要平白为人分去一半，王伯炎觉得这件事对总办实在很难交代。李勉林本来就有上当的感觉，在他的判断，胡雪岩将福克带到左宗棠那里，是以西征转运局委员的身份干预江南的军火采办事宜，京中的『都老爷』参上一本，连左宗棠的面子都不好看，因而叫福克来请他引见。事实上他们暗底下都谈好了，只是利用他来摆个渡而已。

The meeting broke up unpleasantly. Further inquiries gave the indignant Wang fresh cause for anger. Determined to preserve his good relationship with Hu, Focke had reserved a ten-per-cent share for him in the commission on this transaction. Hu had said he did not wish to profit without doing the work, but Focke persisted in his original plan. Twenty per cent was the minimum customary rebate on arms; now half of it was to be given away to someone else for nothing. Wang could scarcely explain this to his director. Li already felt he had been duped. In his view, had Hu introduced Focke to Zuo, he would have been intervening in Jiangnan arms procurement in his capacity as an officer of the Western Expedition Transport Bureau. A censor’s impeachment could have embarrassed even Zuo. That was why they had sent Focke to seek Li’s introduction. In fact, Li supposed, they had settled everything privately and merely used him to ferry the business across.

因此听到王伯炎的报告以后，认为事态很严重，特意去找上海道邵友濂商量。『合肥道赵丁忧，实在不凑巧，北洋是张振轩大有取而代之的意思；这里左湘阴着着进逼，里面一个聂仲芳卧底；外面一个胡雪岩花样百出。制造局是北洋的基础，看来要保不住了。』李勉林忧心忡忡地说∶『小村兄，你一向足智多谋，总要看在大家都是曾文正一脉相传这一点的情份上，帮帮我的忙才好。』『言重，言重。』号『小村』的邵友濂说∶『彼此休戚相关，我决无坐视之理。胡雪岩在左湘阴面前的分量，也大不如前了，你先咬咬牙撑住，等我找个机会，好好来打他一闷棍，叫他爬不起来。』『百足之虫，死而不僵。』『即使不僵也不能有什么作为了。』邵友濂打断他的话说∶『勉林兄，目前最要紧的一件事，你要把聂仲芳敷衍好。』『我明白。』『至于福克的合约，你最好还是让胡雪岩跟他去订。』『喔，这，这有什么讲究吗？』『自然有讲究。这笔经费，将来少不得要在江海关的收入之中开支；如果我这里调度不开，不是害你受人家的逼？』李勉林沉吟了一会，恍然大悟，江海关的税收归邵友濂管，将来该付福克的款子，他可以借故拖延；如果是胡雪岩跟福克签的约，福克自然只能找胡雪岩去办交涉，所以邵友濂的刁难福克，实际上便是与胡雪岩为难。『好，好！』等想通了，李勉林满口应承，『我回去就办。』

After Wang’s report, Li considered the matter serious enough to consult Shao Youlian, the Shanghai intendant. “Li Hongzhang’s mourning has come at a most unfortunate time. In Beiyang, Zhang Zhenxuan plainly intends to replace him. Here, Zuo advances at every step. Nie Zhongfang is planted inside, and Hu Xueyan contrives one scheme after another outside. The arsenal is the foundation of Beiyang’s strength; I fear we shall lose it.” Deeply anxious, he appealed to Shao. “Brother Xiaocun, you are always resourceful. For the sake of our common debt to Lord Wenzheng, you must help me.” “You need not speak so gravely. Our fortunes are bound together; I could never stand by. Hu carries much less weight with Zuo than he once did. Grit your teeth and hold on while I find an opportunity to strike him from behind so hard that he cannot get up again.” “A hundred-footed creature does not stiffen even in death—” “Even if it does not, it can do nothing more,” Shao interrupted. “The first priority, Brother Mianlin, is to keep Nie Zhongfang comfortable.” “I understand.” “As for Focke’s contract, you had better let Hu sign it with him.” “Is there a particular reason?” “Naturally. This money will ultimately have to come from the Shanghai Customs receipts. Suppose I cannot arrange the funds—would I not be exposing you to his demands?” After reflection, Li understood. Shao controlled the customs revenue and could make excuses to delay payment. If Hu had signed the contract, Focke could only ask Hu to intervene. By obstructing Focke, Shao would really be making difficulties for Hu. “Excellent!” Li agreed. “I shall attend to it as soon as I return.”

李勉林的办法是，命王伯炎备公事禀报左宗棠，说福克索价过高，合约谈不拢，福克以前承办西征军火，只有胡雪岩能使他就范，所以为了大局着想，请左宗棠径饬胡雪岩与福克签订合约，同时，福克原拟致送回扣一成，江南制造局决不敢领这笔回扣，请在价款中扣除，庶符涓滴归公之议。这一份『禀帖』说得冠冕党皇，到得两江总督衙门，左宗棠议为言之有理；便将原禀录了一个副本，一并寄交胡雪岩办理。这样由上海而江宁，由江宁而杭州，再由杭州而上海一个大圈子兜下来，函电往来，很快地两个月过去，事情尚无结果，局势却有了重大变化。

Li instructed Wang to prepare a report to Zuo stating that Focke’s price was too high and negotiations had failed. Since Focke had previously supplied the western expedition and only Hu could make him accept terms, the public interest required that Zuo order Hu directly to conclude the contract. The report added that Focke had offered a ten-per-cent rebate, which the Jiangnan Arsenal could not possibly accept; it should therefore be deducted from the price, ensuring that every last fraction went to the public treasury. This impeccably proper-sounding report reached the governor-general’s office in Jiangning. Zuo found it reasonable and sent a copy to Hu with instructions to act. Thus the affair travelled in a great circle, from Shanghai to Jiangning, from Jiangning to Hangzhou, and from Hangzhou back to Shanghai. Amid the exchange of letters and telegrams, two months quickly passed. The transaction remained unsettled, while the wider situation changed profoundly.

原来东邻朝鲜发生内乱，国王李熙暗弱，王妃闵氏当权；李熙的本生父叫李癵应，称号是『大院君』，与王妃争权，已非一日，这一次的内乱是大院君的党羽进攻王宫，伤及王妃，并杀大臣闵谦镐等人。日本见有机可乘，出兵朝鲜；驻日公使黎庶昌急电署直隶总督张树声，建议北洋立派兵舰，与日军抗衡。张树声本就想有声有色地大干一番，接到黎庶昌告警的电报，决定一面出兵观变，一面奏报朝廷。朝廷对张树声能够迅速应变，颇为嘉许，但因法国其时正在图谋越南；朝鲜又有警报，怕张树声无法应付，所以决定命在籍守制的李鸿章夺情复起，即日回津。因而便有人劝张树声说∶『朝中既已命令他主持此事，出兵似以等合肥回任后再办为宜。』张树声不听，说兵贵神速，时机一误，让日本军着了先鞭，中国要落下风。他即负北洋重任，不能因循自误。

Civil disorder had broken out in Korea to the east. King Yi Hui was weak, and Queen Min wielded power. The king’s own father, Yi Haeung, known as the Daewongun, had long been struggling with the queen for control. His followers now attacked the palace, wounded the queen, and killed ministers including Min Gyeom-ho. Japan seized the opportunity to send troops to Korea. Li Shuchang, China’s envoy in Japan, urgently telegraphed acting Governor-General Zhang Shusheng, recommending that Beiyang warships be dispatched immediately to counter the Japanese. Zhang had been eager for a conspicuous achievement. On receiving the warning, he decided to send troops to watch developments while simultaneously reporting to the court. The court commended his prompt response. But with France also threatening Vietnam, it feared that the Korean crisis would exceed Zhang’s capacity. Li Hongzhang was therefore ordered to interrupt his mourning at home and return to Tianjin at once. Someone advised Zhang that, since the court had appointed Li to direct the matter, he should wait until Li resumed office before sending troops. Zhang refused. Speed was essential in war; if the opportunity were lost and Japan acted first, China would be at a disadvantage. Responsible as he was for Beiyang, he could not allow indecision to lead him astray.

于是当第二道催李鸿章动身的电报刚到合肥，李鸿章已复奏即行就道，由上海转天津时，张树声所派的军队，已经在『跨海征东』途中了。张树声所派水陆两员大将，一个是北洋水师记名提督丁汝昌；一个是广东水师提督吴长庆，此人名在水师，实在是陆军，他是淮军宿将，驻扎山东登州；随带淮军六营，由登州坐招商局的轮船出海，幕府中人材济济，总理前敌营务处的，是一个年方二十四岁的江淮世家子弟，就是翰林出身、官至户部侍郎、曾为左宗棠办过粮台的哀保恒的侄子袁世凯。袁世凯从小不喜读书，虽是世家子弟，行为无赖，不齿于乡党。在家乡存不住身，异想天开，召集了无业少年十余人，由河南项城到山东烟台，将同伴留在旅舍中，只身去见吴长庆。吴长庆当时以广东水师提督办理山东军务，他跟袁世凯的嗣父袁保庆是八拜之交，对故人之子，当然要照应，首先动问来意。

Thus, when the second telegram urging Li to depart had only just reached Hefei, and Li had replied that he would leave immediately for Tianjin by way of Shanghai, Zhang’s expedition was already crossing the sea eastward. Its naval and military commanders were Ding Ruchang, a Beiyang naval officer registered for appointment as commander-in-chief, and Wu Changqing, nominally commander-in-chief of the Guangdong navy but in practice a land commander. Wu was a veteran of the Huai Army stationed at Dengzhou in Shandong. He sailed from there in China Merchants’ steamers with six battalions. His headquarters contained many able men. Directing the field military administration was a twenty-four-year-old from an established Jianghuai family: Yuan Shikai, nephew of Yuan Baoheng, the former Hanlin scholar who had risen to vice-minister of revenue and managed supplies for Zuo. Yuan Shikai had disliked study from childhood. Despite his distinguished family, his disreputable behaviour had made him an outcast among his neighbours. Unable to remain at home, he conceived an extraordinary plan: gathering more than ten unemployed youths, he travelled from Xiangcheng in Henan to Yantai in Shandong. Leaving his companions at an inn, he went alone to see Wu. Wu, then handling military affairs in Shandong under his Guangdong naval title, had been a sworn brother of Yuan’s adoptive father, Yuan Baoqing. He naturally wished to help his old friend’s son and first asked what had brought him there.

袁世凯答说∶『身为将门之子，投笔从戎。』又说他带来的十几个少年，都是难得的将才，『请老伯全数录用。』吴长庆大为诧异，不好骂他荒唐、斥之为冒昧。当下派了一名军官携带银票，到旅舍里，将他的同伴好言资遣。当然，袁世凯是被留下来了。『你进了学没有？』『没有。』袁世恺连秀才都不是，不过捐了监生，照例可应北闱顺天乡试；吴长庆便叫他在营读书，拜张謇为师。此人号季直，是南通的名士，他在吴长庆幕府中参赞军务，同时也是吴开庆次子吴保初的业师。

“As the son of a military family, I have laid down the writing brush to take up arms,” Yuan replied. The dozen or so young men he had brought, he added, were all rare military talents. “I ask you, Uncle, to employ every one of them.” Wu was astonished, but could hardly call him absurd or rebuke his presumption. Instead, he sent an officer to the inn with banknotes to dismiss his companions kindly and provide their travelling expenses. Yuan himself was, of course, kept on. “Have you gained admission to the county school?” “No.” Yuan was not even a licentiate. He had merely purchased the status of an Imperial Academy student, which entitled him to sit the Shuntian provincial examination in the northern capital. Wu told him to study in camp under Zhang Jian. Known by the courtesy name Jizhi, Zhang was a distinguished scholar of Nantong. Besides advising Wu on military affairs, he tutored Wu’s second son, Wu Baochu.

既然要应考，张謇当然教他做八股文。袁世恺兴趣缺缺；但陪着张謇谈谈时事，以及用人驭士的手段，却头头是道，很得张謇的赏识。吴长庆幕府中，还有个朱铭盘，也是南通人，与张謇及另一个诗做得极好的范肯堂，号称为『通州三生』；这朱铭盘对袁世恺亦颇有好感，因此，当张謇保荐袁世恺时，而朱铭盘在一旁帮腔以后，吴长庆便委袁为营务处帮办，而且派了两名勤务兵给他。这是前年光绪六年四月间的事。及至朝鲜发生内乱，张树声派丁汝昌特召吴长庆议事。吴长庆带同张謇，在天津密商三日，定策平乱。这年壬午，『子午犯酉，大比之年』，袁世恺奉命入京乡试，恰好也在天津；听说要出兵朝鲜，便去见张謇，想弃文就武，不赴乡试而赴朝鲜。张謇答应了，为他向吴长庆要求，如愿以偿。

Since Yuan was to take the examination, Zhang naturally taught him eight-legged essays. Yuan had little interest in them. But in conversations about current affairs and the management of men, he spoke so cogently that Zhang came to admire him. Another member of Wu’s staff, Zhu Mingpan, was also from Nantong. He, Zhang, and the gifted poet Fan Kentang were known as the Three Scholars of Tongzhou. Zhu too liked Yuan. When Zhang recommended him, with Zhu supporting the recommendation, Wu appointed him assistant in the military administration office and assigned him two orderlies. This occurred in the fourth month of the sixth year of Guangxu, two years before. When the Korean disturbances broke out, Zhang Shusheng sent Ding Ruchang specially to summon Wu for consultation. Wu took Zhang Jian to Tianjin, where three days of secret discussions produced a plan to suppress the disorder. It was the renwu year—one of the years of the Rat, Horse, Rabbit, or Rooster in which the triennial examinations were held. Ordered to go to the capital for the provincial examination, Yuan happened also to be in Tianjin. Hearing of the projected Korean expedition, he sought out Zhang Jian and asked to abandon letters for arms, going to Korea instead of the examination. Zhang agreed to intercede with Wu, and Yuan obtained his wish.

到了烟台以后，吴长庆回登州去调兵遣将，在烟台派船征粮，须备辎重，由张謇负责，事多且杂，张謇顺理成章的找了袁世凯作帮手，由吴长庆下札子委为『前敌营务处』，居然独当一面了。七月十二日黄昏，吴长庆带领大队人马，由烟台抵达朝鲜仁川；可是日本海陆军已经早一小时到达。只是天色已晚，中日两军都住在船上，预备天亮登陆。哪知就夜色苍茫中，闵妃所遣的密使到了。原来朝鲜国王李熙，也象光绪皇帝一样，是旁支入继；李熙的生父『大院君』李是应，便等于醇亲王，所不同的是，『大院君』摄政。

After they reached Yantai, Wu returned to Dengzhou to mobilize his troops. Zhang Jian was responsible for assigning ships, requisitioning provisions, and assembling supplies at Yantai. With so many varied tasks, he naturally turned to Yuan for assistance. Wu issued him a written appointment to the field military administration, and he found himself entrusted with independent responsibility. At dusk on the twelfth day of the seventh month, Wu’s main force arrived at Incheon from Yantai. The Japanese land and naval forces, however, had arrived an hour earlier. As it was already late, both sides remained aboard, intending to land at dawn. Then, through the gathering darkness, secret envoys arrived from Queen Min. Korea’s king, Yi Hui, had succeeded from a collateral branch of the royal house, like the Guangxu emperor. His biological father, Yi Haeung, the Daewongun, occupied a position comparable to Prince Chun’s, with the difference that the Daewongun served as regent.

李熙成年以后，『大院君「自然看不过去，便跟闵妃争权。那闵妃象慈禧太后一样，非常能干，心想朝鲜是中国的藩属，只要倾心结交中国官吏，自然就占上风，此时日本的野心日炽，看朝鲜两派对立，各不相下，便蓄心要找机会，作为入侵的借口。机会终于来了。朝鲜内政不修，人民困苦，士兵的饷欠了好几个月，一再『闹饷』，发又发得不足数，于是便常有造反作乱之事，日本人便买通乱党，故意让他们抢劫日本领事馆，日本便以保护领事馆为名，酝酿出兵朝鲜。闵妃得到消息，向中国官吏告密；驻日公使亦有急电到北洋，中日双方军队都想抢个先着，但同时到达，不分先后，而闵妃的密使一来，情势就不同了。

After Yi Hui came of age, the Daewongun resented the situation and struggled with Queen Min for power. Like the Empress Dowager Cixi, she was highly capable. Since Korea was China’s tributary state, she reasoned, wholeheartedly cultivating Chinese officials would give her the advantage. Japan’s ambitions were growing daily. Seeing two irreconcilable factions in Korea, it watched for an opportunity that could furnish a pretext for invasion. At last one arose. Domestic government was neglected, the people were impoverished, and soldiers had gone unpaid for months. Repeated protests over their pay produced only incomplete payments, so mutinies and disturbances were frequent. The Japanese bought over some of the rebels and deliberately had them plunder the Japanese consulate. They then prepared to send troops under the pretext of protecting it. Queen Min learned of this and secretly informed Chinese officials; China’s envoy in Japan also telegraphed Beiyang urgently. Both countries sought to arrive first, but their forces reached Korea at virtually the same time. The queen’s envoys changed the situation.

这些密使谒见吴长庆、丁汝昌，说日本与李癵应已有勾结。哪一个军队先到朝鲜京城汉城，哪一国便控制了整个局势。这就象楚汉相争，先入咸阳为胜是一样的道理。『为今之计，我们劝天朝大军，乘黑夜登陆，由间道入汉城，一昼夜可以抵达。这条间道捷径是日本人所不知道的。』『主意是很好，可是这一昼夜的供应呢？士兵不能不吃饭啊！』『请放。』闵妃的密使说∶『沿途都设备好了。』吴长庆大喜，立即召集张謇及马建忠密议，决定接受闵妃的计划，先派五百人连夜登陆；另派一千人在黎明下船，其余守在船上待命。密议既定，吴长庆在招商局轮船的大餐间点兵发令。

Meeting Wu and Ding, the envoys said that Japan and the Daewongun were already in collusion. Whichever army reached the capital, Hanseong, first would control the whole situation. It was like the struggle between Chu and Han, when entry into Xianyang determined the advantage. “We advise the Celestial Empire’s forces to land under cover of darkness and take a side road to Hanseong. You can reach it in a day and a night. The Japanese do not know this shortcut.” “An excellent idea. But how are the troops to be supplied for that day and night? They must eat.” “Do not be concerned. Everything has been arranged along the route.” Delighted, Wu immediately called Zhang Jian and Ma Jianzhong into a secret conference. They decided to accept the queen’s plan: five hundred men would land that night, another thousand at dawn, while the rest remained aboard awaiting orders. Once the decision was made, Wu assembled his officers in the China Merchants’ steamer’s dining saloon and issued his commands.

这本来应该是士气昂扬、踊跃争先的一个场面，不过吴长庆下达了命令，肃静无声，约有五分钟之久；这一下气氛便显得很僵硬了。终于有个姓刘的帮带，凑到吴长庆面前低声说道∶『本营都是陆军，从来没有出过海，现在轮船刚停下来，弟兄晕船的很多，能不能请大帅体谅，让大家休息一夜，到天亮再上岸。』此言一出，吴长庆即时变色，偏偏另外还有同样的请求，吴长庆勃然大怒，拍桌骂道∶『这是什么时候，什么地方，你敢不遵我的命令，莫非不知道军法？』说着，要拔令箭斩那个刘帮带。张謇在旁，不等他再开口抢着说道∶『大帅，刘帮带不宜再带兵了；另外派人吧！』『派谁呢？』『我看袁世凯可以接替。』『好！』吴长庆向左右说道∶『把姓刘的先看管起来，等我办完了大事再来处置。』

This should have been an occasion of high spirits and eager volunteers. Instead, after Wu gave his orders, silence lasted for about five minutes. The atmosphere grew painfully strained. At last a deputy battalion commander named Liu approached and spoke quietly. “Our men are land soldiers who have never been to sea. The steamer has only just stopped, and many are seasick. Might you allow them a night’s rest before they go ashore at dawn, Marshal?” Wu’s expression changed instantly. Worse still, others voiced the same request. He flew into a rage and struck the table. “At a time and in a place like this, you dare disobey? Do you not know military law?” He reached for a command token to order Liu’s execution. Zhang Jian hastily intervened before he could speak again. “Marshal, Liu is no longer fit to lead troops. Appoint someone else!” “Whom?” “I suggest Yuan Shikai.” “Very well!” Wu turned to his attendants. “Place Liu under guard. I shall deal with him once the important business is done.”

这时袁世凯已得到通知，进来行了礼；张謇说道∶『大帅有差使派给你，你仔细听着。』吴长庆接口下令∶『刘帮带不遵命令，我已把他革职看管，现在派你为帮带，接管他的队伍；即刻预备，半点钟以后，先领一营人，坐朝鲜派来的船登陆，由朝鲜向导带领，连夜行军。袁世凯，这个差使，你担当得下来，担当不下来？』『能担当。』『好！你部下如有人不遵命，违反军法，准你先斩后报。』说着，吴长庆将手中的令箭，往前一递。袁世凯接令在手，高声答道∶『遵大帅将令。』半点钟不到，袁世凯已扎束停当，草鞋短裤，干净利落，进来向吴长庆禀报∶『已经跟朝鲜的译官商量决定，登陆后连夜急行军，天明到果山早饭，在那里恭候大帅驾临。』辞行既华，立即下船，到得天亮，吴长庆亲统两营，接续前进，中午抵达果山，袁世凯下马迎谒，说已派先锋五百人，由营官率领先走，他特为在此候驾。

Yuan had meanwhile been summoned. He entered and saluted. “The Marshal has a duty for you,” Zhang told him. “Listen carefully.” Wu took up the command. “Deputy Commander Liu has disobeyed orders. I have dismissed him and placed him under arrest. You are now deputy commander and will take over his men. Prepare immediately. In half an hour, lead one battalion ashore in the boats supplied by the Koreans. Their guides will take you forward on a night march. Yuan Shikai, can you carry out this assignment?” “I can.” “Good. If any of your men disobey or violate military law, you may execute them first and report afterwards.” He held out the command token. Yuan accepted it and answered loudly, “I obey your orders, Marshal.” In less than half an hour, he was ready, neatly equipped in short trousers and straw sandals. He returned to report: “I have settled everything with the Korean interpreter. After landing, we shall march rapidly through the night, take breakfast at Guoshan at dawn, and await Your Excellency there.” Having taken his leave, he immediately went ashore. At daybreak, Wu followed with two battalions and reached Guoshan at noon. Yuan dismounted to greet him. He had sent five hundred men ahead under the battalion commander and had remained expressly to await the Marshal.

『路上怎么样？』『一路平安，朝鲜的供应很完备，一切请大帅放心。』『好！』吴长庆又问∶『还有什么事要报告的？』『士兵的纪律不大好，抢民间的东西，还有对妇女无礼，王师戡乱，这样子会让人家看不起，世凯已遵大帅将令，就地正法了七个人。』一听这话，吴长庆放心了。原以为他不会带兵，现在看来，倒真不愧将门之后，当下慰了一番，关照袁世凯继续前进。

“How was the journey?” “Entirely peaceful. The Koreans have supplied everything we need. You may be at ease.” “Good. Anything else to report?” “The men’s discipline was poor. They plundered the inhabitants and insulted women. For an imperial army sent to restore order, such behaviour would earn contempt. Acting under your orders, I have executed seven men on the spot.” Wu felt reassured. He had thought Yuan incapable of commanding troops; now he seemed indeed a worthy descendant of a military family. Wu commended him and instructed him to continue the advance.

当天深夜，先锋五百人到了汉城，在南门扎营。第二天上午十点多钟，吴长庆亲统的一千人亦复疾驰而至，在距汉城七里的屯子山扎下大营，其时『大院君』李癵应已经得到消息，派了他的儿子大将军李载冕来见吴长庆，表示慰劳。吴长庆亦很客气地敷衍了一番，等李载冕一走，立刻进城去拜访李癵应，作礼貌上的周旋。出城回大营以后，吴长庆立即召集高级将领及幕僚密商马建忠建议，擒贼擒王，等李是应来回拜时，设法扣留，送往天津，以寒乱党之胆。倘或乱党不受安抚，再行进剿。吴长庆认为此计大妙，其余的人众都同意，于是密密部署，设下了陷阱，只等李癵应来自投。

Late that night, the five hundred advance troops reached Hanseong and camped at its south gate. After ten the following morning, Wu arrived at speed with his thousand men and established his main camp at Tunzishan, seven li from the city. The Daewongun had learned of their arrival and sent his son, General Yi Jae-myeon, with greetings and gifts for the troops. Wu responded courteously. Once Yi had left, he immediately entered the city to pay the Daewongun a formal visit. Returning to camp, he called his senior officers and advisers into a secret council. Ma Jianzhong proposed seizing the leader to subdue his followers: when the Daewongun returned the visit, they should detain him and send him to Tianjin, intimidating the rebels. If the rebels still refused pacification, military suppression could follow. Wu thought the plan excellent, and everyone agreed. They made their preparations in secret and laid the trap, waiting for the Daewongun to enter it.

李癵应来回拜时，是在下午四点钟，带的卫队有数十名之多；接入帐内，由张謇与马建忠二人，与李癵应笔谈，这样交换意见，即令是泛泛的寒暄，一来一往，亦很费事。等营外李癵应的卫队被隔离开来，看看时候差不多了∶吴长庆便即说道∶『本人奉朝廷之命传旨，着贵藩亲自到北京，面陈乱党的一切。』说完，也不管李癵应听得懂听不懂，由马建忠扶起李癵应出营；外面有一顶轿子，将他塞入轿内，抬起便走，健卒百余人前后夹护，连夜冒雨急驰一百二十里，第二天一早到南阳港口，登上威远兵轮，李是应才知道是怎么回事。

He returned the visit at four in the afternoon, accompanied by several dozen guards. Received inside the tent, he conversed with Zhang Jian and Ma Jianzhong by exchanging written notes. Even commonplace civilities took considerable time in this manner. Once his guards outside had been separated from him, Wu judged the moment ripe. “By command of the court, I convey an imperial order: Your Highness is to go personally to Beijing and report fully on the rebels.” Without waiting to discover whether the Daewongun understood, Ma helped him to his feet and took him out of camp. A sedan chair stood ready. They thrust him inside, lifted it, and hurried away. More than a hundred picked soldiers escorted him before and behind. Through the night and the rain they covered a hundred and twenty li, reaching Namyang harbour early the next morning. Only after he was aboard the warship Weiyuan did the Daewongun understand what had happened.

下一个目标是李癵应的长子，亦即韩王李熙的胞兄李载冕，据说，乱党是由他指挥的。吴长庆派袁世凯领兵入城，逮捕了李载冕，而乱党却已逃散了。当天晚上，吴长庆接到李熙的密报，乱党是屯驻在两个地方，一个叫利泰院，一个叫枉寻里。枉寻里就在吴长庆大营附近，便由他亲自出马；利泰院的任务派了袁世凯，乘黑夜奇袭，抓了一百多人，其余的乌合之众纷纷走避；枉寻里的情形亦差不多。等日军三千人沿大路开到汉城，局势已经平定了。这一来，日军便没有进城的理由，为了避免与清军冲突，驻扎在城外。日本驻韩公使花房义质亦回汉城，向韩国提出赔偿的交涉，这不是吴长庆的事；他将大营移驻东门外关帝庙以后，随即行文北洋，奏请论功行赏。

The next target was his eldest son, Yi Jae-myeon, full brother of King Yi Hui, who was said to direct the rebels. Wu sent Yuan with troops into the city to arrest him, but the rebels themselves had dispersed. That evening, a secret message from the king told Wu that they were concentrated in two places: Itaewon and Wangxunli. Wangxunli lay close to Wu’s main camp, so he dealt with it himself. Yuan was assigned Itaewon. A surprise attack in the darkness captured more than a hundred men, while the remaining rabble fled in every direction. Much the same happened at Wangxunli. By the time three thousand Japanese troops reached Hanseong along the main road, order had already been restored. They therefore had no grounds for entering the city and camped outside to avoid a clash with the Qing forces. Japan’s envoy to Korea, Hanabusa Yoshitada, also returned to Hanseong and opened negotiations for compensation. That was not Wu’s concern. After moving his headquarters to the Temple of Lord Guan outside the east gate, he sent an official report to Beiyang requesting rewards according to merit.

第四章｜CHAPTER FOUR

这本来是件好事，但袁世凯却怀着鬼胎，但亦无法，只好等纰漏出来以后再来想办法——终于有一天，为吴长庆办文案的幕僚。而且也教袁世凯读过书的周家禄，将他找了去有话问。『慰亭，』他问∶『你是中书科中书？』『怎么样？』袁世凯不置可否，先打听出了什么事。『你看！』是北洋来的公事，说庆军保奖一案，中书科中书袁世凯，保升同知，业已奉旨允准。惟本部遍查档册，中书科中书并无袁世凯其人；请饬该员申复云云。袁世凯一则以喜，一则以忧，喜的是平地起楼台，搞了个五品同知，这个职务是武职，故别称『司马』；但官却是文官，前程无量，比二、三品的副将、参将还值钱；忧的是资历上的中书科中书原是假冒的，这个底缺如果不存在，升同知的美梦也就落空了。心里七上八下，表面却很沉着，『周先生，』他笑嘻嘻地说∶『你倒猜上一猜。』『用不着猜，你当初拿来的那张捐官的「部照」，姓不错，是袁，名字不是，当然是借来的。』『是，是，周先生明见万里，这件事，』他打了个千说∶『请周先生成全。』『成全不用说，据实呈复，连庆公都要担个失察的处分。』周家禄紧接着说∶『现在有两个办法，一个容易，一个麻烦，要你自己挑。』『那请周先生指教，是怎么样的两个办法。』『先说容易的，你改用部照上的名字。』周家禄说∶『这个办法，不但容易，而且方便。你方便，只要一角公文，袁世凯为袁某某的改名。恢复原名即可。』袁世凯不愿用这个容易方便的法子，因为他在韩国已是知名人物；尤其有关系的是，朝中自慈禧太后、恭王到总理衙门章京，都知道有个在朝鲜立了功的袁世凯，一改名字，区区同知，有谁知道。

This ought to have been good news, but Yuan Shikai had a guilty secret. There was nothing he could do except wait for the flaw to emerge and then find a remedy. At last Zhou Jialu, a member of Wu Changqing’s secretariat who handled correspondence and had also taught Yuan, summoned him for a question. “Weiting, are you a secretary in the Secretariat Office?” “What is the matter?” Yuan neither confirmed nor denied it, first seeking to learn what had happened. “Look at this.” It was an official communication from Beiyang concerning the Qing Army’s recommendations for rewards. Yuan Shikai, secretary in the Secretariat Office, had received imperial approval for promotion to deputy prefect. But after searching its records, the ministry could find no secretary of that name. He was to be instructed to submit an explanation. Yuan felt both delight and anxiety. His delight was that he had built a tower from level ground and obtained a fifth-rank deputy prefecture. The post carried military duties—hence its other designation, “sima”—but its holder was a civil official with unlimited prospects, making it more valuable than a second- or third-rank military command. His anxiety was that the secretaryship in his record was fraudulent. If that underlying appointment did not exist, his dream of becoming deputy prefect would collapse. Inwardly he was in turmoil; outwardly he remained calm. “Mr Zhou,” he said with a grin, “why not guess?” “There is no need. The ministry certificate for a purchased appointment that you brought originally had the right surname, Yuan, but the wrong personal name. You must have borrowed it.” “Yes, yes. Nothing escapes you, Mr Zhou.” Yuan made a formal bow. “Please help me through this.” “That goes without saying. If the truth were reported, even Commander Wu would be punished for failing to detect it. There are two ways now, one easy and one troublesome. You must choose.” “Please explain them.” “The easy one first: use the name on the certificate. It is not only easy but convenient. A single official note stating that Yuan Shikai is a name adopted by Yuan So-and-so, and that you are reverting to the original name, will suffice.” Yuan did not want this easy solution. He was already well known in Korea. More importantly, everyone at court, from Empress Dowager Cixi and Prince Gong to the secretaries of the Zongli Yamen, knew that a man called Yuan Shikai had distinguished himself there. With a different name, who would recognise a mere deputy prefect?

不过他拒绝的理由，却不是这么说，『周先生，实不相瞒，』他说，『原来的部照，是我一个堂侄子的，此人业已去世，恢复原名，有许多意外的纠葛。请说难的那个办法吧！』『难的那个办法，就是你自己托人到吏部去活动。吏部那些书办，花样之多，意想不到，他们一定有办法，不过「火到猪头烂，钱到公事办」，你这件事，只怕非千金莫办。』『是、是。我照周先生的意思去办。』『好！我暂且把公事压下来，等你到吏部活动，看结果如何，再作道理。』『是。多谢、多谢。』『慰亭！』周家禄笑道∶『我有一首打油送你。』说完，拈起笔来，就桌上起公文的稿纸，一挥而就；袁世凯接过来念道∶『本是中州歪秀才，中书借得不须猜。一时大展经纶手，杀得人头七个来。』等他念完，周家禄哈哈大笑，袁世凯也只好陪着干笑几声，以示洒脱。回到自己营帐，袁世凯自然而然想起了一个人，此人名叫徐世昌，是个举人，办事很扎实，托他去活动，万无一失。只是照周家禄说，花费须一千两银子，款从何出，却费思量。

That, however, was not the reason he gave. “To be frank, Mr Zhou, the certificate belonged to a nephew of my clan who has died. Restoring his name could cause all sorts of unforeseen complications. Please tell me the difficult method.” “You must find someone to approach the Ministry of Personnel on your behalf. Its clerks have more tricks than one can imagine. They will certainly find a way. But ‘Keep the fire going, and the pig’s head softens; provide the money, and official business gets done.’ Your affair may cost a thousand taels.” “Yes, yes. I shall do as you advise.” “Good. I shall hold the papers back for now. Approach the ministry, and we shall decide what to do when we see the result.” “Thank you, thank you.” Zhou smiled. “Weiting, I have a bit of doggerel for you.” Taking up his brush, he wrote at one sweep on a sheet of official draft paper. Yuan read it: “A crooked Henan scholar was his start; / The borrowed secretaryship is plain. / Once he displayed his statesman’s art, / Seven severed heads became his gain.” Zhou roared with laughter. Yuan could only join in with a few dry chuckles to show how lightly he took it. Back in his tent, he naturally thought of Xu Shichang, a provincial graduate and a thoroughly reliable man. Entrusting the matter to him would ensure success. But if, as Zhou said, it would cost a thousand taels, where was the money to come from?

想来想去，只好去找张謇。他兼管着支应所，粮饷出入，大权在握，只要他点头，一千两银子就有着落了。见面招呼，一声『张先生！』张謇便是一楞，原来他称周家禄是『周先生』，叫张謇一向只『老师』二字，如今不但改了『先生』，而且还加了姓，此又何故？一时不便责问，只冷冷地答一声∶『有何见教？』袁世凯也发觉自己错了，但亦不愿再改口，只婉转地说明了自己的困难，请张謇『成全』。『成全不敢当，不过既然是朋友，理当相助。支应所的款子是公款，我不便私下借给你；如今只有一个办法，你的公费每月二百两，你写五张「领结」来，我把你的五个月公费先发给你。』『好！请问领结如何写法？』本来『印结』之结，当作承认事情已经结束来解释，辞句上不大好听，没有人去理会，只是袁世凯心里有病，将张謇所开的印结式样，拿回去一看，上面写的大意是，领到某月份公费二百两，当面点清，成色分两，均未短缺；嗣后倘有短缺，决不致提出任何补偿的要求。倒象防他会耍赖似的，心里已经不大舒服；再翻一翻一部他当作作官秘诀来用的『六部成语』，其中『吏部』有一条常用的成语，叫做『甘结』，注解是∶『凡官府断案既定，或将财物令事主领回者，均命本人作一「情甘遵命」之据，上画花押，谓这甘结』。顿时大为光火；原来所谓印结是这么一种做低服小的表示，不过画花押改为钤印而已，他觉得支应所欺人太甚了。再一想到，这回的保案中，张謇不过是以县丞保用为七品的知县；自己是同知，所谓『五品黄堂』，凭什么要向支应所具印结？当时大发了一顿牢骚，但不具印结，领不到银子，只好忍气吞声照办。可是张謇虽然听说他背后大骂『何物支应所』，觉得小人得志的那副脸嘴，令人齿冷；但还是很帮他的忙。

After much thought, Yuan could only turn to Zhang Jian. Zhang also managed the supply office and controlled the flow of provisions and pay. One nod from him would secure the thousand taels. Yuan greeted him with “Mr Zhang!” Zhang was taken aback. Yuan called Zhou “Mr Zhou”, but had always addressed Zhang simply as “Teacher”. Now he had changed it to “Mr”, and added the surname. Why? It was awkward to challenge him immediately, so Zhang replied coldly, “What can I do for you?” Yuan realised his mistake too, but would not correct himself. He explained his difficulty indirectly and asked Zhang to help him through it. “I would not presume to call it that. But as friends, we ought to help one another. The supply office holds public money, which I cannot lend privately. There is only one course: your official allowance is two hundred taels a month. Give me five signed acknowledgements, and I shall advance you five months.” “Good. How should I write them?” In a “sealed acknowledgement”, the word “acknowledgement” signified that a matter was concluded. The wording was not particularly pleasing, but ordinarily no one troubled about it. Yuan, however, was sensitive. When he took the form away and read it, he found it stated that he had received two hundred taels for a specified month, counted in his presence, with no deficiency in purity or weight, and that he would make no subsequent claim for compensation for any shortage. It seemed designed to prevent him from repudiating the receipt, and he was already displeased. Then he consulted the Expressions of the Six Ministries, a book he used as a guide to official life. Under Personnel was an entry for a “voluntary undertaking”: once a case was decided, or property ordered returned, the person concerned signed a document declaring willing obedience. Yuan flew into a rage. So a sealed acknowledgement was this sort of meek submission, merely replacing the signature with a seal! He felt that the supply office was treating him intolerably. Moreover, in the present recommendations Zhang had merely risen from assistant county magistrate to eligibility for a seventh-rank magistracy. Yuan was a deputy prefect, a “fifth-rank occupant of the yellow hall”. Why should he give a sealed acknowledgement to the supply office? He complained furiously, but without the documents he could not obtain the money and had to swallow his anger and comply. Zhang heard that he had been cursing behind his back—“What sort of thing is this supply office?”—and found his petty arrogance in success contemptible. Nevertheless, he continued to help him.

『慰亭，』他问，『你这银子是要在京里用？』『是的。』『那么你要寄给谁呢？』『我的一个总角之交。』袁世凯答说∶『姓徐，大概已经是新科举人了。』张謇懂他的意思，他这姓徐的朋友应北闱乡试，如今已经发榜，可能榜上有名，不过远在异国，未得京师消息，所以用了『大概』二字。『好！』张謇说道∶『我当然不能发你现银，用银票呢，又怕寄递中途失落了，也很麻烦。我有一个办法，不知道你愿意不愿意。』『喔，请张先生说。』又是『张先生』！听惯了他口口声声叫『老师』，现在第二回听见这个称呼，实在有些刺耳。不过张謇还是很耐心地说∶『本军的饷银，都是由天津「北洋公所」发的；我现在给你一张领据，你寄给你的朋友，由他直接到北洋公所去领，岂不方便。』『好，好！费心张先生了。』『你贵友的大名是哪两个字？』张謇又说∶『领据上指明由某人去领，比较保险。』袁世凯觉得这话也不错，点点头说∶『叫徐世昌。五世其昌的世昌。』『哪里人？』『这也要写在领据上？』『不是这意思。我要写明他的身份，赴北闱当然不是监生，就是生员，生员就要写明哪一县的生员，所以我问他是哪里人。『他是生员。』袁世凯说∶『他原来浙江宁波人，乾隆年间迁居天津，他高祖是河南南阳知县，殁在任上，葬在河南汲县，他家以后就一直寄居在那里，所以他又算浙江人，也算直隶人，或者河南人。』『这样说，他还是天津的生员，如果是汲县进的学，就得在河南乡试。』

“Weiting, is this money to be used in Beijing?” “Yes.” “To whom will you send it?” “A childhood friend named Xu. By now he is probably a newly successful provincial graduate.” Zhang understood. His friend had sat the northern provincial examination, whose results were now out. He might have passed, but Yuan, far away in another country, had not received the news; hence “probably”. “Very well,” Zhang said. “I cannot give you silver to send, of course. A banknote might be lost in the post, which would cause trouble. I have a method, if you agree.” “Please explain, Mr Zhang.” “Mr Zhang” again! After hearing “Teacher” so often, this second use jarred. Still, Zhang patiently continued. “Our army’s pay is issued through the Beiyang Office in Tianjin. I shall give you an order to collect the money. Send it to your friend, and he can draw it there directly. Would that not be convenient?” “Excellent. Thank you for your trouble, Mr Zhang.” “What is your friend’s personal name? It will be safer to name the person authorised to collect it.” Yuan agreed. “Xu Shichang. The characters for shichang come from ‘May his descendants prosper for five generations’.” “Where is he from?” “Must that go on the order too?” “Not for its own sake. I must specify his status. To sit the northern provincial examination, he must be either an Imperial Academy student or a county licentiate. If the latter, I must state the county. That is why I ask.” “He is a licentiate. His family originally came from Ningbo in Zhejiang and moved to Tianjin in the Qianlong reign. His great-great-grandfather served as magistrate of Nanyang in Henan, died in office and was buried in Ji county. The family has lived there since. So he can be considered a Zhejiang man, a Zhili man or a Henan man.” “Then his licentiate registration must still be in Tianjin. If he had qualified in Ji county, he would have had to sit the Henan examination.”

张謇开了领据，指明由『原天津生员徐世昌』具领『。等这张邻据寄寄到徐世昌手里，他已经是新科举人了。徐世昌是与他的胞弟徐世光一起下科场的。三场考毕，在等候发榜的那一个月之中，功名心热，得失这念梗在胸中，有些食不甘味、寝不安枕；常常往来的一个好朋友，便劝他去求一支签。他这个朋友叫柯绍半，字凤笙，山东胶州人。告诉徐世昌说∶『琉璃厂的吕祖祠，那里的签，最灵验不过，有求必应；有应必中。你何妨去求一求看。』徐世昌欣然乐从，到了琉璃厂吕祖祠，看香火比它西面的火神庙还盛，信心便又添了几分。当下虏诚祷祝，抽了一支签出来，上面写的一首诗是∶『八九玄功已有基，频添火候莫差池，待看十二重楼透，便是丹成鹤到时。』『这好象工夫还不到。』徐世昌说∶『今科恐怕无望。』『不然。』柯绍半说∶『照我看，这是指春闱而言，第二句「频添火候莫差池」，是说你秋闱得意以后，要加紧用功，多写写「大卷子」，明年会试中式，殿试得鼎甲，那岂非「十二重楼诱」出？』徐世昌听这一解，大为高兴。再看诗后的『断曰』∶『光前裕后，昌大其门庭』，益发满心欢悦了。

Zhang issued the order, naming “Xu Shichang, formerly a licentiate of Tianjin” as the recipient. By the time it reached Xu, he had indeed become a provincial graduate. He and his younger brother Xu Shiguang had taken the examination together. After its three sessions, during the month’s wait for the results, his eagerness for a degree and anxiety over success or failure robbed his food of flavour and his sleep of ease. A close friend advised him to seek a divination lot. The friend was Ke Shaoban, courtesy name Fengsheng, from Jiaozhou in Shandong. “The lots at the shrine of Immortal Lü in Liulichang are extraordinarily accurate,” he said. “Every question receives an answer, and every answer proves true. Why not try?” Xu readily agreed. At the shrine he found the worship even more fervent than at the Fire God Temple to its west, which strengthened his faith. He prayed sincerely and drew a lot bearing this verse: “The mystic work has laid its base; / Feed the fire well, make no mistake. / When twelvefold towers yield their inner space, / The elixir forms, and cranes awake.” “It seems my work is not yet complete,” Xu said. “Perhaps there is no hope in this examination.” “Not so,” Ke replied. “I think it refers to the spring examination. The second line, ‘Feed the fire well, make no mistake’, means that after success in the autumn, you must study harder and practise more full examination papers. Next year you will pass the metropolitan examination and gain one of the top three places in the palace examination. Would that not be breaking through the ‘twelvefold towers’?” Xu was delighted by this interpretation. Then he read the pronouncement beneath the poem: “Bring glory to those before and abundance to those after; make the family flourish greatly.” His happiness increased still further.

到得登榜那天，由半夜等到天亮，由天亮等到日中，捷报来了，不过徐世昌却格外难堪，原来他的胞弟徐世光中了第九十五名举人。当下开发了喜封，在会馆中乱过一阵，等静下来不由得凄然下泪。『大哥，我看你的闱墨比我强。』徐世光安慰他说∶『一定是五经魁，报来还早呢！』原来乡试发榜，弥封卷子拆一名，写一名，从前一天半夜，一直要写到第二天晚上。向例写榜从第六名开始，前五名称为，『五经魁』，留到最后揭晓，那时已是第二天晚上，到拆五经魁的卷子时，闱中仆役杂工，人手一支红蜡烛，光耀如白昼，称为『闹榜』。其时黄昏未到，所以徐世光说是『报来还早呢』。『报！』外面又热闹了，徐世昌侧耳静听，报的是∶『贵府徐大少爷郎世昌，高中壬午科顺天乡试第一百四十五名举人。』这是真的吗？当然是真的，泥金报条上所写的，还怕会眼花看错，报子『连三元』来讨赏，赏了二十两还不肯，说是∶『大少爷、二少爷，双喜临门，起码得赏个一百两银子。』这总不是假的吧！争多论少，终于以四十两银子打发了『连三元』。不过这是『头报』，接下来还有『二报』、『三报』，少不得还要破费几两银子。这一夜会馆中很热闹，徐氏兄弟棠棣联辉，他们所住的那个院子，更是贺客接踵不断，直到午夜过后，才得清静下来，虽然人已经非常困倦了，但徐世昌的精神亢奋，一点睡意都没有。

On the day the list appeared, he waited from midnight until dawn and from dawn until noon. At last a messenger arrived with good news—but it left Xu Shichang acutely embarrassed. His younger brother, Xu Shiguang, had passed in ninety-fifth place. Rewards were paid and the provincial hostel bustled for a while. Once it grew quiet, Shichang could not help shedding miserable tears. “Elder Brother, I thought your examination essays better than mine,” Shiguang consoled him. “You must be among the top five. It is too early for that news.” When the provincial results were posted, the sealed names were opened and written out one by one, a process lasting from the previous midnight until the following evening. By custom, the list began with sixth place. The top five, the “Leaders of the Five Classics”, were disclosed last, by which time it was evening. When those scripts were opened, every servant and labourer in the examination compound held a red candle, making the place bright as day. This was called “making a stir over the list”. Dusk had not yet come, which was why Shiguang said the news was still to arrive. “News!” There was another commotion outside. Shichang listened intently. “Your household’s eldest young master, Xu Shichang, has passed the Shuntian provincial examination of the Renwu year in one hundred and forty-fifth place!” Could it be true? Of course it was. He might misread the gold-printed announcement, but the messenger, nicknamed “Three Firsts in Succession”, was demanding his reward. Even twenty taels did not satisfy him. “The eldest young master and the second young master—double happiness at your door! At least a hundred taels is called for.” Surely this could not be false.
