After bargaining, they finally sent him away with forty. That was only the first announcement; second and third messengers followed, costing a few more taels. The hostel was lively that night. With both Xu brothers bringing honour to the family, congratulations flowed especially thickly into their courtyard. Not until after midnight was it quiet. Though physically exhausted, Shichang was too excited to feel sleepy.

『二弟，好灵啊！』徐世昌突然跳起来，大声嚷着，倒把徐世光吓一大跳。『大哥，什么东西好灵？』『，二弟，你不能用「东西」这种字眼，我是说吕祖的签好灵。你看。』徐世昌指着签词∶『「光前裕后」，不明明道破，你的名次在前吗？』『呃！』徐世光也觉得有点道理，『真的，吕祖已经明示，我要沾大哥的光。』『不过，二弟，你也别太得意。你将来的成就不及我。』他以兄长的身份，用这样的口吻说话，徐世光自然只有保持沉默。『怎么，徐世昌说∶』你不相信？『『不是我不相信。我将来的成就不及大哥，也是可想而知的；不过刚刚是在谈吕祖的签，大哥一定在签上有所领悟，而没有说出一个究竟来，我就不便置喙了。』『当然！当然是签上透露的玄机，你看∶』昌大其门庭「，不就是我徐世昌才能荣宗耀祖吗？『『徐世光无话可答，只有连声应说是。『只有大哥才能昌大咱们徐家的门庭。』『二弟，』徐世昌神情肃穆地说∶『明天到吕祖祠去磕个头，一则谢谢他老人家的指点；再则今后的行止，也要请他老人家指点。』徐世光听兄长的话，第二天又一起到吕祖祠祝告求签。这回是各求一支，叩问行止，徐世光求得的签，意思不是如回家读书，明年春天会试再来；徐世昌的那一支是∶『出门何所图，胜如家里坐，虽无上天梯，一步高一步。』『二弟，你回去，我不能回去。』徐世昌说∶『签上说得很明白，出门胜似在家。我在京用功为妙。』

“Younger Brother, how accurate it was!” Shichang suddenly sprang up and shouted, giving Shiguang a fright. “What thing was accurate?” “You must not call it a ‘thing’. I mean Immortal Lü’s lot. Look!” He pointed to the words. “‘Glory to those before’—Guang, glory, plainly says that you would rank before me!” “Ah!” Shiguang thought there was something in it. “True. The Immortal indicated that I would share in Elder Brother’s glory.” “But do not be too pleased with yourself. Your future achievements will not equal mine.” As the elder brother spoke in that tone, Shiguang could only remain silent. “What? Do you not believe me?” “It is not that. One can readily imagine that my achievements will be less than yours. But we were discussing the lot. You must have found some meaning in it. Until you explained, it was not for me to speak.” “Of course! It reveals the secret. Look: ‘Make the family flourish greatly.’ Chang, flourish—that means I, Xu Shichang, will bring glory to our ancestors.” Shiguang had no answer beyond repeated agreement. “Only Elder Brother can make our Xu family flourish.” Shichang grew solemn. “Tomorrow we shall bow at the shrine: first to thank the Immortal for his guidance, and then to ask his advice on our next steps.” Shiguang obeyed. The next day they returned and each drew a lot about what to do. Shiguang’s advised him to return home to study and come again for the metropolitan examination in spring. Shichang’s read: “What gain in going from your door? / More than in sitting home once more. / Though no stair reaches Heaven’s height, / Each step will raise you towards the light.” “You go home, Younger Brother. I cannot. The lot is clear: going out is better than staying home. I should study in Beijing.”

徐世光自是听他作主，一个人先回家乡；徐世昌却寻得一个馆地，是兵部尚书张之万家，他们是世交，张之万将他请了去陪他的儿子张瑞荫一起读书，附带办办笔墨，住在张家后院。后院很宽敞，徐世昌布置了卧室、书房以外，还有余屋，打算着设一个神龛，供奉吕祖；主意将定未定之际，夜得一梦，梦见吕祖，告诉他说∶『你果真有心供奉我的香火，事须秘密；我云游稍倦，需要小憩时，自会降临，把你这里作为一个避嚣的静室，不宜有人打搅。』平时做梦，刚醒来时还记得，稍停一停，便忘得精光；只有这个梦，在他第二天起身漱洗时，还清清楚楚地记得。徐世昌认为吕祖托梦，非同小可，不过一定得遵照神灵指示办事，所以一切亲自动手，找一间最隐密的房间，悄悄置了一座神龛；白天门户紧闭，晚上直到院门关紧闩住，方开密室，在神前烧香膜拜，同时置了一副『吕祖神签』，以便疑难不决时，得以请吕祖指点。

Shiguang naturally accepted his decision and went home alone. Shichang found a tutoring place in the household of Zhang Zhiwan, Minister of War. The two families were old friends. Zhang invited him to study alongside his son, Zhang Ruiyin, and help with correspondence, lodging him in the rear courtyard. It was spacious. After setting up a bedroom and study, Xu still had rooms to spare and considered installing a shrine to Immortal Lü. Before he had quite decided, he dreamed that the Immortal said, “If you truly wish to offer me incense, you must keep it secret. When my wanderings tire me and I need a brief rest, I shall come and use your place as a quiet retreat from the world. I must not be disturbed.” Usually he remembered a dream upon waking, then soon forgot it entirely. This one remained perfectly clear even as he washed the next morning. He regarded such a visitation as momentous and was determined to follow the divine instructions. Doing everything himself, he chose the most secluded room and quietly installed a shrine. Its door stayed shut all day. At night, only after the courtyard gate had been closed and bolted would he open the secret room, burn incense and worship. He also obtained a set of “Immortal Lü’s Divination Lots”, so that he could seek guidance whenever in doubt.

这天接到袁世凯的来信，少不得也要求支签，问一问这件事能不能办？签上指示，不但可办，而且要速办；迟则不及。当下便向张瑞荫打听，吏部有没有熟人？『什么事？』『是一个朋友袁慰亭，有点麻烦。』徐世昌细说了缘由。『这是吏部文选句该管。』张瑞荫说∶『这种事找司官，不如找书办。』『正是，袁慰亭信中关照，也是要找书办；我问有没有熟人，就是说吏部书办之中有没有够交情的？』『我们这种人家，怎么会跟胥吏有交情？』张瑞荫说∶『等我来问问门上老牛。』徐世昌知道失言了，脸一红说∶『是，是，我说错了。就拜托你找老牛问一问吧？』

When Yuan’s letter arrived, he naturally drew a lot to ask whether the matter could be managed. The answer was not only yes, but that he must act quickly: delay would make him too late. He immediately asked Zhang Ruiyin whether he knew anyone at the Ministry of Personnel. “What is the matter?” “A friend, Yuan Weiting, has a difficulty.” Xu explained it in detail. “That belongs to the ministry’s Civil Appointments Department,” Zhang said. “For such business, a clerk is more useful than an officer.” “Exactly. Yuan stressed the same thing in his letter. When I asked whether you knew anyone, I meant whether you had a sufficiently friendly connection among the clerks.” “How could a family such as ours be on friendly terms with petty clerks? Let me ask Old Niu at the gate.” Realising his blunder, Xu blushed. “Yes, yes. I misspoke. Please ask Old Niu for me.”

将老牛找了来一问，他说∶『我们熟识一个姓何的，在吏部文选人司很吃得开。不过不知道在在京？』『怎么？吏部的书办不在京里会在什么地方呢？』『老何原籍山西蒲州，前一阵子我听说他要请假回老家去上坟，不知道走了没有？』『你赶快去一趟。』张瑞荫说∶『看看何书办在不在？在呢，就跟他说，有这么一件事。』这件事的始末，由徐世昌告诉了老牛，请老牛去谈。这是有回扣的事，老牛很巴结地，当时便去找何书办接头。到晚来回话，『好险！』老牛说道∶『只差一步，行李都上车了，明儿一大早就走。』『喔，你跟他谈了？』『是的。』『有办法没有？』『他们怎么会没办法？』老牛笑道∶『就怕「盘子」谈不拢。』『他开的「盘口」是多少？』张瑞荫问。

They summoned the gatekeeper. “We know a man called He,” he said. “He has considerable influence in Civil Appointments. But I do not know whether he is in Beijing.” “Why? Where else would a ministry clerk be?” “Old He comes from Puzhou in Shanxi. Some time ago I heard he intended to take leave and visit his ancestral graves. I do not know whether he has gone.” “Go there at once,” Zhang Ruiyin said. “Find out whether he is still here. If he is, tell him about the matter.” Xu explained everything to Old Niu and asked him to negotiate. There was a commission to be earned, so the gatekeeper eagerly went straight to Clerk He. That evening he returned. “A close thing! One step later and we would have missed him. His luggage was already on the cart, and he leaves first thing tomorrow.” “Oh? Did you speak to him?” “Yes.” “Is there a solution?” “How could they lack solutions?” Old Niu laughed. “The only question is whether we can agree on the price.” “What price has he named?” Zhang asked.

『何书办说，这件事一进一出，关系极大，如果袁老爷的中书还不出娘家，不但升同知不必谈，还要追究他何以资历不符，那就不是吏部的事了。』『不是吏部的事，』徐世昌问∶『是哪一部的事呢？』『刑部。』『好家伙！』徐世昌大吃一惊，『还要治罪啊！』『人家是这么说，咱们也不能全听他的。不过，袁老爷正好有这个短处非求他不可，那就只好听他狮子大开口了。』『要多少？』『两千。』正好差了一半；徐世昌面有难色，将袁世凯的信，递给了张瑞荫看。看完信，张瑞荫问道∶『老牛，他跟你说了没有，是怎么个办法？』『大少爷，你倒想，他肯跟我说吗？我倒是盯着问了好半天，他只跟我说了一句话∶「事不难办，不过就告诉了你，你也办不到。』『好吧，跟他讲盘子吧，最多给他一个半数。』张瑞荫又说∶『徐老爷的朋友，不是外人。』这时是暗示老牛别从中乱戴帽子∶『是！既然大少爷交代，我尽力去办就是。』老牛又说∶『我得连夜跟何书办去谈，不然人家天一亮就走人了。』

“Clerk He says the movements into and out of office make this a serious matter. If Mr Yuan’s secretaryship cannot be traced to its proper origin, promotion to deputy prefect is out of the question. There will also be an investigation into the discrepancy in his record. Then it will no longer concern the Ministry of Personnel.” “Which ministry would it concern?” Xu asked. “Punishments.” “Good heavens!” Xu was alarmed. “He could be prosecuted?” “That is what they say. We need not believe every word, but Mr Yuan has this weakness and must ask their help. We can only listen to an extravagant demand.” “How much?” “Two thousand.” Exactly twice the available sum. Looking troubled, Xu handed Yuan’s letter to Zhang Ruiyin. After reading it, Zhang asked, “Old Niu, did he tell you how he means to do it?” “Would he tell me, Young Master? I pressed him for ages. All he would say was, ‘The matter is not difficult, but even if I told you, you could not do it.’” “Very well. Bargain with him. Fifteen hundred at the most. Mr Xu’s friend is not an outsider.” This hinted that Old Niu must not inflate the price for his own benefit. “Yes. With your instructions, Young Master, I shall do my utmost. But I must negotiate with him tonight. He leaves at first light.”

连夜折冲，以一千二百两银子成交；先交一半，等办妥了再交一半。徐世昌第二天到天津，去北洋公所将一千两银子领了出来，存在阜康福钱庄，先打了一张六百两的票子，交给张瑞荫。张瑞荫办事也很周到，将老牛唤了来说道∶『你最好把何书办约出来，大家当面锣、对面鼓说明白。怕的万一出了什么差错，徐老爷对袁老爷也有个交代。』『是。』老牛便去约好何书办，在一家饭馆见面。部中胥吏的身份甚低，尽管衣着比纨绔子弟还讲究，但见了张瑞荫称『大少爷』，对徐世昌叫『徐老爷』，站着说话，执礼甚恭。等把银票递了过去，何书办接到手中，摆在桌上，然后请个安说∶『跟大少爷、徐老爷回，事情呢？一举手之劳，不过要经十三道关口，一关过不去就不成。银票我暂时收着，也不会去兑，等事情办妥了再说。』『是的，你多费心。』张瑞荫问∶『徐老爷应该怎么回复袁老爷？』『请徐老爷告诉袁老爷，说当初捐中书的名字不假，只为将门之后，投效戎行，所以改名「世凯」。只要北洋这么咨复吏部，一准改名，袁老爷的同知就升定了。』这个诀窍说穿了不稀奇，但如果不是在吏部打通了关节，一改名字就可能会把整个前程断送掉，因为要刁难的话，随便找个理由就可以折磨个一年半载，及至一关通过，又有另一关，非把钱花够了数，不能领一张俗称为『部照「的任官』文凭『，而在更名未确定以前，不能分发，不能赴任，只有闲等，先就是一大损失。所以考试发榜，吏部铨选，如果姓名弄错，往往情甘委屈，将错就错，象袁世凯这样顺利的假』更名『，实在很少。

After negotiations that night, they settled at twelve hundred taels: half in advance and half when the business was complete. The next day Xu went to Tianjin, collected the thousand taels from the Beiyang Office, deposited them at Fukangfu Bank and first obtained a note for six hundred, which he handed to Zhang. Zhang was thorough. He summoned Old Niu. “You had better arrange a meeting with Clerk He, so that everyone can speak plainly face to face. If anything goes wrong, Mr Xu must have an account to give Mr Yuan.” “Yes.” Old Niu arranged for them to meet at a restaurant. Ministry clerks occupied a low social position. Though He dressed more elegantly than a rich young dandy, he addressed Zhang as “Young Master” and Xu as “Mr Xu”, standing while he spoke and observing every courtesy. When the banknote was handed over, he placed it on the table, bowed and said, “To explain to you both: the task itself is no more than lifting a hand, but it must pass thirteen gates. Failure at any one of them will stop it. I shall keep the note for now without cashing it until the work is done.” “Very well. Thank you for your trouble,” Zhang said. “How should Mr Xu reply to Mr Yuan?” “Tell him that the name under which the secretaryship was purchased was genuine. As a descendant of a military family, he subsequently volunteered for service and adopted the name Shikai. Let Beiyang send that explanation to Personnel. Once the change of name is approved, his promotion is assured.” The device was simple once explained. Without connections smoothed at the ministry, however, a change of name could destroy an entire career. If the clerks wished to obstruct it, any excuse could delay the matter for six months or a year. After one barrier was passed, another would arise. Until enough money had been spent, no appointment warrant—the document commonly called a ministry certificate—would be issued. While the change remained unresolved, the man could neither be assigned a post nor take one up. Waiting alone was a substantial loss. Consequently, when a name was wrong in examination results or Personnel’s appointments, many willingly endured it and continued under the error. A fraudulent “name change” as smooth as Yuan’s was rare indeed.

即令如此，公文往返，也得半年工夫。其时局势又已有变化，李鸿章的回任已经定局了——从张树声父子无意间得罪了张佩纶以后，李鸿章发觉张树声对北洋有『久假不归』之势，便利用时机，极力拉拢张佩纶，走李鸿藻的路子，搞出来一个与张树声各回本任的结果，但李鸿章母丧尚不满一年，而朝鲜的内乱已经平定，不必再动用武力，就没有『墨从军』而且『夺情』的理由，好在洋务上棘手之事甚多，以需要李鸿章与各国公使折冲的借口，将李鸿章留了下来，等待适当的时机再颁回任之谕。当李鸿章自合肥老家入京时，在上海住了好些日子，对左宗棠打算驱逐李鸿章势力出两江的情形，印象深刻。同时，对洋务、军务的见解，大相径庭，象中国与法国在越南的纠纷，李鸿章认为『彼欲难餍，我饷难支』，应该和平了结，决不可用武，而左宗棠主张支持助越拒法的『黑旗军「刘永福，不但以军火接济云贵总督岑毓英转以援刘，而且正式致书总理衙门，认为』主战主和，不难一言而决『，目前的情势，』不但泰西诸邦多以法为不然；逆料其与中国不协，必致事无结束，悔不可追『，就是法国亦何尝不顾虑，真要与中国开战，危险甚大，不过势成骑虎，不能不虚张声势，如果中国动摇，适中其计。他说他』默察时局，惟主战于正义有合，而于时势攸宜，即中外人情亦无不顺『就因为他一直有这种论调，所以朝廷特派李鸿章前赴广东督办越南事宜；这是一个名义，实际上李鸿章并不必赴广东，在上海、在天津，都可以跟法国公使宝海和谈。但如左宗棠不断鼓吹武力干预，到最后恐终不免要到广东去指挥对法战事，那一来只怕非身败名裂不可。当然，总署亦很持重，不会轻信左宗棠的『正义』说，只是李鸿章跟宝海的交涉，因此而愈感困难。

Even so, the exchange of official documents took half a year. By then the situation had changed again: Li Hongzhang’s return to his former post was settled. After Zhang Shusheng and his son inadvertently offended Zhang Peilun, Li realised that Zhang Shusheng seemed inclined to borrow Beiyang indefinitely without returning it. He seized the opportunity, cultivated Zhang Peilun and worked through Li Hongzao to secure a decision that both men should return to their substantive posts. But less than a year had passed since his mother’s death, and the Korean disturbance had been quelled. With no further need for force, there was no justification for summoning him from mourning to military service. Fortunately, foreign affairs offered many difficult matters. On the pretext that he was needed to negotiate with the foreign ministers, Li was retained, awaiting a suitable moment for the formal order restoring him. Travelling from his Hefei home to Beijing, he spent many days in Shanghai and gained a strong impression of Zuo’s intention to expel his influence from Liangjiang. Their views on foreign and military affairs also differed sharply. Over the dispute with France in Vietnam, Li believed that “their demands cannot be satisfied, while our military funds cannot bear the strain”. A peaceful settlement was necessary; force must not be used. Zuo, by contrast, advocated supporting Liu Yongfu’s Black Flag Army in helping Vietnam resist France. He supplied arms to Cen Yuying, governor-general of Yunnan and Guizhou, for onward delivery to Liu, and formally wrote the Zongli Yamen that “the choice between war and peace can be settled in a word”. At present, he argued, “not only do many Western countries consider France in the wrong; they foresee that discord with China must leave the affair without a conclusion and bring irretrievable regret”. France itself feared the great risks of war with China, but, having mounted the tiger, could only bluff. If China wavered, it would fall into the trap. His assessment was that war accorded with justice, suited the circumstances and conformed to opinion both in China and abroad. Because he kept arguing thus, the court specially ordered Li to Guangdong to supervise Vietnamese affairs. This was nominal: Li need not actually go there, since he could negotiate peace with the French minister Bourée in Shanghai or Tianjin. But if Zuo continued urging armed intervention, Li might eventually have to command a war against France from Guangdong—a prospect that threatened to ruin him. The Zongli Yamen was cautious and would not readily accept Zuo’s argument from “justice”. Nevertheless, his advocacy made Li’s negotiations with Bourée more difficult.

对法如此，对英亦常使李鸿章伤脑筋。英商的海洋电报线希望由吴淞接一条旱线到上海，左宗棠坚持不许；英商希望减轻茧捐，左宗棠亦表反对。而最使李鸿章为难的是，左宗棠倡议洋药土烟加厘一事。『洋药土烟』皆指鸦片，『加厘』便是加『通行税』。左宗棠认为鸦片流毒无穷，主张寓禁于征，奉旨允准后，会同李鸿章与英国公使威妥玛交涉。威妥玛提出洋药进口增加税，行销内地在各关卡所征厘捐不增加；左宗棠也同意了，但每箱的进口税，中国要一百五十两；英国只愿缴八十两。相差太巨，一直没有成议。以后左宗棠外放，交涉由李鸿章接办，而威妥玛奉调回国，希望此一交涉能如英国的条件谈成功，增添他回国以后的面子，李鸿章有心帮忙，却以左宗棠的不肯妥协，以江督的地位表示反对，搞得事成僵局。

Britain, too, gave Li headaches. British merchants wanted to connect their submarine telegraph cable at Wusong to Shanghai by a land line; Zuo firmly refused. They sought a reduction in the cocoon levy; he opposed that as well. Most troublesome was his proposal to raise the likin on imported opium and native-grown opium. He regarded opium’s harm as limitless and proposed using taxation as a means of suppression. After imperial approval, he and Li negotiated with the British minister, Thomas Wade. Wade proposed increasing the import duty while leaving inland transit levies unchanged. Zuo agreed in principle, but China demanded a hundred and fifty taels per chest and Britain offered only eighty. The gap was too wide for agreement. After Zuo took a provincial appointment, Li continued the negotiations. Wade, recalled to Britain, hoped to conclude the matter on British terms and return home with enhanced standing. Li wished to help, but Zuo refused to compromise and opposed the terms as governor-general of Liangjiang, producing a deadlock.

但在事业上最大的冲突是，李鸿章原主『海防』，而张佩纶有个专设『水师衙门』创办新式海军之议，大为李鸿章所欣赏。但左宗棠一到两江，巡阅过海口及长江以后，改变了他原来『陆防』的主张，特意将水师出身的彭玉麟请了来，商量造新式兵舰，而且已经开始在办了。左宗棠首创福建船政，对此道不能说他是外行；因此可以预见的是，将来创办新式海军，左宗棠决不容北洋单独掌权。『海防』、『陆防』之争，只要打倒了他的理论，便无他虑；如今左宗棠亦主张海防，那就变成彼此竞争着办一件事，权不能独专、事不能由心，是李鸿章最不能容忍的一件事。因此，无论看眼前，算将来，李鸿章认为左宗棠是非拔除不可的眼中钉。这得从剪除左宗棠的羽翼着手。李鸿章手下的谋士，都有这样一种见解；且认为第一个目标，应该是胡雪岩。于是上海道邵友濂便与盛宣怀等人，密密商定了一个打击胡雪岩的办法，在洋债还款这件事上，造成胡雪岩的困窘。

Their greatest conflict of policy concerned the navy. Li had originally advocated maritime defence, and greatly admired Zhang Peilun’s proposal for a special Naval Yamen and a modern fleet. But after reaching Liangjiang and inspecting the coast and Yangtze, Zuo changed his earlier preference for land defence. He invited Peng Yulin, whose career had begun in the naval forces, to discuss modern warship construction, and work was already under way. Having founded the Fujian shipbuilding establishment, Zuo could not be dismissed as an amateur. It was therefore foreseeable that he would never permit Beiyang sole authority over the future modern navy. In a dispute between land and sea defence, defeating his argument would have removed the difficulty. Now that he too favoured maritime defence, they would compete to carry out the same enterprise. Li could not tolerate sharing authority or being unable to act as he wished. Whether he considered the present or the future, Zuo was a thorn in his eye that must be removed. The first step was to strip away his support. Li’s advisers agreed, and thought Hu Xueyan should be the first target. Shanghai Circuit Intendant Shao Youlian accordingly made a secret plan with Sheng Xuanhuai and others: they would place Hu in difficulty over repayment of a foreign loan.

其时胡雪岩经手、尚未清结的借款，还有两笔，一笔是光绪四年八月所借的商款，华洋各计，总计六百五十万两；洋款不借借商款，其中别有衷曲，原来光绪三年，由胡雪岩经手，向汇丰银行借款五百万两，借还均用实银，条件是月息一分二厘五，期限七年，连本带利分十四期拔还。每期六个月，仍由浙、粤、江海、江汉四关出票，按期偿还。此外有个附带条件，即商定此项条件后，如果借方作罢，三个月内关票不到，则胡雪岩罚银十五万两；汇丰如果三个月内不交银，罚款相间。这笔借款由于两江总督沈葆桢的介入，一波三折，拖延甚久。其时西征军事颇为顺手，刘锦棠率军自乌鲁木齐南进，并分兵与陕西提督张曜会功吐鲁番，一举克复，回乱首脑之一的白彦虎仓皇西遁；刘锦棠亦推进至吐鲁番盆地西端的托克逊，回众投降者两万余人。但回部首脑经和硕、焉耆，出铁门关在库尔勒地方，跟俄国军方搭上了线，而西征军却因粮饷困难，无法西进，左宗棠着急得不得了。好不容易在五月里谈成功了这笔洋债，至少望梅止渴，军心先是一振；同时在上海、湖北、陕西的三处粮台，借商款应急，亦比较容易措手了。

Two loans handled by Hu remained outstanding. One was merchant funding contracted in the eighth month of the fourth year of Guangxu, with Chinese and foreign portions totalling six and a half million taels. Why had merchant funding been used rather than a foreign loan? There was a story behind it. In Guangxu’s third year, Hu had arranged a five-million-tael loan from HSBC, to be advanced and repaid in actual silver. Interest was one and a quarter per cent a month, with principal and interest repaid in fourteen instalments over seven years. Each instalment fell six months apart. As before, repayment was secured by bills issued by the Zhejiang, Guangdong, Shanghai and Jianghan customs. A supplementary condition stipulated that if the borrower abandoned the transaction after agreement, failing to supply the customs bills within three months, Hu would forfeit a hundred and fifty thousand taels. HSBC would incur the same penalty if it failed to deliver the silver within three months. Intervention by Shen Baozhen, governor-general of Liangjiang, caused repeated complications and long delay. The western campaign was then going well. Liu Jintang had advanced south from Urumqi and sent a force to join Shaanxi provincial commander Zhang Yao in an attack that recovered Turfan at one stroke. Bai Yanhu, one of the Muslim rebel leaders, fled west in haste. Liu reached Toksun at the western end of the Turfan basin, and more than twenty thousand Muslim followers surrendered. But the leadership passed through Hoxud and Karashahr, emerged from Iron Gate Pass and established contact with the Russian military at Korla. Meanwhile, shortages of provisions and pay prevented the expeditionary army from advancing west, to Zuo’s intense anxiety. At last the foreign loan was negotiated in the fifth month. Even the prospect was like imagining plums to quench thirst: army morale revived, and the supply offices in Shanghai, Hubei and Shaanxi found it easier to borrow from merchants for immediate needs.

哪知在办手续时，起了波折，原来英商汇丰银行贷款，照例要由总理衙门出面，致英国公使一个照会，叙明借款条件等等，由英国公使再转行总税务司及驻上海领事，转知汇丰银行照办。这一来，如果贷款放出去收不回，便可由英国向中国交涉；这通照会实际上是中国政府所出的保证书，所以由汇丰银行拟好稿子，交给胡雪岩，再经左宗棠咨请总理衙门办理，而汇丰的稿子中，说明『息银不得过一分』，然则左宗棠的奏折中，何以说是月息一分二厘五？为此，其中处于关键地位的总税务司赫德，表示这笔借款不能成立。这当然要查。左宗棠根据胡雪岩的答复回奏，说汇丰的息银，只有一分，诚然不错，但付款办法是以先令计算，折付银圆；这种银圆，一向在东南各省通用，称之为『烂番银』，西北向不通用，所以仍旧须借以两为单位的现银。但先令的市价，根据伦敦挂牌，早晚不同；到时候如果汇价上涨，胡雪岩便要吃赔帐，所以接洽德商泰来洋行，『包认先令』，这要承担相当风险，泰来洋行得息二厘五，并不为多。左宗棠表示，此案『首尾本属一贯』，只是前次『未经声叙明析』，又力言胡雪岩『息借洋款，实无别故』。很显然的，这是左宗棠硬顶下来的，朝廷不能不买他的老面子；左宗棠心里却觉得很不是味道，从此对胡雪岩的信用便打了一个折扣，可是却不能不用胡雪岩。

Complications arose during the formalities. A loan from the British-owned HSBC ordinarily required the Zongli Yamen to notify the British minister of its terms. The minister then instructed the inspector-general of customs and the Shanghai consul to inform HSBC accordingly. If the loan could not be recovered, Britain could then raise the matter with China. In effect, the note was a Chinese government guarantee. HSBC therefore drafted it and gave it to Hu, who passed it through Zuo with a request that the Zongli Yamen act. Yet the bank’s draft said that interest must not exceed one per cent. Why, then, had Zuo’s memorial specified one and a quarter per cent a month? Robert Hart, the inspector-general of customs whose role was crucial, declared the loan could not proceed. An investigation was inevitable. On the basis of Hu’s explanation, Zuo memorialised that HSBC’s interest was indeed only one per cent. But the loan was reckoned in shillings and paid in silver dollars. Those dollars, commonly called “worn foreign silver”, circulated in the southeastern provinces but not in the northwest. The funds therefore still had to be obtained in actual silver measured in taels. The shilling exchange rate varied with London quotations, even between morning and evening. If it rose at the time of conversion, Hu would suffer a loss. He had therefore arranged with the German Tailai Trading House to guarantee the shilling conversion. This involved substantial risk, for which its additional quarter of a percentage point in interest was not excessive. Zuo insisted that the arrangement had been consistent throughout and merely had not previously been explained clearly. He strongly asserted that Hu had no ulterior motive in borrowing abroad. Plainly, he was taking responsibility and standing firm; the court could hardly refuse to respect his seniority. Inwardly, however, he felt badly about it. His trust in Hu diminished, though he could not do without him.

胡雪岩当然亦想力盖前愆，于是而有借商款的办法，这年——光绪三年年底，左宗棠写给胡雪岩的复信说∶『今岁饷事，拮据殊常，非枢邸严催协饷，筹部款，大局已不可问。洋款枝节横生，非阁下苦心孤诣，竭力维持，无从说起。』现在年关满饷，仍待洋款头批速到，始够支销，除清还鄂欠外，尚须匀拨陕赈及甘属灾黎，所余洋款，除清还沪局借款外，核计敷至明年夏秋之交而止，此后又不知何以为计？尊意以为兵事可慰，饷事则殊可忧，不得不先一年预为之地，洵切实确凿之论，弟心中所欲奉商者，阁下已代为计之，非设身处地，通盘熟筹，不能道其只字，万里同心，不言而喻。『原来胡雪岩早替左宗棠算过了，年底本应发饷；陕甘两省旱灾要赈济，再还了湖北、上海两处借款，到得明年夏秋之间，便又是青黄不接的时期了。借款筹饷要早一年便须着手。可是洋款已不能借。借洋款是国家的责任，虽说由各省协饷，但灾荒连年，各省情形都不好，欠解西征协饷，无法归还欠款，仍须政府设法，所以根本不能再提洋款。而且左宗棠因为借洋款，要受赫德的气，自己亦不大愿意借洋款，尤其是英商的款子。胡雪岩想到左宗棠说过，『息耗太重，如果是商款，楚弓楚得，倒还罢了。洋人赚了我们重利，还要多方挑剔，实在不甘。』同时又一再表示，『何必海关及各省出票？倒象是各省替陕甘来还债；其实还的还是陕甘应得的协饷。我主持西征，筹饷我有全权；协饷不到，先借款子来接济，这就是所谓调度。商人如果相信陕甘相信我，由陕甘出票就可以了，何必劳动总署？』

Hu naturally wished to make amends, and devised the merchant-loan method. At the end of that year, Guangxu’s third, Zuo wrote in reply: “This year our finances have been exceptionally strained. Had the prince at the Grand Council not pressed urgently for provincial assistance and arranged funds from the ministry, the whole undertaking would have been beyond saving. Complications arose at every turn in the foreign loan. Without your painstaking ingenuity and tireless support, nothing could have been done. Even now, the year-end payroll depends on the swift arrival of its first advance. After settling the Hubei debt, we must allocate money for famine relief in Shaanxi and the stricken people of Gansu. Of the remaining loan, after repaying the Shanghai office’s borrowings, enough will last only until the turn of summer and autumn next year. What is to be done after that, I do not know. You say that military affairs give comfort but finance gives grave concern, and preparations must begin a year ahead. That is a thoroughly practical and sound judgement. What I wished to discuss, you have already considered for me. Without putting yourself in my position and examining the whole situation, you could not have said a word of it. Across ten thousand li, we are of one mind without needing explanation.” Hu had indeed calculated it all: the year-end payroll, famine relief after drought in Shaanxi and Gansu, and repayment of the Hubei and Shanghai loans would leave another gap around the following summer and autumn. Borrowing for military finance required a year’s preparation. Yet another foreign loan was out of the question. Such loans were national obligations. Though the provinces were to provide the funds, successive disasters had impoverished them, and arrears in their contributions to the western campaign meant that the government would still have to find repayment. Further foreign borrowing could not even be proposed. Moreover, Zuo resented being subjected to Hart’s obstruction and was reluctant to borrow abroad, particularly from British firms. Hu recalled his words: “The interest and charges are too heavy. With merchant money, what Chu loses Chu gains, and that might be tolerable. But foreigners make great profits from us and still find fault at every turn. It is galling.” He had also said repeatedly, “Why must the customs and provinces issue bills, as though they were repaying Shaanxi and Gansu’s debts? The repayments come from contributions those provinces are already owed. I command the western campaign and have full authority to arrange its funds. Borrowing when assistance is late is simply managing cash flow. If merchants trust Shaanxi and Gansu and trust me, bills from those provinces should suffice. Why trouble the Zongli Yamen?”

因此他设计了一套借商款的办法，往返磋商定议，由胡雪岩邀集商股一百七十五万两；另由汇丰『认股』一百七十五万两，合并三百五十万，组识一个乾泰公司负责借出。照左宗棠的计算，在七年之中，陕甘可得协饷一千八百十万以上，除还洋款以外，至少尚有千万之多，所以借几百万商款，一定能够清偿；但协饷收到的日期不一，多寡不定，所以提出来一个『机圆法话』的要求，第一，不出关票；第二，不定年限，可以早还，亦可以迟还；第三，有钱还钱、无钱暂欠，利息照算，不必定为几个月一期。这是他一厢情愿的想法，胡雪岩只能替他办到不出关票，此外年限定为六年，期次仍是半年一期，利息是一分二。当然借商款亦须奏准；左宗棠于光绪四年八月十六日出奏，一个月以后奉到廷寄∶『借用商款，息银既重，各省关每年除划还本息外，京协各饷，更属无从筹措，本系万不得已之计。此次故念左宗棠筹办各务，事在垂成，准照所议办理。嗣后无论何项急需，不得动辄息借商款，致贻后累。『所谓』京饷『，即是在京的各项开支，包括文武百官的俸给、八旗士兵的饷项，以及一年三次送入宫内供两宫太后及皇帝私人开支的』交进银『在内，是最重要的一笔预算，由于左宗棠动辄借款之累，连京饷都』无从筹措『，这话说得很重了。

Hu therefore devised a merchant-loan scheme. After repeated discussion, he undertook to assemble one million seven hundred and fifty thousand taels in merchant subscriptions, with HSBC “subscribing” the same amount. The combined three and a half million would be lent through a company called Qiantai. Zuo calculated that over seven years Shaanxi and Gansu would receive more than eighteen million one hundred thousand taels in provincial assistance. Even after repaying the foreign loan, at least ten million would remain, so a merchant loan of several million could certainly be discharged. But remittances arrived at uncertain times and in varying amounts. He therefore requested flexible terms: first, no customs bills; second, no fixed maturity, so that repayment could be early or late; third, pay when money was available and owe it temporarily when it was not, with interest continuing but no fixed interval between instalments. This was wishful thinking. Hu could secure only the absence of customs bills. The term was fixed at six years, instalments remained half-yearly, and interest was one and a fifth per cent a month. Merchant borrowing also required imperial approval. Zuo memorialised on the sixteenth day of the eighth month of Guangxu’s fourth year. A month later he received the court’s reply: “Merchant loans carry heavy interest. After annual allocations from the provincial and customs revenues for principal and interest, funds for the capital and other military requirements cannot be raised. Such borrowing is a last resort. In consideration of Zuo Zongtang’s undertakings, now approaching completion, the present proposal is approved. Hereafter, whatever the urgency, merchant loans at interest must not be resorted to lightly, imposing burdens on the future.” “Capital funds” covered the expenses of government in Beijing: civil and military salaries, pay for the Eight Banner soldiers, and the silver delivered to the palace three times a year for the private expenses of the two empress dowagers and emperor. It was the most important budget item. To say that even these funds could not be raised because of Zuo’s repeated borrowing was a severe rebuke.

为此，一直到上年左宗棠奉召入京，为了替刘锦棠筹划西征善后，才迫不得已，在近乎独断独行的情况下，借了汇丰银行招股所贷的四百万两。这两笔款子的风险，都在胡雪岩一个人身上。三百五十万的商款，自光绪五年起分期拔还，几乎已还了一半；而且每期本息约十来万银子，邵友濂亦知道，难不倒胡雪岩，要刁难他，只有在光绪七年所借的那一笔上。这笔款子实收于光绪七年四月，年息九厘九毫五，前两年只付息，不还本，第三年起每年拔本一百万两，分两期给付；光绪九年四月付第一期、十月付第二期，每期各五十万两。以前各次洋债，虽由胡雪岩经手，但如何偿还，不用他来操心，因为各省督抚加了印的『关票』，汇集于江海关后，税务司还要签押负连带责任，如果各省的『关票』不能兑现，税务司可以截留税款，代为抵付。可是这最后一次的四百万两，在借款时为了替刘锦棠解除后顾之忧，左宗棠近乎独断独行，只以为未来数年协饷尚多，不愁无法偿还，所以大包大揽地说∶本银『如期由上海转运局经手交还；如上海无银，应准其向户部如期兑取。』这一惟恐总理衙门及李鸿章策动棘德阴挠，但求成功不惜迁就的承诺，无形之中便将全部风险都加了在胡雪岩的肩头上，因为各省如果不解，汇丰银行一定找胡雪岩，他们不必多费周折，请英国公使出面跟户部打交道；以胡雪岩的财力、信用与担当，每期五十万两银子的本银，亦一定挑得起来。

Not until the previous year, when Zuo was summoned to Beijing, had he borrowed again. Obliged to arrange finance for Liu Jintang’s settlement of the western regions, he acted almost on his own authority and took a four-million-tael loan raised by HSBC through subscriptions. The risks on both loans rested entirely on Hu. Repayment of the three-and-a-half-million merchant loan had begun in Guangxu’s fifth year, and nearly half had been paid. Each instalment of principal and interest came to only a hundred thousand taels or so. Shao knew that would not trouble Hu; to obstruct him, he must use the loan contracted in the seventh year. Those funds were actually received in the fourth month of that year, at an annual rate stated as “nine li, nine hao and five”. For the first two years only interest was payable. From the third year, a million taels of principal fell due annually, in two instalments: the first in the fourth month of Guangxu’s ninth year and the second in the tenth, five hundred thousand taels each. Hu had handled earlier foreign loans without having to worry about repayment. The customs bills bore the provincial chiefs’ seals and, once collected at Shanghai Customs, were countersigned by the commissioner, who assumed joint liability. If provincial bills could not be honoured, he could withhold customs revenue to pay them. But with the last four-million-tael loan, Zuo had acted almost independently to free Liu Jintang of financial anxiety. Assuming that future provincial remittances would easily cover it, he had broadly promised that principal would “be repaid on schedule through the Shanghai Transport Office; if Shanghai has no funds, the lender shall be permitted to obtain payment on schedule from the Ministry of Revenue”. He feared that the Zongli Yamen and Li might prompt Hart to obstruct the loan, and accepted concessions to secure it. The promise quietly placed the entire risk on Hu’s shoulders. If the provinces failed to remit, HSBC would certainly approach him. There was no need for the trouble of asking the British minister to negotiate with Revenue. With his resources, credit and willingness to shoulder responsibility, Hu would surely meet each five-hundred-thousand-tael principal instalment.

话虽如此，五十万两银子到底不是一个小数目。邵友濂与盛宣怀秘密商定，到时候，『挤他一挤』，虽未必能挤倒，至少可以打击打击他的信用。其时——光绪九年春天，中法的关系复又恶化了。本来前一年十一月间，李鸿章与法国公使在上海谈判，已经达成了和平解决在越南的纠纷的三点协议。但法国海军部及殖民部，分别向他们的外交部表示，不满宝海与李鸿章的协议，海军方面且已增兵越南北部的海防。而又恰好法国发生政潮，新内阁的外交部长沙美拉库支持军部的主张，推翻前议，而且将宝海撤任，另派特使德理固专程来华谈判。妙的是法国公使宝海，特为自上海到天津去看李鸿章，他劝李鸿章坚持前议，不防指责法国政府违约；有了这种反对他们政府的法国公使，李鸿章觉得谈和又有把握了，所以仍旧照原定计划，奏请准予给假回籍葬亲。李还不肯回任，但为了开始建设旅顺军港，北洋大臣的差使是接下来了，既然请假，北洋大臣自然由张树声暂署。

Even so, half a million taels was no small sum. Shao Youlian and Sheng Xuanhuai secretly agreed to “give him a squeeze” when the time came. They might not bring him down, but could at least damage his credit. That spring, Guangxu’s ninth year, relations with France deteriorated again. In the eleventh month of the previous year, Li and the French minister had reached a three-point agreement in Shanghai for a peaceful settlement of the Vietnamese dispute. But the French naval and colonial authorities separately told their foreign ministry that they objected to Bourée’s agreement. The navy had already sent reinforcements to Haiphong in northern Vietnam. A political change in France then brought a new cabinet. Its foreign minister, Challemel-Lacour, supported the military position, repudiated the agreement, recalled Bourée and sent Tricou as special envoy to negotiate in China. Curiously, Bourée himself travelled from Shanghai to Tianjin to see Li and urged him to uphold their earlier agreement, even accusing the French government of breaking its word. With a French minister opposing his own government, Li felt confident of peace once more. He therefore followed his original plan and requested leave to return home for his mother’s burial. He was still unwilling to resume his former office, but had accepted the Beiyang commission in order to begin construction of the naval harbour at Lüshun. During his leave, Zhang Shusheng would naturally act in that capacity.

但就在二月里李鸿章在合肥原籍时，法军在越南复又动武，不但攻占越南南定，而且直接侵犯中国在越南权益，招商局运米的船，在海防为法军扣押；设在海防及顺安的两处仓库，为法军占领，其中的存粮及其他物品，当然也被没收了。加以越南政府除行文礼部乞援外，并特派『刑部尚书』范慎来华，效『申包胥哭秦庭』，因此，朝中震动，清议昂扬，都主张采取强硬的对策；甚至驻英兼驻法公使一等毅勇侯曾纪泽，亦打电报回来，建议派军援越，不可对法国让步。当时疆臣亦多主战，云贵总督岑毓英，备战已有多时，但署理两广总督的曾国荃，却不愿轻启战端，清议深为不满，因而主持总署的恭王，一面循外交途径向法国抗议；一面奏准命李鸿章迅回直隶总督本任，接着降谕，派李鸿章以直隶总督的身分迅往广东督办越南事宜，所有广东、广西、云南防军，均归节制。同时命左宗棠筹划江南防军待命南调援越。这时胡雪岩恰好在江宁，便跟左宗棠说∶『好象应该还有张制军回两广本任的上谕；不然，李合肥一到天津，不就是有了两位直隶总署？』『妙就妙在没有张振轩回本任的上谕。』左宗棠答说，『总署也知道李少荃决不会到广东，恐怕也不会回天津。』『这，大人倒多指点指点，让我们也开开茅塞。』『李少荃看在曾文正分上，对曾老九一向是很客气的。当年江宁之围，师老无功，李少荃已经克复了常州，朝命赴援江宁，他按兵不动，为的是不愿分曾老九的功。你想，如今他如果一到广东，曾老九怎么办？』『是，是。』胡雪岩想了一下说∶『大人说李合肥也不会到天津，是怕一到了，张制军就得回广东，那一来不是又要把曾九帅挤走的吗？』『正是如此。』

But in the second month, while Li was at his ancestral home in Hefei, French forces resumed hostilities in Vietnam. They not only captured Nam Dinh but directly infringed Chinese interests. A China Merchants ship carrying rice was detained at Haiphong, and its warehouses at Haiphong and Thuan An were occupied; the grain and other property within them were confiscated. Besides formally seeking aid through the Ministry of Rites, the Vietnamese government specially sent its “Minister of Punishments”, Fan Shen, to China, emulating Shen Baoxu’s weeping appeal at the court of Qin. The court was shaken. Moral criticism rose, and all called for a firm response. Even Zeng Jize, the first-rank Marquis Yiyong and minister to Britain and France, telegraphed to urge military aid to Vietnam and no concessions to France. Many provincial chiefs also favoured war. Cen Yuying had long prepared for it, but Zeng Guoquan, acting governor-general of Liangguang, would not lightly begin hostilities, to the critics’ great displeasure. Prince Gong, directing the Zongli Yamen, protested diplomatically to France while obtaining approval to order Li back at once to his substantive post as governor-general of Zhili. Another decree then sent him, in that capacity, urgently to Guangdong to supervise Vietnamese affairs, placing the defence forces of Guangdong, Guangxi and Yunnan under his command. Zuo was simultaneously ordered to prepare troops in the lower Yangtze region for possible dispatch south. Hu happened to be in Jiangning and said to him, “There ought also to be a decree returning Governor-General Zhang to his substantive post in Liangguang. Otherwise, when Li reaches Tianjin, will there not be two governors-general of Zhili?” “The interesting point is precisely that no decree sends Zhang Zhenxuan back,” Zuo replied. “The Zongli Yamen knows Li will never go to Guangdong, and probably will not return to Tianjin either.” “Please enlighten us, Your Excellency.” “Out of respect for the late Lord Wenzheng Zeng, Li has always treated Zeng the Ninth very considerately. During the siege of Jiangning, Zeng’s weary army had made no progress. Li had already recovered Changzhou and was ordered to help, but kept his troops where they were rather than share Zeng’s glory. Think: if he goes to Guangdong now, what is to become of Zeng?” “Yes, yes.” Hu considered it. “And you say he will not go to Tianjin because his arrival would send Zhang back to Guangdong, again displacing Marshal Zeng?” “Exactly.”

『照此说来，京里只说叫李某某回任，对于张曾两位没不交代，意思也就是要李合能只领虚衔，暂时不必回任。』『不错，举一反三，你明白了。』『那末，李合肥怎么办呢？』左宗棠沉吟了好了一会「问说∶『你看呢？』『我看，他仍旧会到上海。』左宗棠点点头，『我想他也只能先驻上海。』他说∶『而且他也不能忘情上海。』胡雪岩当即说道∶『我本来想跟大人辞了行，回杭州，以后再到上海；照现在看，似乎应该直接到上海的好。』原来各省关应解陕甘，以便还本的协款，都交由江海关代转；所以各省解缴的情况如何，非要胡雪岩到上海去查了才知道。『好，你到上海首先办这件事，看情形如何赶紧写信来。看哪里还没有解到，好及早去催。』胡雪岩的估计很正确，李鸿章果然奏请暂驻上海，统筹全局，察酌南北军情，再取进止。意思是江南防军如果力量不足，无法南调，那就不一定用武，以求和为宜。恭王懂他的用意，奏请准如所请；于是李鸿章在三月底专轮到了上海，驻节天后宫行辕。

“Then the capital’s order restoring Li, with no instructions about Zhang or Zeng, means he is to hold the title for the moment without actually resuming the post.” “Correct. You can infer the rest from one point. You understand.” “What will he do, then?” Zuo pondered for a long time. “What do you think?” “I think he will still come to Shanghai.” Zuo nodded. “I too think he can only stay in Shanghai first. Besides, he cannot quite relinquish it.” Hu answered at once, “I had intended to take leave of Your Excellency, return to Hangzhou and go to Shanghai afterwards. In these circumstances, it seems better to go straight to Shanghai.” The provincial and customs contributions assigned to Shaanxi and Gansu for repayment of principal all passed through Shanghai Customs. Only by going there could Hu discover what had actually been remitted. “Good. Make that your first business in Shanghai and write at once with the position. We must know which remittances are missing so that we can press for them early.” Hu’s estimate proved correct. Li petitioned to remain temporarily in Shanghai, coordinating affairs, assessing the military situation in north and south, and deciding his next move accordingly. His meaning was that if the lower Yangtze forces could not spare troops for the south, war need not follow; peace would be preferable. Prince Gong understood and obtained approval. At the end of the third month, Li arrived in Shanghai by a specially arranged steamer and established headquarters at Tianhou Temple.

第五章｜CHAPTER FIVE

一见古应春的面，胡雪岩一吓跳，他人都瘦得落形了。『应春，你，你怎么弄成这个样子？』『唉！』古应春长长地叹口气，『小爷叔，我的运气太坏！也怪我自己大意。』『你出了什么事？快告诉我。』『我要倾家荡产了。』古应春说∶『都是听信了徐雨之的话。』这徐雨之是广东籍的富商，胡雪岩跟他也很熟。此人单名一个润字，人很能干，运气也很好，在上海一家洋行学生意，深得洋人的器重，从廿二岁开始与人合伙开钱庄，开丝号，开茶栈，无不大发利市。同治二年廿六岁，已经积赀十来万，在江南粮台报捐员外郎，加捐花翎，俨然上海洋场上有名的绅士了。因此，同治十年得了个差使。那时两江总督南洋大臣曾国藩决定挑选幼童出洋留学；事先研究，这批幼童以在广东挑选为宜，因为美国的华侨绝大部分是广东人，广东风气开通，作父兄的固不以幼年子弟在万里重洋之外而不放心；而此辈幼童在美国常有乡音亲切的长辈去看他们，亦可以稍慰思乡之苦。由于徐润是上海『广东帮』商人的领袖，所以曾国藩把这个差使交了给他。徐润策划得很周到，挑选了一百二十个资质很不错的幼童，分四批出洋，每批三十人；第一批在同治十一年七月初上船，由容闳带队，大部分是广东籍，广东籍中又以香山为最多，因为徐润就是香山人。

The sight of Gu Yingchun gave Hu Xueyan a shock. He had grown so thin that his whole appearance had changed. “Yingchun, what—what has happened to you?” Gu sighed deeply. “Little Uncle, my luck has been terrible. I was careless too.” “What happened? Tell me at once.” “I am going to lose everything. All because I listened to Xu Yuzhi.” Xu was a wealthy Guangdong merchant whom Hu also knew well. His personal name was Run. Both capable and fortunate, he had learnt business in a Shanghai foreign firm, winning the foreigners’ high regard. From the age of twenty-two, he had joined partners to establish banks, silk firms and tea warehouses, profiting handsomely in every venture. By the second year of Tongzhi, when he was twenty-six, he had accumulated a hundred thousand taels or more. Through the Jiangnan military supply office he purchased the rank of an assistant departmental director, adding a peacock feather, and became a prominent gentleman of the Shanghai foreign settlement. In Tongzhi’s tenth year, he consequently received a commission. Zeng Guofan, governor-general of Liangjiang and Nanyang minister, had decided to send young boys abroad to study. Preliminary enquiries suggested selecting them in Guangdong. Most Chinese residents in America came from that province, whose people were receptive to new ideas. Fathers and elder brothers would not be unduly alarmed at sending young relatives across ten thousand li of ocean; meanwhile, the boys could be visited in America by older countrymen speaking familiar dialects, easing their homesickness. As a leader of Shanghai’s Guangdong merchants, Xu received the task. He planned carefully, choosing a hundred and twenty promising boys to travel in four groups of thirty. The first sailed early in the seventh month of Tongzhi’s eleventh year under Yung Wing. Most were from Guangdong, especially Xiangshan, Xu’s own county.

当然，也有其他省份的人，但为数极少，只得五个，两个江苏、一个山东、一个福建、还有一个是徽州人，不过是广东招来的，这个十二岁、生在辛酉政变那一年的幼童，叫做詹天佑，他的父亲叫詹作屏，在福建船政局当机器匠，家眷寄居广州。詹天佑应募时，有人劝詹作屏让他的儿子学法律，学成回国，可以做官；但詹屏坚持他的儿子要学技艺，而且要学最新的技艺。第二批是在同治十二年五月放洋的，由徐润的亲家黄平甫领队。这回在挑选的官费生三十名以外，另有七名广东少年，由他们的家长自备资斧，请黄平甫带到美国——风气到底大开了，已经有自费留学的了。第三批是在同治十三年八月间派遣。这回与以前不同的是，除了两个学技艺、一个学机器以外，其余的都念普通学校，年长的念『中馆』；年幼的念『小馆』，但所谓年长，亦不过十三岁，台广东香山的唐绍仪、江苏常州的朱宝奎；而最年幼的，至少也要十岁。第四批放洋在光绪元年九月，增加了十个名额，一共是四十名，这回一律念普通学校，到中学毕业，再视他们性之所近，决定学什么。同时外省籍的幼童也多了，但仍不脱江苏、浙江、安徽三省。幼童放洋是曾国藩所创议，但他不及见第一批幼童放洋，同治十一年二月殁于任上；以后便由李鸿章支持这件事，徐润亦由此获得李鸿章的赏识，由北洋札委为招商局的会办，与盛宣怀同事。

There were only five boys from elsewhere: two from Jiangsu, one from Shandong, one from Fujian, and one whose family came from Huizhou, though he had been recruited in Guangdong. The last was twelve, born in the year of the Xinyou coup, and named Zhan Tianyou. His father, Zhan Zuoping, worked as a machinist at the Fujian shipbuilding establishment, while the family lived in Guangzhou. When Tianyou applied, someone advised his father to let him study law so that he could return and become an official. Zhan insisted that his son learn technical skills, and the newest ones at that. The second group sailed in the fifth month of Tongzhi’s twelfth year, led by Huang Pingfu, whose family was linked to Xu’s by marriage. Besides the thirty government-supported boys, seven more from Guangdong went at their parents’ expense. Their families asked Huang to take them to America: attitudes had indeed changed, and privately funded overseas study had begun. The third group left in the eighth month of Tongzhi’s thirteenth year. This time, apart from two boys studying technical crafts and one studying machinery, all attended ordinary schools: the older boys at middle schools, the younger at elementary schools. Even the older ones were only thirteen, including Tang Shaoyi of Xiangshan and Zhu Baokui of Changzhou, Jiangsu. The youngest were at least ten. The fourth group departed in the ninth month of Guangxu’s first year. Ten additional places brought the total to forty. All would attend ordinary schools and choose a specialisation after completing middle school, according to their aptitudes. More boys came from other provinces, though these were still confined to Jiangsu, Zhejiang and Anhui. Zeng had initiated the scheme but did not live to see the first departure, dying in office in the second month of Tongzhi’s eleventh year. Li Hongzhang subsequently supported it. Xu thereby gained Li’s appreciation and received a Beiyang appointment as associate director of China Merchants, working alongside Sheng Xuanhuai.

在这七八年中，徐润的事业蒸蒸日上，当然还远不及胡雪岩，但亦算是上海『夷场』上的殷商。胡雪岩跟他除了作善举以外，别无生意上的往来，而古应春因为原籍广东，又以跟洋商打交道时，常会聚在一起，所以跟徐润走得很近，也有好些合伙的事业，其中之一是做房地产生意。徐润的房地产很多，地皮有两千九百多亩，建成的洋房有五十一所，市房更多，不下两千间，照帐面上算，值到两百二十几万，但积压的资本太重，空地毫无收入，还要付税；市房则只是收租金，为数有限。于是，他有一个英国朋友，名叫顾林，此人在英国是个爵士，本人热心运动，交游很广，亦很懂生意经，他向徐润建议，彼此合作。顾林亦是古应春的朋友，因此，徐润邀他跟顾林一起谈合作，『我们组织一个大公司，投入资金，在空地上都盖起房子来。』顾林说道∶『造一批，卖一批；卖来的款子造第二批。空地用完了，把旧房子再来翻造，不断更新，外国的大都市，尤其是美国，都是这样建造起来的。』这个周而复始盖房子的决窍，徐润也懂，『可是，「他问∶』这要大批现金，你能不能投资？『『当然，我没有这个意思，不会跟你谈合作。不过，我也是要回国去招股。我们把合作的办法商量好了，拿章程在伦敦市场上传了出去，相信不到三个月，就能把股本募足。』『股本算多少呢？』『这要看你的意思。你拿你的房地产作价——当然是实价；看值多少，我就募多少股本。』『徐润点点头问古应春∶』你看呢？『『他这个法子可行，也很公平。不过，我认为我们这方面股份要多占些。』徐润想了一下，提出很明确的办法，这中英合资的公司股本定为四百万两，华方占五成半，英方占四成半；华方以房地产核实作价，英方四成半计一百八十万两，由英国汇来现金。

During those seven or eight years, Xu’s enterprises had flourished. He remained far below Hu in wealth, but was a substantial Shanghai merchant. Apart from charitable undertakings, Hu had no business dealings with him. Gu, however, came originally from Guangdong, often encountered him when dealing with foreign firms, and had become close to him. They shared several ventures, including property speculation. Xu held more than two thousand nine hundred mu of land, fifty-one foreign-style houses and at least two thousand commercial premises. Their book value exceeded two million two hundred thousand taels, but too much capital was tied up. Vacant land produced nothing and still incurred taxes, while rents from the premises were limited. A British friend named Gulin, a knight keen on sport, widely connected and knowledgeable about business, proposed a partnership. Gulin also knew Gu, so Xu invited him to their discussions. “Let us form a large company and invest capital to build on all the empty land,” Gulin said. “Build one group of houses and sell it, then use the proceeds for the next. Once the vacant land is used, rebuild the old houses and keep renewing them. That is how great foreign cities, especially American ones, have grown.” Xu understood the cycle of building and selling. “But it needs a great deal of ready cash. Can you invest?” “Of course. Otherwise I would not propose a partnership. But I too must return home to raise subscriptions. Once we agree on the terms and circulate the prospectus in London, I believe we can raise the capital in less than three months.” “How much capital?” “That depends on you. Value your property—at its real value, of course—and I shall raise an equal sum.” Xu nodded and asked Gu’s view. “It is workable and fair. But I think our side should hold a larger share.” After consideration, Xu proposed definite terms: capital of four million taels, fifty-five per cent Chinese and forty-five per cent British. The Chinese side would contribute property at an agreed real valuation, and the British side remit one million eight hundred thousand taels in cash.

于是，请律师撰文签订了草约，徐润还送了一万两银子给顾林，让他回国去招股。但是徐润的房地产，照实价只值一百五十万两；还要再买价值七十万两的地皮，才能凑足二百二十万两，合足五成半之数。『应春兄，好朋友利益均沾，这七十万两，你来入股如何？』古应春筹划了一下，愿意出五十万两银子。这是去年年底的话；到这年二月里，地皮买足数了，可是顾林却出了事。原来顾林回到伦敦不久，在一次皇室邀请的狩猎会中，马失前蹄、人从马上倒栽出去，头先着地，脑子受了重伤，请了两位名医诊治，性命虽已保住，但得了个癫痫症，合作设大分司的事，就此无疾而终。这一来徐润跟古应春大受打击，因为中法在越南的纠纷，法国政府不惜推翻已经达成和解的协议，准备动武，且已派水师提督孤拔，率舰东来，同时国会通过，拨款五百万法郎，作为战费，因此上海谣言纷纷，流传最盛的一个说法是，法国军舰不断巡弋在吴淞口外，决定要攻制造局。胆小的人已经开始逃难；在这种风声鹤唳的情况之下房地产根本无人问津。

A lawyer drew up a preliminary agreement, which they signed. Xu also gave Gulin ten thousand taels to go home and raise subscriptions. But his property’s true value was only one and a half million. Another seven hundred thousand taels’ worth of land had to be bought to make up the Chinese share of two million two hundred thousand. “Brother Yingchun, good friends should share the profits. Will you subscribe that seven hundred thousand?” After examining his resources, Gu agreed to contribute five hundred thousand. This was at the end of the previous year. By the second month of the present one, enough land had been purchased—but disaster overtook Gulin. Soon after returning to London, he attended a royal hunting party. His horse stumbled and threw him headfirst, inflicting a grave brain injury. Two eminent physicians saved his life, but he developed epilepsy. The great company project quietly died. Xu and Gu were hit hard. In the Vietnamese dispute, France had repudiated the settlement and prepared for war, sending Admiral Courbet east with a fleet. Its legislature also voted five million francs for military expenditure. Rumours swept Shanghai. The most widespread said that French warships were continually cruising outside Wusong and had decided to attack the arsenal. Timid people were already fleeing. In this atmosphere of panic, no one would even enquire about property.

『我那五十万银子，其中卅五万是借来的；现在银根紧到极点，上海三十几家钱庄，家家心惊肉跳，只怕再来一个风潮，大家提存挤兑，一倒就是多少家。我借的款子，催得很急；实在是急！每天都有钱庄里的伙计上门坐讨，只好不断同人家说好话。』古应春又说∶『还有一层，我怕阿七晓得了着急，还要时时刻刻留心瞒住她。小爷叔，你想，我过的是啥日子？』胡雪岩听了他这番话，再看到他憔悴的形容，恻然心伤，『应春，你放心！』他拍一拍胸脯说∶『我来替你了；都在我身上。』古应春迟疑未答。胡雪岩倒奇怪了，照情理说，现有人替他一肩担承，他应该高兴才是，何以有此显得困惑的神情？『应春，』他问∶『还有啥难处？我们这样的交情，你还有啥在我面前说不出口的话？』『小爷叔，』古应春顿了一下问道∶『莫非上海的市面，你真的一点都不晓得？』『怎么？市面有好有坏，这也是常有的事。』古应春楞住了，好一会方始开口∶『看起来你老人家真的不晓得。我现在说实话吧，来催讨欠款，来催得最厉害的，就是老宓。』此言一出，胡雪岩脸上火辣辣地发烧，真象上海人所说的『吃耳光』一样，一时心里七上八下，竟开不得口了。原来古应春口中的『老宓』，就是他阜康钱庄的档手宓本常。『自己人催欠款催得这么厉害！岂有此理！』胡雪岩非常生气；但转念一想，连自己人的欠款都催得这么厉害，可见得阜康的境况也很窘。这一转念间，惊出一身汗，定一定神说道∶『应春，你晓得的，这几年，阜康的事，我都交老宓，难得问一问；照现在看，阜康的银根好象比哪一家都紧，你倒同我说一说，到底是怎么个情形？』

“Of my five hundred thousand taels, three hundred and fifty thousand were borrowed. Money is now desperately tight. All thirty-odd Shanghai banks are trembling, afraid of another panic. If everyone withdraws their deposits, any number may collapse together. My lenders are pressing hard—very hard. Their clerks come and sit at my door every day, demanding payment. I can only keep pleading with them.” Gu added, “And I am afraid A’qi will hear and worry, so I must constantly hide it from her. Little Uncle, imagine the life I am leading.” Hearing this and seeing his haggard face, Hu felt a sharp pang of pity. “Do not worry, Yingchun!” He struck his chest. “I shall settle it for you. Leave everything to me.” Gu hesitated without answering. Hu was surprised. Surely he should be delighted to have someone shoulder the whole burden? Why look so troubled? “What other difficulty is there? With our friendship, what can you not tell me?” “Little Uncle,” Gu paused, “do you really know nothing of the Shanghai market?” “What do you mean? Markets rise and fall. That is ordinary enough.” Gu stared, then at last spoke. “It seems you truly do not know. Let me tell you plainly: the man pressing hardest for repayment is Old Mi.” Hu’s face burned as though he had been slapped. His thoughts tumbled, and he could not speak. Old Mi was Mi Benchang, the managing clerk of his own Fukang Bank. “Pressing our own people so hard! Outrageous!” Hu was angry. Then another thought came: if even a friend was being pursued so urgently, Fukang itself must be in serious difficulty. He broke into a sweat, steadied himself and said, “You know that for years I have left Fukang to Mi and seldom asked about it. From what you say, its funds seem tighter than anyone’s. Tell me exactly what is happening.”

『小爷叔，你从江宁来，莫非没有听左大人跟你谈上海的市面？』『怎么？上海的市面，莫非┅┅』『从来没有这么坏过。小爷叔，你晓得现在上海的现银有多少？』『有多少？』『这个。』古应春伸一指相示。『一千万？』『一百万。』胡雪岩大吃一惊，『真的？』他问。『你差别老宓就晓得了。』胡雪岩仍旧有点不大相信，『市面这么坏，应该有人告诉左大人啊！』他说，『我在江宁，跟左大人谈起上海他说因为法国称兵，上海市面多少受点影响，不过不要紧。』『哼！』古应春冷笑一声∶『现在做官的，哪个不是瞒上欺下，只会做喜鹊，不肯当乌鸦。』『走！「胡雪岩说∶』我们一起到集贤里去。『阜康钱庄设在英租界集贤里，与胡雪岩的公馆只隔一条马路，他经常是安步当车走了去的。正要出门时，女管家陈嫂赶出来问道∶』老爷，啥辰光回来？『

“You have come from Jiangning, Little Uncle. Did His Excellency Zuo not discuss the Shanghai market with you?” “What? Is the market—” “It has never been worse. Do you know how much ready silver there is in Shanghai now?” “How much?” Gu raised one finger. “Ten million?” “One million.” Hu was shocked. “Really?” “Ask Mi and you will see.” Hu still found it hard to believe. “If the market is so bad, someone should tell His Excellency Zuo. When we discussed Shanghai in Jiangning, he said that French military threats had affected trade somewhat, but it was nothing serious.” Gu gave a cold snort. “Which official nowadays does not deceive those above and bully those below? They all want to be magpies bringing good news; none will be a crow.” “Come. Let us go to Jixian Lane together.” Fukang stood in Jixian Lane in the British settlement, just across a road from Hu’s residence. He usually walked there at leisure. As they were leaving, the housekeeper, Mrs Chen, hurried out. “When will you be back, sir?”

『现在还不晓得。』『刚刚宓先生派徒弟来通知，他说晓得老爷已经来了，吃夜饭辰光他会来。』陈嫂又说∶『今夜难得买到一条很新鲜的鲥鱼，老爷回来吃夜饭吧！』一听宓本常要来，胡雪岩倒有些踌躇了；古应春便即说道∶『即然如此，不如等老宓来，有些话也是在家里谈，比较方便。』胡雪岩听这一说，便从纱背心口袋中掏出打簧表来看，已经四点半了，便点点头说∶『那就叫人去说一声∶请宓先生早一点来。』于是重回客厅去密谈。胡雪岩此时最关心的是要还汇丰银行第一期的本银五十万两。陕甘总督衙门出的『印票』，不过是摆个样子，还款来源是各省交上海道衙门代收的协饷；数目如果不够，他可以代垫，但银根如此之紧，代垫恐有不能，须要及早筹划。『应春，』他问，『汇丰的款子，月底要交，你晓不晓得，邵小村那里已经收到多少了？』『前十来天我听说，已经收到半数了。这几天，总还有款子进来。差也不过差个百把万，不过，现在全上海的现银只有一百万，』古应春吸着气说∶『这件事恐怕也是个麻烦。』胡雪岩的心一沉，『我的信用，伤不得一点点。应春，』他说∶『只有半个月的工夫了。你有没有啥好主意？』『一时倒还没有。』古应春答说∶『且等老宓来了再说。』宓本常一直到天黑才来。据他说，一接到通知，本来马上就要赶来，只为有几个大客户提存，调度费时，所以耽误了工夫。

“I do not know yet.” “Mr Mi has just sent an apprentice to say he knows you have arrived and will come at dinnertime.” She added, “We have found a very fresh shad today, which is rare. Do come back for dinner.” Hearing that Mi was coming, Hu hesitated. Gu said, “Then perhaps we should wait for him. Some things are more easily discussed at home.” Hu took his striking watch from his gauze waistcoat pocket. It was already half past four. “Then send word asking him to come early.” They returned to the drawing room for a private discussion. Hu’s chief concern was the first five-hundred-thousand-tael principal payment to HSBC. The sealed notes issued by the governor-general’s office in Shaanxi and Gansu were little more than a formality. Repayment came from provincial contributions collected through the Shanghai circuit intendant. Hu could cover a shortfall, but with money so tight he might be unable to do so. He must plan early. “Yingchun, the HSBC payment is due at the end of the month. Do you know how much Shao Xiaocun has received?” “Ten days or so ago I heard that half had arrived. More should have come in since. The shortage should be no more than a million or so. But there is only a million in ready silver in all Shanghai.” Gu drew a breath. “This may be troublesome too.” Hu’s heart sank. “My credit cannot suffer the slightest blemish. There is only half a month left. Have you any good idea?” “Not at the moment. Let us wait for Mi.” Mi did not arrive until dark. He said he had meant to come immediately upon receiving the message, but several large customers were withdrawing deposits. Arranging the funds had delayed him.

胡雪岩知道，所谓调度，无非先开出银票，问客户到何处提款；然后通兑付的联号。譬如客户要提五万银子的存款，说要到江宁去提；便用最快的方法通知江宁的阜康。如果江宁『头寸』不足，再查何处有多余的『头寸』——上海阜康是总号，各联号存款进出的情形，都有帐可查；查清楚了，透过同行的汇划，以有余补不足。不过这是近来的情形，早些日子说要提现银，还要照付；胡雪岩便查问那些现银都到哪里去了？『都分散到内地去了。』宓本常说∶『不靠水路码头的联号，存款都增加了。不过照我计算，转到别处的只占十之六七；还有十之三四，是摆在家里了。这些现银，要到市面平空了，才会派到市面上。』『喔，』胡雪岩沉吟了好一会儿说道∶『这十之三四的现银，也要想个法子，早点让它回到市面上。你开个单了给我，看哪几处地方，存款增加了。』『我说过了，只要不是水路上的大码头，存款都比以前多。』

Hu knew what those arrangements involved. First a banknote was issued and the customer asked where he wished to collect the silver; then the paying branch was notified. Suppose he wanted fifty thousand taels in Jiangning. The fastest means would be used to alert Fukang there. If that branch lacked funds, they would look for another with a surplus. Shanghai Fukang was the head office and kept records of deposits and withdrawals at every branch. Once the position was clear, remittances through other banks could use one branch’s surplus to cover another’s shortage. But that was the recent practice. Earlier, customers asking for ready silver had been paid it. Hu asked where all that silver had gone. “It has dispersed inland,” Mi said. “Deposits are up at branches away from major navigable-waterway ports. By my reckoning, sixty or seventy per cent of the money has moved elsewhere; the other thirty or forty per cent is being kept at home. That will return to circulation only when the market calms.” Hu thought for a long time. “We must find a way to bring that thirty or forty per cent back sooner. Give me a list of the places where deposits have increased.” “As I said, everywhere except the major ports on navigable waterways.”

『那是怕中法一开仗，法国兵轮会到水路大码头。』胡雪岩问∶『京里怎么样？』『加了很多，而且都是大数目。』宓本常说∶『文中堂的三十万都提走了。不过，北京存了四十六万。』文中堂便是前年升了协办大学士的刑部尚书文煜；提三十万存四十六万，表示他对阜康的信心十足，胡雪岩自然深感安慰。『难怪大家都想做官。』胡雪晨说∶『他调到京里，也不过三、四年的工夫，倒又积了十六万银子了。』『不！』宓本常说∶『其中十万两是他的本家的。』『不管他了，总是他的来头。』胡雪岩又问∶『上海几十家钱庄，现银只有一百万，大家是怎么应付的呢？』『全靠同心协力，在汇划上耍把戏。』『喔，』胡雪岩从受知于左宗棠开始，一面要办西征粮台；一面又创办了好些事业，而且做生意的兴趣，集中在丝上，对于钱庄的经营，差不多完全交给宓本常主持，钱庄的制度，有所改变，亦很隔膜，『汇划』上能够，『耍把戏』，却不甚明白。在过去，他可以不求甚解，现在出现了危机，他就非问问清楚不可了。

“People fear that French warships will reach those ports if war begins. What about Beijing?” “Deposits have risen greatly, and in large amounts. Grand Secretary Wen withdrew all his three hundred thousand here, but deposited four hundred and sixty thousand in Beijing.” Wen was Wenyu, Minister of Punishments, promoted to assistant grand secretary two years earlier. Withdrawing three hundred thousand but depositing four hundred and sixty thousand showed complete confidence in Fukang, and Hu was deeply reassured. “No wonder everyone wants to be an official. He has been in Beijing only three or four years and has accumulated another hundred and sixty thousand.” “No,” Mi corrected him. “A hundred thousand of it belongs to a member of his clan.” “Never mind. It all comes through him.” Hu asked, “With dozens of banks and only a million taels of ready silver, how is everyone managing?” “Entirely by working together and juggling the clearing accounts.” “Oh?” Since gaining Zuo’s patronage, Hu had managed the western expedition’s supply office and founded many enterprises. His commercial interest had centred on silk, while banking was left almost wholly to Mi. He had little knowledge of changes in its practices and did not quite understand what “juggling” the clearing accounts meant. Once he might have been content with a vague idea. Now that a crisis had arisen, he must understand it fully.

『说穿了，一句话∶等于常在一起打牌的朋友一样，赌得再大，不过赌筹码，今天我输他赢，明天你赢他输，听起来很热闹，无非数数筹吗，记一笔帐，到时候结一结就轧平了。不过，这只好常常在一起的朋友这么办，夹一个外头人进来，赢了一票，要拿现款走；这个把戏就耍不下去了。所以┅┅『所以上海的钱庄，由阜康领头，联络了十来家』大同行『，成立了一个』汇划总会『，仿照日本在明治十二年所设立的』手形交换所『的办法，用交换票据来代替现银收解。票据交换，不能私下办理，一定要送总会；凡是汇划钱庄，到期的银票，一律先送总会，分门别类理齐，派老司务送到各钱庄『照票』。如果不误，这家钱庄便将银票收了下来，另外打出一张收据，名为『公单』，规定以五百两为基数，不足五百两，或用现金找补，或者记帐另外再算。这些『公单』大概在下午三、四点钟，都已集中到总会，算盘一打，立刻可以算出哪家该收多少、该付多少；譬如，阜康应收各庄银票共计一百万，本号开出的银票只有八十四万，有十六万头寸多。

“Put simply, it is like friends who play cards together regularly. However large the stakes, they use counters. I lose and he wins today; you win and he loses tomorrow. It sounds lively, but all we do is count the counters and make entries, settling the balance at the proper time. It works only among regular friends. Let an outsider join, win a large sum and demand cash, and the trick cannot continue. So—” Thus Fukang had taken the lead in joining with a dozen or so of Shanghai’s major banks to establish a clearing association. Following the model of the bill-exchange institution founded in Japan in the twelfth year of Meiji, they exchanged notes instead of transferring silver. The exchange could not be conducted privately; everything had to go through the association. Participating banks first sent all maturing notes there. These were sorted, and senior clerks took them to the issuing banks for verification. If correct, a bank accepted its notes and issued a receipt called a “public clearing certificate”. These used five hundred taels as the basic unit. Smaller amounts were either made up in cash or entered separately for later settlement. By three or four in the afternoon, the certificates had generally all reached the association. A few calculations on the abacus showed what each bank should receive or pay. Suppose Fukang held a million taels in notes due from other banks, while only eight hundred and forty thousand of its own notes were presented: it had a surplus balance of a hundred and sixty thousand.

有多就有少，由总会开出『划条』交阜康向欠头寸的钱庄先收现银。时间规定是在第二天下午两点钟以前。那么，缺头寸的钱庄怎么办呢？不要紧，第二天上午可以到公会向有头寸多的同行去拆进，利息以日计，称为『银拆』这种一两天的同业借款，不必打收据，由公会记一笔帐就可以了。至于利息的计算，又分两种，不打收据的拆借，称为『活拆』，利息高低视银根松紧而定。另外一种同业长期的拆借，称为『呆拆』，要立票据，议定利息；在此期间，不受每天挂牌的『银拆』的影响。『这种打「公单」的法子，就好象赌钱发筹码，所不同的是，第一，赌场的筹码，只有头家可以管；公单只要是汇划钱庄，家家可开。第二，赌场的筹码，不能拿到外面去用，公单可以化成本号的银票，到处可用。说实了，无非无中生有，凭空生出几千万银子来；所以现银不过一百万，市面上的大生意照样在做。这就是要汇划的把戏。』接下来便谈到丝茧的情形。丝茧业下乡收值，多仰赖钱庄放款，胡雪岩也就因为有钱庄在手里，所以成为丝业领袖，这两年因为抵制新式缫丝厂，收的茧子与丝更多。宓本常虽非胡雪岩经营丝业方面的档手，但可以从各联号存放款进出的总帐中，看出存货有多少。

Where one bank had a surplus, another had a deficit. The association issued a transfer order authorising Fukang to collect silver from a bank short of funds, with payment due before two the following afternoon. What could the bank in deficit do? No matter: the next morning it could borrow through the association from another bank with a surplus. Interest was calculated by the day, the transaction being called a “silver loan”. Such interbank advances for a day or two required no receipt, only an entry in the association’s books. There were two methods of charging interest. Loans without receipts were “floating advances”, their rates rising and falling with the money market. Longer interbank loans were “fixed advances”, supported by written instruments at an agreed rate, unaffected by the daily quotation. “Issuing public clearing certificates is like handing out gambling counters,” Mi explained. “There are two differences. First, only the house controls a casino’s counters, while every participating bank can issue these certificates. Second, casino counters cannot be spent outside. A clearing certificate can be converted into the bank’s own notes and used anywhere. In plain terms, it creates something from nothing—tens of millions of taels out of thin air. That is why great transactions continue even though there is only a million in actual silver. That is the clearing trick.” They then turned to silk and cocoons. Buyers going into the countryside depended largely on bank loans. Hu’s control of a bank had helped make him a leader of the silk trade. During the past two years, his campaign against modern reeling mills had led him to buy still more cocoons and silk. Mi did not manage the silk business, but could judge the size of its stocks from the consolidated records of deposits and loans at the branches.

『大先生，』宓本常神情严肃地说∶『现在存丝总有六七千包，茧子更多，我看用不着这么多存货。』『你是说吃本太重？』『是啊。』宓本常说∶『粗估一估差不多有三百万银子的本钱压在那里。不是因为这样子，古先生的十万银子，我也不好意思来讨。』『呃！』胡雪岩立即接口∶『这十万银子转到我名下。』他紧接着又转脸对古应春说∶『另外的，再想办法。好在你有地皮在那里，不过现金一时周转不开而已。』古应春满怀忧虑一扫而空；但自己虽不愁了，又为胡雪岩发愁，『小爷叔，』他说∶『现在三家缫丝厂都缺货，你何妨放几千包茧子出去；新式机器，做丝快得很，一做出来，不愁外洋没有买主，那一来不就活络了？』『古先生这话一点不错。』宓本常也说，『今年「洋庄」不大动，是外国人都在等，等机器的丝，凭良心说，机器做的丝，比脚踏手摇土法子做的丝，不知道要高明多少。』『我也晓得。』胡雪岩用低沉的声音说∶『不过，做人总要讲宗旨，更要讲信用，说一句算一句，我答应过的，不准新式缫丝厂来抢乡下养蚕做丝人家的饭碗，我就不能卖茧子给他们。现在我手里再紧一紧，这三家机器缫丝厂一倒，外国人没有想头了，自然会买我的丝，那时候价钱就由我开了。『古应春与宓本常，都认为他打的如意算盘。不过，古应春是好朋友的身分，而宓本常是伙计，所以只有古应春还可以劝他。

“Sir,” Mi said gravely, “there must now be six or seven thousand bales of silk in stock, and still more cocoons. I do not think we need so much.” “You mean too much capital is tied up?” “Yes. At a rough estimate, nearly three million taels are immobilised there. Otherwise I would be ashamed to press Mr Gu for his hundred thousand.” “Ah!” Hu answered at once. “Transfer those hundred thousand to my account.” He turned to Gu. “We shall find a way with the rest. You have the land, after all. It is only a temporary shortage of cash.” Gu’s own anxiety vanished, but concern for Hu took its place. “Little Uncle, all three reeling mills are short of material. Why not release a few thousand bales of cocoons? The new machines work very quickly. Once the silk is made, there will be no shortage of foreign buyers. Would that not get things moving?” “Mr Gu is quite right,” Mi said. “The export buyers have been inactive this year because the foreigners are waiting for machine-reeled silk. In fairness, it is far superior to what our old foot-driven and hand-turned methods produce.” “I know,” Hu said quietly. “But a man must have principles, and above all he must keep his word. I promised not to let modern mills steal the livelihood of country families who raise silkworms and reel silk. I cannot sell them cocoons. If I tighten my grip a little longer and those three mills collapse, the foreigners will have nothing to wait for. They will buy my silk, and then I shall name the price.” Both Gu and Mi thought this wishful calculation. But Gu was a close friend, Mi an employee; only Gu could continue trying to persuade him.

『小爷叔，如果那三家新式缫丝厂倒闭了洋商当然只好仍旧买我们土法子做的丝；可是那三家厂不倒呢？』『不倒而没有货色，跟倒了有啥两样？』『还有一层，小爷叔要想到，茧子虽然烘干了，到底也还是摆不长的。一发黄就卖不起价钱了。』『这话是不错。不过，你说上海现银不到一百万，我就放茧子出去，也换不出现银。』『有英镑、有花旗票就可以了。』宓本常接口来个快，『譬如说，现在要还汇丰五十万，如果大先生有卖茧子的外国钱在汇丰，就可以折算给他，收进五十万现银，周转不就活络了？』胡雪岩沉吟了一会说∶『为了维持我的信用，只好抛茧子，这话我说得响的。明天我去看邵小村，看看这五十万两银子，到底收得齐收不齐？如果银数不够，决定照你们的办法，卖茧子来拿它补足。不然，我另有主意。』『小爷叔，你是啥主意？』胡雪岩笑笑，『天机不可泄漏。』他说，『是蛮狠的一着。』

“Little Uncle, if the three mills close, foreigners will naturally have to buy our traditionally reeled silk. But what if they do not close?” “If they have no material, what difference does staying open make?” “There is something else to remember. Dried cocoons still cannot be stored indefinitely. Once they yellow, they lose their value.” “True. But you say Shanghai has less than a million taels of silver. Even if I release the cocoons, I cannot turn them into cash.” “Sterling or American notes would do,” Mi put in quickly. “For example, you owe HSBC five hundred thousand. If the foreign currency from selling cocoons were deposited there, it could be converted and credited against that sum. Bringing in five hundred thousand in silver would free up our funds, would it not?” Hu considered. “If I must sell cocoons to preserve my credit, that is a reason I can stand behind. Tomorrow I shall see Shao Xiaocun and find out whether the full five hundred thousand can be collected. If it falls short, I shall follow your proposal and sell cocoons to make up the difference. Otherwise, I have another idea.” “What idea?” Hu smiled. “Heaven’s secrets cannot be revealed. It is a rather ruthless move.”

吃完了饭，宓本常告辞，古应春却留了下来，因为胡雪岩刚到上海，尚未露面，到第二天消息一传，应酬就会忙不过来，那时候就没有工夫细谈了。当然胡雪岩也要跟他谈谈近况，第一个关切的是七姑奶奶，『怎么样？』他问∶『七姐好点了？』『好得多了。』古应春的神气不同了，显得很有生气的模样，『本来右半身完全瘫了，现在有点知觉了。』『那好！说不定还会复原呢！』这一说，使得古应春很不安，只好老实说了，『小爷叔，我心里有个疙瘩，从瑞香一进门，没有几天就有消息。顾林在英国女皇的行宫外面，从马上摔下来，把脑子摔坏了。』他迟疑着说∶『我怕她跟我八字上不大相配。』『！』胡雪岩大不以为然，『你蛮洋派的人，怎么也相信这个。要不然，你拿你们两个人的八字，叫吴铁口去合一合看。』提到吴铁口，不免令人失笑；当初罗四姐去合八字，原是七姑奶奶跟他串好一的出双簧。胡雪岩也知道其中的奥妙，竟真的相信吴铁口是真的铁口，岂非自欺欺人？『你笑点啥？』胡雪岩说∶『你当我荒唐？实在说一句∶假的说成真的，「真的是真的，那就是不折不扣的铁口。』听他说得象绕口令似的，古应春不由得笑了，『好，好！我听小爷叔的话，叫吴铁口去合她的八字，不过，』他说∶『她的八字我不晓得。』『我来问她。』『慢慢，总要等阿七有了表示以后。』『当然。』胡雪岩说∶『我明天看了七姐，包你当天就有好消息。』

After dinner, Mi took his leave, but Gu stayed. Hu had only just arrived in Shanghai and had not yet appeared publicly. Once the news spread tomorrow, social obligations would leave no time for a long conversation. Hu too wanted to hear his recent news. His first concern was the Seventh Lady. “How is Seventh Sister? Any better?” “Much better.” Gu’s expression brightened. “Her whole right side was paralysed. Now some feeling has returned.” “Good! Perhaps she may recover completely.” This made Gu uneasy, and he had to speak frankly. “Little Uncle, something troubles me. Only a few days after Ruixiang entered our household, the news came that Gulin had fallen from his horse outside the British queen’s country palace and injured his brain.” He hesitated. “I fear her birth signs may not agree with mine.” Hu strongly disagreed. “You are so Western in your ways. How can you believe that? If necessary, take both sets of birth particulars to Wu Ironmouth and let him compare them.” Mention of Wu was enough to provoke a smile. When Madam Luosi had once consulted him about birth signs, it had been a performance secretly arranged with the Seventh Lady. Hu knew the trick. To believe Wu’s pronouncements genuinely infallible now would be deceiving himself and others. “What are you smiling at?” Hu asked. “Do you think me ridiculous? Let me say this: when a falsehood is made true, and the truth really is true, that is an infallible Ironmouth.” Gu laughed at the tongue-twister. “Very well. I shall do as you say and ask him to compare her birth signs. But I do not know her birth particulars.” “I shall ask her.” “Wait. We must first have some indication from A’qi.” “Of course. Once I see Seventh Sister tomorrow, I guarantee good news that very day.”

『怎么？』古应春问∶『小爷叔是打算当面她明说。』『当面是当面，不是明说。你到明天就晓得了。』『复原是办不到，只望她能够起床就好了。』古应春又说∶『谈到这一点，实在要谢谢瑞香。』『对了！』胡雪岩谈到他第二件关心的事，『七姐对瑞香怎么样？』『那没有话说，当她自己妹子一样。当然这也一半是看罗四姐的面子。』『照这样说，应该是照她的锦囊妙计，一步一步走拢来；七姐对你有没有表示？』『有。不过我没有答腔。』『咦！』胡雪岩大为诧异∶『为啥？』『小爷叔，你看我现在弄得这样焦头烂额，哪里还有讨小的意恳。』『这倒也是实话。』胡雪岩问∶『阜康的十成旭不必再提了，你还差多少头寸？』应春想了一下答说∶『还差十二、三万。』『差点是现款，能够变现就好。』胡雪岩说∶『我再借五百包丝给你，你洋行里的朋友多，总可以卖得掉。』古应春打的正是这个主意，踌躇好久，难于启齿，不想胡雪岩自己说了出来；心里的那份感激与痛快，难以形容了。『小爷叔，你真是杭州人说的，是我的「救命王菩萨」。』他说∶『我把道契都抵给你。』

“What? Do you mean to speak to her directly?” “Face to face, yes; directly, no. You will see tomorrow.” “Complete recovery is impossible. I only hope she can get out of bed,” Gu said. “For that, we really must thank Ruixiang.” “Exactly!” Hu came to his second concern. “How does Seventh Sister treat her?” “There is nothing to complain of. Like her own younger sister. Of course, half of that is out of regard for Madam Luosi.” “Then we should follow the ingenious plan she gave us, step by step. Has Seventh Sister said anything to you?” “Yes, but I did not answer.” “Why?” Hu was astonished. “With my affairs in such a mess, Little Uncle, how could I think of taking a concubine?” “True enough. Leaving aside the hundred thousand at Fukang, how much more do you need?” Gu thought. “Another hundred and twenty or thirty thousand.” “What you lack is cash. Anything you can turn into cash will serve. I shall lend you five hundred bales of silk. With all your friends in foreign firms, you should be able to sell them.” This was precisely what Gu had hoped for but had long hesitated to ask. Now Hu had offered it himself. Gu’s gratitude and relief were beyond description. “Little Uncle, as we say in Hangzhou, you are my life-saving King Bodhisattva. I shall pledge all the title deeds to you.”

『不必，不必，我们弟兄何在乎此？不过应春，你开价不能太低，不然，有个盘口在那里，以后我就抬不高了。』『是的。』古应春凝神想了一下说∶『这样，小爷叔，你索性再借两百包给我∶七面百丝抵押十四万银子，一定可以，那就什么都摆平了。』『好！光押不卖，就不算把行情压低。准定如此。』胡雪岩紧接着说∶『你现在有心思想瑞香了吧？』这一点，古应春还是不能爽爽快快地答复；沉吟未答之际，胡雪岩少不得要追问了。『这件事老太太都蛮关心的。罗四姐更不用说，应春，你要晓得，不光是你，她对瑞香也要有个交代。』第二天一大早，胡雪岩就到了古家。七姑奶奶已知道胡雪岩要来，叫瑞香替她栉发梳妆；又关照预备菜留胡雪岩吃饭，大为兴奋。

“No need. Between brothers, why trouble about that? But you must not ask too low a price. Once a market quotation is established, I cannot raise mine later.” “Yes.” Gu thought carefully. “Then lend me another two hundred bales. I can certainly raise a hundred and forty thousand taels by pledging seven hundred bales. That will settle everything.” “Good. Pledging without selling will not depress the market price. We shall do that.” Hu added immediately, “Now can you think about Ruixiang?” Even so, Gu could not answer freely. As he hesitated, Hu pressed him. “My mother is concerned about this, and Madam Luosi even more so. Remember, Yingchun, it is not only you. She owes Ruixiang an answer too.” Early the next morning, Hu went to the Gu household. The Seventh Lady knew he was coming and had Ruixiang comb her hair and help her dress. She also ordered food prepared so that he could stay for a meal, and was much excited.

胡雪岩一来，当然请到病榻前面，『七姐，』他很高兴地说，『看起来精神是好得多了。』『是啊，都要谢谢四姐。』『为啥？』『不是四姐派了瑞香来帮我的忙，我不会好起来，小爷叔你看！』七姑奶奶将右手提高了数寸，『现在手能够动了，都是瑞香，一天给按摩多少遍。』『喔！』胡雪岩看一看瑞香，想要说话，却又住口，仿佛有难言之隐似的。七姑奶奶虽在病中，仍旧神智清明，察言辨色的本事一点也不差；殷殷地从胡老太太起，将胡雪岩全家大小都问到了。直到瑞香离去，她才问道∶『小爷叔，刚才提到瑞香，你好象有话没有说出来。』

Hu was naturally shown straight to her sickbed. “Seventh Sister,” he said gladly, “you do look much stronger.” “Yes. It is all thanks to Fourth Sister.” “Why?” “If she had not sent Ruixiang to help me, I would never have improved. Look, Little Uncle!” She raised her right hand a few inches. “I can move it now. Ruixiang massages it so many times every day.” “Oh!” Hu glanced at Ruixiang as though about to speak, then stopped, suggesting something difficult to say. Though ill, the Seventh Lady remained clear-minded and as skilled as ever at reading faces. She enquired warmly after everyone in Hu’s household, beginning with his mother. Only when Ruixiang had left did she ask, “Little Uncle, when we mentioned Ruixiang, you seemed to leave something unsaid.”

『是的。我有句话，实在不想说，不过又非说不可。』『那么，小爷叔我们两家是一家，你说嘛！』『话句话是罗四姐要我带来的。』胡雪岩说∶『瑞香是好人家出身，他哥哥现在生意做得还不错，想把他妹子赎回去。』『赎回去？』七姑奶奶脸色都变了，『当初不是一百两银子卖到胡家的？』『不是。罗四姐弄不清楚，我也记不起来，捡出老契来一看，才知道当初是典的一百两银子，规定八年回赎；今年正好是第八年。』『那，四姐的意思呢？』『四姐当然不肯，尤其听说在你这里还不错，更加不肯了。』『四姐待我好。』七姑奶奶用殷切盼望的眼色看着胡雪岩说∶『她晓得我离不开瑞香，应该替我想想办法。』『办法何尝不想。不过，她哥哥说出一句话来，四姐就说不下去了。』『喔，一句什么话。』『她哥哥说，要为她妹子的终身着想。意思是把瑞香赎回去，要替她好好寻个婆家。』『真的？』看七姑奶奶是不信的语气，胡雪岩也就正好说活终话，『哪晓得他是真是假？不过，』他又把话说回来∶『男大当婚，女大当嫁，就算他是假话，也驳不倒他。三个人抬不过去一个理字。七姐，你说呢？』

“Yes. There is something I do not want to say, but must.” “Then say it. Our two families are one.” “Madam Luosi asked me to bring this message. Ruixiang comes from a respectable family. Her brother’s business is doing fairly well now, and he wants to redeem her.” “Redeem her?” The Seventh Lady’s expression changed. “Was she not sold to your household for a hundred taels?” “No. Madam Luosi was uncertain, and I could not remember. We found the old contract and discovered that she had been pledged for a hundred taels, with redemption after eight years. This is the eighth.” “What does Fourth Sister think?” “Naturally she does not want to let her go, especially after hearing how well she is doing here.” “Fourth Sister is good to me.” The Seventh Lady looked at Hu with eager hope. “She knows I cannot do without Ruixiang. She ought to find a way for me.” “She has tried. But her brother said something she could not answer.” “What?” “He said he must consider his sister’s future. He means to redeem her and find her a good husband.” “Really?” The disbelief in her voice gave Hu room to manoeuvre. “Who knows whether he means it? Still,” he turned the argument back, “when men and women grow up, they should marry. Even if he does not mean it, we cannot refute the reason. Three men together cannot carry away a sound argument. What do you say, Seventh Sister?”

『依我说，』七姑奶奶微微冷笑，『小爷叔，你手下那么多人，莫非就不能派一个能干的去打听打听他哥哥的情形，是真的为瑞香着想呢，还是说好听话，拿他妹子赎回去，另打主意？』『打啥主意？』『知人知面不知心。照瑞香这份人材，在她身上好打的主意多得很。』胡雪岩不作声，这是故意作出盘马弯弓的姿态，好逼七姑奶奶往深处去谈。七姑奶奶此时心事如麻，是为瑞香在着急；盘算了好一会，方又开口说道∶『小爷叔，你同四姐决不可以让瑞香的哥哥把她赎回去。不然会造孽。』『造孽？』胡雪岩故意装出吃惊的神气，『怎么会造孽？』『如果瑞香落了火坑，不就是造孽？』『七姐，』胡雪岩急急问说∶『你是说，她哥哥会把她卖到堂子里？』『说不定。』胡雪岩想了一下说∶『不会的。第一，瑞香不肯；第二，她哥哥也不敢。如说我胡某某家的丫头，会落到堂子里；他不怕我办他一个「逼良为娼」的罪？』『到那时候就来不及了。小爷叔，你既然想到你的面子，何不早点想办法？』

“I say,” she answered with a faint, cold smile, “that with so many people working for you, surely you can send someone capable to investigate her brother. Is he really thinking of Ruixiang, or merely saying pleasant things so that he can redeem her for some other purpose?” “What purpose?” “You can know a man’s face without knowing his heart. With Ruixiang’s looks and qualities, there are plenty of plans he might make for her.” Hu said nothing, deliberately holding his horse and drawing his bow, as it were, to force her further into the subject. The Seventh Lady’s thoughts tangled with anxiety for Ruixiang. After considerable reflection she said, “You and Fourth Sister must not let him redeem her. It could be a terrible sin.” “A sin?” Hu feigned alarm. “How?” “If she fell into the pit of fire, would that not be a sin?” “Do you mean,” Hu asked urgently, “that he might sell her to a brothel?” “He might.” Hu considered. “I do not believe so. First, she would refuse. Second, he would not dare. If a maid from my household ended in a brothel, would he not fear my prosecuting him for forcing a respectable woman into prostitution?” “By then it would be too late. If your reputation concerns you, why not find a way now?”

『对！』胡雪岩很快地接口，『七姐，你倒替我想个法子看。』『法子多得很。第一，同他哥哥去商量，再补他多少银子，重新立个卖断的契。』『不，不！这点没有用。』胡雪岩说∶『如果有用，罗四姐早就办了。我不说过，人家生意做得蛮好，赎瑞香不是打钱的主意。』『好！就算他不是打钱的主意，诚心诚意是为瑞香的终身；不过，他替他妹子到底挑的是什么人家？男家好不好要看一看；瑞香愿不愿意也要问一问。如果是低三下四的人家，瑞香又不愿意，小爷叔，那就尽有理由不让他赎回去了。』『这话——』胡雪岩不便驳她太武断，急转直下地说∶『我看，只有一个办法，他为瑞香好，我们也是为瑞香好，替瑞香好好找份人家，只要瑞香自己愿意，他哥哥也就没话说了。』七姑奶奶想了一下说∶『小爷叔，我想请四姐来一趟，请她来劝一劝瑞香。』『劝啥？』胡雪岩答说∶『莫非我就不能劝她？』『我怕小爷叔说话欠婉转；瑞香是怕你，就肯答应，也是很勉强的。这种事，一勉强就没有意思了。』『什么事要瑞香答应？而且要心里情愿？七姐，你何妨同我实说；你晓得的，我们家的丫头都不怕我的，倒是对四姐，她们还有忌惮。』『即然如此，我就实说吧！小爷叔，我在瑞香来的第二天，心里就在转念头了，我一直想替应春弄个人，要他看得上眼，要我也投缘，象瑞香这样一个拿灯笼都寻不着的人，四姐替我送了来，我心里好高兴；本想等小爷叔你，或者四姐来了，当面求你们，哪知道其中还有这么一层曲折，真教好事多磨了。』『七姐，你说实话，我也说实话。』胡雪岩很恳切地答道∶『我们也想到，你要有个好帮手，凡事能够放心不管，病才好得起来。不过你们夫妻的感情，大家都晓得的，这件事只有你自己来发动，我们决不好多说。如今七姐你既然这样说了，我同四姐没有不赞成的。不过，这件事要三方面都愿意。』『哪三方面？』七姑奶奶抢着问说。

“Exactly!” Hu responded quickly. “Suggest one for me.” “There are plenty. First, bargain with her brother. Pay him more and draw up a new contract for outright sale.” “No, that would be useless. If it could work, Madam Luosi would have done it already. Did I not say his business is doing well? He is not redeeming her for money.” “Very well. Suppose he truly cares about her future. What sort of family has he chosen? We must see whether the man’s household is suitable, and ask whether Ruixiang is willing. If the family is disreputable and she unwilling, there is every reason to prevent her redemption.” “Well—” Hu could hardly tell her how arbitrary that was, so changed direction. “I think there is only one course. He wishes her well, and so do we. Let us find her a good marriage. If she herself agrees, her brother can have nothing to say.” The Seventh Lady considered. “I should like Fourth Sister to come and persuade her.” “Persuade her of what? Could I not do it?” “I fear you might not be tactful enough. Ruixiang is afraid of you. Even if she agreed, it might be unwillingly. There is no point in forcing a matter like this.” “What must she agree to, and willingly? Tell me plainly. You know our maids are not afraid of me. If anything, they are more in awe of Madam Luosi.” “Then I shall speak plainly. On the second day after Ruixiang arrived, I began thinking of it. I have always wanted to find someone for Yingchun, someone he would like and with whom I too would feel at ease. Fourth Sister sent me Ruixiang, a girl one could not find even by searching with a lantern, and I was delighted. I meant to wait until you or Fourth Sister came and ask in person. Who could have imagined this complication? Good things meet so many obstacles.” “Since you speak frankly, so shall I,” Hu answered earnestly. “We too thought you needed a good helper, someone to whom you could leave matters without worry, so that you might recover. But everyone knows the closeness between you and your husband. The initiative had to come from you; we could not say more. Now that you have spoken, Madam Luosi and I can only approve. But all three parties must agree.” “Which three?” she asked quickly.

『你，应春，还有瑞香。』胡雪岩紧接着说∶『瑞香我来劝她；我想，她一定也肯的？』小爷叔，你怎么晓得她一定肯？『『我们家常常来往的女太太，不管是亲戚，还是朋友，少说也有二、三十位，一谈起人缘，瑞香总说∶』要算七姑奶奶「，从这句话上，就可以晓得了？『胡雪岩编出来这套话，使得七姑奶奶面露微笑，双眼发亮，显然大为高兴。『七姐，』胡雪岩问说∶『现在我要提醒你了，你应该问一问应春愿意不愿意。』『他不愿也要愿。』七姑奶奶极有把握地，『小爷叔你不必操心。』『不见得。』胡雪岩摇摇头∶『去年他去拜生日，老太太问过他，他说他决不想，好好一个家，何苦生出许是非？看来他作兴不肯讨小。』七姑奶奶『哈』一声笑了出来，『世界上哪个男人不喜欢讨小？』她说∶『小爷叔，你真当我阿木林？』『阿木林』是洋场上新兴起来的一句俗语，傻瓜之意。胡雪岩听她语涉讥嘲，只好报以窘笑。

“You, Yingchun and Ruixiang. I shall speak to Ruixiang. I think she will certainly agree.” “How do you know?” “Among the ladies who visit our house, relatives and friends, there must be twenty or thirty at least. Whenever we speak of whom everyone likes best, Ruixiang always says, ‘The Seventh Lady.’ That tells us enough, does it not?” Hu’s invented story brought a smile to her face and a brightness to her eyes. She was plainly delighted. “Now I must remind you to ask whether Yingchun is willing.” “Willing or not, he will have to be,” she said confidently. “You need not worry.” “Not necessarily.” Hu shook his head. “When he came for my mother’s birthday last year, she asked him. He said he would never consider it. Why bring trouble into a happy home? He may well refuse a concubine.” The Seventh Lady laughed aloud. “What man in the world does not like the idea? Do you really take me for an amulin?” Amulin was a new expression in the foreign settlement, meaning a fool. At her mocking tone, Hu could only answer with an embarrassed smile.
