『艾小姐，你是哪里人？』『我出生在爱丁堡。』艾敦一面调着奶茶，一面答说。螺蛳太太不知道这个地名，古应春便即解释∶『她是英国人。』『喔！』螺蛳太太说道∶『你们英国同我们中国一样的，都是老太后当权。』艾敦虽会说中国话，也不过是日常用语，什么『老太后当权』，就跟螺蛳太太听到『爱丁堡』这个地名一样，瞠目不知所对。这就少不得又要靠古应春来疏通了∶『她是指你们英国的维多利亚女皇，跟我们中国的慈禧太后。』『喔，』艾敦颇为惊异，因为她也接待过许多中国的女顾客，除了北里娇娃以外，间或也有贵妇与淑女，但从没有一个人在谈话时会提到英国女皇。因为如此，便大起好感，招待螺蛳太太用午茶，非常殷勤。接着，管事的捧来了三个长方盒子，一律黑色真皮，上烫金字，打开第一个盒子，蓝色鹅绒上，嵌着一双光芒四射的白金钻镯，镶嵌得非常精致。

“Where are you from, Miss Ai?” “I was born in Edinburgh,” she replied while preparing tea with milk. Madam Luosi did not know the place, so Gu explained that she was British. “Then your country is like ours,” Madam Luosi said. “Both are ruled by an old empress dowager.” Aidun’s Chinese covered everyday conversation only. Talk of an “empress dowager ruling” left her as bewildered as the name Edinburgh had left Madam Luosi. Gu had to explain again. “She means your Queen Victoria and our Empress Dowager Cixi.” Aidun was surprised. She had served many Chinese women—courtesans, and occasionally ladies of rank or respectable young women—but none had mentioned the British queen. She warmed to Madam Luosi and served her afternoon tea with particular care. The manager then brought three oblong boxes, all of black leather with gold lettering. Inside the first, a pair of brilliantly sparkling platinum bracelets set with diamonds rested on blue velvet. The workmanship was exquisite.

仔细看去，盒子虽新，白金的颜色却似有异，『这是旧的？』她问。『是的。这是拿破仑皇后心爱的首饰。』『我不管什么皇后。』螺蛳太太说∶『嫁妆总是新的好。』『这两副都是新的。』另外西副，一副全钻，一副镶了红蓝宝石，论贵重是全钻的那副，每一只有四粒黄豆大的钻石，用碎钻连接，拿在手里不动都会闪耀；但谈到华丽，却要算镶宝石的那副。『什么价钱？』『这副三万五，镶宝石的这副三万二。』管事的说∶『胡太太，我劝你买全钻的这副，虽然贵三千银子，其实比镶宝的划算。』螺蛳太太委决不下，便即说道∶『艾小姐，请你戴起来我看看。』

On closer inspection, however, the box looked new while the metal’s colour seemed otherwise. “These are used?” she asked. “Yes. They were favourite jewels of Napoleon’s empress.” “I care nothing for empresses. New things are better for a dowry.” “Both these other pairs are new.” One was set entirely with diamonds; the other combined diamonds with rubies and sapphires. The all-diamond pair was the more valuable. Each bracelet held four diamonds the size of soybeans, linked by smaller stones, and glittered even when held still. For splendour, however, the coloured stones excelled. “What are the prices?” “Thirty-five thousand for these; thirty-two thousand for the pair with coloured stones. I advise the all-diamond pair, Madam Hu. It costs three thousand more but is better value.” Unable to decide, she asked Aidun to put them on.

艾敦便一只手腕戴一样，平伸出来让她仔细鉴赏，螺蛳太太看了半天转眼问道∶『七姐夫，你看呢？』『好，当然是全钻的这副好，可惜太素净了。』这看法跟螺蛳太太的完全一样，顿时作了决定，『又是新娘子，又是老太太在，不宜太素净。』她向管事说道∶『我东西是挑定了，现在要谈价钱，价钱谈不拢，挑也是白挑。我倒请问你，这副镯子是啥时候来的？』『一年多了。』『那末一年以前，你的标价是多少？』『三万。』『这不相信，你现在只涨了两千银子，一成都不到。』『我说的是实话。』管事的从天鹅绒衬底的夹层中，抽出来一张标鉴说∶『古先生，请你看。』标签上确是阿拉伯字的『三万』；螺蛳太太也识洋数码，她的心思很快，随即说道∶『你刚才自己说过，买全钻的这副划算，可见得买这副不划算。必是当初就乱标的一个码子，大概自己都觉得良心上过不去，所以只涨了一成不到，是不是？』『胡太太真厉害。』管事的苦笑道∶『驳得我都没有话好说了。』

Aidun wore one kind on each wrist and held out her arms for inspection. After a long look, Madam Luosi turned to Gu. “What do you think, Brother-in-law?” “The all-diamond pair is better, certainly, but rather too plain in colour.” This exactly matched her own view, and she decided at once. “For a bride, with the Old Madam present, the effect should not be too subdued.” She turned to the manager. “I have chosen. Now we must discuss the price, or choosing is pointless. When did these bracelets arrive?” “More than a year ago.” “What price did you mark them at then?” “Thirty thousand.” “I do not believe it. You have added only two thousand—less than ten per cent.” “It is true.” He drew a label from beneath the velvet lining. “Mr Gu, please look.” The Arabic numerals did indeed read thirty thousand. Madam Luosi could read them too and responded quickly. “You yourself said the all-diamond pair was better value, so this one must be poor value. You must have marked an excessive price originally and felt too guilty to raise it by even ten per cent. Is that not so?” The manager smiled ruefully. “You are formidable, Madam Hu. I have no answer.”

螺蛳太太一笑说∶『大家驳来驳去，尽管是讲道理，到底也伤和气。这样，镯子我一定买你的，现在我们先看别的东西，镯子的价钱留到最后再谈，好不好？』是，是。『。于是看水晶盘碗、看香水、看各种奇巧摆设；管事的为了想把那副镶宝钻镯卖个好价钱，在这些货色上的开价都格外公道。挑停当了，最后再谈镯价。『这里一共是一万二。』螺蛳太太说道∶『我们老爷交代，添妆不能超过四万银子；你看怎么样？』她紧接着又说∶『不要讨价还价，成不成一句话。』『胡太太，』管事的答说∶『你这一记「翻天印」下来，教我怎么招架？』『做生意不能勉强。镯子价钱谈不拢，我只好另外去物色；这一万二是谈好了的，我先打票子给你。』管事的楞住了，只好示意艾敦招待螺蛳太太喝茶吃点心，将古应春悄悄拉到一边，苦笑着说∶『这胡太太手段我真服了。为了迁就，后来看的那些东西，都是照本卖的，其中一盏水晶大吊灯，盛道台出过三千银子，我们没有卖，卖给胡太太只算两千五。如果胡太太不买镯子，我这笔生意做下来，饭碗都要敲破了。』『她并不是不买，是你不卖。』

She smiled. “However reasonable our arguments, too much disputing spoils goodwill. Let us do this: I shall certainly buy the bracelets from you. Show me the other goods first, and we shall settle their price last.” He agreed. They examined crystal plates and bowls, perfumes, and all manner of ingenious ornaments. Hoping to obtain a good price for the gem-set bracelets, the manager priced these other articles especially fairly. Once her choices were complete, they returned to the bracelets. “These goods total twelve thousand,” she said. “My husband told me not to spend more than forty thousand on additions to the dowry. What do you say? No bargaining now—yes or no.” “Madam Hu, how can I withstand such a crushing stroke?” “Business cannot be forced. If we cannot agree on the bracelets, I must look elsewhere. The twelve thousand is settled; I shall draw you a draft for that now.” Stunned, the manager signalled Aidun to serve more tea and refreshments, then quietly drew Gu aside. “I truly admire her methods,” he said with a bitter smile. “To accommodate her, I offered those later goods at cost. Intendant Sheng offered three thousand for one of the large crystal chandeliers, and we refused; I have charged her only two thousand five hundred. If she does not buy the bracelets, this transaction will cost me my job.” “She is willing to buy. You are unwilling to sell.”

『哪里是我不卖？价钱不对。』古应春说∶『做这笔生意，赚钱其次；不赚也就是赚了！这话怎么说呢？胡财神嫁女儿，漂亮的嫁妆是别发洋行承办的，你想想看，这句话值多少钱？』『原就是贪图这个名声，才各外迁就，不过总价四万银子，这笔生意实在做不下来！』『要亏本？』『亏本虽不至于，不过以后的行情——』『以后是以后，现在是现在。』古应春抢着说道∶『说老实话，市面很坏，有钱的人都在逃难了；以后你们也未见得有这种大生意上门。』管事的沉默了好一会才说了句∶『这笔生意我如果答应下来，我的花红就都要赔进去了。』古应春知道洋行中的规矩，薪金颇为微薄，全靠售货的奖金，看他的神情不象说假话，足见螺蛳太太杀得太凶；也就是间接证明，确是买到了便宜货，因此觉得应该略作让步，免得错过了机会。

“It is not that I am unwilling. The price is wrong.” “Profit is secondary here. Even without it, you gain. Consider the value of people saying that Biefa supplied the splendid dowry when Hu, the God of Wealth, married off his daughter.” “That reputation is precisely why I have been so accommodating. But I really cannot conclude the business at forty thousand.” “Would you lose money?” “Not exactly. But future prices—” “The future is the future; this is now. Frankly, trade is poor, and wealthy people are already fleeing the danger. You may not see another order like this later.” After a long silence, the manager said, “If I accept, I shall sacrifice my entire bonus.” Gu knew how foreign firms worked: salaries were modest, and employees depended on sales bonuses. The man seemed truthful. Madam Luosi had plainly bargained very hard—which also confirmed that she was obtaining excellent value. Some concession was needed lest they lose the opportunity.

『你说这话，我要帮你的忙。』他将声音放极轻，『我作主，请胡太太私下津贴你五百两银子，弥补你的损失。』管事的未餍所欲，但人家话已说在前面，是帮他的忙，倘或拒绝，变成不识抬举，不但生意做不成，而且得罪了大主顾，真正不是『生意经』了。这样一转念头，别无选择，『多谢古先生。』他说∶『正好大班在这里，我跟他去说明白。古先生即然能替胡太太作主，那么，答应我的话，此刻就先不必告诉胡太太。』古应春明白，他是怕螺蛳太太一不小心，露出口风来，照洋人的看法，这种私下收受顾客津贴的行为，等于舞弊，一旦发觉，不但敲破碗饭，而且有吃官司的可能。因而重重点头，表示充分领会。于是，管事的向螺蛳太太告个罪，入内去见大班。不多片刻，带了一名洋人出来，碧眼方颐，留两撇往上翅的菱角须，古应春一看便知是德国人。

“In that case, I shall help you,” Gu said, lowering his voice. “I shall take responsibility for having Madam Hu give you five hundred taels privately to compensate for your loss.” The manager wanted more. But Gu had presented this as a favour; refusing would look ungrateful, lose the sale, and offend a major customer. That was hardly sound business. He had no real choice. “Thank you, Mr Gu. The director happens to be here. I shall explain the position to him. Since you can decide for Madam Hu, there is no need to tell her of your promise just yet.” Gu understood. The manager feared that she might carelessly let something slip. To the foreign owners, accepting a private payment from a customer amounted to dishonesty. Discovery could mean not only dismissal but prosecution. Gu nodded emphatically to show that he fully understood. The manager excused himself to Madam Luosi and went to see the director. Soon he returned with a blue-eyed foreigner, square-jawed and wearing an upward-curving moustache. Gu recognized him at once as German.

果然，是别发的经理威廉士，他不会说英语，而古应春不通德文，需要管事的翻译；经过介绍，很客气地见了礼。威廉士表示，他亦久慕胡雪岩的名声，爱女出阁，能在别发洋行办嫁妆，在他深感荣幸。至于价格方面，是否损及成本，不足计较，除了照螺蛳太太的开价成交以外，他打算另外特制一只银盘，作为贺礼。听到这里，螺蛳太太大为高兴，忍不住对古应春笑道∶『有这样的好事，倒没有想到。』『四姐，你慢点高兴。』古应春答说∶『看样子，另外还有话。』『古先生看得真准。』管事的接口，『我们大班有个主意，想请胡太太允许，就是想把胡三小姐的这批嫁妆，在我们洋行里陈列一个月，陈列期满，由我们派专差护送到杭州交货。』在他说到一半时，古应春已经向螺蛳太太递了个眼色；因此，她只静静地听着，不置可否，让古应春去应付。『你们预备怎么样陈列？』

The newcomer was indeed Biefa’s manager, Weilianshi. He spoke no English, while Gu knew no German, so the head clerk had to interpret. Introductions and courteous greetings followed. Weilianshi said that he had long admired Hu’s reputation and felt deeply honored that Biefa should supply the dowry for his beloved daughter’s marriage. Whether the price fell below cost was hardly worth considering. He would accept Madam Luosi’s offer and, in addition, have a silver salver specially made as a wedding gift. Delighted, she could not resist turning to Gu with a smile. “Such good fortune! I never expected it.” “Do not rejoice too soon, Sister Luo,” he answered. “I think there is something more.” “Mr Gu sees very clearly,” the clerk said. “Our manager has a proposal for Madam Hu. He would like to display the Third Miss’s dowry here for a month. Afterward, we would send a special escort to deliver it in Hangzhou.” Halfway through this explanation, Gu had given Madam Luosi a warning glance. She therefore listened quietly, neither agreeing nor refusing, and left him to answer. “How would you display it?”

『我们辟半间店面，用红丝绳拦起来，作为陈列所。』『要不要作说明？』『当然要。』管事的说∶『这是大家有面子的事。』『不错，大家有面子。不过，这件事我们要商量商量。』古应春问道∶『这是不是一个交易的条件？』管事的似乎颇感意外——在他的想法，买主决无不同意之理∶因而问道∶『古先生，莫非一陈列出来，有啥不方便的地方。『是的，或许有点不方便，原因现在不必说。能不能陈列，现在也还不能定规，只请你问一问你们大班，如果我们不愿意陈列，这笔交易是不是就不成功了。』管事的点点头，与他们大班用德国话交谈了好一会，答复古应春说∶『我们大班说∶这是个额外的要求，不算交易的条件。不过，我们真的很希望古先生能赏我们一个面子。』『这不是我的事。』古应春急忙分辩，『就象你所说的，这是大家有面子的事，我亦很希望能陈列出来。不过，胡大先生是朝廷的大员，他的官声也很要紧。万一不能如你们大班的愿，要请他原谅。』一提到『官声』，管事的明白了，连连点头说道∶『好的，好的。请问古先生，啥辰光可以听回音？』

“We would set aside half the shop and cordon it off with red silk ropes.” “Would there be an explanation?” “Naturally. It is something that brings distinction to everyone.” “Certainly. But we must discuss it. Is this a condition of the sale?” The clerk looked surprised. He had never imagined that the buyers could object. “Would displaying it cause some inconvenience, Mr Gu?” “Possibly. There is no need to explain why just now. Nor can we yet decide whether to agree. Please ask your manager only this: if we decline the display, does the sale fall through?” The clerk nodded and spoke with his employer in German for some time. “He says this is an additional request, not a condition of the sale. But we sincerely hope you will do us the honor.” “It is not my decision,” Gu explained quickly. “As you say, the display would bring credit to everyone, and I too would like to see it. But Mr Hu is a senior servant of the court. His public reputation matters. If we cannot meet your manager’s wishes, we must ask his understanding.” At the mention of an official’s reputation, the clerk understood and nodded repeatedly. “Of course. When might we hear your answer?”

古应春考虑了一会答说∶『这样，你把今天所看的货色，开一张单子，注明价钱，明天上午到我那里来，谈付款的办法。至于能不能陈列，明天也许可以告诉你，倘或要写信到杭州，那就得要半个月以后，才有回音』『好的，我照吩咐办。』管事的答说∶『明天我亲自到古先生府上去拜访。』对于这天的『别发』之行，螺蛳太太十分得意，坐在七姑奶奶床前的安乐椅上，口讲指划，津津乐道古应春谈到私下许了管事五百两银子的津贴，螺蛳太太不但认帐，而且很夸奖他处理得法。见此光景，七姑奶奶当然亦很高兴。『还有件事，』螺蛳太太说∶『请七姐夫来讲。』『不是讲，是要好好商量。』古应春谈了陈列一事，接着问道∶『你们看怎么样？』『我看没有啥不可以。』螺蛳太太问道∶『七姐，你说呢？』『恐怕太招摇。』『尤其，』古应春接口，『现在山东在闹水灾；局势又不大好，恐怕会有人说闲话。』听得这话，螺蛳太太不作声，看一看七姑奶奶，脸色阴下来了。

After considering, Gu said, “Prepare a list of everything we saw today, with the prices, and come to my house tomorrow morning to discuss payment. I may be able to answer about the display then. If we must write to Hangzhou, however, it will take another half month.” “Certainly. I shall call on you myself tomorrow.” Madam Luosi was extremely pleased with the day’s visit to Biefa. Sitting in the armchair beside the Seventh Lady’s bed, she described it with lively words and gestures. When Gu mentioned privately promising the clerk five hundred taels in compensation, she not only accepted the expense but praised his handling of it. Naturally, the Seventh Lady was pleased too. “There is one other matter,” Madam Luosi said. “Let my brother-in-law explain.” “It needs discussion, not merely explanation.” Gu described the proposed display and asked their opinion. “I see no objection,” Madam Luosi said. “What do you think, Seventh Sister?” “It might be too ostentatious.” “Especially now,” Gu added, “with floods in Shandong and the general situation unsettled. People may criticize.” Madam Luosi fell silent. She glanced at the Seventh Lady, and her face darkened.

『应春，』七姑奶奶使个眼色，『你给我摇个「德律风」给医生，说我的药水喝完了，再配两服来。』古应春会意，点点头往外便走，好容她们说私说话。『七姐，』螺蛳太太毫不掩饰她内心的欲望，『我真想把我们三小姐添妆的这些东西陈列出来，让大家看看。』七姑奶奶没有想到她对这件事如此重视，而且相当认真，不由得楞在那里说不出话。在螺蛳太太，做事发议论，不发则已一发就一定要透彻，所以接着她自己的话又说∶『那个德国人，不说我再也想不到∶一说，我马上就动心了。七姐，你想想，嫁女儿要花多少工夫，为来为去为点啥？为的是一个场面。发嫁妆要教大家都来看，人愈多，愈有面子，花了多少心血，光看那一天，人人称赞、个个羡慕，心里头就会说∶』喏，这就叫人生在世！「七姐，拿你我当初做女儿的辰光，看大户人家嫁女儿，心里头的感想，来想想」大先生「现在的心境，你说，那个德国人的做法，要不要动心？『

“Yingchun,” the Seventh Lady said, giving him a look, “telephone the doctor for me. Tell him my mixture is finished and ask for two more doses.” Understanding, Gu nodded and went out, leaving the women free to speak privately. “Seventh Sister,” Madam Luosi said without disguising her wish, “I truly want these additions to our Third Miss’s dowry displayed for everyone to see.” The Seventh Lady had not imagined that she cared so deeply and seriously about it. For a moment she could say nothing. Once Madam Luosi expressed an opinion, she always carried it through thoroughly. “If that German had not suggested it, the idea would never have occurred to me. The moment he spoke, I wanted it. Think of all the work that goes into marrying off a daughter. What is it for? To make a splendid occasion. When the dowry goes out, everyone should come to look. The more people, the greater the honor. After all the care and effort, to hear praise and envy on that one day makes one think: ‘Ah, this is what it means to live!’ Remember how we felt as girls, watching wealthy families marry their daughters. Think of Mr Hu’s feelings now. Would the German’s proposal not tempt him?”

大姑奶奶的想法，开始为她引入同一条路子了。大贵大富之家，讲到喜庆的排场，最重视的是为父母做寿及嫁女儿，但做寿在『花甲』以后，还有『古稀∶』古稀『以后还有八十、九十，讲排场的机会还有；只有嫁女儿，风光只得一次，父母能尽其爱心的，也只有这一次，所以踵事增华，多少阔都可以摆。七姑奶奶小时候曾看过一家巨室发嫁妆，殿后的是八名身穿深蓝新布袍的中年汉子，每人手里一个朱漆托盘，盘中是一本厚厚的毛蓝布面的簿子，这算什么陪嫁？问起来才知道那家的陪嫁中，有八家当铺。那八名中年汉子，便是八家当铺的朝奉，盘中所捧，自然是那当铺的总帐。这种别开生面的』嫁妆『，真正是面子十足，令人历久难忘。如今别发洋行要陈列胡三小姐的一部分嫁妆，在上海这个五方杂处的地方，有这样一件新闻，会弄得云贵四川，再僻远的地力也会有『胡雪岩嫁女儿』如何阔气这么一个传说，这是花多少钱也买不来的一件事，难怪螺蛳太太要动心。『大先生平生所好的是个面子；有这样一件有面子的事，我拿它放过了，自己觉得也太对不起大先生了。七姐，你说呢？』

The Seventh Lady’s thoughts began to follow the same course. In rich and distinguished families, the grandest celebrations were parents’ birthdays and daughters’ marriages. Yet after sixty came seventy, and after seventy, eighty or ninety: there would be further opportunities for splendid birthdays. A daughter’s wedding came only once. Parents had only that occasion on which to show their love in full, and could therefore add magnificence to magnificence without limit. As a child, the Seventh Lady had seen one great household’s dowry procession end with eight middle-aged men in new dark-blue robes. Each carried a red-lacquered tray holding a thick ledger bound in coarse blue cloth. What sort of dowry was that? On asking, she learned that the bride was receiving eight pawnshops. The eight men were their senior appraisers, and the books their general ledgers. Such an extraordinary dowry displayed wealth so impressively that the memory never faded. Now Biefa proposed to exhibit part of the Third Miss’s dowry in Shanghai, where people from every region gathered. News of it could carry stories of Hu’s lavish wedding preparations to Yunnan, Guizhou, Sichuan, and the remotest places. No amount of money could buy that publicity. Small wonder Madam Luosi was tempted. “What Mr Hu loves above all is prestige. If I let such an opportunity pass, I should feel I had failed him. Do you not agree?”

『那，』七姑奶奶说∶『何不问问他自己？』『这不能问的。一问┅┅』螺蛳太太停了一下说∶『七姐，你倒替他设身处地想一想呢！』稍为想一想就知道行不通。凡是一个人好虚面子，口中决不肯承认的，问到他，一定拿『算了，算了』这些不热中但也不反对的语气来答复。不过，现在情势不同，似乎可以跟他切切实实谈一谈。

“Then why not ask him?” the Seventh Lady suggested. “We cannot. If we ask—” Madam Luosi paused. “Put yourself in his place.” A moment’s thought showed the difficulty. A man fond of outward show would never admit it. Asked directly, he would say, “Never mind, never mind,” in a tone neither eager nor actually opposed. Yet circumstances were different now; perhaps they could discuss the matter candidly with him.

念头尚未转定，螺蛳太太却又开口了，『七姐，』她说，『这回我替我们三小姐来添妆，说实话，是件吃力不讨好的事，价钱高低，东西好坏，没有个「准稿子」，便宜不会有人晓得，但只要买贵了一样，就尽有人在背后说闲话了。现在别发把我买的东西陈列出来，足见这些东西的身价，就没有人敢说闲话了。到于对我们老太太，还有三小姐的娘，胡家上上下下我也足足可以交代了，我要教大家晓得，我待我们三小姐，同比我自己生的还要关心。』最后这句话，打动了七姑奶奶，这件事对螺蛳太太在胡家的声名地位很重要。由于别发洋行陈列了胡三小姐的嫁妆，足以证明螺蛳太太所采办的都是精品，同时也证明了螺蛳太太的贤慧，对胡三小姐爱如己出。从另一方面看，有这样一个出风头的机会，而竟放弃了，大家都不会了解，原因是怕太招摇，于胡雪岩的官声不利；只说都因为是些拿不出手的不值钱的东西，怕人笑话，所以不愿陈列，这一出一入之间关系的变化是太重要了。七姑奶奶沉吟了好一会说∶『别发的陈列，是陈列给洋人看的；中国人进洋行的很少，陈列不陈列，不和多大的关系。所以别发陈列的这些东西，我看纯然是拿给洋人看的。既然如此，我倒有个想法，你看行不行？』

Before the Seventh Lady settled that thought, Madam Luosi spoke again. “To tell the truth, buying these additions to the dowry is a thankless task. There is no fixed standard for prices or quality. No one will notice a bargain, but let me overpay for one thing and there will be plenty of talk behind my back. If Biefa displays what I have bought, that proves its worth, and no one will dare complain. I can account for myself to our old lady, the Third Miss’s mother, and everyone in the Hu household. I want them to know that I care for her even more than if she were my own daughter.” The last words moved the Seventh Lady. This mattered deeply to Madam Luosi’s reputation and standing in the family. The exhibition would prove both the excellence of her purchases and her generosity toward a daughter she loved as her own. Conversely, if such an opportunity for distinction were refused, no one would understand that the reason was to avoid ostentation harmful to Hu’s official reputation. They would say the goods were worthless and too embarrassing to show. The difference between these impressions was crucial. After pondering for some time, the Seventh Lady said, “Biefa’s displays are for foreigners. Few Chinese enter foreign shops, so it makes little difference to them whether the goods are displayed. These things would chiefly be shown to foreigners. In that case, I have an idea. See what you think.”

『你说。』『陈列让他陈列，说明都用英文，不准用中国字，这样子就不显得招摇了。』螺蛳太太稍想一想，重重地答一声∶『好。』显得对七姑奶奶百依百顺似的。于是七姑奶奶喊一声∶『妹妹！』喊瑞香为『妹妹』，已经好几个月了；瑞香亦居之不疑，答应得很响亮，但此时有螺蛳太太在座，却显得有些忸怩，连应声都不敢，只疾趋到床前，听候吩咐。

“Tell me.” “Let them exhibit the goods, but require every description to be in English. No Chinese characters. That way it will look less ostentatious.” After a moment Madam Luosi answered with an emphatic “Good,” seeming entirely willing to follow her lead. The Seventh Lady then called, “Little Sister!” For several months she had addressed Ruixiang that way, and Ruixiang had accepted it naturally, answering readily. With Madam Luosi present, however, she grew shy and did not even dare reply aloud. She merely hurried to the bedside to await instructions.

『你看老爷在哪里？请他来。』瑞香答应着走了，螺蛳太太便即轻声说道∶『七姐，我这趟来三件事，一是我们三小姐添妆，二是探望你的病，还有件事就是瑞香的事。怎么不给他们圆房？』『我催了他好几遍了。』这个他是指古应春；此时已经出现在门外，七姑奶奶便住了口，却对螺蛳太太做个手势，递个眼色，意思是回头细谈。『应春，我想到一个法子，罗四姐也赞成的。』七姑奶奶接着便说了她的办法。古应春心想，这也不过是掩耳盗铃的办法；不过比用中文作说明，总要好些，当下点点头说∶『等别发的管事来了，我告诉他。不过┅┅』

“Find out where the master is and ask him to come.” Ruixiang went. Madam Luosi lowered her voice. “I came for three things: the Third Miss’s dowry, a visit to you in your illness, and Ruixiang. Why have they still not begun married life together?” “I have urged him several times.” “Him” meant Gu, who had now appeared at the door. The Seventh Lady fell silent but gestured and gave Madam Luosi a look indicating that they would discuss it later. “Yingchun, I have thought of a way, and Sister Luo agrees.” She explained the plan. Gu thought it scarcely better than stopping one’s ears while stealing a bell. Still, it was preferable to Chinese labels. He nodded. “I shall tell Biefa’s clerk when he comes. But—”

他没有再说下去。七姑奶却明白，『只要不上报，就招摇不到哪里去了。』她说∶『你同「长毛状元」不是吃花酒的好朋友？』『对！你倒提醒我了；我来打他一个招呼。』古应春问道。『还有什么话？』『就是这件事。』『那，』古应春转脸说道∶『四姐，对不起，今天晚上我不能陪你吃饭。我同密本常有个约，很要紧的，我现在就要走了。喔，还有件事，他也晓得你来了，要你吃饭，看你哪天有空？』『不必，谢谢他罗。』螺蛳太太说∶『他一个人在上海，没有家小，请我去了也不便。姐夫，你替我切切实实辞一辞。『等他一走螺蛳太太有个疑团急于要打开，不知道』长毛状元『是怎么回事？』这个人姓王，叫王韬，你们杭州韧光的韬。长毛得势的时候开过科，状元就是这个王韬。上海人都叫他「长毛状元」。『』那末，上报不上报，关长毛状元啥事情？『『长毛状元在《申报》馆做事，蛮有势力的；叫应春打他一个招呼，别发陈列三小姐的嫁妆那件事，不要上报，家里不晓得就不要紧了。』原来如此！『螺蛳太太瞄了瑞香一眼。

He left the sentence unfinished, but the Seventh Lady understood. “Provided it stays out of the newspapers, it cannot attract too much attention. Are you not a friend of the ‘Rebel First Scholar’—the man you go drinking with in the pleasure quarters?” “Yes! You have reminded me. I shall speak to him. Is there anything else?” “Only that.” Gu turned to Madam Luosi. “I am sorry I cannot dine with you tonight, Sister Luo. I have an important appointment with Mi Benchang and must leave now. Oh, he knows you are here and wants to invite you to dinner. When would you be free?” “No, thank him for me. He lives in Shanghai without his family; it would be awkward for me to visit. Please decline firmly on my behalf.” Once Gu had gone, Madam Luosi hastened to ask about the “Rebel First Scholar.” “His surname is Wang, and his name Tao—the same character as in Hangzhou’s Taoguang,” the Seventh Lady explained. “When the rebels were powerful, they held examinations, and Wang Tao came first. Everyone in Shanghai calls him the ‘Rebel First Scholar.’” “What does that have to do with the newspapers?” “He works at Shenbao and has considerable influence there. If Yingchun asks him to keep the dowry display out of the paper, the family will not hear about it, and there will be no trouble.” “I see.” Madam Luosi glanced toward Ruixiang.

七姑奶奶立即会意，便叫瑞香去监厨；调开了她好谈她的事。『我催了应春好几次，他只说∶慢慢再谈。因为市面不好，他说他没心思来做这件事。你来了正好，请你劝劝他；如果他再不听，你同他办交涉。『』办交涉？『螺蛳太太诧异，』我怎么好同姐夫办这种交涉？『『咦！瑞香是你的人，你要替瑞香说话啊！』『喔！』螺蛳太太笑了，『七姐，什么事到了你嘴里，没理也变有理了。？』本来就有理嘛！『七姑奶奶低声说道∶』他们倒也好，一个不急；一个只怕是急在心里，嘴里不说。苦的是我，倒象亏欠了瑞香似的。『』好！『螺蛳太太立即接口，』有这个理由，我倒好同姐夫办交涉，不怕他不挑日子。『』等他来挑，又要推三阻四了。不如我们来挑。『七姑奶奶又说∶』总算也是一杯喜酒，你一定要吃了再走。『』当然。『螺蛳太太沉吟着说∶』今天八月廿八，这个月小建，后天就交九月了。三小姐的喜事只得两个月的工夫，我亦真正是所谓归心如箭。『』我晓得，我晓得。『七姑奶奶说∶』四姐，皇历挂在梳妆台镜子后面，请你拿给我。『取皇历来一翻，九月初三是』大满棚『的日子。由于螺蛳太太急于要回杭州，不容别作选择，一下就决定了九月初三为古应春与瑞香圆房。

The Seventh Lady understood at once and sent Ruixiang to supervise the kitchen, freeing them to discuss her. “I have urged Yingchun several times. He always says we can talk later. Business is poor, he says, and he has no heart for such matters. Your visit is timely. Persuade him for me. If he still refuses, take it up with him.” “Take it up with him?” Madam Luosi was astonished. “How could I approach my brother-in-law on a matter like that?” “Why not? Ruixiang came from you. You ought to speak for her.” “Ah!” Madam Luosi laughed. “Once a thing passes through your mouth, even the unreasonable becomes reasonable.” “It is reasonable.” The Seventh Lady lowered her voice. “They are all right: one is in no hurry, and the other is probably impatient inwardly but says nothing. I am the one who suffers, feeling as though I owe Ruixiang something.” “With that reason, I certainly can take it up with him. He will have to choose a day.” “If we wait for him, he will find more excuses. Let us choose it ourselves. There must at least be a wedding cup of wine, and you must stay to drink it.” “Of course.” Madam Luosi considered. “Today is the twenty-eighth of the eighth month. This is a short month; the ninth begins the day after tomorrow. The Third Miss’s wedding is only two months away. I really am longing to get home.” “I know, I know. The almanac hangs behind the dressing-table mirror. Please bring it.” They consulted it and found the third day of the ninth month auspicious for everything. Madam Luosi’s eagerness to return to Hangzhou left no time to choose another date. They settled at once on that day for Gu and Ruixiang to begin their married life together.

『总要替她做几件衣服，打两样首饰，七姐，这算是我的陪嫁，你就不必管了。』『你陪嫁是你的。』七姑奶奶说∶『我也预备了一点，好象还不大够；四姐，你不要同我客气。』说着，探手到枕下，取出一个阜康的存折，『请你明天带她去看看，她喜欢啥，我托你替她买。』彼此有交情在，不容她客气，更不容她推辞；螺蛳太太将折子接了过来，看都不看，便放入口袋了。『七姐，我们老太太牵挂你得好厉害。十一月里，不晓得你能不能去吃喜酒？』『我想去！就怕行动不便，替你们添麻烦。』『麻烦点啥？不过多派两个丫头老妈子照应你。而况还有瑞香。』七姑奶奶久病在床，本就一直想到哪里去走走，此时螺蛳太太一邀，心思便更加活动了，但最大的顾虑，还在人家办喜事已忙得不可开交，只怕没有足够的工夫来照料她。果然有此情形，人家心里自是不安；自己忖度，内心也未见得便能泰然。因此任凭螺蛳太太极力怂恿，她仍旧觉得有考虑的必要。

“She must have some new clothes and a couple of pieces of jewelry,” Madam Luosi said. “Those will be my dowry gift. You need not trouble about them.” “Your gift is yours,” the Seventh Lady replied. “I have prepared something too, though perhaps not enough. Do not stand on ceremony.” Reaching beneath her pillow, she took out a Fukang passbook. “Take her shopping tomorrow. Whatever she likes, please buy it for her on my behalf.” Their friendship allowed neither polite hesitation nor refusal. Madam Luosi took the passbook and put it into her pocket without looking inside. “Our old lady worries about you so much. Will you be able to come to the wedding in the eleventh month?” “I should like to. But I may have difficulty getting about and cause you trouble.” “What trouble? We need only assign another couple of maids or older serving women to look after you. And Ruixiang will be there.” Long confined to bed, the Seventh Lady had often wished to go somewhere, and the invitation made her still more eager. Her chief concern was that a household already overwhelmed with wedding preparations might lack time to care for her properly. They would feel uneasy, and she herself might not be comfortable either. Despite Madam Luosi’s encouragement, she still felt the matter required thought.

『太太，』瑞香走来说道∶『你昨天讲的两样吃食，都办来了。饿不饿？饿了我就开饭。』『哪两样？』螺蛳太太前一天晚上闲话旧事时谈到当年尝过的几种饮食，怀念不置，不知瑞香的是哪两样，所以有此一问。『太太不是说，顶想念的就是糟钵头，还有菜圆子？』『对！』螺蛳太太立即答说∶『顶想这两样，不过一定要三牌楼同陶阿大家的。』『不错，我特为交代过，就是这两家买来的。』瑞香又说∶『糟钵头怕嫌油腻，奶奶不相宜，菜圆子可以吃。要不，我就把饭开到这里来。』『好！好！』七姑奶奶好热闹，连连说道∶『我从小生长在上海，三牌楼的菜圆子，只闻其名，没有见过，今天倒真要尝尝。』『三牌楼菜圆子有好几家，一定要徐寡妇家的才好。』『喔，好在什么地方？』原来上海称元宵的汤圆为圆子。三牌楼徐寡妇家的圆子，货真价实。有那省俭的顾客，一碗肉圆子四枚，仅食皮子，剩下馅子便是四个肉圆，带回家用白菜粉条同烩，便可佐膳。但徐寡妇家最出名的却是菜圆子，『她说有秘诀，说穿了也不稀奇。』螺蛳太太说∶『我去吃过几回，冷眼看看，也就懂了。秘诀就是工要细，拣顶好的菜叶子，黄的、老的都不要；嫩叶子还要抽筋，抽得极干净，滚水中捞一捞，斩得极细倒在夏布袋里把水分挤掉，加细盐、小磨麻油拌匀，就是馅子，皮子用上好水磨粉，当然不必说。』『那末，』七姑奶奶恰好有些饿了，不由得咽了口唾沫，惹得螺蛳太太笑了。

“Madam,” Ruixiang said, coming in, “both dishes you mentioned yesterday have arrived. Are you hungry? If so, I shall serve dinner.” “Which dishes?” The previous evening Madam Luosi had fondly recalled several foods she used to enjoy and could not tell which two Ruixiang meant. “Did you not say you missed zaobotou, the wine-lees casserole, and dumplings stuffed with greens most of all?” “Exactly! But they must come from Sanpailou and Tao A’da’s.” “They do. I gave particular instructions to buy from those places. The casserole may be too rich for my mistress, but she can eat the vegetable dumplings. Shall I bring dinner in here?” “Yes, yes!” The Seventh Lady loved company. “I grew up in Shanghai and have always heard of Sanpailou’s vegetable dumplings, but never seen them. I must try them tonight.” “Several shops there make them. Widow Xu’s are the best.” “What is so good about them?” In Shanghai, the glutinous-rice dumplings eaten at the Lantern Festival were called yuanzi. Widow Xu’s, at Sanpailou, gave full value. Frugal customers would buy a bowl containing four meat dumplings, eat only the wrappers, and take the four balls of filling home to stew with cabbage and bean-thread noodles for another meal. Her greatest specialty, however, was the vegetable variety. “She says she has a secret, but once explained it is nothing remarkable,” Madam Luosi said. “I ate there several times and watched quietly until I understood. The secret is painstaking work. Only the finest leaves are chosen; yellow and old ones are discarded. Even the tender leaves have every vein stripped out. They are briefly blanched, chopped very fine, put into a ramie-cloth bag, and squeezed dry. Mixed thoroughly with fine salt and stone-ground sesame oil, they make the filling. Naturally, the wrappers use the best wet-milled rice flour.” “Then—” The Seventh Lady was just hungry enough to swallow involuntarily, making Madam Luosi laugh.

『七姐，我老实告诉你，那种净素的菜圆子，除了老太太以外，大家都是偶尔吃一回还可以，一多，胃口就倒了。』螺蛳太太又说∶『我自己也觉得完全不是三牌楼徐家的那种味道。』糟钵头是上海道地的所谓『本帮菜』，通常只有今天才有，用猪肚、猪肝等等内脏，加肥鸡同煮，到够火候了，倾陶钵加糟，所以称之为糟钵头『。糟青鱼切块，与黄芽菜同煮作汤菜，即是』川糟『。『那末，你觉得比陶阿大的是好，还是坏？』『当然不及陶阿大的。』螺蛳太太说∶『不然我也不会这么想了。』『只怕现在不会象你所想的那样子好。』『喔，』螺蛳太太问道∶『莫非换过老板？』『菜圆子我没有吃过，县衙前陶阿大的糟钵头，我没有得病以前是吃过的。去年腊月里五哥从松江来了，还特为去吃过。人家做得兴兴旺旺的生意，为啥要换老板？』『那末，』螺蛳太太也极机警，知道七姑奶奶刚才的话，别有言外之意，便即追问∶『既然这样子，你的话总有啥道理在里头吧？七姑奶奶想了一下说∶』我是直性子；我们又同姊妹一样。我或者说错了，你不要怪我。『』哪里会！七姐，你这话多余。『

“To tell you the truth, Seventh Sister, apart from the Old Lady, everyone finds those plain vegetable dumplings pleasant enough once in a while, but too many of them spoil the appetite,” Madam Luosi said. “Even to me, ours taste quite different from the Xu family’s at Sanpailou.” The wine-lees casserole was an authentic Shanghai dish, one of the so-called local specialities, usually available only at this time. Pork tripe, liver, and other offal were simmered with a plump chicken; when everything was properly cooked, it was poured into an earthenware pot and wine lees were added—hence its name. Wine-lees-cured black carp, cut into pieces and cooked with pale-hearted Chinese cabbage as a soupy dish, was called chuanzao. “And do you think yours is better or worse than Tao A’da’s?” “Worse, of course. Otherwise I should not keep thinking of his.” “I suspect his would not seem quite as good now as you remember.” “Oh? Has the shop changed hands?” “I have never tried those dumplings, but before I fell ill I used to eat Tao A’da’s casserole, outside the county yamen. Last year, when Brother Five came from Songjiang in the twelfth month, he made a special trip there. Their business is flourishing. Why should it change hands?” Madam Luosi was quick to detect a further meaning. “Then there must be a reason for what you said.” The Seventh Lady considered. “I speak my mind, and we are as close as sisters. If I say something mistaken, do not take offence.” “How could I? Seventh Sister, there is no need to say that.”

『我在想，做菜圆子，或者真的有啥诀窍；至于糟钵头，我在想，你家吃大俸禄的大司务，本事莫非就不及陶阿大？说到材料，别的不谈，光是从绍兴办来的酒糟，这一点就比陶阿大那里要高明了。所以府上的糟钵头，决不会比陶阿大来得差。然而，你说不及陶阿大的糟钵头这是啥道理。』『七姐！』螺蛳太太笑道∶『我就是问你，你怎么反倒问我？』『依我看，糟钵头还是当年的糟钵头，罗四姐不是当年的罗四姐了。』七姑奶奶紧接着说∶『四姐，我这话不是说你忘本，是说此一时，彼一时，这番道理，也不是我悟出来的，是说书先生讲的一段故事，唐朝有个和尚叫懒残——』讲了懒残和尚煨芋的故事，螺蛳太太当然决不会觉得七姑奶奶有何讽刺之意，但却久久无语，心里想得很深。

“There may indeed be some special knack to making those dumplings. But as for the casserole, surely your highly paid master cook is no less skilful than Tao A’da? And as for ingredients, leaving everything else aside, the wine lees you have brought from Shaoxing are better than anything he uses. Your casserole cannot possibly be inferior to his. Yet you say it is. Why?” “Seventh Sister!” Madam Luosi laughed. “That was what I asked you. Why turn the question back on me?” “The casserole, I think, is still the casserole of those days. But Sister Luo is no longer the Sister Luo of those days.” She hastened to add, “I do not mean that you have forgotten your beginnings. I mean that times and circumstances change. Nor did I discover this truth for myself. It came from a story I heard a storyteller tell. In the Tang dynasty there was a monk called Lancan—” She told the story of the monk roasting taro. Madam Luosi certainly felt no suggestion of mockery in it, but she remained silent for a long time, deep in thought.

这时瑞香已带了小大姐来铺排餐桌，然后将七姑奶奶扶了起来，抬坐在一张特制的圈椅上，椅子很大，周围用锦垫塞紧，使得七姑奶奶不必费力便能坐直，前面是一块很大的活动木板，以便置放盘碗，木板四周镶嵌五分高的一道『围墙』以防汤汁倾出，以不致流得到处都是。那张圈椅跟『小儿车』的作用相同；七姑奶奶等瑞香替她系上『围嘴』以后，自嘲地笑道∶『无锡人常说「老小、老小」，我真是愈老愈小了。』『老倒不见得。』螺蛳太太笑道∶『皮肤又白又嫩，我都想摸一把。』说着便握住她的手臂，轻轻捏了两下，肌肉到底松弛了。『是先吃圆子，还是先吃酒？』瑞香问道。菜圆子，已经煮好了，自然先吃圆子；圆子很大，黄花累瓷饭碗中只放得下两枚，瑞香格外道地加一几条火腿后，两三片芫荽，红绿相映，动人食欲。

Ruixiang now brought a maid to lay the table. They helped the Seventh Lady up and lifted her into a specially made round-backed chair. It was large, with brocade cushions packed around her to keep her upright without effort. Across the front lay a broad, removable wooden tray for plates and bowls, edged with a little “wall” five fen high to prevent spilled soup from running everywhere. The chair served much the same purpose as a child’s chair. When Ruixiang had tied on her bib, the Seventh Lady laughed at herself. “People in Wuxi are always saying that the old become children again. The older I grow, the more of a child I am.” “You scarcely look old,” Madam Luosi said with a smile. “Your skin is so fair and soft that I want to feel it myself.” She took her arm and gave it a couple of gentle squeezes. The flesh had lost its firmness, after all. “Dumplings first, or wine?” Ruixiang asked. The dumplings were already cooked, so naturally they came first. They were so large that only two would fit in a rice bowl decorated with yellow flowers. Ruixiang had taken particular pains, adding a few strips of ham and two or three coriander leaves. The contrast of red and green was appetizing.

『我来尝一个。』七姑奶奶拿汤匙舀了一枚，嘘口气，咬了一口，紧接着便咬第二口，，欣赏之意显然。螺蛳太太也舀了一枚送入口中，接着放回圆子舀口汤喝，『瑞香，』她疑惑地问∶『是三牌楼徐寡妇家买的？』『是啊！』瑞香微笑着回答。看她的笑容，便知内有蹊跷，『你拿什么汤下的圆子？』她问。『太太尝出来了。』瑞香笑道∶『新开一家广东杏花楼，用它家的高汤下的。』『高汤？』在小馆子，『高汤』是白送的；肉骨头熬的汤，加一匙酱油，数粒葱花便是。这样的汤下菜圆子能有这样的鲜味，螺蛳太太自然要诧异了。『杏花楼的高汤，不是同洗锅水差不多的高汤；它是鸡、火腿、精肉、鲫鱼，用文火熬出来的汤，论两卖的。』『怪不得！』七姑奶奶笑道∶『如说徐寡妇的菜圆子有这样的味道，除非她是仙人。』『瑞香倒是特别巴结我，不过我反而吃不出当年的味道来了。』

“Let me try one.” The Seventh Lady lifted a dumpling in her spoon, blew on it, and took a bite, followed immediately by another: her enjoyment was plain. Madam Luosi also put one to her lips, then set it down and tasted the soup. “Ruixiang,” she asked doubtfully, “did these come from Widow Xu’s at Sanpailou?” “Yes.” Ruixiang’s smile suggested a secret. “What soup did you cook them in?” “You noticed, madam! A Cantonese restaurant called Xinghua Lou has just opened. I used their stock.” “Stock?” In a modest eating house, stock was free: bone broth with a spoonful of soy sauce and a sprinkling of chopped scallions. Madam Luosi naturally wondered how such a broth could make vegetable dumplings taste so delicious. “Xinghua Lou’s stock is nothing like that pot-rinsing water. They simmer chicken, ham, lean pork, and crucian carp over a low flame. It is sold by the liang.” “No wonder!” the Seventh Lady laughed. “If Widow Xu’s dumplings tasted like this by themselves, she would have to be an immortal.” “Ruixiang has gone out of her way to please me,” Madam Luosi said, “but now I cannot recover the taste I remember at all.”

『那末太太尝尝糟钵头，这是陶阿大那里买回来以后，原封没有动过。』螺蛳太太点点头，挟了一块猪肚，细细嚼；同时极力回忆当年吃糟钵头的滋味，可是没有用，味道还不如她家厨子做的来得好。『七姐，你的话不错。我罗四姐，不是当年的罗四姐了。』七姑奶奶默不作声，心里还颇有悔意，刚才的话不应该说得那么率直，惹起她的伤感。

“Then try the casserole, madam. We bought it at Tao A’da’s and have done nothing to it.” Madam Luosi nodded, picked up a piece of tripe, and chewed it carefully, doing her utmost to recall the flavour of the old days. It was no use. This tasted less good than her own cook’s. “Seventh Sister, you were right. I am no longer the Sister Luo I used to be.” The Seventh Lady said nothing. She rather regretted speaking so bluntly and making her friend sad.

瑞香却不知她们打的什么哑谜，瞪圆了一双大眼睛发楞。罗四姐便又说道∶『瑞香，你总要记牢，吃得苦中苦，方为人上人。』瑞香仍旧不明她这话的用意，只好答应一声∶『是。』『话要说回来，人也不是生来就该吃苦的。』七姑奶奶说道∶『有福能享，还是要享。不过——』她觉得有瑞香在旁，话说得太深了也不好，便改口说道∶『就怕身在福中不知福。』『七姐这句话，真正是一针见血。』螺蛳太太说∶『瑞香，你去烫一壶花雕来，我今天想吃酒。』螺蛳太太的酒量很不错，烫了来自斟自饮，喝得很猛；七姑奶奶便提了一句∶『四姐，酒要吃得高兴，慢慢吃。』『不要紧，这一壶酒醉不倒我。』『醉虽醉不倒，会说醉话；你一说醉话，人家就更加不当真的了。』这才真正是哑谜，只有她们两人会意。螺蛳太太想到要跟古应春谈瑞香的事，便听七姑奶奶的劝，浅斟低酌，闲谈着将一壶酒喝完，也不想再添，要了一碗香粳米粥吃完，古应春也回来了。

Ruixiang, who could make nothing of their riddling conversation, stared wide-eyed in bewilderment. “Ruixiang,” Madam Luosi said, “remember this: only those who endure the bitterest hardships can rise above others.” Still unable to understand what she meant, Ruixiang could only answer, “Yes.” “Then again, people are not born simply to suffer,” the Seventh Lady said. “If you have the good fortune to enjoy life, you should enjoy it. Only—” With Ruixiang present, she thought it better not to go too deeply into the subject, and changed her words. “The danger is to live in good fortune without knowing it.” “Seventh Sister, that goes straight to the heart of it. Ruixiang, warm a pot of huadiao wine. I feel like a drink today.” Madam Luosi could hold her wine well. When it came, she poured for herself and drank rapidly. “Sister Luo, wine is for pleasure,” the Seventh Lady reminded her. “Take it slowly.” “Do not worry. One pot will not make me drunk.” “Perhaps not, but you may talk as though you were. And once you do, people will take you even less seriously.” That was a riddle only the two of them understood. Remembering that she had to speak to Gu about Ruixiang, Madam Luosi accepted the warning. She took small, leisurely sips as they talked, finished the pot, and asked for no more. After she had eaten a bowl of fragrant rice porridge, Gu returned.

先是在七姑奶奶卧室中闲话；听到钟打九下，螺蛳太太便即说道∶『七姐只怕要困了；我请姐夫替我写封信。』『好！到我书房里去。』等他们一进书房，瑞香随即将茶端了进来，胡家的规矩，凡是主人家找人写信，下人是不准在旁边的，她还记着这个规矩，所以带上房门，管自己走了。『姐夫，写信是假，跟你来办交涉是真。』『什么事？』古应春说∶『有什么话，四姐交代就是。』『那末，我就直说。姐夫。你把我的瑞香搁在一边，是啥意思。』看她咄咄逼人，看有点办交涉的意味，古应春倒有些窘了。本来就是件不容易表达清楚的事，在这样的情况之下，自然更是讷讷然无法出口。罗四姐原是故意作此姿态，说话比较省力，既占上风，急忙收敛，『姐夫，』她的声音放得柔和而恳切，『你心里到底是啥想法？尽管跟我说；是不是日子一长，看出来瑞香的人品不好。』『不、不！』古应春急急打断，『我如果心里有这样的想法，那就算没良心到家了。』

At first they chatted in the Seventh Lady’s bedroom. When the clock struck nine, Madam Luosi said, “Seventh Sister must be sleepy. Brother-in-law, I should like you to write a letter for me.” “Certainly. Come to my study.” As soon as they entered, Ruixiang brought tea. She remembered the Hu household rule that servants must withdraw when a member of the family asked someone to write a letter, and closed the door behind her. “Brother-in-law, the letter is a pretence. I have really come to settle a matter with you.” “What is it? Whatever you wish, Sister Luo, just tell me.” “Then I shall speak plainly. What do you mean by leaving my Ruixiang to one side?” Faced with this aggressive challenge, Gu was embarrassed. The matter was hard enough to explain at the best of times; under these circumstances he could scarcely find a word. Her manner was deliberate, intended to save argument. Having gained the advantage, she immediately softened her tone. “Brother-in-law, what is really in your mind? Tell me freely. Have you found, after knowing her longer, that Ruixiang’s character is not what it should be?” “No, no!” Gu broke in hastily. “If I thought anything of the kind, I should have no conscience at all.”

『照你说，瑞香你是中意的。』『不但中意┅┅』古应春笑笑没有再说下法。『意思是不但中意，而且交关中意？』『这也是实话。』『即然如此，七姐又巴不得你们早早圆房，你为啥一点都不起劲。姐夫，请你说个道理给我听。』螺蛳太太的调子又拉高了。古应春微微皱眉，不即作答；他最近才有了吸烟的嗜好——不是鸦片是吕宋烟；打开银烟盒，取出一支『老美女』用特制的剪刀剪去烟头，用根『红头火柴』在鞋底上划燃了慢慢点烟。霎时间螺蛳太太只闻到浓郁的烟香，却看不见古应春的脸，因为让烟雾隔断了。

“Then you do like her?” “More than like her…” Gu smiled and left the sentence unfinished. “You mean you do not merely like her—you like her very much?” “That is true too.” “Then, with Seventh Sister longing for you to begin your married life together, why do you show no enthusiasm? Brother-in-law, give me a reason.” Her voice rose again. Gu frowned slightly and did not answer at once. He had recently taken to smoking—not opium, but Manila cigars. Opening his silver case, he took out an Old Beauty, clipped its end with a special cutter, struck a red-headed match against his shoe sole, and slowly lit it. A moment later Madam Luosi could smell the rich tobacco but could no longer see his face through the smoke.

『四姐，』古应春在烟雾中发声∶『讨小纳妾，说实话，是我们男人家人生一乐。既然这样子，就要看境况、看心情，境况不好做这种事，还可以说是苦中作乐；心情不好，就根本谈不到乐趣了。』这个答复，多少是出人意外的；螺蛳太太想了一会说∶『大先生也跟我谈过，说你做房地产受了姓徐的累，不过现在事情已经过去了，心情也应该不同了。』『恰恰相反，事情也应该不同了。』『为啥呢？』『四姐，小爷叔待我，自然没有话说；十万银子，在他也不会计较。不过，在我总是一桩心事，尤其现在市面上的银根极紧；小爷叔不在乎，旁人跟他的想法不一样。』最后这句话，弦外有音，螺蛳太太不但诧异，而且有些气愤，『这旁人是哪一个？』她问∶『旁人的想法，同大先生啥相干？你为啥要去听？』古应春不作声，深深地吸了口烟，管他自己又说∶『小爷叔帮了我这么大一个忙，我想替小爷叔尽心尽力做点事，心里才比较好过。上次好不容易说动小爷叔，收买新式缫丝厂，自己做丝直接销洋庄；哪晓得处处碰钉子，到今朝一事无成。尤五哥心灰意冷，回松江去了。四姐，你说我哪里会有心思来想瑞香的事？』这番话说得非常诚恳，螺蛳太太深为同情；话题亦就自然而然地由瑞香转到新式缫丝厂了。

“Sister Luo,” his voice emerged from the cloud, “taking a concubine is, to speak frankly, one of the pleasures of a man’s life. That being so, it depends on circumstances and on one’s state of mind. If circumstances are bad, one may still find a little pleasure amid one’s troubles. But if one’s heart is troubled, there can be no pleasure at all.” The answer was rather unexpected. Madam Luosi considered it. “The Master told me that you were dragged into trouble over property by that man Xu. But it is over now. Surely your spirits ought to be different?” “Quite the contrary. Things are different now, too.” “Why?” “Little Uncle could not have treated me better. He would never begrudge me a hundred thousand taels. But it weighs on my mind, especially with money so tight in the market. Little Uncle does not mind; other people do not think as he does.” The implication in his last words made Madam Luosi both astonished and indignant. “Who are these other people? What have their opinions to do with the Master? Why should you listen to them?” Gu did not reply. He drew deeply on his cigar and continued with his own thought. “Little Uncle has helped me so much that I can feel easier only if I do something for him with all the ability and devotion I possess. Last time, after a great deal of effort, I persuaded him to buy modern silk-reeling mills, produce his own silk, and sell directly to the foreign firms. Who could have expected obstacles at every turn? To this day I have achieved nothing. Brother You has lost heart and gone back to Songjiang. Sister Luo, how can I be in the mood to think about Ruixiang?” His sincerity deeply moved her, and their conversation naturally shifted from Ruixiang to the mills.

『当初不是筹划得好好的？』她问∶『处处碰钉子是啥缘故；碰的是啥个钉子？』『一言难尽。』古应春摇摇头，不愿深谈。螺蛳太太旁敲侧击，始终不能让古应春将他的难言之隐吐露出来。以致于螺蛳太太都有些动气了。但正当要说两句埋怨的话时，灵机一动想到了一个激将法。『姐夫，你尽管跟我说，我回去决不会搬弄是非；只会在大先生面前替你说话。』一听这话，古应春大为不安。如果仍旧不肯说，无异表示真的怕她回去『搬弄是非』。同时听她的语气，似乎疑心他处置不善，甚至怀有私心，以致『一事无成』。这份无端而起的误会，亦不甘默然承受。于是，古应春抑制激动的心情，考虑了一会答说∶『四姐，我本来是「打落牙齿和血吞」，有委屈自己受。现在看样子是非说不可了！不过，四姐，有句话，我先要声明，我决没有疑心四姐会在小爷叔面前搬弄是非的意思。』『我晓得，我晓得。』螺蛳太太得意地笑道∶『我不是这样子逼一逼，哪里会把你的话逼出来？』

“Was not everything carefully planned at the start? Why these obstacles at every turn? What sort of obstacles?” “It is a long story.” Gu shook his head, unwilling to go further. She tried approaching him from several directions but could not draw out what troubled him. She was beginning to feel annoyed and was about to reproach him when an idea occurred to her: she would provoke him into speaking. “Brother-in-law, tell me freely. When I go home I shall certainly not make mischief. I shall only put in a good word for you with the Master.” This made Gu extremely uneasy. To refuse further would be virtually to admit that he feared she would “make mischief.” Her tone also suggested that she suspected him of mismanagement, or even of pursuing some private interest that had left him with nothing accomplished. He was unwilling to accept such an unfounded misunderstanding in silence. Suppressing his agitation, he considered for a moment and said, “Sister Luo, I meant to swallow my broken teeth with the blood—to bear my grievance myself. Now it seems I must speak. But first let me make one thing clear: I have never suspected you of making mischief between Little Uncle and me.” “I know, I know.” She smiled triumphantly. “If I had not pressed you like that, how could I ever have got it out of you?”

听得这话，古应春才知道上当了∶『我说是说。不过，』他说∶『现在好象是我在搬弄是非了。』『姐夫，』螺蛳太太正色说道∶『我不是不识轻重的人。你告诉我的话，哪些能说，哪些不能说，我当然也会想一想。为了避嫌疑不肯说实话，就不是自己人了。』最后这句话，隐然有着责备的意思，使得古应春更觉得该据实倾诉∶『说起来也不能怪老宓，他有他的难处——』『是他！』螺蛳太太插进去说，『我刚就有点疑心，说闲话的旁人，只怕是他，果不其然。他在阜康怎么样。』『他在阜康有情形我不清楚，我只谈我自己。我也弄不懂是什么地方得罪了老宓，有点处处跟我为难的味道。』原来，收买新式缫丝厂一事，所以未成，即由于宓本常明处掣肘、暗处破坏之故。他放了风声出去，说胡雪岩并无意办新式缫丝厂，是古应春在做房地产的生意上扯了一个大窟窿，所以买空卖空，希图无中生有，来弥补他的亏空。如果有缫丝厂想出让，最好另找主顾；否则到头来一场空，自误时机。

Only then did Gu realize that he had been caught. “I shall tell you. But now it feels as though I am the one making mischief.” “Brother-in-law,” she said gravely, “I do understand what matters. Of course I shall consider what may be repeated and what must be kept to myself. If you refuse to tell me the truth merely to avoid suspicion, you are not treating me as one of your own.” There was an undertone of reproach in her final words, and Gu felt all the more obliged to speak frankly. “Strictly speaking, we cannot blame Old Mi altogether. He has his own difficulties—” “So it is he!” she interrupted. “I began to suspect that the other person making remarks might be him. And sure enough! How is he doing at Fukang?” “I know nothing about his affairs at Fukang. I can speak only of my own. I cannot understand where I have offended Old Mi, but he seems bent on making things difficult for me at every turn.” The reason the purchase of the modern mills had failed to go through was that Mi Benchang was obstructing it openly and sabotaging it in secret. He had spread the word that Hu Xueyan had no intention of running such mills. Gu, he said, had made a tremendous loss in property and was now speculating in purchases and sales he could not finance, hoping to conjure up money to fill the hole. Any mill owner wishing to sell would do better to find another buyer; otherwise he would end up with nothing and miss his opportunity through his own mistake.

这话使人将信将疑，信的是古应春在上海商场上不是无名小卒，信用也很好。只看他跟徐愚斋合作失败，而居然能安然无事，便见得他不是等闲之辈了。疑的是，古应春的境况确实不佳；而更使人觉得不可思议的是，胡雪岩一向反对新式缫丝，何以忽然改弦易辙？大家都知道，胡雪岩看重的一件事是∶说话算话。大家都想不起来，他做过什么出尔反尔的事。因为如此，古应春跟人家谈判，便很吃力了，因为对方是抱着虚与委蛇的态度。当然只要没有明显的决裂的理由，尽管谈判吃力，总还要谈下去，而且迟早会谈出一个初步的结果。

These reports left people divided between belief and doubt. In Gu’s favour was the fact that he was no obscure figure in Shanghai business, and his credit was good. That he had emerged unscathed from his failed partnership with Xu Yuzhai showed that he was a man of more than ordinary resources. Against him stood the fact that his circumstances were indeed poor. Still harder to understand was why Hu Xueyan, who had always opposed modern silk-reeling, should abruptly change course. Everyone knew how highly Hu valued keeping his word. No one could recall an occasion on which he had gone back on it. Gu’s negotiations consequently became laborious: the other side was merely humouring him. Nevertheless, so long as there was no clear reason for an outright rupture, he had to continue, however difficult the talks, and sooner or later they were bound to yield some preliminary result.

其时古应春谈判的目标是公和永的东主黄佐卿。他跟怡和、公平两洋行，同时建厂，规模大小相仿，都有上百部的丝车，买的是意大利跟法国的丝车；公平洋行的买办叫刘和甫，提议三厂共同延请一名工程师，黄佐卿同意了，由刘和甫经手，聘请了一个意大利人麦登斯来指导厂务、训练工人，此人技术不错，可是人品甚坏，最大的毛病是好色。原来那时的工人，以女工居多，称之为『湖丝阿姐』。小家碧玉为了帮助家计，大多以帮佣为主；做工是领了材料到家来做，旧式的如绣花、糊锡箔；新式的如糊火柴匣子、缝军服。但做『湖丝阿姐』，汽笛一响，成群结队，招摇而过，却是前所未有，因而看湖丝阿姐上工、放工，成了一景。这些年轻妇女，抛头露面惯了，行动言语之间，自然开通得多；而放荡与开通不过上下床之别，久而久之便常有荡检逾闲的情事出现；至于男工，『近水楼台先得月』，尤其是『小寡妇』，搭上手的很多。当然这是『互惠』的，女工有个男工作靠山，就不会受人欺侮；倘或靠山是个工头，好处更多，起码可以调到工作轻松的部门。相对的，工头倘或所欲不遂，便可假公济私来作报复，调到最苦的缫丝间，沸水热汽，终年如盛暑；盛暑偶尔还有风，缫丝间又热又闷，一进去要不了一顿饭的工夫，浑身就会湿透，男工可以打赤膊，着短裤，女工就只好着一件『湿布衫』，机器一开就是十二个钟头，这件火热的『湿布衫』就得穿一整天。夏天还好，冬天散工，冷风一吹，『湿布衫』变成『铁衣』，因而致病，不足为奇，所以有个洋记者参观过缫丝间以后，称之为『名副其实的活地狱』。

At this time Gu was negotiating with Huang Zuoqing, the owner of Gongheyong. Huang had built his mill at the same time as Jardine Matheson and Gongping Trading House had built theirs. All three were of much the same size, with over a hundred reeling machines apiece, bought from Italy and France. Liu Hefu, Gongping’s comprador, proposed that the three jointly engage an engineer. Huang agreed, and Liu hired an Italian named Maidengsi to supervise operations and train the workers. He was technically capable but a man of very bad character; his chief failing was his appetite for women. Most mill hands then were women, known as the Silk Sisters. Women from modest families who needed to help support their households had generally gone into domestic service. Those who did paid piecework took the materials home: embroidery and the pasting of tinfoil were older trades, while making matchboxes and sewing uniforms were newer ones. But the Silk Sisters, gathering in groups at the sound of the factory whistle and making their conspicuous way through the streets, were something wholly new. Watching them go to and from work became a public spectacle. These young women grew accustomed to appearing in public and naturally became much freer in speech and manner. The distinction between an open manner and loose morals, however, was little more than a matter of getting into bed; in time, breaches of convention became common. The male workers, being nearest at hand, enjoyed the first opportunities, and many formed liaisons, particularly with young widows. There were benefits on both sides, of course. A woman with a male worker to support her would not be bullied; if her protector was a foreman, the advantages were greater still. At the least, she might be transferred to an easier department. Conversely, a foreman whose wishes were denied could use his official authority to avenge a private grievance, assigning a woman to the worst work of all in the reeling room. There boiling water and steam created a perpetual midsummer. Even midsummer had an occasional breeze; the reeling room was both hot and airless. Before one had been inside for the time it took to eat a meal, one was drenched from head to foot. Men could strip to the waist and wear shorts; women had to remain in their soaking clothes. Once the machinery started, it ran for twelve hours, and they had to wear those scorching, wet garments all day. Summer was bearable enough. In winter, when the shift ended and the cold wind struck, the wet clothing became an iron shell. That this should cause illness was hardly surprising. After visiting a reeling room, one foreign journalist called it “a veritable hell on earth.”

工头如此，工程师自然更可作威作福，麦登斯便视蹂躏湖丝阿姐为他应享的权利，利用不肖工头，予取予求，黄佐卿时常接到申诉，要求刘和甫警告麦登斯，稍为好几天，很快地复萌故态，如是几次以后，黄佐卿忍无可忍，打算解雇麦登斯，哪知刘和甫跟人家订了一张非常吃亏的合约，倘或解雇须付出巨额的赔偿。为此黄佐卿大为沮丧，加以生意又不好做，才决定将公和永盘让给古应春。

If a foreman could wield such power, an engineer could lord it over the workers even more easily. Maidengsi treated the sexual exploitation of the Silk Sisters as his rightful privilege, using unscrupulous foremen to satisfy his demands. Huang Zuoqing frequently received complaints and asked Liu Hefu to warn him. He would improve for a few days, then soon revert to his old behaviour. After several repetitions, Huang could bear it no longer and decided to dismiss him. He then discovered that Liu had signed a deeply disadvantageous contract: dismissal would require a huge payment in compensation. Huang was thoroughly discouraged. With business poor as well, he finally decided to sell Gongheyong to Gu Yingchun.

条件都谈好了，厂房、生财、存货八万银子『一脚踢』。古应春便通知宓本常，照数开出银票；哪知所得的回答是∶『不便照拨。』『怎么？』古应春诧异，『不是有「的款」存在那里的吗？』当初汇丰借出来的五十万银子，除了左宗棠所借的二十万以外，余数由胡雪岩指明，借给尤五出面所办的茧行，作为收买新式缫丝厂之用，这一点宓本常并不否认，但他有他的说法。

All the terms had been settled: the buildings, equipment, and stock were to go for a lump sum of eighty thousand taels. Gu Yingchun therefore notified Mi Benchang to issue a bank draft for the full amount. The answer, to his astonishment, was that the funds could not be released. “What?” he asked. “Was there not a sum specifically set aside for this?” Of the original five hundred thousand taels borrowed from HSBC, two hundred thousand had been lent to Zuo Zongtang. Hu Xueyan had expressly designated the remainder as a loan to the cocoon firm headed by Brother You, to finance the purchase of modern silk-reeling mills. Mi did not deny this, but he had his own explanation.

『应春兄，「死店活人开」，大先生是有那样子一句话，不过我做档手的，如果只会听他的话，象算盘珠一样，他拨一拨、我动一动，我就不是活人，只不过比死人多口气。你说是不是呢？』古应春倒抽一口冷气，结结巴巴说∶『你的话不错，大先生的话也要算数。』『我不是说不算数，是现在没有钱，有，钱又不是我的，我为啥不给你。』『这钱怎么会没有？指明了做这个用途的。』『不错，指明了作这个用途的。不过，应春兄，你要替我想一想，更要替大先生想一想。几次谈到缫丝厂的事，你总说「难，难，不晓得啥辰光才会成功？」如果你说∶快谈成功了，十天半个月就要付款，我自然会把你这笔款子留下来。你自己都没有握，怎么能怪我？』『你不必管我有没有把握，指明了给我的，你就要留下来。』

“Brother Yingchun, a shop may be dead, but the man who runs it must be alive. The Master did say that, certainly. But if, as his manager, I can do nothing except obey him—if I am like a bead on an abacus, moving only when he pushes me—then I am hardly a living man. I have only one breath more than a corpse. Would you not agree?” Gu drew in a sharp breath. “You—you are right,” he stammered, “but the Master’s word must count for something too.” “I am not saying it counts for nothing. I am saying there is no money at present. If there were, why should I withhold it? It is not mine.” “How can the money be gone? It was expressly assigned to this purpose.” “Quite true. But you must consider my position, Brother Yingchun—and the Master’s even more. Every time we discussed the silk mills, you said, ‘Difficult, difficult! Who knows when anything will come of it?’ If you had told me, ‘The agreement is almost settled; payment will be due in ten days or a fortnight,’ naturally I should have kept the money for you. When you yourself had no certainty, how can you blame me?” “My certainty is no concern of yours. Money expressly assigned to me ought to have been kept for me.”

这话很不客气；宓本常冷笑一声说道∶『如果那时候你请大先生马上交代，照数拨给你，另外立个折子，算是你的存款，我就没有资格用你这笑钱。没有归到你名下以前，钱是阜康的。阜康的钱是大先生所有；不过阜康的钱归我宓某所管。受人之禄，忠人之事，银根这么紧，我不把这笔钱拿来活用；只为远在杭州的大先生的一句话，把这笔钱死死守住，等你不知道哪天来用，你说有没有这个道理？』这几句话真是将古应春驳得体无完肤，他不能跟他辩，也不想跟他辩了。

This was distinctly brusque. Mi gave a cold laugh. “Had you asked the Master then and there to order the sum transferred, with a separate passbook opened so that it became your deposit, I should have had no right to use it. Until it was entered in your name, however, the money belonged to Fukang. Fukang’s money belongs to the Master, but its management belongs to me. A man who takes another’s pay must serve his interests faithfully. With credit as tight as it is, am I to leave that money idle merely because of a word from the Master, far away in Hangzhou, and guard it until some unknown day when you may want it? Would that make sense?” These arguments left Gu without a leg to stand on. He could not argue further and no longer wished to.

可是宓本常却还有话∶『你晓得的，大先生的生意愈做愈大，就是因为一个钱要做八个钱、十个钱的生意。大先生常常说∶』八个坛子七个盖，盖来盖去不穿帮，就是会做生意。「以现在市面上的现款来说，岂止八个坛子七个盖？顶多只有一半，我要把他搞得不穿帮，哪里是件容易的事。老兄，我请问你，今天有人来提款，库房里只有那二十几万银子，我不拿来应付，莫非跟客户说∶那笔银子不能动，是为古先生留在那里收买缫丝厂用的？古先生啊古先生，我老宓跟你，到那时候，不要说本来就是阜康的钱，哪怕是两江总督衙门的官款，明天要提了去给兄弟们关饷，我都要动用。客户这一关过不去，马上就有挤兑的风潮，大先生就完完大吉了。『』四姐，老宓的说法，只要是真的，就算不肯帮我忙，我亦没话说。因为虽然都是为小爷叔办事，各有各的权限，各有各的难处，我不能怪他。『』那末，『螺蛳太太立即钉一句∶』你现在是怪他罗？『古应春老实答道∶』是的。有一点。『』这样说起来，是老宓没有说真话！不然你就不会怪他。『螺蛳太太问道∶』他那几句话不真？『

Mi, however, had more to say. “You know how the Master’s business keeps expanding: he makes one coin do the business of eight or ten. He is always saying, ‘Eight jars and seven lids—keep moving the lids without letting anyone see an uncovered jar, and you know how to do business.’ With the cash available in the market today, we have nothing like seven lids for eight jars. At most we have half as many lids as jars. Do you think it is easy to keep everything covered? Let me ask you this, my dear fellow. If a depositor came to withdraw his money today, and the only cash in the vault was that two hundred thousand taels and more, should I refuse to use it? Should I tell him, ‘That money cannot be touched; it is being kept for Mr Gu to buy silk mills’? My dear Mr Gu, at such a moment I tell you I would use not merely Fukang’s own money, but even official funds belonging to the Governor-General of Liangjiang, due to be withdrawn tomorrow to pay the troops. Fail to satisfy our customers and we shall have a run on the bank at once. That would finish the Master.” “Sister Luo,” Gu went on with his account, “if what Old Mi said was true, then even if he would not help me, I had nothing to complain of. We both work for Little Uncle, but each of us has his own authority and his own difficulties. I could not blame him.” “Then,” she immediately pressed him, “you do blame him now?” “Yes,” Gu admitted frankly. “A little.” “In that case Old Mi cannot have been telling the truth, or you would not blame him. Which part of what he said was untrue?”

『还不是头寸。』话到此处，古应春如箭在弦，不发不可，『他头寸是调得过来的，而且指定了收买缫丝厂的那笔款子，根本没有动，仍旧在汇丰银行。』一听这话，螺蛳太太动容了，『姐夫，』她问，『你怎么知道他没有动过？』『我听人说的。』『是哪个？』『这——』古应春答说∶『四姐，你不必问了。我的消息很靠得住。』螺蛳太太有些明白了，阜康管总帐的周小棠，跟宓本常不甚和睦，也许是他透露的消息。

“The part about the shortage of funds, of course.” Gu had now drawn the bow and could no longer hold back the arrow. “He could have arranged the funds. Moreover, the money earmarked for purchasing the mills has never been touched. It is still at HSBC.” Madam Luosi’s expression changed. “Brother-in-law, how do you know he has not touched it?” “Someone told me.” “Who?” “Well—Sister Luo, please do not ask. My information is thoroughly reliable.” She thought she understood. Zhou Xiaotang, who kept Fukang’s general accounts, was not on particularly good terms with Mi Benchang. Perhaps he had let the information out.

『姐夫要我不问，我就不问。不过我倒要问姐夫，这件事现在怎么办？』『收买缫丝厂的事，已经不必再谈了。现在就有八万银子，也买不成功；人家黄佐卿看我拿不出现银，另外寻了个户头，卖了九万五千银子。』古应春说到这里，摇一摇头，脸色非常难看，『四姐，我顶难过的是，在上海滩上混了几十年，听了一句教人要吐血的话。』『噢！』螺蛳太太大为同情，『你说了出来，我来替你出气。』『出气？』古应春连连摇头，『那一来变成「窝里反」了，不好「不好。』『就算我不响，你也要说出来；心里有委屈，说出来就舒服。』古应春沉吟了说∶『好，我说。那天——』

“If you would rather I did not ask, I shall not. But there is something else I must ask: what are you going to do now?” “There is no longer any point in discussing the purchase. Even with eighty thousand taels in hand, I could not buy the mill now. When Huang Zuoqing saw that I could not produce the cash, he found another buyer and sold it for ninety-five thousand.” Gu shook his head, his face dark with distress. “What hurts me most, Sister Luo, is that after all my decades in Shanghai, I had to hear something that could make a man spit blood.” “Oh!” Her sympathy was immediate. “Tell me what he said. I shall get satisfaction for you.” “Satisfaction?” Gu shook his head repeatedly. “That would mean fighting among ourselves. No, no, that would never do.” “Even if I say nothing about it, you must tell me. Speaking about an injustice makes it easier to bear.” Gu hesitated. “Very well. I shall tell you. That day—”

那天——螺蛳太太到上海的前两天，黄佐卿发了个帖子请古应春吃花酒。买卖不成，朋友还是朋友，古应春准时赴约；场面很热闹，黄佐卿请了有近二十位的客，两桌麻将，一桌牌九，打了上千大洋的头。接下来吃花酒，摆的是『双双台』；客人连叫来的局，不下五十人之多，须将整楼三个大房间打通，才摆得下四桌酒。

That day—two days before Madam Luosi arrived in Shanghai—Huang Zuoqing had sent Gu an invitation to a banquet with courtesans. Though their business had fallen through, they were still friends, and Gu arrived punctually. It was a lively gathering, with nearly twenty guests. Two tables of mahjong and one of pai gow yielded over a thousand silver dollars in table fees. The banquet that followed was arranged with courtesans seated alongside the guests. Counting the women summoned to join them, there were at least fifty people. The partitions between three large upstairs rooms had to be opened before there was room for four dining tables.

主客便是收买公和永的潮州帮『鸦片大王』陈和森；古应春也被邀在这一桌坐。笙歌嗷嘈之余，黄佐卿举杯向古应春说道∶『应春兄，我特为要敬你一杯酒；如果十天之前不是你头寸不便，我就不会跟「陈大王」谈公和永，也就少卖一万五千银子了。说起来这一万五千两，是你老哥挑我赚的，我是不是应该敬杯酒。』说完哈哈大笑，管自己干了酒。讲完了这一段，古应春又说∶『四姐，你想，这不是他存心给我难堪？当时，我真正是眼泪往肚子里流。』螺蛳太太亦为他难过，更为他不平，『这件事，大先生晓不晓得？』她问。

The guest of honour was the purchaser of Gongheyong, Chen Hesen, the Chaozhou merchants’ “Opium King.” Gu was seated at the same table. Amid the clamour of music and singing, Huang raised his cup to him. “Brother Yingchun, I particularly want to drink to you. Had you not been short of funds ten days ago, I should never have discussed Gongheyong with King Chen, and I should have received fifteen thousand taels less. In a manner of speaking, it was you who helped me make that extra fifteen thousand. Ought I not to drink to you?” He laughed heartily and drained his cup. Having related this, Gu added, “Sister Luo, was he not deliberately trying to humiliate me? I truly had to swallow my tears.” She grieved for him and felt the injustice even more keenly. “Does the Master know about this?” she asked.

『这件事，我怎么好告诉大先生？不过收买公和永不成这一节，我已经写信给大先生了。』『我在杭州没有听说。』古应春想了一下说∶『算起来你从杭州动身的时候，我的信还没有到。』

“How could I bring myself to tell him such a thing? But I have written to him about the failure to buy Gongheyong.” “I heard nothing of it in Hangzhou.” Gu considered a moment. “My letter probably had not arrived when you left.”

『好！这一节就不去谈它了。至于老宓勒住银不放，有意跟你作对，这件事我一定要问问他。』『不！』古应春说∶『请四姐一定要顾大局，现在局势不大好，全靠大家同心协力，你一问他，必生是非，无论如何请你摆在心里。』『你晓得的，我也同七姐一样，有不平的事，摆在心里，饭都吃不下的。』螺蛳太太说∶『我只要不「卖原告」，他哪里知道我的消息是哪里来的。

“Very well, we shall say no more about that. But as for Old Mi holding back the money and deliberately obstructing you, I must certainly question him.” “No!” Gu exclaimed. “Please think of the larger situation, Sister Luo. Things are difficult at present; everything depends on our working together. If you question him, it is bound to stir up trouble. Whatever you do, please keep this to yourself.” “You know I am like Seventh Sister. If I keep an injustice bottled up, I cannot even eat. So long as I do not give away the complainant, how can he know where my information came from?”

看她态度非常坚决，古应春知道无法打消她的意向；考虑了一会说∶『四姐，你以为不提我的名字，他就不会疑心到我，那是自己骗自己。你总要有个合情理的说法，才可以瞒得过他。』『你讲，应该怎么个说法？』『在汇丰银行，你有没有认识的人？』螺蛳太太想了一下说道∶『有个张纪通，好象是汇丰银行的。』『不错，张纪通是汇丰银行「二写」。』古应春问∶『四姐跟他熟？』『他太太，我们从前是小姊妹。去年还特为到杭州来看过我。』『好！那就有说法了。四姐，你如果一定问这件事，见了老宓就这样子说∶你说，古应春告诉我，阜康的头寸紧得不得了；可是，我听张政通的太太说∶阜康有廿几万银子，一直存在汇丰没有动过。看他怎么说？』

Seeing her determination, Gu knew he could not dissuade her. After some thought he said, “Sister Luo, if you think that merely leaving out my name will keep him from suspecting me, you are deceiving yourself. You need a plausible explanation if you are to put him off the scent.” “Tell me, then. What explanation should I give?” “Do you know anyone at HSBC?” She thought for a moment. “There is a Zhang Jitong. I believe he works there.” “Yes, he is HSBC’s second clerk. Do you know him well?” “His wife and I were friends as girls. She made a special trip to Hangzhou to see me last year.” “Good. Then you have your explanation. If you insist on questioning Old Mi, say this when you see him: ‘Gu Yingchun tells me that Fukang is desperately short of funds. Yet I hear from Mrs Zhang Jitong that Fukang has more than two hundred thousand taels sitting untouched at HSBC.’ See what he says to that.”

『我懂了，我会说得一点不露马脚；明天早晨我先去看张太太，做得象真的一样。我看他一定没话可说；那时候我再埋怨他几句，替你出气。』『出气这两个字，不必谈它。』『好，不谈出气，谈你圆房。』螺蛳太太急转直下地说∶『这件事就算不为你，也不为瑞香，为了七姐，你也要趁我在这里，请我吃这杯喜酒。』古应春终于答应了。于是螺蛳太太便将与七姑奶奶商量好的计划，一一说知；事到如今，古应春除了唯唯称是以外，别无话说。

“I understand. I shall give nothing away. Tomorrow morning I shall first call on Mrs Zhang, to make the story look genuine. I am sure he will have no answer. Then I can reproach him a little and get satisfaction for you.” “Please leave satisfaction out of it.” “Very well. Let us leave that and discuss your wedding night.” She changed the subject with startling abruptness. “Even if you will do nothing for your own sake or Ruixiang’s, you must do this for Seventh Sister: hold the wedding while I am here, so that I may drink a cup at your celebration.” Gu finally agreed. Madam Luosi then explained, point by point, the arrangements she had settled with the Seventh Lady. Matters had gone so far that Gu could only murmur his assent.

第二天早饭既毕，螺蛳太太便催瑞香瑞得出门。这是前一天晚上就说好了；但瑞香因为一出门便是一整天，有好些琐屑家务要安排好，因而耽误了工夫，七姑奶奶帮着一催再催，快到不耐烦时，方始相偕登车，看表上已经十一点了。『刚刚当着七姑奶奶，我不好说，我催你是有道理的，先要到张太太家去一趟，稍为坐一坐到阜康去开银票。现在，辰光不对了，吃中饭的时候去了，一定留住；下半天等去了阜康，就办不成事了。看首饰不能心急；不然十之八九要后悔。现在，没法子，张家只好不去了。』『都是我不好。』瑞香陪笑说道∶『太太何不早跟我说一句。』

The next morning, as soon as breakfast was over, Madam Luosi urged Ruixiang to get ready to go out. This had been agreed the previous evening. But since they would be away all day, Ruixiang had a number of small household matters to arrange, and these delayed her. The Seventh Lady joined in urging her to hurry. They were almost out of patience before the two women finally entered the carriage together; a glance at the watch showed that it was already eleven. “I could not explain in front of the Seventh Lady just now, but I had a reason for hurrying you. We were to call briefly on Mrs Zhang, then go to Fukang to get the bank drafts. The timing is all wrong now. If we arrive at lunchtime, she is sure to keep us for the meal. By the time we have been to Fukang in the afternoon, we shall get nothing done. One must take one’s time choosing jewellery, or nine times out of ten one regrets it. We shall have to miss the Zhangs.” “It is all my fault,” Ruixiang said with an apologetic smile. “Why did you not tell me earlier, Madam?”

『我也不晓得你这么会磨！摸东摸西，忘记掉辰光。喔！』螺蛳太太特为关照∶『回头我同宓先生说，我们是从张家来，你不要多说什么，免得拆穿西洋镜。』瑞香答应着，随同螺蛳太太坐轿子到了阜康；宓本常自然奉如上宾，他的礼貌很周到，从胡老太太起，胡家全家，——问到。接下来又敷衍瑞香，笑嘻嘻地问道∶『瑞姑娘，哪天请我们吃喜酒？』

“I did not know you could dawdle so! Fiddling with this and that until you forget the time. Oh—and remember: when I tell Mr Mi that we have come from the Zhangs, do not say anything that will give the game away.” Ruixiang agreed and accompanied her by sedan chair to Fukang. Mi Benchang naturally treated Madam Luosi as an honoured guest. His courtesies were meticulous: beginning with Old Madam Hu, he asked after every member of the family in turn. He then turned his attentions to Ruixiang and asked with a broad smile, “Miss Rui, when are you going to invite us to your wedding feast?”

瑞香红着脸不答；螺蛳太太接口∶『快了，快了！』她说∶『今天就是为此到钱庄来的，我想支两千银子，七姑奶奶也有个折子在这。』取出七姑奶奶的折子来一看，存银四千五百余两，螺蛳太太作主，也提二千，一共是四千银子，关照宓本常开出数目大小不等的十来张银票，点收清楚，要谈古应春的事了。『宓先生，』她闲闲问说∶『这一晌，上海市面怎么样？』『不好，不好！银根愈来愈紧了。』『我们阜康呢？』『当然也紧。』

Ruixiang blushed and said nothing. “Soon, soon!” Madam Luosi answered for her. “That is why we have come to the bank today. I should like to withdraw two thousand taels. I also have the Seventh Lady’s passbook here.” She took it out and found a balance of more than four thousand five hundred taels. On her own authority, she withdrew another two thousand from that account, making four thousand in all, and asked Mi to issue a dozen or so bank drafts in assorted amounts. Once she had counted and received them, she was ready to raise Gu’s affairs. “Mr Mi,” she asked casually, “how has business been in Shanghai lately?” “Bad, bad! Money grows tighter every day.” “And at our Fukang?” “Tight here too, of course.”

『既然紧，』螺蛳太太摆出一脸困惑的神情，『为啥我们有廿几万银子摆在汇丰银行，动都不动？』一听这话，宓本常心里一跳；正在难于作答时，不道螺蛳太太又添了一句话，让松了口气。『这笔款子是不是汇丰借出来的？』『是的。』

“If money is tight,” she said with a look of bewilderment, “why do we leave more than two hundred thousand taels at HSBC without touching it?” Mi’s heart gave a jolt. He was struggling for an answer when she unexpectedly added a question that allowed him to breathe again. “Is this the money borrowed from HSBC?” “Yes.”

『汇丰借出来的款子，当然要出利息；存在汇丰虽也有利息，不过一定放款利息高，存款利息低，是不是？』『是的。』『借他的钱又存在他那里，白贴利息的差额；宓先生，这把算盘是怎么打的，我倒不太懂了。』这时宓本常已经想好了一个很巧的理由，可以搪塞；因而好整以暇地答说∶『罗四太太，这里头学问很大，不是我吹，其中的诀窍是我跟了大先生十几年才摸出来的。我们先吃饭，等我慢慢讲给罗四太太你听。』已是午饭辰光，而且宓本常已有预备，螺蛳太太也就不客气了。不过既无堂客相陪，而瑞香的身分不同，不肯与螺蛳太太同桌，却颇费安排；最后是分了两样菜让瑞香在另一处吃，密本常陪螺蛳太太一面吃、一面谈。

“Naturally, interest must be paid on money borrowed from HSBC. A deposit there earns interest too, but surely the rate they charge on a loan is higher than the rate they pay on a deposit?” “Yes.” “Then we borrow their money and leave it with them, paying the difference in interest for nothing. Mr Mi, I cannot quite follow the reckoning.” By now Mi had thought of an ingenious excuse with which to fend her off. At his leisure, he replied, “Madam Luo, there is a great deal to be learned here. I am not boasting: I discovered the secret only after working for the Master for more than ten years. Let us eat first, and I shall explain it to you slowly.” It was lunchtime, and Mi had already made preparations, so she accepted readily. Seating them posed some difficulty, however. There was no lady of the household to accompany her, while Ruixiang, conscious of their difference in status, would not sit at the same table. In the end two dishes were set aside for Ruixiang to eat elsewhere, and Mi kept Madam Luosi company over lunch and conversation.

『罗四太太，阜康有款子存在汇丰，想来是应春告诉你的？』『不是。』螺蛳太太从从容容地答说∶『今天去看一个张太太，他们老爷也在汇丰，是她告诉我的。』『呃，是弓长张，还是立早章？』『弓长张。』『那末是张纪通？』『对的，他们老爷叫张纪通。』

“Madam Luo, I imagine it was Yingchun who told you that Fukang had money deposited at HSBC?” “No,” she answered composedly. “I called on a Mrs Zhang today. Her husband works at HSBC too. She told me.” “Ah, is that Zhang written with ‘bow’ and ‘long,’ or Zhang written with ‘stand’ and ‘early’?” “The one with ‘bow’ and ‘long.’” “Then it must be Zhang Jitong?” “Yes. Her husband’s name is Zhang Jitong.”

宓本常心想，螺蛳太太明明是撒谎。张纪通跟他也是朋友，前一天还在一起打牌；打到深夜一点钟，张纪通大输家，『扳轿杠』一定要再打四圈。当时就有人说∶『老张，你向来一到十二点，一定要回去的。今天夜不归营，不怕张大嫂罚你跪算珠珠、顶马桶盖。』原来张纪通惧内，所以这样打趣他；哪知他拍一拍胸脯说∶『放心，放心，雌老虎前天回常熟娘家，去吃她侄儿的喜酒去了。』这是所谓『欲盖弥彰』，愈发可以证实，汇丰存款的消息是古应春所泄露。不过他绝不说破，相反地，在脸上表现了对古应春抱歉的神态。

Mi knew perfectly well that she was lying. Zhang Jitong was a friend of his; they had played cards together only the previous evening. At one in the morning, Zhang, the heaviest loser, had insisted on another four rounds to try to recover his money. Someone had teased him: “Old Zhang, you always go home on the stroke of twelve. If you stay out all night today, are you not afraid your wife will make you kneel on abacus beads and balance a chamber-pot lid on your head?” Zhang was afraid of his wife, hence the joke. But he had slapped his chest and declared, “No fear, no fear! The tigress went back to her family in Changshu the day before yesterday, for her nephew’s wedding.” Here was an attempt at concealment that only made the truth more conspicuous. It gave Mi further confirmation that Gu had disclosed the HSBC deposit. He said nothing to expose the lie, however. Instead, he assumed an expression of regret towards Gu.

『螺蛳太太，阜康的存款、放款都有帐可查的，存在汇丰的这笔款子当然也有帐；不过每个月倒贴的利息，在帐上看不出是亏损。啥道理呢？这笔利息的差额是一厘半，算起来每个月大概要贴四百两银子，我是打开销里面，算正当支出。』说到这里他停了下来，看螺蛳太太的表情。她当然是面现惊异之色，『是正当开支？』她问，仿佛自己听错了似的。『如果她声色不动，宓本常便不能确定，她是不是把他的话听了进去；而惊讶却是正常的，他就更有把握能将她的疑团消除了。

“Madam Luosi, all Fukang’s deposits and loans are recorded in the books, including this deposit at HSBC. But the extra interest we pay each month does not appear as a loss. Why? The difference in the rates is 0.15 percent a month, which costs us about four hundred taels monthly. I enter it among our expenses, as a legitimate operating cost.” He paused to observe her expression. Naturally, she looked astonished. “A legitimate operating cost?” she asked, as if she must have misheard. Had her expression remained unchanged, Mi could not have been sure she had taken in his words. Surprise was the natural response and increased his confidence that he could dispel her doubts.

『不错，是正当开支，好比逢年过节要应酬官场一样，是必不可少的正当开支。』他说∶『螺蛳太太，你晓得的，阜康全靠公家同大户的存款，阜康的利息比人家低，为啥愿意存阜康，就因为可靠如果有人存点疑惑怕靠不住，来提存款，一个两个不要紧，人一多，消息一传，那个风潮一闹开来，螺蛳太太我就只有一条路好走。』『喔！哪一条路？』

“Exactly. A legitimate expense—as necessary as the money spent maintaining relations with officials at New Year and the other festivals. You know, Madam Luosi, that Fukang depends entirely on deposits from public offices and wealthy households. We pay less interest than other banks. Why do people still put their money with us? Because we are dependable. If someone begins to doubt that and comes to withdraw his deposit, one or two withdrawals do no harm. But if the numbers grow, word spreads, and a run begins, there will be only one road left for me.” “Oh? What road is that?”

『死路。不是一条绳子，就是三钱鸦片烟。』宓本常说∶『我只有来生报答大先生了。』螺蛳太太再精明，也不能不为宓本常蓄意表示尽忠负责的神态所感动，『宓先生，你不要这么说！只要你实心实力，一定不会没有好结果。』她说∶『你的忠心，大先生晓得的。』『就为了大先生得罪了人也值得。』宓本常马上又将话拉回来，『螺蛳太太，有阜康这块金字招牌，存款不必我去兜揽，自会送上门来。我的做法，就是要把我们的这块金字招牌擦得晶光丈亮，不好有一点点不干净的地方。款子存在汇丰，倒贴利息，就是我保护金字招牌的办法。』『嗯！嗯！』螺蛳太太想了一会说∶『你的意思是阜康有廿几万银子在汇丰，不去动它，显得阜康的头寸很宽裕，人家就放心不来提存了。』『一点不错。螺蛳太太，你真是内行。』宓本常举一举杯，自己喝了一大口，得意之情，溢于言表。

“The road to death. A rope, or three mace of opium. I could repay the Master only in my next life.” Shrewd as she was, Madam Luosi could not help being moved by this carefully contrived display of loyalty and responsibility. “Mr Mi, you must not talk like that! So long as you do your utmost in good faith, things cannot fail to turn out well. The Master knows how loyal you are.” “For his sake, even offending people is worthwhile.” Mi immediately brought the conversation back to his point. “With Fukang’s golden signboard, I need not go out soliciting deposits. They come to our door of their own accord. My method is to polish that signboard until it positively shines, without the smallest blemish. Keeping money at HSBC and paying the difference in interest is how I protect our reputation.” “I see, I see.” She thought for a moment. “You mean that leaving those two hundred thousand taels and more untouched at HSBC makes Fukang appear amply supplied with ready money. People feel reassured and do not come to withdraw their deposits.” “Exactly! Madam Luosi, you are a true expert.” Mi raised his cup and took a long drink, unable to conceal his satisfaction.

『原来有这样一招在里面。说起来也是迫不得已。』『先是迫不得已，后来我才悟出诀窍，实在是正当的做法，就银根不紧，也应该这么办。有一回法大马路周道台的五姨太来提款，我说∶你是不是要转存汇丰？如果要存汇丰，我打汇丰的票子给你，转帐不但方便，而且进出不必「贴水」，比较划算。螺蛳太太，你道她听了我的话怎么说？』『我猜不着。她怎么说？』

“So that is the trick. Necessity drove you to it, then.” “At first it was necessity. Later I understood the principle and saw that it was really the right thing to do. We ought to do it even when money is not tight. Once the fifth concubine of Intendant Zhou, who lives on French Main Road, came to withdraw some money. I asked her, ‘Are you transferring it to HSBC? If so, I can give you a draft on HSBC. The transfer will be convenient, and you will lose nothing in exchange deductions when the money goes out and comes in. It will be more economical.’ Madam Luosi, what do you think she said?” “I cannot guess. What did she say?”

『她说∶算了，算了。我们老爷说，现在市面上银根紧，阜康只怕要紧要慢的时候，没有现银，不如存到外国银行。现在听人你这样子说，我倒不好意思了。还是存在你们这里好了。螺蛳太太，我当时悟出一个诀窍，我们这块金字招牌，要用外国货的擦铜油来擦。啥叫外国货的擦铜油，就是跟外国银行往来，我要到所有外国银行去开户头，象遇到周家五姨太那种来提存的户头，我问她要哪家外国银行的票子，说哪家就是哪家；这一下阜康的招牌不是更响了。』螺蛳太太因为他的话中听，所以能够深入，这时听出来一个疑问∶『法子是蛮好，不过这一来不是有大笔头寸搁在那里了？』『哪里，哪里！』宓本常乱摇着双手，『那样做法不是太笨了？』『不笨怎么办？』『这里头又有诀窍了。每家银行开个户头，存个三两千银子；等开出票子，我先一步把头寸调足送进去，就不会穿帮了。』『来得及吗？』

“She said, ‘Never mind, then. My husband says money is tight and Fukang might not have ready silver when we urgently need it, so a foreign bank would be safer. But after what you have said, I feel rather ashamed. I shall leave it with you.’ Madam Luosi, that taught me a trick. We must polish our golden signboard with foreign metal polish. What is that polish? Dealings with foreign banks. I mean to open an account with every one of them. If a customer like Intendant Zhou’s fifth concubine comes to withdraw money, I shall ask which foreign bank’s draft she wants. Whichever she names, she shall have it. Would that not make Fukang’s name more impressive still?” Madam Luosi found his argument persuasive and followed it closely. Now a question occurred to her. “A fine method, but would it not leave large sums lying idle in those banks?” Mi waved both hands. “No, no! That would be foolish.” “What is the clever way, then?” “There is another trick. Open an account at each bank and deposit two or three thousand taels. When a draft is issued, I arrange to send in enough funds before it is presented, and nothing goes wrong.” “Can you do it in time?”

『来得及，来得及。喏，这就是德律风根的好处，拿起话筒摇过去，说有这么一回事，那里的行员，自会替我们应付。』螺蛳太太听他的谈论，学到很多东西；中国钱庄经营的要诀，她听胡雪岩谈过几回，并不外行，但外国银行的情形，却不知其详，这时听宓本常说得头头是道，遇事留心的她，自然不肯放弃机会，所以接上来便问，是如何应付？人家又为什么会替阜康应付？『应付的法子多得很，不过万变不离其宗，就是拖一拖辰光，等我们把头寸调齐补足。』『万一调不齐呢。』『不错，不怕一万，只怕万一。这种情形，从来没有过，不过不能不防。说到这上头，就靠平常的交际，外国银行的「康白度」，我都有交情的；那班「洋行小鬼」，平时也要常常应酬，所以万一遇到头寸调不齐，只要我通知一声，他们会替我代垫。这是事先说好了的，代垫照算拆息，日子最多三天。』宓本常特为又重复一句∶『不过，这种情形从来没有过。』『喔，』螺蛳太太又问∶『我们跟哪几家外国银行有往来？』统统有。『

“Certainly. That is where the telephone comes in. Lift the receiver, ring them up, and explain. Their staff will find a way to accommodate us.” Madam Luosi was learning a great deal. Hu had explained the essentials of Chinese banking several times, so she was no novice; foreign banks, however, were less familiar. Hearing Mi speak so confidently, she would not miss the chance to learn. How did the staff accommodate Fukang, and why should they do so? “There are many ways, but the principle is always the same: gain a little time until we have assembled and supplied the funds.” “What if you cannot?” “Exactly. However unlikely, the worst possibility must be allowed for. It has never happened, but we must be prepared. Then we depend on ordinary social connections. I know all the foreign banks’ compradors and regularly entertain their junior Chinese clerks too. If ever I cannot assemble the money, I need only send word and they will advance it. That is agreed beforehand. We pay the usual short-term interest, and the advance may last no more than three days.” He repeated emphatically, “But it has never happened.” “Which foreign banks do we deal with?” she asked. “All of them.”

接下来，宓本常便屈指细数。上海的外国银行，最有名的是英文名称叫做『香港上海银行有限公司』的汇丰银行，但最老的却是有利银行，咸丰四年便已开办；不过后来居上的却是麦加利银行，这家银行的英文名称叫做∶Ｃｈａｒｔｅｒｃｄ－ＢａｎｋｏｆＩｎｄｉａ，ＡｕｓｔｒａｌｉａａｎｄＣｈｉｎａ。但香港分行与上海分行的译名不同，香港照音译，称为渣打银行；上海的银钱业嫌它叫起来不响，而且顾名不能思义，所以用他总经理麦加利的名字，称之为麦加利银行。『麦加利是英国女皇下圣旨设立的，不过这家银行是专门为了英国人在印度、澳洲同我们中国经商所开的，重在存放款跟汇兑，纯然是商业银行，跟汇丰银行带点官派的味道不大一样。』宓本常又说∶『自从左大人到两京，大先生亦不经手偿洋债了，我们阜康跟汇丰的关系就淡了。所以我现在是向麦加利下工夫。这一点顺便拜托罗四太太告诉大先生。』『好的，我晓得了。』螺蛳太太对宓本常的长袖善舞，印象颇为深刻；观感当然也改变了，觉得他是为了本身的职司，要对得起老板，就免不了得罪朋友。不过，自己是在古应春面前夸下海口，要来替他出气。如今搞成个虎头蛇尾，似乎愧对古应春。

Mi began counting them off. Shanghai’s best-known foreign bank was HSBC, whose English name was the Hongkong and Shanghai Banking Company Limited. The oldest, however, was the Mercantile Bank, opened in the fourth year of Xianfeng. The Chartered Bank had come later but overtaken its rivals. Its full English name was the Chartered Bank of India, Australia and China. Its Hong Kong and Shanghai branches nevertheless had different Chinese names. Hong Kong used the phonetic “Zhada,” while Shanghai bankers considered that insufficiently resonant and uninformative. They called it Maijiali, after its manager. “It was established by a royal charter from the British queen. But it exists specifically to serve British trade in India, Australia, and China. Its main business is deposits, loans, and remittances—a purely commercial bank, unlike HSBC, which has something of an official air. Since His Excellency Zuo came to Liangjiang, Mr Hu no longer handles foreign-debt repayments, and Fukang’s connection with HSBC has weakened. I am now concentrating on the Chartered Bank. Please mention that to Mr Hu, Madam Luo.” “I shall.” Mi’s skill in managing his connections impressed her and changed her opinion. In fulfilling his duties and serving his employer, he could hardly avoid offending friends. Yet she had promised Gu that she would obtain satisfaction for him. To begin boldly and end feebly would seem a betrayal.

这样转着念头，脸上自不免流露出为难的神气。善于察言观色的宓本常便即问道∶『罗四太太，你是不是有啥话，好象不大肯说，不要紧的，我跟大先生多年，就同晚辈一样；罗四太太，你是长辈，如果我有啥不对，请你尽管说！我是、我是——掉句书袋，叫做「有则改之，无则加勉」。』螺蛳太太听他的话很诚恳，觉得稍为透露也不妨，于是很含蓄地说∶『你没有啥不对，大先生把阜康交给你，你当然顾牢阜康，这是天经地义。不过，有时候朋友的事，也要顾一顾，到底大家都是在一条船上的人。』这一下等于是泄了底，螺蛳太太是为了他勒住该付古应春的款子来兴师问罪，当即认错，表示歉意∶『是！是！我对应春，是想到阜康是大先生事业的命脉，处理得稍为过分了一点；其实公是公、私是私！我同他的交情是不会变的。如今请罗四太太说一句我应该怎么样同他赔不是？我一定遵命。』『赔不是的话是严重了。』螺蛳太太忽然灵机一动∶『眼前倒有个能顾全你们交情的机会。』她朝外看了一下，没有再说下去。宓本常稍为想一想，便能领悟，是指古应春纳宠而言。她刚才看一看，是防着瑞香会听见。『我懂了。我来办；好好替他热闹热闹。』

Her difficulty showed in her face. Quick to read an expression, Mi asked, “Is there something you hesitate to say? Please do not. After so many years with Mr Hu, I am almost one of the younger generation. You are my elder. If I have done anything wrong, tell me. To borrow a literary phrase, I shall correct any fault I have, and guard against those I have not.” His apparent sincerity persuaded her that a hint could do no harm. “You have done nothing wrong. Mr Hu entrusted Fukang to you; naturally you must protect it. But sometimes you must consider a friend’s affairs too.
